It lets her cross Dark Aether without clinging to Light Crystals and Light Beacons, cutting the atmosphere's drain from five units of energy a second to one, but it gives no protection against Ingstorms or Dark Water.

Light Suit. A fusion of Luminoth and Chozo battlesuit technology, given to Samus by U-Mos in the Main Energy Controller as thanks for returning light energy to the Agon, Torvus and Sanctuary temples after Quadraxis fell. It is pearl-colored, with openings that emit beams of light and four circles on the chest that glow brightly in Dark Aether, and its visor is colorless and transparent, showing her face. The Light Suit is fully resistant to Dark Aether: she can submerge in Dark Water and stand in Ingstorms unharmed, though light and dark attacks still hurt her. It also lets her ride the Luminoth light-based teleportation system between restored Energy Controllers, and it was needed to gather the Sky Temple Keys before the assault on the Emperor Ing (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes).

Neither suit appears on later missions. Apart from its color, the Light Suit closely resembles the form Dark Samus took during the Phazon crisis. See The Luminoth, U-Mos, Aether and Dark Aether, and The Ing and the Emperor Ing.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Light_Suit</sub>


### Equipment: Energy Tanks and suit energy

*Keys:* Energy Tank, Energy Tanks, E-Tank, E-Tanks, Reserve Tank, Reserve Tanks, Energy Part, Energy Parts, suit energy, energy capacity, low on energy, energy reserves


Energy is the power Samus's Power Suit uses for every function; its shielding draws on it, and she needs it to keep the armor running and to survive. Every hit from a creature or hazard drains it, and if it falls to zero the suit fails and Samus goes down. Energy is replenished by the small energy orbs that destroyed creatures and broken objects release, by absorbed X parasites, by Phazon units during the Phazon crisis, by Recharge Stations and the Bottle Ship's Navigation Booths, and by Energy Tanks at the moment they are acquired.

Energy Tanks. The Chozo-built Energy Tank is the most common expansion to the suit and has turned up on every mission, in Chozo Statues and item pods, in hidden chambers on Tallon IV and Aether, in the sectors of the BSL station and across ZDR. Each raises her maximum energy by 100 units and refills her on acquisition ("Energy Tank acquired. Energy capacity increased."). The tanks show as a row of small cells at the top of her visor display. They are never required to finish a mission, only to survive it comfortably, and like her other enhancements they are lost whenever the suit is stripped.

Reserve Tanks. On her return to Zebes, Samus found four Reserve Tanks, her emergency energy source (per the manual), each holding 100 units and empty when found. Set to automatic, a Reserve Tank poured itself into her suit the instant her energy hit zero; set to manual, she drew on it at will (Super Metroid).

Energy Parts. On the Bottle Ship and on ZDR the tanks also came in pieces: collect four Energy Parts to create one Energy Tank, sixteen Parts in all aboard the Bottle Ship.

Energy, Phazon and Dark Aether. The PED Suit demanded the energy of one full Energy Tank to enter Hypermode, and on Phaaze its emergency vent emptied every tank she carried to slow her corruption. On Dark Aether the atmosphere itself drank five units a second until the Dark Suit slowed it to one and the Light Suit stopped it. The Energy Cells of the Phazon crisis are unrelated: they are power units for the derelict G.F.S. Valhalla, not suit energy. See Save, Recharge, Map and Navigation stations.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Energy_Tank · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Reserve_Tank · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Energy_Part · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Energy · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran</sub>


### Equipment: Flash Shift, Phantom Cloak and Slide (Dread-era movement)

*Keys:* Flash Shift, Flash Shifting, Phantom Cloak, Phantom Cloaking, cloaking, invisibility, slide under, slide through, sliding under, E.M.M.I. Zone, EMMI Zone


On ZDR (Metroid Dread) Samus moved differently from any earlier mission. Raven Beak defeated her on arrival and stripped her suit of most of its abilities, so she rebuilt her mobility piece by piece, and two of the pieces drew on Aeion rather than on suit power.

The Slide needs no equipment. Samus drops into a sliding run that carries her through tight gaps and under obstacles; in Artaria she could slide beneath the ribs of Corpius, and the Federation's brief on the planet sums up her repertoire as parkour over obstacles, sliding through tight spaces and countering enemies. The Morph Ball later took over the smallest holes.

The Flash Shift, found in Burenia, is an Aeion ability that moves her to either side almost instantly, a short-range teleport she can chain up to three times in quick succession. It exists for getting out of trouble: away from a strike, across a gap, past a threat. She needed it before she could reach the Central Unit in Dairon that stood between her and the Speed Booster.

The Phantom Cloak is the Aeion ability that makes her fully invisible and silent, at the price of slowed movement, and it is her answer to the E.M.M.I. The hunting units track her by sight and by the noise she makes, and every one of them can be fooled by the cloak while it holds; dropping it, or running the Aeion gauge dry, exposes her again. On ZDR Aeion regenerates once she acts, but she cannot draw on an ability while the gauge refills, and an ability she has switched off cannot be reactivated until the gauge is full, so timing the cloak and the shift mattered as much as having them.

The rest of her ZDR mobility came from elsewhere: the Spider Magnet from E.M.M.I.-02SM in Artaria, the Morph Ball from E.M.M.I.-03MB in Cataris, the Speed Booster from E.M.M.I.-04SB in Dairon, the Space Jump in Ferenia, the Screw Attack in Artaria, and the Pulse Radar for finding hidden routes.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aeion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/ZDR · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Artaria · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Speed_Booster · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/E.M.M.I. · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread</sub>


### Equipment: Fusion Suit

*Keys:* Fusion Suit, Fusion Suits, organic suit, Metroid vaccine, the vaccine, vulnerable to cold, weakness to cold, cold weakness, infected suit, surgically removed


The Fusion Suit is the organic armor Samus wore after the X parasites nearly killed her, so named for her fusion with Metroid cells.

Origin. While escorting Biologic Space Laboratories researchers on SR388, Samus was infected by an X parasite; she lost consciousness, her gunship crashed, and the BSL crew brought her to a Galactic Federation hospital. The X had invaded her central nervous system, and the organic components of her Power Suit had become so integrated with her body that the suit could not be fully removed while she was unconscious. Surgeons cut away large infected portions and sent them to the BSL station for study; the X inside that capsule later took her form as the SA-X. A vaccine made from the cells of the baby Metroid cured her, letting her absorb X for nourishment and leaving her with the Metroids' vulnerability to cold.

The suit. Rebuilt around what remained, the Fusion Suit is slimmer and less bulky than the Power Suit, blue over bright yellow, and far weaker: most of its systems and weapons were gone, the Ice Beam was incompatible with her new physiology, cold struck her harder than before, and its Charge Beam released spreading waves rather than one burst. In exchange she could pull herself onto ledges without the Power Grip, and any X that touched her was absorbed, restoring energy and returning whatever capability its host had carried. The Varia Suit data, swallowed by the Mega Core-X in Sector 2, came back when she absorbed it and recolored the suit yellow-green over magenta; the Gravity Suit added a purple scheme. In the final hours of the BSL incident the SA-X attacked the Omega Metroid that had cornered her and was shattered; Samus absorbed its Core-X, regaining the Ice Beam and an orange-and-yellow suit that recalled her old Varia Suit (Metroid Fusion).

Aftermath. Samus kept the organic suit after the station fell into SR388. Over the years that followed it gradually returned toward its original mechanical Power Suit form, which is why her armor on ZDR again looked like the suit of old (Metroid Dread). She was never cured of the Metroid in her. See Samus Aran – Metroid DNA, SA-X, X Parasites and The BSL incident.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Fusion_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SA-X · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Varia_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.nintendo.com/en-gb/News/2021/July/Metroid-Dread-Report-Vol-3-Seven-points-that-define-the-2D-saga-2007780.html</sub>


### Equipment: Grapple Beam

*Keys:* Grapple Beam, Grapple Beams, Grapple Lasso, Grapple Swing, Grapple Voltage, Hyper Grapple, Ship Grapple, Grapple Point, Grapple Points, Grapple Block, grappling


The Grapple Beam is a tether of energy that latches onto Grapple Points, Grapple Blocks and other magnetic anchors and lets Samus swing across gaps she could never jump. On most of her suits it fires from the Arm Cannon; on the suits she wore on Tallon IV, Aether and through the Phazon crisis it was an attachment on her left arm, the hand she otherwise rarely uses. Besides swinging, it can hold her still in midair, grab small creatures, pull open hatches and, per the original manual, seize metallic blocks with holes in their centers.

She recovered it in Norfair after killing Crocomire, on her return to Zebes, and with it crossed to the Wave Beam and made short work of Draygon. On Tallon IV the Frigate Orpheon explosion took it and she retrieved it from Storage Depot B in the Phazon Mines, solving a Spider Ball track while Metroids closed in; that version fired slowly but could hook the magnetic undersides of Gliders. On Aether the Grapple Guardian in Torvus Bog held it. Aboard the Bottle Ship, Adam Malkovich authorized it through her visor when she spotted a Grapple Point above a Rhedogian holding Anthony Higgs over lava in Sector 3 (Pyrosphere), and she swung in to knock him free; later Vorash swallowed a Grapple Point, which lodged in its throat, and she hauled the creature onto a platform by its mouth. On ZDR it waited in Artaria.

During the Phazon crisis the beam grew into a family of tools, each a green-glowing emitter on her left glove:
- Grapple Lasso (yellow), found in the first room of Norion: a lasso she hurls at shimmering objects to tear away blast shields, strip armor and shields from Shield Pirate Troopers and Gragnol Adults, and wrench at Rundas, Omega Ridley and Aurora Unit 313.
- Grapple Swing (blue), earned on Bryyo while fending off Reptilicus: the classic swing, with the freedom to fire while hanging or to hold fast, as to SkyTown's zipline.
- Ship Grapple Beam (gray): the Gunship's counterpart, directed through the Command Visor to carry heavy objects; it assembled the Theronian Bomb that broke the shield of Elysia's Leviathan.
- Grapple Voltage (purple), dropped by Gandrayda on the Pirate Homeworld: a conduit that draws energy out of a target into Samus, refilling her slowly, or forces her energy into it, draining her quickly; either way it can kill, and it powers up or shuts down machinery that nothing else can touch.
- Hyper Grapple (Phazon-colored), taken after Omega Ridley in Bryyo's Leviathan: Grapple Voltage running on Phazon, needed to reach the portal to Phaaze, where she used it to vent excess Phazon into Phaz-Ings and keep her own corruption in check.

On Viewros the lock-on that steadies her aim also guides the Grapple Beam between platforms.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Grapple_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Grapple_Lasso · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Grapple_Voltage · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Upgrade · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond</sub>


### Equipment: Gravity Suit

*Keys:* Gravity Suit, Gravity Suits, Gravity Feature, Gravity Boost, Hazard Shield, underwater movement, move underwater, purple suit, extreme cold


The Gravity Suit is the Chozo enhancement that frees Samus from the drag of liquids and gravity. Wearing it she moves, jumps and fires underwater as freely as on land, in most places she can cross magma unharmed, and incoming damage is reduced. It shades the armor purple; on the Bottle Ship, where it was built in as the Gravity Feature, it instead surrounded her with a purple aura.

History and limits:
- On her first Zebes mission a Chozo Statue in Chozodia held it, but her original armor could only register it as unknown; it activated once the Ruins Test granted her the Legendary Power Suit. It negated lava but not the liquid of Tourian (Metroid: Zero Mission).
- On Tallon IV she took it from the Gravity Chamber in Phendrana Drifts; it sharpened her underwater vision and let her explore the sunken Frigate Orpheon, though magma still burned her there.
- Aether had no Gravity Suit; the Luminoth Gravity Boost from Torvus Bog gave underwater lift and no protection. During the Phazon crisis the Hazard Shield, a unit she stole on the Pirate Homeworld, filled the role: pulsing purple hexagonal plating on her shoulders and an orange outline let her endure Acid Rain and Fuel Gel.
- On her return to Zebes it lay just outside the Wrecked Ship, usable only after Phantoon fell and power returned; Maridia and Ridley's lair were impassable without it.
- The Gravity Feature switched on when the module holding Sector Zero detached from the Bottle Ship; unusually, lava still hurt her there.
- On the BSL station it let the Speed Booster and Space Jump work underwater and in the magma of Sector 3; on the Fusion Suit it wore a purple scheme.
- On ZDR she took it from Burenia; there it also shielded her from extreme cold and let her touch magma, and during the final battle with Raven Beak it became the Metroid Suit.

Samus relies on it so often that she treats water as a passage rather than a wall, and together with the Varia Suit it cuts the damage she takes to a fraction.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gravity_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hazard_Shield · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Legendary_Power_Suit · https://www.nintendo.com/en-gb/News/2021/September/Metroid-Dread-Report-Vol-8-Surveying-Planet-ZDR-2043940.html</sub>


### Equipment: Hi-Jump Boots, Space Jump and Wall Jump

*Keys:* Hi-Jump Boots, High Jump Boots, Hi-Jump, High Jump, Space Jump, Space Jump Boots, Wall Jump, wall jumping, Kick Climb, double jump, spin jump, somersault


Hi-Jump Boots (formerly written High Jump Boots) raise Samus's jump height; per the original manual they let her jump one and a half times higher, per the SR388 manual more than twice as high. She found them in Norfair on Zebes on both her Zebes missions and regained them on the BSL station by destroying Zazabi in Sector 2 (TRO). On the first Zebes mission and on the BSL station the boots also carried the Spring Ball, letting the Morph Ball hop.

The Space Jump lets Samus keep somersaulting through the air, chaining spin jumps indefinitely; it engages just after a spin jump peaks. It debuted on SR388 and has been part of nearly every mission since. On the return to Zebes it lay in Maridia; on the first Zebes mission the Chozo cache in Kraid's domain held it, but her suit reported only "Unknown item" until she wore the Legendary Power Suit from Chozodia. On the BSL station she took it from Yakuza, the spider-like X mimic in the Reactor Silo of the Main Deck, whose own leaps resembled the ability. On the Bottle Ship she activated the Space Jump and Screw Attack on her own authority while chasing the Deleter, though that suit's version only sustained her height. On the SR388 extermination mission the Diggernaut ejected the Space Jump after she hid from it in a cave in Area 4. On ZDR it waited in Ferenia. In the Prime-era suits the equivalent is the Space Jump Boots, which grant a single extra mid-air jump and only after a jump from the ground, never after walking off a ledge; she found them in the Alcove of Tallon Overworld, lost them to a Warrior Ing on Aether and reclaimed them from the Jump Guardian in Dark Agon Wastes, and had them throughout the Phazon crisis, where the Screw Attack extended them into several more spinning jumps.

The Wall Jump is technique, not equipment. Samus kicks off a wall for extra height, and she has done it on every mission since the return to Zebes without any special gear, apart from the Prime-era suits, where the Screw Attack provides it on designated Wall Jump Surfaces. On Zebes she could chain kicks up a single wall; the Etecoons and the Dachora of Brinstar perform the same move. On the Bottle Ship it is called the Kick Climb (hold toward the wall and jump with good timing to "kick your way up the wall", per the manual). On ZDR she can also wall-jump off a Spider Magnet surface and cling to it again.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hi-Jump_Boots · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Jump · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Jump_Boots · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Wall_Jump · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Yakuza · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns</sub>


### Equipment: Ice Beam

*Keys:* Ice Beam, Ice Beams, Ice-Beam, freeze beam, freezing beam, frozen enemies, frozen solid, freeze ray


The Ice Beam is a Chozo enhancement for the Arm Cannon that fires frigid energy. A target that absorbs enough of it freezes solid; frozen enemies can be shattered with a Missile or used as footholds to reach otherwise unreachable heights, and weak creatures simply die outright. Because Metroids are acutely sensitive to cold, the Ice Beam is their classic counter: a frozen Metroid cracked open by Missiles is one of the few ways to kill one, and on her first Zebes mission Samus could not have cleared the Metroids of Tourian without it.

The Chozo left it in several places. On Zebes it waited in Brinstar and in a hidden corner of Norfair during her first mission, reached with the Power Grip, and in Norfair past a stretch demanding the Speed Booster on her return. On SR388 she found one in Chozo ruins behind a Red Door early on, and another in the deepest region, where juvenile Metroids could only be hurt while frozen. On Tallon IV it lay in the Antechamber of the Chozo Ruins; that version fired slow shots, froze foes on a charged hit and fed the Ice Spreader combo. Aboard the Bottle Ship, Adam Malkovich authorized it in Sector 3 (Pyrosphere); there freezing was often partial, locking a Dessgeega's legs or a Zebesian's claws, and frozen bodies dropped under gravity rather than hanging in place. On Aether and during the Phazon crisis she carried no Ice Beam at all, relying on the Luminoth Dark Beam and on Ice Missiles instead.

The BSL incident gave the weapon a personal edge. She arrived with it, lost it to the X, and then learned that the Metroid vaccine that had saved her life had also made her vulnerable to cold: her altered body could not tolerate an Ice Beam, so the Galactic Federation issued Ice Missiles in its place. The SA-X, wearing her stolen suit, turned the Ice Beam against her again and again. Only after she absorbed the SA-X's Core-X was her cellular structure stable enough to wield it, and she used it at once to freeze and kill the Omega Metroid in the station's docking bay.

Per the manga, a single panel shows her pouring Ice, Wave and Plasma fire into Ridley. The Federation's 07th Platoon carried Freeze Guns as their equivalent on the Bottle Ship. Ice is, in short, the weapon her guardians left against the Metroids, and the one her own Metroid blood briefly denied her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ice_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ice_Missile · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Missile</sub>


### Equipment: Ice, Diffusion, Seeker and Storm Missiles

*Keys:* Ice Missile, Ice Missiles, Diffusion Missile, Diffusion Missiles, Seeker Missile, Seeker Missiles, Seeker Launcher, Storm Missile, Storm Missiles, Hyper Missile, Hyper Missiles, Multi-Lock


Ice Missiles freeze what they hit while delivering a Missile's blast, and Metroids succumb to the cold faster than anything else. The Galactic Federation issued them to Samus on the BSL station, in a Sector 5 (ARC) Data Room, because it feared the Metroid DNA spliced into her would make her reject an Ice Beam; frozen foes served her as stepping stones to unreachable heights, and once she had Super Missiles those froze as well. During the Phazon crisis she took Ice Missiles from Rundas after defeating him in the Temple of Bryyo; they froze enemies and flowing Fuel Gel, broke White Blast Shields and set up frozen targets to be shattered by a charged beam. On ZDR, E.M.M.I.-05IM in Ghavoran yielded them.

The Diffusion Missile, downloaded on the BSL in Sector 4 (AQA) soon after she destroyed Nightmare, is a charged Missile: held in the launcher and released, it bursts across a wide radius and destroys everything within reach. Fired with Ice Missiles it freezes every freezable creature in sight, much as the Ice Spreader did on Tallon IV. Each shot costs one Missile.

The Seeker Missile locks onto up to five targets at once, or five times onto one, and fires a Missile at each; the longer she holds the lock, the more hits land. Its Luminoth form, the Seeker Launcher, was once the weapon of the champion A-Kul and lay sealed in a vault in the Hall of Honored Dead; A-Voq's message urged her to "claim this weapon from its sacred vault." It breaks Multi-Lock Blast Shields and overwhelms regenerating weak points such as those of Mekenobites and the adult Chykka, though Pirate Commandos can evade it. During the Phazon crisis she recovered one in the Xenoresearch Labs of SkyTown. Aboard the Bottle Ship she obtained the Seeker Missile Launcher in Sector 1 (Biosphere) on her own, without Adam Malkovich's authorization; there a fully charged volley loosed a Super Missile at the main target while the extra Missiles acquired targets by themselves.

The Storm Missile is the ZDR answer to the same need: absorbed from the Core-X left by Escue in Ferenia, it locks onto as many as five enemies or blocks and fires three Missiles at each in rapid succession, breaks Storm Missile boxes and can hurt enemies sheathed in electricity.

The Hyper Missile was a child of Phazon: taken from Helios in the core of Elysia's Leviathan, usable only in Hypermode, a Phazon-packed warhead that flies straight without homing, blasts an area around the impact and hits like ten ordinary Missiles while consuming half her Phazon reserve. It was her answer to Omega Ridley and Aurora Unit 313.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ice_Missile · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Diffusion_Missile · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Seeker_Missile · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hyper_Missile · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Upgrade · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/ZDR</sub>


### Equipment: Luminoth and Phazon beams (Dark, Light, Annihilator, Phazon, Nova)

*Keys:* Dark Beam, Light Beam, Annihilator Beam, Annihilator, Phazon Beam, Nova Beam, Entangler, Lightblast, Disruptor, Dark Ammo, Light Ammo, Luminoth beams


The Luminoth of Aether built beams to fight the Ing. Having identified the Ing's attacks as dark energy, they first built the Dark Beam, meaning to overload energy-based foes; their Lore records the error: "the Dark Beam was not of great effect on the Ing." The Light Beam that followed worked, and I-Sha wrote that all the Ing fear its power. The Annihilator Beam, an advanced design uniting both energies, belonged to the Luminoth champion A-Kul; when she fell the Ing took it, and O-Lir's record laments that "the strongest weapon of our champion is in the hands of the enemy." Samus recovered all three during the Aether incident: the Dark Beam from the Space Pirates' base in Agon Wastes after her first defeat of Dark Samus, the Light Beam in Dark Agon Wastes, and the Annihilator from the wreck of Quadraxis in the Ing Hive.
- Dark Beam: slow bolts of dark matter that hinder a target and, with repeated hits, encase it; the charged Entangler wraps targets in shadow tendrils at once and spreads to their neighbors. It does heavy damage to Light Aether creatures, opens portals into Dark Aether, extinguishes Light Crystals and opens purple hatches. One Dark Ammo per shot, five for the Entangler; the Darkburst is its combo.
- Light Beam: ignites and keeps burning what it touches, with extra damage to dark creatures; the charged Lightblast is a shotgun spread that homes hard enough to knock Dark Samus out of her Boost Ball. It opens portals back to Aether, energizes Light Crystals and opens white hatches. One Light Ammo per shot, five for the Lightblast; the Sunburst is its combo.
- Annihilator Beam: homing shots that need no lock, one Light and one Dark Ammo apiece; the charged Disruptor (five of each) does not home but stuns. It carries a sonic component that, with the Echo Visor, operates sonic devices, and it supercharges Light Crystals into Super Crystals that lure Ing to their deaths. The Sonic Boom is its combo.

Phazon gave Samus two beams of its own. In the Impact Crater on Tallon IV, the second form of the creature Metroid Prime expelled pools of pure Phazon; standing in one, her Phazon Suit entered a hyper state and the Arm Cannon poured out the Phazon Beam, the only thing that could hurt it. On Aether she finished Dark Samus by charging her cannon to draw in the Phazon blasts Dark Samus expelled and firing them back. During the Phazon crisis, corrupted by Dark Samus and fitted with the Federation's PED Suit, she could enter Hypermode at will and turn the Power Beam into a Phazon Hyper Beam: a continuous stream when charged, the most destructive fire she has ever commanded, paid for from her own Phazon reserve, with the Hyper Missile, Hyper Ball and Hyper Grapple beside it. All of it ended when Phaaze died and Phazon ceased to exist.

The Nova Beam is Space Pirate work. On the Pirate Homeworld it sat inside a mining mechanism that pulled Phazon from rock, its high frequency reaching Phazon that Phazite plating otherwise shielded; Samus took it after destroying the mechanism during a major Pirate confrontation. Stronger than the Plasma Beam, it keeps the Power Beam's rate of fire and the Plasma Beam's welding heat, and used with the X-Ray Visor it passes through Phazite to strike internal organs, felling Metroid Hatchers, Phazon Metroids, Omega Ridley and the Infant Leviathan through their critical points.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Light_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Annihilator_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hyper_Beam_(Phazon) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Nova_Beam</sub>


### Equipment: Melee Counter

*Keys:* Melee Counter, Melee Counters, Finish Counter, counterattack, counter-attack, parry, parried, parrying, melee strike


The Melee Counter is a close-quarters technique Samus brought to her extermination mission on SR388: a hard swing of the Arm Cannon timed against a creature's lunge. Many creatures of SR388 and ZDR telegraph a physical attack with a brief flash; if she strikes at that instant the blow is turned aside and the attacker is left stunned and open. Against a stunned Metroid her next shot comes out fully charged, and she can pour several Missiles into the opening. Each successful counter on SR388 also shed Aeion Orbs that refilled her Aeion reserve. On the same mission she gained full freedom of aim, able to fire in any direction rather than the limited angles of earlier suits.

On ZDR the counter became a matter of survival. An E.M.M.I. that seizes her drives a needle toward her chest; she is given one extremely brief window, then a second, to counter and break free, and failure means the needle goes through her and she dies on the spot. She escaped E.M.M.I.-01P this way at the outset. The Chozo Soldiers of the Mawkin, and Raven Beak himself, fight in the same rhythm, and a missed Finish Counter against them throws her back hard, survivable only while she has energy to spare. She can counter even while clinging to a magnetic surface with the Spider Magnet.

Samus's style has always leaned on distance, armor and overwhelming fire; the counter is the proof that she is just as dangerous inside arm's reach, and on ZDR it is the only answer she has to a machine her weapons cannot scratch until the Omega Cannon is in her hands.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/E.M.M.I. · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns</sub>


### Equipment: Metroid Suit (Dread)

*Keys:* Metroid Suit, full Metroid, becoming a Metroid, Metroid transformation, Metroid powers, energy drain, drain energy, draining energy, Quiet Robe X


The Metroid Suit is the form Samus's armor took on ZDR when the Metroid DNA from her vaccine awakened in full.

Awakening. Raven Beak, who lured Samus to ZDR, wanted her Metroid genes active: he spared her life so that her Metroid powers would surface, intending to clone her into an army of Metroids under his control. After Quiet Robe's death and Raven Beak's revelations, Samus found she could drain the energy of other beings by touch, as a Metroid does; she used it to destroy the last E.M.M.I., but each use pushed her closer to becoming a Metroid herself.

Transformation. During the final battle aboard Itorash the full awakening came: her Gravity Suit changed into the Metroid Suit, an organic armor with claw-like natural spikes and glowing patches of green and red that evoke a Metroid's body. In it, all of her Arm Cannon's powers merged into the Hyper Beam, a wide red-pink beam that pierces terrain, and anything she touched was drained of its energy. She drained Itorash itself, sending it crashing into ZDR, and destroyed the X that had infected Raven Beak's body.

Cost and cure. With ZDR tearing itself apart, Adam warned her not to board her gunship: in that state her touch would drain the ship's power. The X that had taken the form of Quiet Robe, a Thoha Chozo, appeared aboard the gunship and let itself be absorbed into her; the Thoha genes it carried stabilized her Metroid DNA, and the Metroid Suit reverted to her normal armor, so she could lift off before the planet exploded (Metroid Dread). The Metroid Suit has not appeared since, but the Metroid within her remains, and Samus left ZDR knowing the Thoha's gift is all that keeps it in check. See Samus as the last Metroid, Raven Beak, Quiet Robe and The X on ZDR.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://www.inverse.com/gaming/metroid-dread-ending-explained-metroid-suit-rewards · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread</sub>


### Equipment: Missiles and Super Missiles

*Keys:* Missile Launcher, Missile Tank, Missile Tanks, Missile Expansion, Missile Expansions, Super Missile, Super Missiles, missile ammo, Red Door, red hatch, Ship Missile


Missiles are long-range concussive warheads, the one weapon Samus has carried on every mission. They require the Missile Launcher, an Arm Cannon attachment engaged by rotating her arm inside the housing, and each shot spends one unit of Missile Ammo. She starts a mission able to hold five; every Missile Expansion (Missile Tank) she recovers adds five more, up to 255 in most suits. Spent ammunition is replenished from the capsules fallen enemies leave behind, from supply crates and at Recharge Stations.

Their job is to break what beams cannot: Red Doors and red hatch locks, rock, Radion and Brinstone, the shielding on armored creatures. A frozen target struck by a Missile shatters, which is why ice and Missiles together are a Metroid's bane. On the BSL station the thorny membrane of a Core-X could only be ruptured by Missile fire. On Tallon IV, Aether and the worlds of the Phazon crisis a locked-on Missile homed on its target, and in the Alimbic Cluster hers were the only homing Missiles in the field. On Viewros they remain highly accurate, target-tracking projectiles. Aboard the Bottle Ship a Missile was a lock-on weapon and a fully charged volley spent five to loose a Super Missile. Adam Malkovich once remarked that with Missiles her chance of survival "climbs to 20%".

Super Missiles are the bulkier, green-tipped successor: roughly five times the force, with a shockwave that shakes a chamber and knocks wall-clinging creatures loose. They open green hatches and clear dense material such as Cordite. On her first Zebes mission the creature Imago yielded them; on her return a cache in Brinstar restored them. On Tallon IV and Aether they existed as a Charge Combo of the Power Beam, recovered in the Phendrana Drifts Observatory after she activated the Pirates' astronomical holographic projector, and in Torvus Temple. On the BSL station she downloaded them in a Sector 3 (PYR) Data Room and used them at once on the Security Robot B.O.X.; there they merged into her ordinary missile fire, each costing a single unit and later taking on the Ice Missiles' freezing. She found them again on SR388 during the extermination mission and on ZDR in Ghavoran.

Her Gunship has ordnance of its own. During the Phazon crisis the Galactic Federation furnished Ship Missiles at its landing site on Bryyo; targeted through the Command Visor, the ship made bombing runs against Maldium walls and other targets she could not reach, drawing on its own Ship Missile Ammo.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Missile · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Missile · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ship_Missile · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Charge_Combo · https://www.nintendo.com/us/whatsnew/available-now-explore-and-battle-across-an-unknown-planet-in-metroid-prime-4-beyond/</sub>


### Equipment: Morph Ball and Spring Ball

*Keys:* Morph Ball, Morphing Ball, Maru Mari, Morph Ball form, Morph Ball mode, morphball, Spring Ball, Jumpball, Jump Ball, ball form, roll into a ball, rolled into a ball


The Morph Ball is the function of Samus Aran's Power Suit that lets her collapse into an armored metallic sphere roughly one meter across and roll through passages she could never fit through standing. On her first mission it was called the Maru Mari, from the Japanese for "roll up", and on the return to Zebes the Morphing Ball; per the original manual Samus simply "grows small and round like a ball", and per the Zero Mission manual the suit curls into a ball form. The sphere takes on the look of whatever suit she is wearing (the Gravity and Phazon forms are visibly different), gravity still acts on it so inclines take effort to climb, and it is the base for a family of Chozo-built extensions: the Morph Ball Bomb and Power Bomb, the Spring Ball, the Boost Ball and the Spider Ball, and the Phazon-fed Hyper Ball she gained on Bryyo after destroying Mogenar during the Phazon crisis.

Samus has carried the Morph Ball on practically every mission, and it is usually among the first abilities she recovers when her suit is stripped. She found it on a pedestal in Brinstar on her first Zebes mission and on the same pedestal years later; aboard the Frigate Orpheon an explosion knocked it offline and she regained it in the Ruined Shrine of the Chozo Ruins on Tallon IV; on Aether the Ing swarm stole most of her systems but she kept the ball; on the BSL station it returned on the Main Deck after her suit had been cut away; on ZDR she reclaimed it by destroying E.M.M.I.-03MB in Cataris with the Omega Cannon; on Viewros it still worked. Adam Malkovich never restricted its use on the Bottle Ship, only the bombs it lays. Comparable alternate forms exist: each rival hunter of the Alimbic Cluster incident had one, so the technique is not unique to her.

The Spring Ball (called the Jumpball on the BSL station) lets the Morph Ball hop without laying a bomb. On SR388 and on the return to Zebes it was a separate acquisition; on the first Zebes mission and the BSL station it came bundled with the Hi-Jump Boots; during the Phazon crisis it came with the Morph Ball Bombs; it returned on the SR388 extermination mission. The Spider Magnet of ZDR does not work in ball form.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Morph_Ball · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Spring_Ball · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/E.M.M.I. · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Spider_Magnet · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond</sub>


### Equipment: Omega Cannon (Dread)

*Keys:* Omega Cannon, Omega Blaster, Omega Stream, Central Unit, Central Units, kill an E.M.M.I., destroy an E.M.M.I.


On ZDR, Samus's ordinary weapons could not scratch an E.M.M.I. Her only answer was the Omega Cannon, a temporary transformation of the Arm Cannon fueled by a mysterious energy she absorbs from a Central Unit, the armored brain-and-eye organism that presides over each E.M.M.I. Zone. A Central Unit fights in two phases, first armored and then exposed; when it falls from the ceiling the energy passes into her, the cannon's muzzle unfolds, and the weapon is live for exactly one machine. She received it first beside the already-fallen Central Unit of E.M.M.I.-01P, and once an E.M.M.I. is destroyed the cannon deactivates until the next Central Unit.

It fires two ways. The Omega Stream is a barrage of small projectiles, like a rotary gun, that slowly melts the faceplate shielding an E.M.M.I.'s head and slows the machine for as long as the stream stays on its face. The Omega Blaster is a charged shot, slower to build than a normal charge, that releases a massive white blast able to destroy an E.M.M.I.'s cores, which nothing else can harm; it can also break certain otherwise indestructible doors. A Blaster shot that lands anywhere but the exposed cores is wasted, so the pattern is fixed: stream to strip the faceplate, then plant the blast. She used it in this manner on E.M.M.I.-02SM and most of the others. The last, E.M.M.I.-07PB, she did not need it for: by then her Metroid DNA had awakened, and she drained the machine, and its Central Unit with it, through her bare hand.

Most E.M.M.I. she destroys yield one of the abilities Raven Beak stripped from her. The weapon shares its name with the Alimbic Omega Cannon she fired at Gorea in the Alimbic Cluster, but the two are unrelated designs. It is also the closest thing in her career to the Hyper Beam the hatchling gave her in Tourian: a borrowed power, briefly held, for an enemy she could not otherwise beat.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Cannon_(Metroid_Dread) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Central_Unit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/E.M.M.I. · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Upgrade · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread</sub>


### Equipment: Phazon Suit and PED Suit (Hypermode)

*Keys:* Phazon Suit, PED Suit, PED, Phazon Enhancement Device, Hypermode, Hyper Mode, Hyper-Mode, corrupted suit, black suit


Two of Samus's suits were born of Phazon, the mutagenic substance seeded from Phaaze.

Phazon Suit (Tallon IV). When the Omega Pirate collapsed onto Samus after their battle, the Phazon in its body seeped into her Power Suit and triggered a viral corruption that reshaped the armor, helmet and torso into a jet-black suit lined with red, the inverse of Phazon's blue-and-white glow. It made her immune to the blue Phazon of Tallon IV (more potent strains still burned), greatly strengthened her shielding, let her lift the Artifact of Newborn from its Phazon bed unharmed and, by drawing Phazon from the environment, let her fire the Phazon Beam that alone could destroy Metroid Prime in the Impact Crater. As Metroid Prime died it tore the Phazon Suit from her, leaving her in her Gravity Suit and free of corruption; the creature used the stolen suit and her DNA to remake itself as Dark Samus (Metroid Prime).

PED Suit (the Phazon crisis). The Phazon Enhancement Device was a Galactic Federation technology developed from Phazon ore recovered by the crew of the G.F.S. Anhur. When Dark Samus's Phazon strike on Norion left Samus comatose for a month, the Federation fitted her suit with a PED aboard the G.F.S. Olympus. Federation PED Marines drew Phazon from backpacks; Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda drew on the Phazon lodged in their own bodies. By injecting the energy of one Energy Tank she entered Hypermode: temporary invulnerability and vastly stronger beam, missiles, bombs and grapple, lasting until the Phazon was spent. The price was corruption. Every use deepened it, Phazon tendrils crept across her vision when she overused it, a Space Pirate Phazon Grenade could force her into runaway Hypermode, and with each Leviathan she destroyed the suit's colors darkened toward red and blue while her Morph Ball glowed Phazon-blue. On Phaaze the PED triggered an emergency vent that emptied her Energy Tanks to slow the corruption and left her locked in Hypermode, where total saturation meant death. Her three fellow hunters succumbed and were lost to Dark Samus. When Phaaze was destroyed the Phazon in her body dissipated with it and the corruption ended (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption).

Samus has worn neither suit since. See Phazon, Phazon corruption and Hypermode, Samus and Phazon, and Dark Samus.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/PED_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hypermode · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption</sub>


### Equipment: Power Suit (Samus's Chozo armor)

*Keys:* Power Suit, Power Suits, Legendary Power Suit, Fully Powered Suit, Ruins Test, Chozo armor, Chozo armour, her armor, her suit, Samus's suit, Samus' suit, battle suit


The Power Suit is the powered battle armor Samus Aran wears on every mission: a Chozo exoskeleton modified for a human body, with the Chozo scientist Dryn credited for much of its development. Per the manga, the Chozo of Zebes infused the orphaned Samus with their own DNA so she could endure the planet, then armored and trained her; Mother Brain told her during that training that the Power Suit reacts to her thoughts but cannot think for her, and Samus once failed a trial because her focus wavered. The suit is bonded to her body and answers to her will. She materializes it around herself and withdraws it into her body at will, leaving only the Zero Suit beneath (seen in the manga, on the Bottle Ship and after the Aether and Phazon crises), and when she is struck down and loses consciousness the armor dissolves and leaves her in the Zero Suit. Its components are partly organic: Federation surgeons later found them so integrated with her nervous system that the suit could not be fully removed while she lay unconscious (Metroid Fusion).

Design: modular. Built around the Arm Cannon and Morph Ball technology, it accepts weapons, visors, climate resistance, defensive and maneuvering additions and uploaded data, and adapts to each immediately. It provides life support, and its shielding draws on suit energy that every hit depletes (see Energy Tanks).

Lineage:
- The first suit. Samus's original armor, orange and without shoulder pauldrons, was lost when Space Pirates shot down her gunship as she left Tourian on her first Zebes mission; she was stripped of her Power Suit with only an emergency pistol.
- The Legendary Power Suit (also called the Fully Powered Suit). Inside the Chozo Ruins of Chozodia, beneath the Space Pirate Mother Ship, Samus passed the Ruins Test, a trial the Chozo left to judge whether she was worthy, and was granted this stronger armor: the pauldroned design she has worn ever since. It restored everything she had gathered and could integrate advanced Chozo technology her first suit could only register as unknown: the Gravity Suit, Space Jump and Plasma Beam.
- She wore this suit on Tallon IV, in the Alimbic Cluster, on Aether, through the Phazon crisis, on her return to Zebes and aboard the Bottle Ship, until an X parasite infected it on SR388. The infected portions were cut away and replaced by the organic Fusion Suit, which over the following years gradually returned toward its mechanical form (Metroid Dread).

Variants built onto it: Varia Suit, Gravity Suit, Phazon Suit, PED Suit, Dark Suit, Light Suit, Hazard Shield, Fusion Suit, Metroid Suit, and the Lamorn-made Vi-O-La Suit and Legacy Suit. Nearly every mission has begun with the suit stripped of its enhancements, whether by the Frigate Orpheon's malfunction, Dark Samus's Phazon strike on Norion, Raven Beak's assault or the artifact on Tanamaar, and Samus rebuilds its capabilities as she goes. Metroid remnants that clung to the suit after Zebes supplied the cellular material the Galactic Federation used to breed Metroids on the Bottle Ship.

Samus regards the armor as an extension of herself: it was given by the people who raised her, and losing it has left her hunted, unarmed and exposed more than once.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Power_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Legendary_Power_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Paralyzer · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zero_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M</sub>


### Equipment: Psychic abilities and the Vi-O-La (Viewros)

*Keys:* psychic abilities, psychic ability, psychic powers, psychic power, psychic crystal, Psychic Mote, Vi-O-La, ViOLa, Vi O La, motorcycle, motorbike, Sol Valley


On Viewros (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond) Samus gained two things no earlier mission had given her: psychic powers and a vehicle.

Psychic abilities. The official brief describes them as mysterious powers that Samus acquires while exploring the planet, to be wielded wisely to clear her path forward. Exploring the Chrono Tower she acquired a crystal with psychic capabilities, and with it she can manipulate objects at a distance: shifting the lock mechanism of a door, tracing outlines, guiding a beam she has already fired toward its target, operating specialized Lamorn mechanisms and perceiving hidden platforms. The powers are of a piece with the Lamorn themselves, the vanished people of Viewros, whose statues house Psychic Motes, whose structures run on psychic power, and whose prophecy named Samus the Chosen One. At the end of the mission she used the psychic crystal to accelerate the growth of the Lamorn memory fruit.

The Vi-O-La. In the official description, "A technologically advanced bike that Samus can ride. Use it to get around the environment or battle while out in the open." Samus acquired it on Viewros and uses it to cross Sol Valley, the open expanse that links the planet's regions and the only place where the green crystals her progress depends on can be gathered; its maneuvers give it bursts of speed and let her fight from the saddle. She rode it into battle against Sylux. Myles MacKenzie, the stranded Federation technician she rescued in the jungles of Fury Green, ran a base camp that supported her through the mission.

Her established systems travelled with her: the Morph Ball, the Scan Visor, Missiles, the Charge Beam and Bombs all served on Viewros.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Lamorn · https://www.nintendo.com/us/store/products/metroid-prime-4-beyond-switch/</sub>


### Equipment: Screw Attack

*Keys:* Screw Attack, Screw Attacks, screw attacking, screwattack, spinning attack, ball of deadly energy


The Screw Attack turns Samus's spin jump into a weapon. While she somersaults, her suit wraps her in a field of destructive energy (per the manual for the Aether mission she becomes "a mobile ball of deadly energy"; the ZDR data describes an electric coating during spin jumps), and most creatures she passes through are destroyed outright. Per the original manual it is a power item that "spins in flight to attack the enemy"; per the Super Metroid manual she can "somersault into enemies and blast them out of the air". Some things can only be destroyed with it. Very few creatures resist: Rippers, which shrug off nearly everything else, are shredded by it, while Omega Ridley simply leaps clear and takes nothing from a hit. Without the Gravity Suit the attack fails underwater, except in the Bottle Ship suit; on Aether the Luminoth Gravity Boost stood in for it below the surface. In the Aether and Phazon-crisis suits it also chains several extra spinning jumps after a Space Jump and lets her kick off Wall Jump Surfaces to climb great heights.

Samus has recovered the Screw Attack on nearly every mission, usually late. On both Zebes missions it waited in Norfair beyond Ridley's lair; on the BSL station she regained it by destroying Ridley-X in Sector 1 (SRX), her suit reporting "Screw Attack ability regained"; on Aether it rested in a Luminoth vault chamber; during the Phazon crisis it was on Bryyo and let her clear enormous gaps; on the Bottle Ship she switched it on herself, together with the Space Jump, while chasing the Deleter rather than wait for Adam Malkovich's authorization; on ZDR it lay in Artaria. It also served her on SR388 during the extermination mission.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Screw_Attack · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Jump · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Wall_Jump · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/ZDR · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Artaria</sub>


### Equipment: Speed Booster and Shinespark

*Keys:* Speed Booster, Speed Boost, speed boosting, Shinespark, Shine Spark, shinesparking, Shinesparks, supersonic, Speed Booster blocks, boost blocks


The Speed Booster lets Samus run at supersonic speed. After a short build-up at a full sprint her suit flares and she becomes a projectile: she smashes through Speed Booster blocks and barriers of rock, ice and metal, obliterates most creatures she touches, clears wide gaps and charges up steep inclines. Per the manual of her first Zebes mission she can "dash at supersonic speeds and crash through certain barriers and enemies"; the ZDR data calls it super-human speed.

The Shinespark is the Speed Booster's secondary technique. At full speed Samus crouches to store the charge, then releases it to launch herself at hyper speed in a straight line: left, right, straight up or diagonally (in the Bottle Ship suit, vertically or any direction). If she sparks into a sloped surface she drops straight back into a boosted run, which lets her reach places that look unreachable. With the Hi-Jump Boots on her first Zebes mission she could even spark in Morph Ball form. On the return to Zebes the spark bled energy from her suit for as long as it lasted, a cost that never recurred. During the BSL incident the station computer once remarked on her expert use of the Shinespark after she used it to bypass something she had been directed to deal with first.

On the first Zebes mission the Speed Booster sat in the hands of a Chozo Statue beyond Kraid's lair; on the return to Zebes it was in Norfair. On the BSL station she regained it by destroying the X that had mimicked Serris, the aquatic creature the computer described as moving at ultra-high speed ("Speed Booster was regained. Run until speed boost begins."). On the Bottle Ship Adam Malkovich authorized it in Sector 2, the Cryosphere, and that suit uniquely let her steer mid-boost, as through the spiral structure of the Biosphere. It was absent from her SR388 extermination mission. On ZDR she had to retrieve the Flash Shift from Burenia before she could reach the Central Unit in Dairon, take up the Omega Cannon and destroy E.M.M.I.-04SB, the yellow unit that carried both the Speed Booster and the Shinespark.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Speed_Booster · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Shinespark · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/E.M.M.I. · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/ZDR · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns</sub>


### Equipment: Thermal, X-Ray, Dark, Echo and Command Visors

*Keys:* Thermal Visor, X-Ray Visor, X-Ray Scope, Dark Visor, Echo Visor, Command Visor, thermal imaging, x-ray, xray, heat signature, heat signatures, sound waves


Beyond the Combat and Scan Visors, Samus has fitted five specialized visors, most of them taken from the worlds she fought on.

Thermal Visor (Tallon IV). Found in a Space Pirate research lab in Phendrana Drifts. It renders heat signatures: she can see in total darkness and foul weather, pick out Shriekbats hiding above her, counter the cloaking of Shadow Pirates, and spot Power Conduits that "radiate some heat that is invisible in the normal spectrum" so she can energize them with the Wave Beam. It carries no radar, and bright light, explosions and intense heat impair it; creatures no warmer than their surroundings are hard to find, Thardus's Phazon radiation defeated her auto-targeting outright, and in the superheated Magmoor Caverns it is nearly useless. The Pirates used the same thermal imaging to find the richest Phazon in ore and the weak points in Thardus's casing.

X-Ray Visor (Tallon IV, later the Pirate Homeworld). Taken from the Life Grove of the Tallon Overworld. It looks through walls and hollow structures, exposes hidden passages, doors and Chozo Artifacts, reveals invisible platforms such as those over the Phazon of Metroid Quarantine A, and tracks cloaked enemies: the Omega Pirate, the intermittently invisible Metroid Prime creature, and Chozo Ghosts, which "phase in and out of existence" so that x-ray scanning is the only reliable way to follow them. During the Phazon crisis she regained it on the Pirate Homeworld, where it also reads the hidden passwords of Pirate consoles and, paired with the Nova Beam, sees through Phazite to weak points that drop an enemy in one shot. Some machines jam it with static. Its ancestor is the X-Ray Scope she found in Brinstar on the return to Zebes, which shows the composition of objects and hidden passages but roots her in place while in use.

Dark Visor (Aether). Luminoth work, once worn by A-Kul, the Champion of Aether: "Our champion wore a visor that pierced the veil of Dark Aether's eternal night." Samus received it after destroying Chykka in the Dark Torvus Temple. It highlights creatures and objects in red and shows whatever is in transdimensional flux: hidden platforms, Dark Pirate Commandos, Ing in their amorphous state, Flying Ing Caches and Dark Samus during their clash in Sanctuary Fortress.

Echo Visor (Aether). Also the champion's: "The last of our champion's visors used sound to target the unseen." The Luminoth built this sonic imaging to let their blind see. Samus found it in the Aerie of Sanctuary Fortress after driving off Dark Samus. It renders sound waves, exposing Shriekers and other unseen enemies, disarming sonic security, opening echo gates with the Annihilator Beam, revealing the high-sonic link between Quadraxis's body and its head, and tracking Dark Samus when Phazon hid her from the Dark Visor.

Command Visor (Phazon crisis). A link to her Gunship rather than a sensor: through it she calls the ship down to land, orders Ship Missile strikes and bombing runs, and has it lift objects too heavy to move by hand with the Ship Grapple. It was offline during the attack on Norion until Rundas pulled her clear of Space Pirate ATCs, and she later used it to destroy the shield generators on Bryyo, assemble the Theronian Bomb on Elysia and reach the Leviathan on the Pirate Homeworld.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Thermal_Visor · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/X-Ray_Visor · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/X-Ray_Scope · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Visor · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Echo_Visor · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Command_Visor</sub>


### Equipment: Varia Suit

*Keys:* Varia, Varia Suit, Varia Suits, Varia Feature, Barrier Suit, heat protection, heat resistance, extreme heat, heat shielding


The Varia Suit is the Chozo enhancement that hardens Samus's Power Suit against hostile environments. Its name is a corruption of "barrier", and the manuals for her SR388 extermination mission called it the Barrier Suit (per the manual). It is the one suit present on every mission, usually the first armor enhancement she recovers, and most depictions of Samus show her wearing it.

Function: It reduces the damage she takes from attacks and lets her walk through superheated regions unharmed, such as Norfair on Zebes, the Magmoor Caverns of Tallon IV, Sector 3 (PYR) of the BSL station and Cataris on ZDR. It does not let her wade through lava or acid; immersion in the deepest magma of Norfair or the liquid of Tourian still burns, and that protection belongs to the Gravity Suit. On the Fusion Suit the Varia also shielded her from intense cold, which mattered because the Metroid vaccine had made her vulnerable to it; on ZDR, cold resistance came instead with the Gravity Suit.

Appearance: Her original armor turned pink with the Varia. Since the Legendary Power Suit the Varia has been an orange-and-yellow recoloring, and the broad rounded pauldrons people associate with it belong to the Legendary suit itself. On the Fusion Suit it showed as yellow-green over magenta.

History: Samus found it in Brinstar on her first Zebes mission; took it from Kraid's lair on her return to Zebes; lost it with the rest of her enhancements in the Frigate Orpheon's malfunction and regained it after destroying Flaahgra on Tallon IV; recovered its data on the BSL station by absorbing the Mega Core-X in Sector 2 that had consumed it; and took it from Cataris on ZDR. On the Bottle Ship it was already built in as the Varia Feature, but Samus refused to activate it until Adam Malkovich authorized it during the fight with Goyagma, after she had already crossed the Pyrosphere's heat (Other M). On Tanamaar she landed wearing it; early events on Viewros stripped it from her and she regained it only at the end (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). Combined with the Gravity Suit, the two enhancements cut the damage she takes to a fraction.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Varia_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Legendary_Power_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gravity_Suit · https://www.gamereactor.eu/metroid-prime-4-beyond-guide-to-all-of-samus-arans-upgrades-and-suits-1645383/ · https://www.nintendo.com/en-gb/News/2021/September/Metroid-Dread-Report-Vol-8-Surveying-Planet-ZDR-2043940.html</sub>


### Equipment: Vi-O-La Suit and Legacy Suit (Viewros)

*Keys:* Vi-O-La Suit, Vi-O-La IC Suit, IC Suit, Legacy Suit, Lamorn suit, Lamorn armor, Altar of Legacy


On Viewros Samus wore three suits fabricated from Lamorn technology, the only armor in her career of neither Chozo nor Galactic Federation make.

Loss and recovery. Samus landed on Tanamaar in her Varia Suit to defend the artifact unearthed at the Federation research facility there. When Sylux damaged the artifact, it activated and hurled everyone nearby to the unknown planet Viewros, and in the aftermath her suit's enhancements, the Varia among them, were stripped away; she rebuilt them across the planet and regained the Varia only at the very end.

Vi-O-La Suit. Once Samus restored the first tower at Volt Forge, a Lamorn fabrication platform printed a suit that let her interface with the Vi-O-La, the Lamorn neural motorcycle she rode across Sol Valley; the machine answers to its rider through the suit.

Vi-O-La IC Suit. A second suit printed on the same Volt Forge platform. It let Samus withstand extreme heat and switched the Vi-O-La into its IC mode over lava, which carried her across the lava sea of Flare Pool.

Legacy Suit. The last of the three and the finest armor she wore on Viewros: a Lamorn design altered from the IC Suit, conceived in their Machine Era and refined in their Psychic Era. She received it at the Altar of Legacy in Fury Green together with the Memory Fruit, after she had deposited enough of the Green Energy Crystals, the life energy the Lamorn had spread across the planet. Through the Psychic Visor the Legacy Suit converts a store of her Missiles into a protective barrier that shields her from hazards and destroys most lesser creatures on contact; the barrier lasts only briefly, burns out faster the more it absorbs, and collapses if she curls into the Morph Ball (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond).

Samus carried the Lamorn's legacy off their dying world and planted the Memory Fruit with the psychic crystal; whether she kept the suits afterward is not recorded. See The Lamorn, Viewros, Vi-O-La and Sol Valley, Psychic abilities and the Vi-O-La, and Sylux.


<sub>Sources: https://www.gamereactor.eu/metroid-prime-4-beyond-guide-to-all-of-samus-arans-upgrades-and-suits-1645383/ · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Vi-O-La_Suit</sub>


### Equipment: Wave Beam, Spazer and Plasma Beam

*Keys:* Wave Beam, Wave Beams, Spazer, Spazer Beam, Plasma Beam, Plasma Beams, Wave Trooper, Plasma Trooper, three-way beam, piercing beam, electrical beam


The Wave Beam is a Chozo weapon made of energy waves that pass through solid matter and strike whatever lies behind, with a wider area of effect than the Power Beam. The Chozo left it in Norfair on Zebes and in the ruins of SR388. On her first Zebes mission a Kiru Giru larva barred the way to it; its shielded underside could only be hit by the beam it guarded. On her return it lay deeper in Norfair past a span crossable only with the Grapple Beam, and paired with the Plasma Beam it split that beam into two shots. Aboard the Bottle Ship, Super Zebesians fitted with Federation-made Wave Beams trapped Samus in a glass cage in Sector 2 (Cryosphere) and fired through it until Adam Malkovich authorized her own; there it crossed glass but not the ship's thick walls. On ZDR, E.M.M.I.-06WB carried it in Ferenia. The Tallon IV version was different in kind: the only weapon of hers that fires electricity, as oscillating pulses that home on a locked target, paralyze on a charged hit, re-energize dead Power Conduits and open Purple Doors. It waited in the Chapel of the Elders in Phendrana Drifts, guarded by Sheegoths. The Space Pirates reverse-engineered it for their Wave Troopers, inferior copies vulnerable to the original, and the creature Metroid Prime absorbed the same power.

The Spazer Beam (originally the Spazer Laser Beam) splits each shot into three parallel beams. On SR388 the other two beams continued if one struck; on Zebes, where it waited in Brinstar, the whole shot stopped on contact. It could not be used together with the Plasma Beam, and with a Power Bomb it produced a Spazer Shield. In later missions its role passed to the Wide Beam, the Wave Beam and the Diffusion Beam.

The Plasma Beam is the strongest Chozo beam: a blast that pierces any number of targets, and on SR388 even solid walls. On her first Zebes mission she found it in Crateria but could not use it until she earned the Legendary Power Suit in Chozodia; on her return a Chozo Statue held it beyond Draygon in Maridia. On the BSL station the X plant Nettori, shaped like a Chozo Statue, fired it until she absorbed its Hard Core-X in Sector 2 (TRO); the SA-X fired Ice, Wave and Plasma together. On the Bottle Ship she first used it against the mature Ridley clone in the Pyrosphere, and on ZDR she recovered it on Elun. The Tallon IV version fires superheated plasma that melts ice and frigidite, ignites foes, reduces most enemies to ash on a charged hit and opens Red Doors; Pirate Plasma Troopers carried a weaker copy. During the Phazon crisis the hunter Ghor wielded a faster, longer-ranged Plasma Beam; Samus took it after defeating him in SkyTown's Main Docking Bay and used its heat to repair Aurora Unit 217 and other damaged circuitry, the beam doubling as an arc welder. Gandrayda, mimicking Ghor, and Dark Samus wielded it as well. Per the manga, she turned Ice, Wave and Plasma on Ridley together.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Wave_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Wave_Beam_(Metroid_Prime) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Spazer_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Plasma_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Plasma_Beam_(Metroid_Prime) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/ZDR</sub>


### Equipment: Wide, Diffusion and Hyper Beams

*Keys:* Wide Beam, Wide Beams, Diffusion Beam, Hyper Beam, Hyper Beams, rainbow beam, scatter shot, FG-1000


The Wide Beam fires three beams at once across a swath roughly Samus's own height, about three times the width of a charged Power Beam shot, making a target far easier to hit. On the BSL station a Human Core-X, an X that had mimicked a recently dead crewman, attacked her with it before dropping its disguise in Sector 3 (PYR); Adam Malkovich observed that the X absorb the memories and knowledge of their prey. On ZDR she found it in Dairon, where combined with the Charge Beam it shatters Wide Beam Blocks. It stacks with the Charge Beam and Plasma Beam.

The Diffusion Beam is a charge enhancement. A fully charged shot scatters at the point of impact into a burst that strikes every enemy close enough, and the effect carries into every beam she acquires afterward. She obtained it aboard the Bottle Ship, in the Main Sector, after destroying three machines armed with the FG-1000 beam; it was one of only two pieces of equipment there she took on her own rather than through Adam's authorization, the other being the Seeker Missile Launcher. On ZDR it waited in Cataris, where charged shots diffuse on contact and reach through thin walls to a degree.

The Hyper Beam was never Chozo work. In Tourian, as Mother Brain's own Hyper Beam was overwhelming Samus, the baby Metroid returned, drained Mother Brain to the brink, and poured that energy into Samus to heal her; a revived Mother Brain killed the hatchling with blasts from her mouth, and its remains fell onto Samus, who rose with the rainbow-colored Hyper Beam in her cannon. It destroyed any enemy in a single shot, broke through some walls, worked with every weapon except the Charge Beam, and was the only thing that could kill Mother Brain. Samus escaped Zebes before the planet detonated (Super Metroid); no later mission records the beam, which died with the planet and the child that gave it. The Phazon-fuelled Hyper Beam of her PED Suit during the Phazon crisis is a separate weapon.

Her feelings about the gift are on record. Reflecting after the BSL incident, she wrote that she owed the hatchling her life twice over: once for Tourian, and once for the Metroid vaccine made from its cells that cured her of the X. On ZDR that same Metroid DNA awakened fully: she drained energy by touch, brought the fortress Itorash crashing down onto the planet, and with that power destroyed the X that had seized Raven Beak's body.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Wide_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Diffusion_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hyper_Beam_(Mother_Brain) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Hatchling · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Upgrade · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Charge_Beam</sub>


### Equipment: Zero Suit and Paralyzer

*Keys:* Zero Suit, Zero Suit Samus, Paralyzer, Paralyzer pistol, emergency pistol, stun pistol, bodysuit, undersuit, under-suit, skin-tight, skintight, without her suit


The Zero Suit is the skin-tight bodysuit Samus wears beneath the Power Suit and every variant of it. It is cyan-blue with magenta Chozo sigils and glowing light-red markings along the back, built for maximum flexibility, and it is what remains when the armor withdraws into her body or fails. In it Samus has no shielding, no Morph Ball, no Arm Cannon and a far shorter jump; she must crawl or slide beneath obstacles and rely on stealth.

Zebes. After she escaped Tourian on her first mission, Space Pirates shot down her gunship over Chozodia. Stripped of her Power Suit, with only an emergency pistol, she infiltrated the Space Pirate Mother Ship on foot, evading and stunning Zebesians, until she passed the Chozo's Ruins Test and received the Legendary Power Suit (Metroid: Zero Mission).

Later appearances. She is seen in the Zero Suit after the Alimbic, Aether and Phazon missions and aboard the G.F.S. Olympus. On the Bottle Ship her Zero Suit was a more metallic blue with higher heels and a black holster on the left thigh; she boarded her gunship wearing it, and when she returned days later to recover Adam Malkovich's helmet she left the self-destructing ship in the Zero Suit, sliding under hatches without the Morph Ball (Other M). It is also what she is left in when a battle knocks her unconscious and the armor dissolves.

The Paralyzer. Samus's only secondary weapon, once called the emergency pistol, is a small sidearm she carries when she lacks the Power Suit. A fully charged shot stuns a target but cannot kill; an uncharged shot does nothing to a living foe. It can shatter certain blocks, yet it could not harm the Zebesians of the Mother Ship, where only her Bombs could. On Zebes it charged itself and she defeated the Ruins Test with it alone; on the Bottle Ship it needed no charge to stun, a charged shot held enemies longer, and it also opened hatches. Samus treats it as a last resort rather than a weapon of choice. See Samus Aran – appearance, physique and physiology, and Chozodia, the Chozo Ruins and the Space Pirate Mother Ship.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zero_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Paralyzer · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Legendary_Power_Suit</sub>


## Chozo


### Chozo: Chozo Ghosts

*Keys:* Chozo Ghost, Chozo Ghosts, the Turned, Turned Chozo, Chozo spirits, Chozo spirit, Chozo specter, Chozo specters, ghostly Chozo, Chozo haunting, Chozo phantom, corrupted Chozo


Chozo Ghosts, called the Turned in the Chozo Lore, are the spectral remnants of the Chozo of Tallon IV, corrupted by the Great Poison, Phazon. The Chozo's own record describes them as beings utterly corrupted by the Great Poison who still hold to their Chozo forms but whose minds are black with fell intentions: doomed souls in limbo who seek to disrupt the Artifacts that seal the Poison's source. A Holobanner states the cause plainly: The Great Poison corrupts all, even the Chozo. As the Chozo faded from the physical world after the meteor strike, those who did not pass on cleanly became the Turned, driven mad until they could no longer tell friend from foe.

They haunt places of spiritual significance, above all the Chozo Ruins: the Hall of the Elders, where Samus first met them, and the Sunchamber, where the Artifact of Wild lay among spirits drawn to a corrupted, beast-infested site. They phase in and out of the visible spectrum, strike quickly and vanish, and are aggressive and erratic, attacking anything that enters their sacred grounds, yet they can act on the physical world. Natural energies such as fire, ice and electricity pass through them; only the Power Beam and Super Missiles can harm them, and the X-Ray Visor is the one reliable way to track them. Space Pirates studying the Ruins took casualties from them and recorded with satisfaction that even ghosts can be destroyed.

Not every spirit was lost. When Meta Ridley shattered the twelve Chozo Totems of the Artifact Temple, twelve Chozo Ghosts appeared in their place, restored the totems in ethereal form and activated the transport into the Impact Crater, opening the way for Samus to destroy the creature Metroid Prime (Metroid Prime). The Chozo Lore had promised as much: the Chozo's will would persist until the Entrusted One arrived.

For Samus the Turned were a bitter fight. The Chozo who raised her were of Zebes, but the Ghosts wore the shape of her foster people and attacked her in the halls that held the Chozo's last words to her. She destroyed the corrupted and was aided by the sane, fulfilling the Totem's charge to free the spirits. See also Phazon, Chozo Artifacts, Artifact Temple, Meta Ridley, Chozo Lore, Tallon IV.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ghost · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Artifact_Temple · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Totem · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hall_of_the_Elders · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ruins</sub>


### Chozo: Chozo history, philosophy and decline

*Keys:* Chozo history, Chozo decline, Chozo philosophy, Chozo extinction, Chozo colonies, Chozo colony, ancient Chozo, declining species, where did the Chozo go, what happened to the Chozo, Chozo empire, Chozo teachings


Chozo history is a long arc from expansion to withdrawal. Roughly 1,500 years before Samus's time the Chozo began building SkyTown among the clouds of Elysia, hovering research pods from which they studied the cosmos. About fifty years in they created the Elysian mechanoids to handle construction and maintenance, and a Chozo researcher there detected a distant living planet, later known as Phaaze, before the data was lost. In the Elysian year 400 the Chozo left SkyTown, bound for Tallon IV, to share their knowledge with other races, asking the Elysians to keep searching for the sentient world.

On Zebes the Chozo built a civilization and the supercomputer Mother Brain, and there they later raised Samus Aran. On SR388 the Thoha and Mawkin tribes created and then struggled to contain the Metroids. On Tallon IV they made a deliberate break with their past: they chose to eschew a civilization of advanced technology, building halls of stone and woven branches around ancient trees and living guided by the providence of the universe. Their sages taught that "the truths of the universe hide within the tumbling currents of time's flow"; in time many Chozo developed visions of past and future, which they took as a sign of their burgeoning harmonization with the infinite. Those visions showed them a Worm that would devour the world from within, and a Defender who might stop it.

The Chozo preached restraint and understanding. Old Bird taught the young Samus that befriending and understanding others can solve problems (per the manga), and the Chozo warned the people of Bryyo against adopting technology too fast. Not every Chozo held to this: the Mawkin were a warrior tribe, and their leader Raven Beak lived by the creed "Hadar sen olmen", power is everything.

Their decline had several causes. Per the manga, the science that gave the Chozo unusual longevity compromised their ability to reproduce, and their numbers and strength dwindled; Old Bird told Samus plainly that the Chozo were a declining species. On Zebes, Mother Brain turned on its makers and the Space Pirates expelled the Chozo (per the manga). On Tallon IV a meteor, a Leviathan, brought the Great Poison, Phazon, about fifty years before Samus arrived; the Chozo could not cure it, sealed its source beneath the Cradle behind a lock of twelve Artifacts, and then faded: their minds weakened as they drifted through time and space, many passed on, and those who remained became the Turned. Their last record speaks of leaving the planet pristine and withdrawing beyond the illusion of the physical world. On SR388 Raven Beak's massacre of the Thoha, and on ZDR the X Parasites, ended the last known living Chozo.

Today the Chozo survive only in their ruins, statues and records, in their creations (the Elysians, the Metroids' legacy, Samus's Power Suit), and in Samus herself, who carries Chozo DNA and was raised in their ways. See also Thoha, Mawkin, Chozo Lore, Chozo Ghosts, Elysians, Mother Brain.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ruins · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain</sub>


### Chozo: Chozo language and the Chozo Lore of Tallon IV

*Keys:* Chozo language, Chozo script, Chozo runes, Chozo writing, Chozo Lore, Chozo prophecy, Holobanner, Holobanners, Chozo Totems, Hadar sen olmen, Entrusted One, Chozo records


The Chozo language is a runic script of great complexity inscribed on the walls, gates, totems and statues of Chozo settlements. Samus's Translator Module can render it, though at times with difficulty; Space Pirate linguists fared worse and complained they could not find the Artifacts of Tallon IV until they could decipher the messages carved into the statues of the Artifact Temple. On Tallon IV the Chozo used their writing as locks: paired Runic Symbols on gates must be read and activated to open the way, and the twelve Chozo Totems of the Artifact Temple carry rune clues to the hidden Artifacts. Holobanners, flat orange holograms that vanish when viewed edge-on, bear short messages: The Hall of the Elders; That which fouls the waters seeks the Sun; Only the holder of twelve shall face the Worm; The Great Poison corrupts all, even the Chozo. The spoken tongue is heard on ZDR, where Raven Beak and Quiet Robe speak it; Raven Beak's creed is "Hadar sen olmen", power is everything (Metroid Dread). Samus, raised among Chozo, follows it.

The Chozo Lore is the body of inscribed records the Chozo of Tallon IV left in their own voice, which Samus read during the Phazon incident there (Metroid Prime). It tells of their choice to live simply and in harmony with nature; of visions of past and future they called harmonization with the infinite; of the prophecy of the Worm, a creature born of parasites and nurtured in a poisoned womb that would devour the world, and of a Defender who would face it; of the meteor that brought the Great Poison, Phazon; of the Cradle and the twelve-Artifact Cipher built to contain it; of the Space Pirate infestation; of the Turned; and of the Chozo's own fading and exodus from the physical world.

Its most personal entries concern Samus, whom the Chozo remembered as "the child we found so long ago" and watched across the void. They call her the Hatchling, the Newborn, the Shining One and the Entrusted One: "a fledgling orphaned on a savaged planet", a lone figure "dressed for war" who "hunts the corrupted" and "bears our legacy" in Chozo armor and weapons. They wondered whether she still lived, whether her parents had died to the same creatures, and whether she dreamed of the Chozo, and they left weapons, armor and messages so that she could gather the Artifacts and reverse the evil, something only the Entrusted One could do. Reading these words on a poisoned world, Samus learned that the Chozo's hopes for her reached far beyond Zebes. See also Chozo Artifacts, Artifact Temple, Chozo Ghosts, Tallon IV, Phazon.
