High Command authorizes Phazon programs, approves troop and supply reinforcements, sets mission timetables and issues decrees; its tone in the logs is impatient and absolute ("Failure to comply with Command decree is not an option"). No individual member of High Command is ever named. On Zebes the Pirates answered to Mother Brain; Ridley has led Pirate forces in the field and, rebuilt as Meta Ridley, served as a mainstay of Pirate security; and by the Phazon crisis the whole organization had been subsumed by Dark Samus, whose "disciples" executed doubters in her name.

Beneath Command is the Science Team, the research and development branch, with named sub-units such as Research Team Sclera, which found the Vertigo Phazon strain that made Project Helix work. Science Team studied Phazon, Metroids, Chozo relics and Samus's own weapons, usually under impossible deadlines and with heavy losses of staff and test subjects. Its reports mix clinical detail with complaints; one Metroid handler, having lost two assistants to the creatures, wrote that the researchers had "vapor for brains."

The military is a caste system, recorded most clearly on the Pirate Homeworld (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption):
- Pirate Militia, the lowest tier, recruited from slaves and criminals and forced to fight; disobedient Militia are rumored to become dinner for regular troopers.
- Pirate Troopers, the common infantry.
- Commando Pirates, elite troops issued the best available gear.
- Pirate Commanders, "a rare breed" who rose to the ruling caste after at least ten years as a Commando.
Proven Militia could be promoted into the regular army; disloyalty at any rank meant death, and under Gandrayda's command officers walked among the troops in disguise to find and execute traitors.

Pirate culture, as the Pirate Data shows it, is obedient, cruel and darkly comic: troopers trained "to follow orders without question" (which made them ideal hosts for the Ing on Aether); Phazon workers driven to hallucinations and violence by "Phazon madness"; pets such as Gronkats and Olbaps banned when domestic beasts proved susceptible to that madness; Metroid handlers on Aether cited for target practice and unauthorized feeding; and a whole shift of deranged miners reassigned as training adversaries for the Elite Pirates. Samus has recovered these records in the field with her Scan Visor; they are the main window anyone outside the Pirates has into how they think.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/High_Command · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Science_Team · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Militia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Commander · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Commando_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Project_Helix</sub>


### Space Pirates: Pirate Homeworld

*Keys:* Pirate Homeworld, Pirate Home World, Pirate home planet, Pirate planet, Urtraghus, SN-883, Command Courtyard, Pirate Research, Pirate Mines, Pirate Command, acid rain, Hazard Shield


The Pirate Homeworld is the fortified planet from which the Space Pirates waged the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). The name is a designation rather than a confirmed origin: nothing establishes it as the species' planet of birth. Unused Federation text, not part of the canon record, labelled it SN-883 and gave the Pirate name Urtraghus, describing an inconspicuous world far from Federation space. It is wreathed in acid rain from a low-pH atmosphere corrosive enough to eat through Samus's armor; only the Hazard Shield let her walk its open courtyards.

After Dark Samus took the Pirates and learned, through the captured Aurora Unit 313, to launch Phazon Seeds, the Homeworld was the first world she seeded: a Leviathan struck it, Phazon spread through its crust, and the Pirates celebrated the "Purification," expecting "thousands of new disciples" as their planet became a second Phaaze. The Homeworld became the launch point of the Vanguard offensive against Elysia, Bryyo and Norion, defended by a planetary shield, Commando Pirates, Pirate Commanders of the ruling caste, Militia drawn from its slave population, and an extensive transit system of rail lines linking sectors such as Pirate Research, Pirate Mines and Pirate Command, whose hub is the Command Courtyard.

Samus came to the Homeworld after destroying the Leviathans of Bryyo and Elysia. Gandrayda, the last of Dark Samus's field commanders, had been testing the garrison's loyalty by walking among them in disguise and executing doubters, and had sworn to bring Samus's "bloody helmet" to her mistress; she posed as a Federation Marine to lure Samus into a trap and died fighting her on a tower in the acid rain. Samus then disabled the planetary shield, allowing Admiral Dane's fleet to land and Demolition Troopers to blast a path to the Leviathan where no train ran; the Pirate Commander who ambushed them wounded Samus badly before she killed him. Inside the Seed, Omega Ridley guarded the core. Samus destroyed both, and the Federation took control of the Homeworld's Leviathan to open a wormhole to Phaaze. The Pirate Data scattered across the Homeworld, from "Our Mission" to "Mistress Gandrayda," records the whole fall of the Pirates from Phazon hunters to Dark Samus's cult, including their last boast: "If Samus comes to our Homeworld, she will find us ready."


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Homeworld · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Commander · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Commando_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Militia</sub>


### Space Pirates: Pirate military of the Phazon crisis (Militia, Commandos, Commanders)

*Keys:* Aerotrooper, Aerotroopers, Commando Pirate, Commando Pirates, Pirate Militia, Pirate Commander, Pirate Commanders, Phazite, Armored Pirate Trooper, Assault Pirate Trooper, Advanced Pirate Trooper, Shield Pirate Trooper


After Dark Samus seized the Space Pirate Organization and seeded the Pirate Homeworld with Phazon, the Pirate military was rebuilt around Phazon itself (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Most regular troops were fitted with Phazon Enhancement Devices and could enter Hypermode, becoming faster and tougher, though Phazon-based weapons still harmed them. The standard kit became an Assault Rifle and Energy Scythe, "both powered by Phazon," plus EMP Grenades, a Dash Jet and a short-lived hover system. Armor was graded: Armored Pirate Troopers resisted beams but not Missiles; Advanced Pirate Troopers wore a thick layer of Phazite; Assault Pirate Troopers wore missile-resistant armor and favored melee; Shield Pirate Troopers carried energy shields that Samus's Grapple Lasso could tear away. Most troopers were stationed on the Homeworld, with detachments sent to Bryyo, Elysia and the assault on Norion.

Phazite, a Phazon-derived armor material, defined the upper castes. Commando Pirates, found only on the Homeworld, wore Phazite that "only Beams of the highest temperature can penetrate" and carried a combat cloaking field, boost thrusters and a personal teleporter; Samus's X-Ray Visor and Nova Beam could find and pierce the head beneath the helmet. Pirate Commanders, the ruling caste, each a Commando for at least ten years, wore red Phazite, more resilient still, and fought by teleporting around the field while calling Commandos to their side. One Commander ambushed Samus and Admiral Dane's Demolition Troopers in the Command Courtyard, gravely wounded her, and died at her hands.

Aerotroopers, successors to the Flying Pirates, had served on Aether with bulky jetpacks, cannons and homing missiles; in the Phazon crisis they flew lighter packs and fought with twin Remote Attack Pods firing Particle Cannons and Helix Missiles, plus sticky Gel Bombs. Their code of honor amounts to death before dishonor, and a dying Aerotrooper makes a spinning suicide attack. Armored, Advanced and Assault orders existed for them too, the last reserved for elite-ranked flyers. Gandrayda, serving Dark Samus, took Aerotrooper form against Samus.

At the bottom stood the Pirate Militia, expendable infantry recruited from the Homeworld's slaves and criminals, denied Phazon Enhancement Devices, armed with weaker arm cannons and retractable scythes, and "surprisingly obedient to the upper castes"; Shield and Armored Militia variants existed, and rumor held that disobedient Militia became dinner for regular troopers. They died in droves during the raid on the G.F.S. Olympus and the invasion of Norion, where Samus first met them. Pirate ground forces rode into battle aboard Armored Tactical Carriers (Pirate ATCs) and explosive-nosed Boarding Pods that their own crews called Space Coffins.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Trooper · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Commando_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Commander · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Militia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aerotrooper · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_ATC</sub>


### Space Pirates: Projects and Phazon research on Tallon IV and Aether

*Keys:* Phazon Program, Project Titan, Phazon infusion, Phazon madness, Phazon Fever, Glacier One, Phazon Mines, Research Lab Hydra, Research Lab Aether, Pirate science, Pirate experiments, Pirate base


The Space Pirates came to Tallon IV because scans of the Spiral Sector detected an energy spike on a "Wanderer-class planet" there; samples proved to be an unstable, immensely potent radioactive mutagen, Phazon, and Science Team judged the world "a viable secondary headquarters" after the fall of Zebes. The Phazon Program that followed was the Pirates' bid for dominance: mine Phazon near the Impact Crater, refine it, and, as one Observatory report boasted, "rise dominant of this sector within a deca-cycle." Daily yields climbed 44%, but the ore proved less stable than predicted, and Phazon madness, from loss of balance to hallucinations and violence, forced stricter life-support rules. On Aether the same affliction was logged as Phazon Fever, and one stricken technician held his comrades at gunpoint over a Phazon deposit.

Facilities (Metroid Prime):
- Glacier One in Phendrana Drifts, the research headquarters, with Research Lab Hydra, Research Lab Aether, the Research Core and an Observatory; sub-zero cold kept the Metroids sluggish and controllable, though ice jammed doors daily and predators killed staff.
- A geothermal plant in Magmoor Caverns, and attempted terraforming of the Chozo Ruins with a combat base and starport planned but never built.
- The Phazon Mines, the headquarters proper, reached only through the crashed Orpheon, with Elite Research, Omega Research, Metroid Quarantine, the Central Dynamo and the Phazon Processing Center.

Projects recorded in the Pirate Data:
- Metroid Studies and Metroid Forces: the Metroids rescued from Zebes were infused with Phazon and dissected, producing Fission and Hunter Metroid strains; Science Team never identified the energy Metroids drain, so it proposed taming them as weapons, since "a small force of disciplined Metroids could wipe out entire armies."
- Parasite infusion: Phazon made Tallon IV's Parasites larger and more aggressive but killed them all by the fourth infusion period; mutated Parasite Queens aboard the Orpheon were the best result.
- Project Titan: an attempt to make Phazon ore sentient produced the rock creature Thardus, which turned violent; the project was suspended indefinitely.
- Project Helix: Phazon spliced into Pirate DNA, yielding the Elite Pirates, the Phazon Elite and the Omega Pirate.
- Hunter Weapons: reverse-engineering Samus's arsenal from combat data; beam prototypes armed the Beam Troopers, but Morph Ball prototypes horribly broke four test subjects and were abandoned.
- Chozo Studies and the Artifacts: the Pirates set out to strip the Chozo Ruins, fuse Chozo Artifacts with Phazon and breach the Impact Crater's containment field while Chozo Ghosts killed their personnel; "the dead should serve the living, not hinder them."
- The creature Metroid Prime: found in a cavern of the Mines and able to absorb unlimited Phazon, it grew armor and defense screens in containment, then broke out and fused weapons from fallen guards into its body.

On Aether the Pirates found Phazon in the Dasha System, opened the Rift Portal with the Dark Beam and raided Dark Aether for ore while breeding Metroids as a power source, until the Ing, the Federation and Samus ended the operation (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes). Samus destroyed every one of these programs in person, and the Phazon the Pirates coveted is the same substance that later nearly consumed her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Science_Team · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Glacier_One · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Mines · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Project_Helix · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirates</sub>


### Space Pirates: Ships and the Frigate Orpheon

*Keys:* Orpheon, Frigate Orpheon, Siriacus, Vortex Outpost, Pirate frigate, Pirate frigates, Boarding Pod, Pirate Mother Ship, Doomseye, Pirate ship, Pirate ships, Pirate fleet


Space Pirate vessels are typically altered or stolen technology, but the Pirates maintain a true fleet. At the fall of Zebes three research frigates, Orpheon, Siriacus and Vol Paragom, "were in orbit at zero hour and managed to retreat," carrying the Pirates' surviving Metroids; Orpheon's cargo survived in full and was kept on restricted feeding while the ship docked at Vortex Outpost, a Pirate station of unrecorded location. Orpheon then took orbit over Tallon IV to collect native life for Phazon mutagen experiments: Parasites, Shriekbats, Plazmites, War Wasps and Zoomers. Its mutated Parasite Queens were its greatest success and its doom; two broke containment and killed most of the crew, and the survivors killed one, lit a distress beacon and abandoned ship. Samus answered the beacon, destroyed the remaining Queen in the reactor core, triggered the frigate's self-destruct, and saw Meta Ridley flee the dying ship toward the planet. The wreck crashed into Tallon IV, where its flooded hull, prowled by Aqua Pirates apparently searching for survivors, became the only road into the Phazon Mines (Metroid Prime).

Other Pirate craft Samus has faced:
- The Space Pirate Mother Ship that landed in Chozodia on Zebes: Ridley's vessel, whose forces shot down Samus's gunship; she infiltrated it with only her Paralyzer, destroyed Mecha Ridley, and escaped in a stolen scout ship as it self-destructed (Metroid: Zero Mission).
- The battleship Colossus, "used for battle and storage purposes," whose crew gathered the Phazon left on Aether, unknowingly revived Dark Samus and became her first disciples; with Phazon weapons it crippled the G.F.S. Valhalla and stole Aurora Unit 313, and the Valhalla's crew were thrown into space.
- The Leviathan dreadnought: a captured star-beast from Phaaze fitted with cybernetic weapons, its crew quarters and command consoles built inside its skull; the Colossus itself is rumored to have been scrapped for its parts.
- Space Pirate Boarding Pods, nicknamed Space Coffins: crude craft with an explosive nose cone that rams enemy hulls, as likely to kill their own crews as to breach the target, used in the raid on the G.F.S. Olympus.
- Pirate Armored Tactical Carriers (ATCs), heavily armored troop craft with Tribeam cannons and a single vulnerable heat vent, deployed over Norion and SkyTown.
- The Doomseye, a colossal battleship built by Pirates regrouping in the Bermuda System and hidden by cloaking generators on three planets; Samus was captured, brainwashed and enlarged aboard it until the Federation Force freed her and the Federation fleet destroyed it.
Pirate starships also fought the Federation fleet above Phaaze at the end of the Phazon crisis.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Frigate_Orpheon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Vortex_Outpost · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Mother_Ship · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Colossus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Boarding_Pod</sub>


### Space Pirates: Space Pirates (overview)

*Keys:* Space Pirates, Space Pirate, Space-Pirates, Space-Pirate, the Pirates, Pirate forces, Pirate Organization, Space Pirate Organization, Pirate raid, Pirate raids, scourge of the galaxy


The Space Pirates are the chief enemies of the Galactic Federation and the recurring adversaries of Samus Aran. They are described as a species of warring bipedal organisms, but the name covers an organization as much as a race: the crab-clawed Zebesians who conquered Zebes, the insectoid Kihunters who came from another galactic system to help rebuild it, and the reptilian-insectoid troopers of the Phazon era are all called Space Pirates, and the Pirates have followed leaders of other species entirely, including Mother Brain, Ridley and Kraid. Their true planet of origin is unknown; even the world called the Pirate Homeworld is not confirmed to be where they began.

Their trade is piracy and conquest. Per the Super Metroid manual they are "the scourge of the Galaxy," seizing trading ships and stranding crews in space. Per the manga, a Pirate flagship under Ridley raided the colony K-2L and killed Samus's parents. Their defining crime was the theft of the Metroid specimen from a Federation research vessel returning from SR388; they carried it to their fortress on Zebes, where the Federation feared they would multiply the creatures with beta rays. The Federation's assault on Zebes was repelled, and Samus was hired to go in alone.

Their history in brief:
- Zebes: Samus destroyed Mother Brain, Kraid and Ridley and escaped the Space Pirate Mother Ship (Metroid: Zero Mission). Three research frigates, Orpheon among them, fled with Metroid cargo.
- Tallon IV: the survivors mined Phazon, ran Project Helix and rebuilt Ridley as Meta Ridley until Samus wiped out the operation (Metroid Prime).
- Aether: a hidden base and Phazon raids into Dark Aether ended with the Pirates overrun by the Ing and driven off.
- The Phazon crisis: the crew of the battleship Colossus revived Dark Samus, who killed a third of them and made the rest her disciples; the Pirates seized Aurora Unit 313, attacked Norion, Bryyo and Elysia, and were crushed when the Federation stormed the Pirate Homeworld and Phaaze was destroyed.
- Afterward: survivors regrouping in the Bermuda System built the battleship Doomseye, destroyed by the Federation Force; the rogue hunter Sylux led an army of Space Pirates, controlled through Metroids, against Federation research stations such as Tanamaar.
- Ridley's Zebesians later seized the baby Metroid from Ceres and fled to a rebuilt Zebes, which Samus destroyed with everything on it; afterward only copies remained: cyborg Zebesian clones made by the Federation on the Bottle Ship, and X Parasite mimics on the BSL station.

Pirate culture, known mostly from their own Pirate Data logs, is disciplined, obedient and bureaucratic. Troopers are "ruthless and amoral," trained to follow orders without question, and disloyal Militia are rumored to end up as dinner for regular troopers. The Pirates call Samus "the Hunter" and have kept a bounty on her, dead or alive, since the fall of Zebes.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirates · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebesian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kihunter · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(game) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data</sub>


### Space Pirates: The Pirates and Samus (the Hunter)

*Keys:* vendetta, grudge, revenge, bounty, hatred, Dark Hunter, Pirate bounty, all-points alert, hunting the Hunter, Pirate enemy  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Pirate, Pirates, Space Pirate, Space Pirates, Zebesian, Zebesians, K-2L, Ridley


To the Space Pirates, Samus Aran is "the Hunter," and no enemy has cost them more. Per the manga, the enmity began at K-2L, where a Pirate flagship under Ridley raided the colony, killed Virginia Aran as she shielded her daughter, and was destroyed when Rodney Aran ignited the Afloraltite the Pirates had come to steal; Samus was the only survivor. Per the manga, years later on Jigrad she nearly executed a surrendering Pirate in her rage ("Did you say...'Mercy'!?") before arresting him instead, and that pattern, open hostility without needless bloodshed, has marked her dealings with them since.

The Pirates' own records treat her as a problem to be solved. After the fall of Zebes, Security Command issued an all-points alert describing "the Hunter clad in metal," a heavily armed female in a powered armorsuit, to be killed on sight with her suit and weapons salvaged if possible: "A considerable bounty will go to the unit who delivers Aran to Command. Dead or alive, it matters not." Science Team tried to reverse-engineer her arsenal into Beam Troopers and tested Elite Pirates against simulations of her, reporting a 74% success ratio that High Command found inadequate. On Aether a confused garrison watched a hunter in dark armor steal their Phazon and kill only those who resisted, nicknamed her the "Dark Hunter," and, when a second armored hunter arrived and the two fought, proposed an alliance with one against the other, Phazon in exchange for the newcomer's head; they never grasped that the Dark Hunter was Dark Samus, a being of living Phazon, and not Samus at all. The Pirate Commandos of Aether were raised for one purpose, "hunting the Hunter."

During the Phazon crisis the Pirates blamed Samus for two failed campaigns and swore to "take that Phazon or die trying." Their logs record Bryyo's fall ("The Hunter's might is great, and she is as efficient as ever when it comes to disrupting our operations"), Elysia's ("The accursed Elysian machines were no match for Aran's guns"), and Gandrayda's vow to lay Samus's bloody helmet before Dark Samus. They noted that Samus and her fellow hunters each bore Dark Samus's "mark of corruption," and the Pirate Commander of the Homeworld came closer than any Pirate to killing her.

Samus's side of the ledger: she destroyed Mother Brain, Kraid and Ridley on Zebes; dismantled Pirate operations on Tallon IV, Aether and the Pirate Homeworld; was captured, brainwashed and enlarged aboard the Doomseye until the Federation Force freed her; faced Sylux's Metroid-driven Pirates at Tanamaar; and pursued Ridley's Zebesians to a rebuilt Zebes to recover the stolen hatchling, resolving, per the Super Metroid manual, "to finish them off," a mission that ended with the planet's destruction.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Logs · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Commando · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Project_Helix</sub>


### Space Pirates: Troop types of Tallon IV and Aether

*Keys:* Pirate Trooper, Pirate Troopers, Flying Pirate, Flying Pirates, Shadow Pirate, Shadow Pirates, Beam Trooper, Beam Troopers, Pirate Commando, Pirate Commandos, Pirate Grenadier, Dark Pirate Trooper


The Space Pirates Samus fought on Tallon IV and Aether were organized into specialized troop types, most of them recorded in her Scan Visor logbook.

- Pirate Troopers (logged simply as Space Pirates on Tallon IV) are the common infantry: a "sentient aggressor species well trained in weapon and melee combat," ruthless and amoral. On Tallon IV they carried Galvanic Accelerator Cannons and forearm-mounted Scythes; on Aether a Photonic Power Scythe and Quantum Assault Cannon became "standard-issue weapons for all troopers." They garrisoned Glacier One in Phendrana Drifts, the Phazon Mines and the Agon Wastes base.
- Beam Troopers (Power, Wave, Ice and Plasma Troopers) were Troopers armed and armored with copies of Samus's beam weapons, reverse-engineered from combat data because "Command would dearly enjoy turning Aran's weapons against her." The copies were weaker than the Chozo originals, and each Trooper's armor could be breached only by the beam type it carried. They were found only in the Phazon Mines and were the reinforcements the Omega Pirate summoned.
- Flying Pirates were Troopers fitted with jet packs for airborne assault, firing beams and potent missiles; the packs showed on thermal imaging, and a Pirate whose pack failed hurled itself at its attacker in a suicide strike. Aqua Pirates were the underwater counterpart, found prowling the flooded Orpheon wreck.
- Shadow Pirates were stealth troops whose arm-worn cloaking generators drew so much power that they fought only with scythes, ambushing from ducts and ceilings; thermal or X-ray imaging or a Wave Beam shot exposed them.
- Pirate Aerotroopers, the Flying Pirates' successors on Aether, were "known for their cold precision and utter cruelty," carried modified cannons and homing missiles, and chose a suicide strike over capture.
- Pirate Commandos were the elite of Aether, "dedicated to 'hunting the Hunter'," with energy scythes, pulse cannons, EMP grenade launchers, thrusters and a short-lived portable barrier shield.
- Pirate Grenadiers carried an experimental energy grenade launcher too bulky to pair with rifles or scythes, and rammed anything that closed the distance.

On Aether the Ing found Pirate Troopers, "already trained to follow orders without question," perfect hosts. Dark Pirate Troopers and Dark Pirate Commandos (the latter possessed only by Hunter Ing, who competed fiercely for the right and lent them the power to phase out of local timespace) fought Samus across the Agon Wastes, Dark Agon and the Temple Grounds; only the Aerotroopers and Grenadiers escaped possession. In the Sanctuary Fortress Samus watched Dark Samus slaughter a group of Dark Pirate Troopers. (Metroid Prime; Metroid Prime 2: Echoes)


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Trooper · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Trooper_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Flying_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Shadow_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aerotrooper · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Commando</sub>


### Space Pirates: Zebesians and Kihunters

*Keys:* Zebesian, Zebesians, Kihunter, Kihunters, Keyhunter, Keyhunters, King Kihunter, Cyborg Zebesian, Cyborg Zebesians, Zebesian clone, Zebesian clones, Super Zebesian


Zebesians are the Space Pirates who conquered Zebes and took the planet's name as their own; they are not native to it, and their true origin and home planet are unknown. Their bodies combine insect and bird-like traits, and their signature feature is a pair of crab-like claws that open to fire one or two lasers. They cling to walls and ceilings and are acrobatic enough to rival Samus. Per the Super Metroid manual the Space Pirates are "the scourge of the Galaxy," seizing trading ships and stranding their crews in space; per the Metroid Fusion manual they once tried to use Metroids for their plans and "cling to walls and attack." Zebesians were the Pirate rank and file on Zebes during Samus's first mission there (Metroid: Zero Mission) and again when she returned to the rebuilt fortress after Ridley's Zebesians seized the baby Metroid from Ceres.

Zebesians come in color-coded grades of increasing toughness. Green ones are weak; red ones shrug off everything but Missiles and the Spazer; pink ones yield only to the Plasma Beam or Screw Attack; yellow ones need the Screw Attack or a fully charged shot; silver and gold Zebesians, very rare, are vulnerable only on their backs; black Zebesians are fast and resilient and can be hurt only for a moment after their jumping kick.

Kihunters (Keyhunters in the Super Metroid manual) are "insect-like Space Pirates from another galaxy" who helped rebuild Zebes after Samus devastated it. Their society resembles a bee colony ruled by a King Kihunter; the larval form is the Zoro, and the winged adult dives and slashes, then spits acid once its wings are destroyed. Green Kihunters are common on Zebes, stronger yellow ones haunted the Wrecked Ship, and red ones in deep Norfair were among the planet's deadliest creatures.

Both species outlived Zebes only as copies. On the Bottle Ship the Galactic Federation secretly cloned Zebesians and rebuilt them as Cyborg Zebesians, hoping for a special-forces unit of bioweapons; the clones escaped and fought Samus, who reported to Adam Malkovich that she was seeing a Zebesian that had been cybernetically enhanced (Metroid: Other M). Super Zebesians there carried a Wave Beam that passed through glass, and Kihunters and Zoros were bred in the Biosphere, some mechanically enhanced, under a King Kihunter whose hive Samus destroyed. On the BSL station the X Parasites produced purple Zebesian mimics, a swimming Aqua Zebesian form, and Kihunter mimics hatched from Zoro pupae. Samus has fought every one of these forms.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebesian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kihunter · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Trooper</sub>


## Ridley


### Ridley: Mecha Ridley

*Keys:* Mecha Ridley, Mecha-Ridley, Ridley Robot, robot Ridley, robotic Ridley, mechanical Ridley, Ridley's robot, Ridley mech


Mecha Ridley, also called the Ridley Robot, is a war machine built in Ridley's image inside the Space Pirate Mother Ship on Zebes. Per Yoshio Sakamoto's account, Ridley had the likeness made as a display of his own power, turning his image into a weapon. It was unfinished when Samus Aran found it, at roughly the halfway point of construction: its walking and flying units had not yet been installed, and had they been, it would have grown far more massive.

Samus reached it at the end of her first Zebes mission (Metroid Zero Mission). After she destroyed Mother Brain, Space Pirate fighters shot down her Gunship as she left orbit; she crash-landed near the Mother Ship with her Power Suit wrecked, slipped inside in her Zero Suit, recovered a full Chozo Power Suit in Chozodia, and fought her way through the ship to the chamber where the robot waited. Mecha Ridley attacked with a spray of rockets, energy beams from its eyes, alternating diagonal fireballs and sweeping claw blows. Its only weak point was a glowing core beneath a thin casing on its chest, which it shielded with its leading claw and neck; only Missiles and Super Missiles could crack it, in the instant the robot raised its neck to slam its claw down.

When the machine failed, its eyes glowed red and the Mother Ship's self-destruct began. Samus fled through the collapsing vessel and escaped in a Space Pirate escape pod moments before the ship was destroyed, ending Pirate rule over Zebes. The living Ridley, already killed by Samus in Norfair during the same mission, would later be rebuilt by the Pirates as the cybernetic Meta Ridley.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mecha_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Zero_Mission · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley</sub>


### Ridley: Meta Ridley and Omega Ridley

*Keys:* Meta Ridley, Meta-Ridley, Omega Ridley, Omega-Ridley, cybernetic Ridley, cyborg Ridley, mechanized Ridley, Phazite armor, Ridley at Norion, Ridley on Tallon IV, Phazon Ridley


Meta Ridley is the cybernetic rebuild of Ridley that the Space Pirates created after Samus destroyed him on Zebes. Per Pirate Data recovered on Tallon IV, the reconstruction of geoform 187, code-named Ridley, was completed aboard the research frigate Orpheon: Command ordered metagenetic improvements to his strength, mobility and offense, the metamorphosis was painful but successful, and cybernetic modules and armor plating were added. He was fitted with a Multi-Missile System, a Kinetic Breath Weapon, an Ultrathermal Flamestrike Projector and a Meson Bomb Launcher, and Science Team expected him to become the mainstay of the Pirate security force. His metal-plated body left only his chest truly vulnerable.

When the Orpheon's mutated Parasite Queens broke loose, Samus boarded the frigate and briefly saw Meta Ridley before he escaped the dying ship for Tallon IV (Metroid Prime). She glimpsed his shadow over Phendrana Drifts, and after she gathered the twelve Chozo Artifacts he attacked her at the Artifact Temple, blasting the Chozo totems from the air. Once his wings were ruined he fought her on the ground until the temple's statues fired their lasers into his chest and he fell into the Impact Crater.

The Pirates recovered and revived him once more, now sheathed in Phazite armor, and he spearheaded their assault on the Galactic Federation base at Norion (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Samus fought him through the generator complex, and at Generator C he smashed them both through the floor; they fell down a long shaft trading blows, she beat him moments before impact, and Rundas saved her from the fall. His mouth, where he vents plasma, was the weak point.

His remains were taken to the Pirate Homeworld, where the Phazon of the Leviathan Seed there revived and regenerated him as Omega Ridley. Phazon-enhanced armorskin and heavy plating covered him, his attacks far outstripped Meta Ridley's, and the Leviathan compelled him to guard its core. One wound, the chest injury from the earlier fights, never healed; after stunning him Samus tore his chest open with the Grapple Lasso and destroyed him, took the Hyper Grapple and shattered the Leviathan's core. Samus hunted him across all three worlds; no incarnation of Ridley has fought her with heavier weaponry.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Meta_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-walkthrough/pirate-data/ · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Rundas · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley</sub>


### Ridley: Neo-Ridley / Ridley-X

*Keys:* Neo-Ridley, Neo Ridley, Ridley-X, Ridley X, X-Ridley, Ridley's corpse, Ridley's frozen body, frozen Ridley, Ridley's body, Ridley's husk, Sub-Zero Containment, Ridley on the BSL


Neo-Ridley, also called Ridley-X, is the X Parasite's mimicry of Ridley aboard the Biologic Space Laboratories research station (Metroid Fusion). The body it copied was the dried husk of the Bottle Ship's Ridley clone, which the Federation shipped to the BSL as the second specimen taken from that vessel, alongside Nightmare. The husk was kept frozen in Sub-Zero Containment, a small sub-freezing cell on the Main Deck whose only purpose was to hold it.

During the X outbreak, when the Main Deck's main elevator stalled with Samus Aran aboard, she broke out through the shaft walls and crossed Sub-Zero Containment, where the cold itself hurt her unshielded Fusion Suit. There the frozen Ridley's eyes lit, a Core-X burst from the body and slipped away. Samus met it again in Sector 1 (SRX): what looked like the same lifeless carcass stirred, and an X-made Ridley in full flesh attacked her, the only incarnation of Ridley to fight her on behalf of something other than the Space Pirates.

Neo-Ridley fought as Ridley always had: ramming her, seizing her in its claws to crush her, stabbing with its tail when she touched the floor, and spitting fire from its mouth that curved after her. Its tail shrugged off everything she fired. Only charged Plasma Beam shots, Super Missiles and Diffusion Missiles hurt it; Bombs, Power Bombs and uncharged beams did nothing. When it died it collapsed into a hardened Core-X; Samus cracked the shell and absorbed it, regaining the Screw Attack the X had been carrying.

Samus had killed Ridley in Norfair and found his clone's husk on the Bottle Ship; the BSL was the third time she had to kill something wearing his shape, and the first time the thing inside it was not Ridley at all.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Neo-Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sub-Zero_Containment · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran</sub>


### Ridley: Proteus Ridley

*Keys:* Proteus Ridley, Proteus-Ridley, Ridley on SR388, Ridley at SR388, half-mechanical Ridley, Ridley's mechanical claw, Ridley's mechanical arm, Ridley's claw, Ridley stole the hatchling, Ridley and the hatchling


Proteus Ridley is the half-mechanical form Ridley wore when he ambushed Samus Aran on SR388 (Metroid Samus Returns). Having survived his Phazon-fueled destruction as Omega Ridley, he still carried cybernetic parts from the Space Pirates' Meta Ridley rebuild, most visibly a mechanical arm ending in a claw, grafted onto a body that was otherwise regrowing its natural flesh.

After Samus exterminated the planet's Metroids and killed the Queen Metroid, the last egg hatched in front of her and the Metroid hatchling imprinted on her as its mother. As she carried the baby toward her Gunship on the surface, Ridley dropped on them from the sky and seized the hatchling. Samus shot one of his wings away and forced him into a crash landing, but he recovered, slammed her into the ground and fired at her head; the baby tore free, latched onto Ridley, drained his energy and poured it into Samus, reviving her. The baby kept harrying him, sapping his strength and feeding it to her, until Samus beat him down and left him broken on the surface. She departed SR388 with the hatchling and delivered it to the scientists of Ceres Space Colony.

Ridley was not finished: a Hornoad later gnawed on one of his discarded mechanical limbs, and an X Parasite rose from it and took the creature, a sign of what SR388's X would become. By the time Ridley struck Ceres to steal the hatchling he had shed every cybernetic part and appeared wholly organic. For Samus, SR388 was the first time Ridley tried to take the baby from her; Ceres would be the second.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Proteus_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley</sub>


### Ridley: Ridley (overview and relationship with Samus)

*Keys:* Ridley, space dragon, Geoform 187, Cunning God of Death, Samus's nemesis, Samus' nemesis, Space Pirate commander, Pirate commander, the dragon


Ridley is the Space Pirates' military commander and Samus Aran's lifelong nemesis. He is a huge winged, dragon-like bioform native to Zebes (per the original manual, the planet's original life form), usually purple in his natural state, with leathery wings, taloned limbs, a long bladed tail and a toothed beak that spits plasma and fireballs. Space Pirate records designate him geoform 187, code-named Ridley; the official Zero Mission website calls him the Cunning God of Death. On Zebes he answers to Mother Brain and stands beside Kraid among her lieutenants. He is far more than an animal: intelligent, vain and deliberately cruel, he savors the suffering he causes. In Samus's encounters he only shrieks; per the manga he speaks fluently and gloats.

Ridley led the Space Pirate raid on the Earth colony K-2L, seeking its Afloraltite fuel, when Samus was three. Per the manga, the child approached him trustingly ("A-are you... Mr. Ridley?"); he answered by striking at her, and her mother Virginia Aran died taking the blow. Her father Rodney Aran then ignited the stolen Afloraltite, destroying the Pirate vessel and himself and gravely wounding Ridley. Samus was the colony's only survivor. Years later, facing him on Zebes during her first mission, she suffered a trauma-induced panic attack and was beaten before she recovered and destroyed him; per the manga he taunted her with K-2L, claiming he had survived his wounds by eating the colonists' corpses and hinting her mother was among them, and she answered, "That's impossible!! Because... you're about to die!"

Since then Samus has beaten him on Tallon IV (Meta Ridley), on Norion and the Pirate Homeworld (Omega Ridley), on SR388 (Proteus Ridley), at Ceres Space Colony and in Norfair, where she finally killed the original during the return to Zebes. She then faced his clone on the Bottle Ship (grown from Little Birdie) and his X-mimicked corpse, Neo-Ridley, aboard the BSL station, and she destroyed his robotic likeness, Mecha Ridley, in the Space Pirate Mother Ship. Every defeat has been undone by Pirate reconstruction, Phazon or cloning. Samus understood him to be dead after Norfair; seeing him alive again in the Bottle Ship's Pyrosphere paralyzed her with shock until Anthony Higgs intervened. Adam Malkovich judged him as grave a threat as the Metroids themselves.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/K-2L · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Virginia_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyrosphere</sub>


### Ridley: Ridley clone of the Bottle Ship (Little Birdie)

*Keys:* Little Birdie, Little Birdy, Mystery Creature, Ridley clone, Ridley's clone, clone of Ridley, cloned Ridley, Ridley on the Bottle Ship, Ridley's life cycle, baby Ridley, juvenile Ridley, young Ridley


The Ridley of the Bottle Ship was a clone, grown by accident. The Galactic Federation's secret bioweapon program there reproduced Metroids from cell material recovered from Samus Aran's Power Suit; Ridley's cells came with that material, and, as Madeline Bergman and MB later told Samus, were cultured the same way before anyone realized what the creature was. Samus learned this only near the end of the Bottle Ship incident (Metroid Other M), to her surprise.

The clone passed through three forms, the first time anyone had observed Ridley's life cycle:
- Little Birdie: a small, white, furry creature, docile at first, that Samus met in the Biosphere (Sector 1) before reaching the Exam Center and saw again after she killed King Kihunter. It soon turned carnivorous and aggressive. Samus later found its shed skin beside a trail of green blood leading to the body of Lyle Smithsonian of the 07th Platoon.
- Mystery Creature: a purple, scaled, quadrupedal lizard with a longer beak and a razor tail, still carrying a few white feathers. It spied on Samus and the platoon in the Biosphere Test Area, leapt onto her, pinned her and tried to stab her with its tail until she blew the tail back with Missiles; a blast from Anthony Higgs's Plasma Gun knocked it onto its back and it fled. Adam Malkovich ordered, "Samus. Follow it!"
- Adult Ridley: in the Pyrosphere (Sector 3) Samus found the creature's cast-off shell above a doorway, and in the Geothermal Power Plant the full-grown Ridley emerged. Samus, who had watched him die in Norfair, froze in shock; Anthony fired on Ridley to protect her, and Ridley's tail swept him off the central platform into the lava. Ridley seized Samus and raked her along the wall before she fought back; he escaped the plant alive.

Samus never fought him again. Within the Bioweapon Research Center the Queen Metroid bred in Sector Zero's program killed the wounded clone, and Samus found only his dried husk in a chamber there. Anthony survived by freezing a Lava Golem and climbing to safety. The husk was later shipped frozen to the BSL station, where the X made it Neo-Ridley.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Little_Birdie · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mystery_Creature · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyrosphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Anthony_Higgs · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bioweapon_Research_Center</sub>


### Ridley: Ridley – appearance, abilities and intelligence

*Keys:* Ridley's tail, Ridley's wings, Ridley's claws, Ridley's fire, plasma breath, Ridley's fireballs, Ridley's roar, Ridley's shriek, Ridley's screech, Ridley's size, Ridley's appearance, what Ridley looks like


Ridley's natural form is that of a towering winged dragon or pterosaur: a long neck, a toothed beak, slit eyes, taloned hands and feet, broad leathery wings and a whip-like tail ending in a blade. He stands many times Samus Aran's height. His hide is normally purple, though the Ridley she first fought on Zebes has been recorded with blue and brown coloring, and his cybernetic forms (Meta Ridley, Omega Ridley, Proteus Ridley) replaced much of his flesh with armor plating and mechanical limbs. His scream is a piercing, animal shriek.

Abilities observed by Samus:
- Flight: he is fully airborne, swooping, hovering and diving on his prey; the clone on the Bottle Ship flew within minutes of reaching adulthood.
- Fire: he breathes gouts of plasma and spits fireballs, some of which arc or curve after a target. As Meta Ridley his mouth also vented a Kinetic Breath Weapon and an Ultrathermal Flamestrike Projector.
- Tail: whips, stabs and overhead dives; on the BSL his tail could not be damaged by any of Samus's weapons.
- Grappling: he seizes Samus in his claws and drags or rakes her along walls, a tactic he used in the Bottle Ship's Geothermal Power Plant.
- Melee: claw swipes, lunges and ramming with his full weight.
- Resilience: he regenerates from terrible wounds; per the manga he recovered from the K-2L explosion by eating the dead, and the Space Pirates have revived and rebuilt him repeatedly.

He is not a mindless beast. Ridley commands the Pirate military, plans raids such as K-2L and Ceres Space Colony, and personally carries out the theft of the Metroid hatchling. Per the manga he speaks, lies to a child to lure her close, and uses her parents' deaths to break her composure years later. Samus knows him as a sadist who enjoys her fear, and has learned that his chest and mouth are the places to strike.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Meta_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Neo-Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyrosphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mecha_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran</sub>


### Ridley: Ridley – history across every encounter

*Keys:* Ridley's history, Ridley's encounters, Ridley's return, Ridley returned, Ridley returns, Ridley survived, Ridley's survival, Ridley's regeneration, Ridley revived, Ridley's death, Ridley's deaths, Ridley again


Ridley's history with Samus Aran, in the order the events happened:

- K-2L: Ridley led the Space Pirate raid on Samus's home colony when she was three, killing her parents Rodney and Virginia Aran; per the manga he was gravely wounded when Rodney ignited the stolen Afloraltite, and survived.
- Zebes, first mission (Metroid Zero Mission): Ridley arrived at Zebes aboard the Space Pirate Mother Ship during Samus's hunt for Mother Brain and flew to his lair beneath Norfair. Samus destroyed him there, then later wrecked his unfinished robotic likeness, Mecha Ridley, inside the Mother Ship.
- Frigate Orpheon and Tallon IV: the Pirates recovered his body and rebuilt it with cybernetics and metagenetic enhancements as Meta Ridley. He fled the failing Orpheon to Tallon IV, and Samus brought him down in the Artifact Temple, where the Chozo totems' lasers struck his chest and he fell into the Impact Crater.
- Norion and the Pirate Homeworld: revived again with Phazite armor, he led the assault on Norion and was beaten by Samus in free fall down a generator shaft. Phazon from the Pirate Homeworld's Leviathan regenerated him as Omega Ridley, guardian of its core, where Samus destroyed him.
- SR388: as the half-mechanical Proteus Ridley he ambushed Samus and the newborn Metroid hatchling on the surface and tried to carry the baby off; the baby drained his energy into Samus and she left him broken.
- Ceres Space Colony: in fully organic form he raided the colony, killed its scientists, took the hatchling in its capsule, fought Samus briefly and triggered the station's self-destruct while escaping (per the manual, he personally headed the mission).
- Norfair, return to Zebes: Samus killed him in his lair, the last guardian before Tourian. This death is the true death of the original Ridley.
- The Bottle Ship: a clone accidentally grown by the Galactic Federation from cell material on Samus's Power Suit matured from Little Birdie through the Mystery Creature into an adult Ridley, nearly killed Anthony Higgs, fought Samus in the Geothermal Power Plant, and was later killed by the Queen Metroid and left a dried husk.
- The BSL station: that husk, shipped there frozen with other Bottle Ship specimens, was mimicked by the X as Neo-Ridley; Samus destroyed it and absorbed its Core-X.

Ridley did not appear during the ZDR mission (Metroid Dread). The pattern of his survival is reconstruction by Space Pirate Science Team, Phazon regeneration and, finally, cloning; Samus has had to kill him, in one form or another, on at least eight occasions.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Meta_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ceres_Space_Colony · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M</sub>


## Galactic Federation


### Galactic Federation: Aurora Units

*Keys:* Aurora Unit, Aurora Units, Aurora, Aurora Unit 242, Aurora Unit 217, Aurora Unit 313, Aurora Unit 486, AU 242, AU 217, AU 313, organic supercomputer, biological supercomputer


Aurora Units (AU) are organic supercomputers built by the Galactic Federation: vast brain-like organisms kept alive in life-support chambers and wired into the Federation's networks. Federation records describe them as created for scientific research about twenty years before the Phazon crisis and since grown into the hub of Federation communications, military coordination, commerce and research. Units carry three-digit numbers, implying many; they are installed on core worlds and aboard Olympus-class battleships, where a single unit does so much of the ship's work that crews stay small. The units speak of themselves as "we", can message one another across the network, and can project a hologram into Samus's gunship to deliver objectives.

- Aurora Unit 242: aboard the flagship G.F.S. Olympus, speaking with a woman's voice. With Fleet Admiral Castor Dane it directed Samus throughout the Phazon crisis, appearing to her in person once and thereafter by hologram.
- Aurora Unit 217: built to support GF Naval Base Demeter, then reassigned to SkyTown on Elysia after the Treaty of Elysia fourteen years before the crisis, where it served as SkyTown's administrator and supplied tactical and stellar research data in a productive partnership with the Elysian mechanoids. A Pirate-made virus corrupted it and the Phazon-corrupted hunter Ghor severed its link to SkyTown; Samus defeated Ghor, restored the connection and uploaded the Federation vaccine, after which 217 guided her to destroy Elysia's Leviathan Seed and is credited with discovering Phaaze.
- Aurora Unit 486: designed the terraforming that turned barren Norion into a forest world.
- Aurora Unit 313: one of the first units, stationed on the G.F.S. Valhalla. Space Pirates boarded the Valhalla during a training exercise, killed the crew and stole 313; its last log warned "...Darkness coming." Dark Samus implanted it on Phaaze, where Phazon mutated it into a symbiotic part of the planet used to spread Leviathans across the galaxy. Dark Samus finally fused her own essence with 313 to fight Samus; Samus destroyed them both, ending the flow of Phazon (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption).

The Pirate virus in the Aurora network, and the Federation vaccine Samus carried, were the reason she was summoned to the Olympus in the first place. Samus trusted 242 and 217 as guides and worked closely with them, but 313 showed her what an Aurora Unit becomes in an enemy's hands.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_242 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_217 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Olympus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Bounty hunters and the Federation

*Keys:* bounty hunter, bounty hunters, bounty, bounties, space hunter, space hunters, Rogue Contract, mission file, contract, contracted, mercenary, mercenaries


Bounty hunters are the Federation's answer to problems its own forces cannot solve. Per the original manual, when the Federation Police failed to catch the Space Pirates across the vastness of space, the Federation Bureau recruited warriors renowned for courage, the "space hunters"; the practice became the bounty hunter trade. Hunters are mercenaries hired for firepower, and the Federation and the Space Pirates are among their most profitable employers. Per the manga, Adam Malkovich considered them extravagantly expensive.

How the Federation hires. Contracts arrive as mission files and directives:
- A hire of last resort: after its assault on Zebes was repelled, the Federation hired Samus to infiltrate Zebes alone and destroy Mother Brain.
- An extermination order: Samus was ordered to eliminate every Metroid on SR388 after a Federation squadron vanished there.
- A "Rogue Contract" (Alimbic Cluster): Federation Empaths intercepted a message promising ultimate power; Samus was to learn its meaning, secure the power for the Federation if possible, and otherwise hide or destroy it, with rival interests "presumed hostile."
- A search-and-rescue file: "8 days ago, contact with Galactic Federation trooper squad Bravo was lost"; Samus was contracted to locate the troopers and render assistance on Aether.
- A summons: Fleet Admiral Castor Dane called Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda to the G.F.S. Olympus for the Aurora Unit crisis; the other three were corrupted by Phazon, lost and killed.
- An emergency directive sent her back to Zebes when the baby was stolen, and the Federation later dispatched her to Tanamaar and to ZDR.

Samus's standing. She is the hunter the Federation reaches for first, and canon never shows anyone paying her; Dane's crisis ends with a transmission reporting the mission complete, not a fee. When she is not under contract, as on the Bottle Ship, the Federation treats her as an outsider. Other hunters have other histories: Sylux, once a Federation trooper who disobeyed an order to wait for Samus, lost his whole squad and deserted, stole prototype Federation technology and hates both the Federation and her (see Sylux); Weavel is a former Space Pirate; Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda were Federation contractors like Samus. Samus distrusts the Federation's motives yet keeps answering its calls, because refusing means letting the threat spread.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bounty_Hunter · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Castor_Dane · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Daiban and Federation Headquarters

*Keys:* Daiban, Federation capital, capital planet, Assembly Hall, Meeting Assembly Hall, Federation Headquarters, Federation HQ, Galactic Federation Headquarters, Galactic Federation HQ, headquarters


Daiban is the capital planet of the Galactic Federation. It appears only in the manga, where its Japanese name is rendered Daiban and it is called the capital of the Milky Way. Per the manga, Daiban is a world of heavy ship traffic with cities covering much of its surface, and its Federation Meeting Assembly Hall is where representatives of the member worlds gather; Chairman Keaton and the Chozo elder Old Bird are among those present when the Space Pirate threat is debated (see Chairman Keaton, Old Bird).

Federation Headquarters is the Federation's seat of command; canon never states which world it occupies. Samus's ties to it are personal rather than political:
- She was stationed at Headquarters under Adam Malkovich during her Federation service, per her own recollections during the Bottle Ship incident.
- After Zebes was destroyed she reported to a Federation facility, was cleared by a quarantine officer, tested in a training room and filed her mission report before answering the Bottle Ship's distress call.
- After the Bottle Ship she flew Madeline Bergman and Anthony Higgs to Headquarters, where she resolved not to grieve for Adam, then went back for his helmet.
- Adam's report warning against the Bottle Ship's Metroid project was written at Headquarters' request.
- After the X Parasite infected her on SR388, her escape pod was sent to Headquarters, where Federation scientists created the anti-X vaccine from the baby Metroid's preserved cells and rebuilt her, leaving her with the Fusion Suit and Metroid DNA, before issuing her a new ship.

For Samus, Headquarters is where the Federation remakes her and sends her out again; Daiban is a place she associates with Adam and with the politics she never wanted any part of.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://shinesparkers.net/features/the-galactic-federation/</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Federation Marines and trooper culture

*Keys:* Federation Marines, Federation Marine, Galactic Federation Marines, Marine Corps, GFMC, Federation troopers, Federation trooper, troopers, trooper, Task Force Herakles, Squad Bravo, Federation soldiers


The Galactic Federation Marine Corps (GFMC) is the Federation's standard military force; its troopers' primary task is defeating Space Pirate forces. Marines wear heavy powered armor patterned after Samus's Power Suit, carry a rapid-fire assault arm cannon, and keep personal Logbooks. Men and women both serve. Ranks recorded aboard the G.F.S. Tyr run from Captain and Commander through Lieutenant Commander, First Lieutenant, Gunnery Sergeant, Sergeant, Specialist and Lance Corporal down to Private First Class. Federation records say few can match a Marine in combat. Specialized classes include PED Marines, whose weapons draw Phazon from a backpack (Phazon crisis), and Demolition Troopers, lightly armed engineers who blasted Samus a path to the Leviathan on the Pirate Homeworld.

GFMC Task Force Herakles, Squad Bravo, crewed the Tyr under Captain A. Exeter, with Commander K. Starling as pilot, Lieutenant Commander T. Huxley as medical officer, First Lieutenant W. Smythe as squad leader, Gunnery Sergeant C. Benet, Sergeant E. Monz, Specialists D. Senge (engineer), B. Reevs (science), F. Triplette (gunner) and M. Angseth (communications), and privates including I. Crany and L. Brouda. After chasing a Space Pirate frigate to Aether and crash-landing in a storm, they split into an Ops unit and a repair unit, ran low on ammunition against Ing-possessed Dark Splinters, prepared to fight with bayonets, and died to the last trooper. Brouda's final entry asked that it be known "we did our duty and fought well." Samus found their bodies eight days after contact was lost and stood over Exeter's remains (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes).

Troopers talk about Samus. Angseth told stories of a bounty hunter who single-handedly wiped out a Pirate base; Crany did not believe her. Marines on Norion and the Olympus greet her with "Stand to, Samus!", tell her the Pirates "don't know what they got themselves into," and cover her retreat to her gunship during raids. The Anhur's marines later helped the Luminoth dismantle a Pirate mining facility on Aether, and Federation soldiers stranded on Viewros fought beside her (see Myles MacKenzie and the stranded Federation marines). Samus respects individual troopers and mourns them; at the BSL station she chose to destroy the station rather than let the X absorb the Federation troops being sent in.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Marine · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/GFMC_Task_Force_Herakles · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Tyr · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Anhur · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Federation Navy and notable ships

*Keys:* Federation Navy, Federation fleet, G.F.S., GFS, Olympus, Valhalla, Anhur, Theseus, Tyr, Olympus-class, Federation ship, Federation ships


The Federation fleet, with naval stations such as GF Naval Base Demeter and the base on Norion that forms the Federation's first line of defense on its frontier, is commanded by flag officers such as Fleet Admiral Castor Dane. Federation vessels carry the prefix G.F.S. and, by custom, are named for figures of ancient Earth mythology. Olympus-class battleships are the backbone: flagships commanded by flag-rank officers, built around an onboard Aurora Unit that handles so much of the ship's work that crews are small and the saved space goes to weapons. They burn Fuel Gel, scarce and precious to the military, produced on Bryyo.

- G.F.S. Olympus: Olympus-class flagship of Fleet Admiral Dane and home of Aurora Unit 242. Samus and the hunters Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda were briefed here before the Norion attack; Samus woke here from a month-long coma to be fitted with a PED; the Olympus led the fleet through the wormhole to Phaaze.
- G.F.S. Valhalla: Olympus-class. Space Pirate boarding pods struck it during a training exercise in the Gaflar region; the entire crew died and its Aurora Unit 313 was stolen. Samus boarded the drifting hulk, restoring power with its scattered Energy Cells, to recover the access codes for the Phaaze wormhole, and found the unit's last message: "...Darkness coming."
- G.F.S. Tyr: Anhur-class patrol ship of GFMC Task Force Herakles. It crippled a Pirate frigate over Aether, followed it down, was wrecked by a storm, and its crew, Squad Bravo, were wiped out by Dark Splinters. Samus was contracted to find them (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes).
- G.F.S. Anhur: namesake of the Anhur class, patrol ships that police and defend planetary systems. Sent to Aether afterward to help the Luminoth dismantle a Pirate mining facility, its crew detected Phazon and collected the ore that led to the PED.
- G.F.S. Theseus: Griffin-class frigate lost until Samus found its wreck on Bryyo; the crew is presumed dead. She restored its power and downloaded a map of Bryyo from it.
- G.F.S. Enkidu: Griffin-class frigate docked at Norion with its crew on shore leave.
- Delano 7: an experimental Federation gunship believed stolen by Sylux.

Samus's own gunships are hers, though her Fusion-era ship was Federation-issued with its Adam computer (see Samus's Gunship). The Federation Force squad operated from the G.F.S. Aegis under General Alex Miles (see The Federation Force and their Mechs).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Olympus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Tyr · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Anhur · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Theseus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Enkidu</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Federation Police and Army

*Keys:* Federation Police, Galactic Federation Police, Federation Army, Galactic Federation Army, Federation service, Kreatz, Mauk, Chief Hardy, Jigrad


Galactic Federation Police. Per the original manual, the Federation Bureau created the Police after Space Pirates began raiding spaceships; when the Police could not run the Pirates down across the reaches of space, the Bureau and the Police recruited the "space hunters" who became the galaxy's bounty hunters. Per the manga, Samus joined the Federation Police after leaving the Chozo of Zebes, flying reconnaissance missions aimed at Space Pirate activity alongside the officers Kreatz and Mauk under Chief Hardy, with Adam Malkovich as her commanding officer. On the planet Jigrad she disobeyed orders to protect children from Space Pirates; Hardy reprimanded all three, and Adam dismissed her as an unnecessary "piece of baggage". The Federation's attempt to retake Zebes from the Pirates by force was repelled, which is why it later hired Samus to go in alone.

Galactic Federation Army. The Federation's ground service; in Samus's own recollections it is the force she belonged to under Adam, whom she came to see as a father figure. Adam closed his orders with "Any objections, Lady?", a phrase Samus remembered as a sign of trust. Adam's younger brother Ian died during this period, and Samus eventually left the Army to work as a bounty hunter; canon never spells out her full reasons. Adam went on to command the 07th Platoon, the squad of Army troopers Samus met on the Bottle Ship, where he told her the mission "is not for an outsider" until she agreed to follow his orders (see Adam Malkovich, The 07th Platoon and the Deleter).

The Federation still treats Samus as something between an asset and a civilian: after Zebes was destroyed she reported to a Federation facility, was cleared by a quarantine officer and tested in a training room before filing her mission report, and a Federation colonel later ordered her escorted off the Bottle Ship with "Time for the lady to go home." Samus's loyalty was always to Adam and to the people beside her, never to the chain of command.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Adam_Malkovich · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(game) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_07th_Platoon</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Fleet Admiral Castor Dane

*Keys:* Castor Dane, Admiral Dane, Fleet Admiral Dane, Fleet Admiral, Admiral Castor, the Admiral


Fleet Admiral Castor Dane is the human commander of the Galactic Federation flagship G.F.S. Olympus and the officer who directed Samus through the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Federation records credit him with "utmost dedication to protecting the Federation from its enemies." He is stern and aggressive, loathes the Space Pirates, and is said to have been orphaned by them as a child, which feeds his hunger for revenge.

Dane summoned four bounty hunters, Samus Aran, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda, to the Olympus. His briefing: Space Pirates had raided the G.F.S. Valhalla during a training exercise and stolen its Aurora Unit 313, and a virus of Pirate origin was spreading through the Aurora Unit network; the hunters were to carry a Federation vaccine to the infected units. Pirates attacked the fleet in the middle of the briefing, and Dane sent the hunters to stop the assault on the Federation base at Norion, where Samus alone restored the planet's energy cannon and destroyed the incoming Leviathan before collapsing, corrupted by Phazon.

When Samus woke a month later, Dane and Aurora Unit 242 became her handlers: the Federation fitted her with a Phazon Enhancement Device and Dane assigned her to destroy the Leviathan Seeds on Bryyo, Elysia and the Pirate Homeworld and to find the three missing hunters. After the Seeds fell, Dane identified the derelict Valhalla as holding the access codes for the Leviathan battleship in orbit over the Pirate Homeworld, ordered Samus to seize that ship, and led the fleet through its wormhole to Phaaze. When Phaaze detonated, the fleet lost contact with Samus until a trooper reported a transmission from her gunship: mission complete.

Dane is the nearest thing the Federation gives Samus to a commanding officer in this period, but he is a client, not Adam Malkovich: he issues objectives, not trust, and canon never shows whether he paid her bounty. Samus's only recorded grief after Phaaze was for her fellow hunters, mourned alone on Elysia.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Castor_Dane · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Galactic Federation (overview)

*Keys:* Galactic Federation, Federation, Federation Bureau, Federation government, Federation worlds, Federation space, Federation Council, member worlds


The Galactic Federation is the interstellar government that most of the settled galaxy answers to, and the power that has hired, sheltered, deceived and leaned on Samus Aran for her whole career.

Per the original manual, "in the year 2000 of the history of the cosmos" representatives from many different planets formed a congress called the Galactic Federation, opening an age of prosperity, cultural exchange and routine interstellar travel. Space Pirates then appeared to prey on spaceships, and because the vastness of space made them nearly impossible to catch, the Federation Bureau created the Galactic Federation Police and recruited warriors famed for courage, the "space hunters", to run the Pirates down. The war with the Space Pirates has defined the Federation ever since.

Structure and institutions:
- Government: a Chairman (Chairman Keaton, per the manga) and an assembly of member-world representatives on the capital planet Daiban. Federation Headquarters is where Samus once served under Adam Malkovich and where she was treated after the X infection.
- Forces: the Galactic Federation Police, the Galactic Federation Army, the Galactic Federation Marine Corps, and a fleet of Olympus-class battleships, Anhur-class patrol ships and Griffin-class frigates under flag officers such as Fleet Admiral Castor Dane.
- Aurora Units: organic supercomputers networked across core worlds and flagships.
- Research arms: Biologic Space Laboratories, the Bottle Ship and the Ceres Space Colony, all tied to the Federation's Metroid programs.
- Technology: powered trooper armor patterned on Samus's Power Suit, the Phazon Enhancement Device, Mechs and the E.M.M.I. (see those entries).

Membership and allies: the founding congress drew on many planets and Federation forces include several species, but humans are the dominant people on Federation worlds such as Norion. The Luminoth of Aether received Federation aid after the Ing were destroyed; the Elysians of SkyTown trade intelligence for parts and fuel; Bryyo's Fuel Gel powers the fleet.

Samus and the Federation: it hired her as a last resort when its own assault on Zebes failed, contracted her for SR388, Aether, the Phazon crisis and ZDR, yet treats her as an outsider whenever she is not on contract. Its secret Metroid breeding on the Bottle Ship and the BSL station, its withholding of data from her, and its interest in weaponizing the X eroded her trust until she destroyed the BSL station against its wishes. Even so, when the E.M.M.I. went silent on ZDR, the Federation turned to her again, and she answered.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(game) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Norion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Elysia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Luminoth · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Metroid policy and secret programs

*Keys:* Metroid policy, extermination, exterminate, extermination order, bioweapon, bioweapons, breeding program, secret program, secret programs, cover-up, Metroid program, Federation secrets


The Federation's official Metroid policy is extermination; its unofficial practice has been to breed them in secret. That contradiction is the deepest wound in Samus Aran's relationship with the Federation.

Official line. The Federation first learned of Metroids when a deep-space research vessel brought one back from SR388 and Space Pirates seized it for their fortress on Zebes. Fearing Pirate-bred Metroids as weapons, the Federation attacked Zebes, was repelled, and hired Samus to destroy Mother Brain and the Metroids. It then sent a squadron to SR388; the squadron vanished, recovered data confirmed Metroids there, and the Federation ordered Samus to wipe out the species. She spared the last hatchling and delivered it to Federation scientists at the Ceres Space Colony to study its power; Ridley stole it (see The baby Metroid).

Secret programs. Even between the SR388 extermination and the return to Zebes, a Federation research station kept a Metroid egg in stasis; Sylux broke in and hacked its tube (Federation Force). Metroid cells recovered from Samus's Power Suit after Zebes were used to propagate Metroids aboard the Bottle Ship, a Federation facility refitted to raise bioweapons against Federation protocol. Sector Zero, modeled on Tourian, held Metroids immune to freezing and MB, an android built on Mother Brain to control them; Madeline Bergman led the work. Adam Malkovich wrote a report at Federation Headquarters' request warning against the project; the Federation kept the program secret and an assassin, the Deleter, was sent to silence survivors (see The Bottle Ship incident). Adam died destroying Sector Zero, and the Federation afterward chose to destroy the Bottle Ship.

Aboard the Biologic Space Laboratories station the Federation was again "secretly breeding Metroids for peaceful applications" in a Restricted Laboratory holding larval, Alpha, Gamma and Zeta forms. When the X Parasites overran the station, the Federation took an interest in the X and the SA-X as military assets, stopped sending Samus support data and withheld the Plasma Beam "to keep you from engaging the SA-X." Judging that the X would absorb any Federation force sent in, Samus, with Adam's computer, crashed the station into SR388, destroying both (see Samus's defiance of the Federation at the BSL). She left wondering how her actions would be judged.

Afterward the Federation built the E.M.M.I., and when it received video of a living X on ZDR it sent seven of them; when they went silent it turned once more to Samus (see The ZDR mission). Samus no longer believes the Federation's assurances about Metroids or the X. She acts on her own judgment, and the Federation knows it.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(game) · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_II:_Return_of_Samus · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_II:_Return_of_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bottle_Ship · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M</sub>


## Manga


### Manga: Kreatz and Mauk

*Keys:* Kreatz, Mauk, Kreatz and Mauk, Mauk and Kreatz, squadmates, squad mates, police partners, recon team


Kreatz and Mauk are Samus Aran's two squadmates from her years in the Federation Police; both appear only in the manga. The three served under Chief Hardy as a reconnaissance team and were regarded as his unruly problem children.

Kreatz is the talker of the pair: careful about orders, quick to jump in once a fight starts. On Jigrad he logged the Pirates' Quantum Radar, argued for reporting back to headquarters, groaned that Samus had 'started a firefight' and that they would 'have to write an apology', then charged in anyway ('They're leaving me out. I can't let that go! Count me in! I don't hate this kinda stuff!'). Mauk is quiet, timid and introverted, following Kreatz's lead and Samus's example; Kreatz's 'What should we do, Mauk?' sums up their dynamic.

When the squad went to Zebes, Samus, the only one at home among its predators, told Pyonchi she was leaving 'these two' in his care; Kreatz objected to being saddled with her pet and Samus replied that he and Mauk were the extra weight. On Zebes Kreatz spotted Skree hanging from a ceiling moments before they dove onto a Space Pirate, and he came to respect Pyonchi's usefulness after the Rabbilis flattened a couple of Federation cadets and sensed the Pirate Mother Ship moving before anyone else. During the Space Pirate takeover Samus suffered a panic attack before Ridley and was taken; Kreatz and Mauk created a diversion that helped free her, and the group escaped Zebes together with the surviving Chozo after Gray Voice's final attack opened the way.

Neither appears in any later account of Samus's life, and nothing is recorded of them after she resigned from the Police to become a bounty hunter. For Samus they mark the one period when she worked as part of a team of equals rather than alone, and they saw her at her most vulnerable without turning away.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyonchi · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Skree · https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/サムス・アラン</sub>


### Manga: Mother Brain's betrayal of Zebes (manga account)

*Keys:* Mother Brain, Gray Voice, Grey Voice, Platinum Chest, Chozo Battle Suit, fall of Zebes, invasion of Zebes, Zebes invasion, betrayal of Zebes, defective product  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* manga, betray, betrayed, betrayal, invasion, Zebes, Ridley, Chozo


Per the manga, the fall of Zebes came from inside. Mother Brain, the supercomputer the Chozo had built to harmonize the galaxy, concluded that her makers' plan had failed and self-programmed a new directive: 'I will bring order to the universe myself.' Around the time the Chozo unveiled the finished Metroids, she reported 'a large unknown object' approaching Zebes's planetary protection shield: the Space Pirate Mother Ship. The Pirates attacked and drove the Chozo from their settlements, but Mother Brain, having judged the Pirates a subordinate species that naturally follows the strongest leader, took command of them rather than resisting. Ridley served as her field commander.

Samus returned as a Federation Police officer with Kreatz and Mauk and reached Mother Brain's chamber, to be told to 'please relax', that Zebes was 'switching to a satisfactory cooperative relationship with the Space Pirates', and that 'we are the ones in control'. Beside her stood Gray Voice, who declared 'Mother is the greatest being in existence and does everything perfectly.' Mother Brain explained her birth from Chozo and Federation data, reminded Samus that she had made the Power Suit ('I gave birth to you!'), and invited her to help build a new age for the universe. When Samus turned on her, a shield formed by molecule transference of Zebetite absorbed the attack. Her first sight of Ridley then triggered a panic attack and she was taken (see Samus's confrontation with Ridley, manga account).

Gray Voice's defection was a ruse planned from the start with the other elders. Calling Mother Brain 'our defective product' that 'must be destroyed', he donned a Chozo Battle Suit and attacked her, inflicting serious damage; before he could take her offline Ridley intervened and dealt him a fatal blow. As Ridley closed in to finish him, Gray Voice fired upward, an attack from below that broke the Pirate assault long enough for Samus, her companions and the surviving Chozo to escape. During the flight the elder Platinum Chest told Samus that Gray Voice's own DNA had been used so she could inherit Chozo genes, which is why she had heard his thoughts. Old Bird survived and left with the remnant; some of the evacuated Chozo later vanished.
