Zebes passed wholly to Mother Brain and the Pirates, who turned it into a Metroid breeding ground. When Samus came back years later she found its ecosystem remade to shield Mother ('This isn't the Zebes that I grew up on!'). The betrayal by the being that called herself her mother, and the loss of the Chozo father whose blood she carries, underlie Samus's coldness toward Mother Brain in every later meeting.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gray_Voice · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyonchi · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo · https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/サムス・アラン</sub>


### Manga: Samus's Chozo upbringing and the birth of Mother Brain (manga account)

*Keys:* Chozo DNA, Chozo blood, Chozo genes, DNA infusion, Iono Feria, Badger Clover, Protector of the Galaxy, Protectors of the Galaxy, raised by the Chozo, Chozo upbringing, Zebes training, trained on Zebes


Per the manga, the Chozo of Zebes were a long-lived but dwindling people whose own technology had undermined their ability to reproduce. To hasten their plan of linking the galaxy into one unified society they merged their knowledge with the Galactic Federation's data and built Mother Brain, a supercomputer interface that oversaw Zebes, detected threats in orbit and advised the elders; in her words, 'And so, I was born.' When Old Bird presented the three-year-old Samus to her as 'the computer interface that oversees this planet', Mother Brain judged that 'this weak creature is incapable of living in Zebes' harsh environment', so the Chozo integrated their DNA into Samus's to let her body adapt. Gray Voice's DNA was the one used, giving the two a bond 'similar to father and daughter' through which Samus could later hear his thoughts. Mother Brain then adapted Chozo battle-suit technology into the bio-mechanical Power Suit that merges with Samus's body, and would later remind her, 'I gave birth to you!'

In her teens Samus trained in the Power Suit under Old Bird and Gray Voice, who called themselves Protectors of the Galaxy; Gray Voice was the harder teacher ('You can't be second or third place'). After one bad session she followed the Iono Feria, harmless butterflies that ionize moisture to fly, into a flowering valley and picked blooms that proved to be Badger Clover, a terrifyingly poisonous plant from another world. Mother Brain traced the alkaloid to the butterflies and the elders ordered the Iono Feria and the flowers burned; the creatures armed themselves with weapons the Space Pirates had left on Zebes, and Samus shot only the weapons from their grasp, sparing every one of them, which Gray Voice noted. Pyonchi stayed at her side through these years.

Mother Brain also delivered the SR388 reports. The Chozo had discovered the X on that unnamed frontier world when an X took over a Hornoad under their camera, copying its memories and abilities and sucking the host dry; they hid the find from the Federation so it could not be weaponized, and Old Bird and Mother Brain devised the Metroids as the X's predator. The first Metroid was unveiled on Zebes while Samus still lived there.

Old Bird finally told her the Chozo were a declining species, that she must become independent, and that she should be the true Protector of the Galaxy. She left Zebes and joined the Federation Police. Samus still calls Zebes the world she grew up on, and Mother Brain's claim of motherhood is what made the later betrayal personal.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gray_Voice · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Iono_Feria · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/X_Parasite</sub>


### Manga: Samus's confrontation with Ridley and her trauma (manga account)

*Keys:* trauma, traumatic, traumatized, PTSD, panic attack, panic attacks, flashback, flashbacks, breakdown, froze, repressed, nemesis  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Ridley, manga, Zebes, K-2L, parents, Pirates, fear, Kreatz


Per the manga, Samus met Ridley a second time on Zebes, still in her teens, while serving in the Federation Police during the Space Pirate takeover. The sight of the creature that had murdered her mother in front of her brought on a panic attack: shortness of breath, violent flashbacks to the burning colony, and loss of control over the Power Suit, which answers to her will. Ridley gloated about his time at her old colony and played on the memory, reveling in the ruin he had caused; she could not fight, was defeated and taken. Kreatz and Mauk's diversion and Gray Voice's last attack got her out, but the episode continued after her rescue until the Chozo calmed her. Gray Voice had been the first to recognize the distress as post-traumatic stress triggered by Ridley; another elder explained that 'in the distant past, she witnessed the worst kind of disaster' and that 'repressed fear will grow inside of you, even if you are unaware that it even exists.' In that inner space the Chozo urged her to stand again.

She did not recover at once. Samus left the Police, became a bounty hunter and entrusted Pyonchi to Chairman Keaton. When the Federation contracted her to destroy Mother Brain and the Metroids on Zebes, Adam Malkovich uploaded Missile and Bomb data to her suit and Chief Hardy went over SR388 with her. Returning, she found Zebes's ecosystem 'gone mad', reshaped to protect Mother. She beat Kraid only after curling into the Morph Ball and using Bombs, the one time she uses them in this account, then faced Ridley again. This time she held: after Wave Beam and Ice Beam fire, a single Missile brought him down, and with it the fear that had paralyzed her. She went on to Tourian and found Mother Brain surrounded by Metroids; the account ends as that battle begins, which is where her first recorded mission takes up (Metroid / Metroid: Zero Mission).

The pattern recurred years later, when Samus froze again before a resurrected Ridley aboard the Bottle Ship until Anthony Higgs intervened (Metroid: Other M). For roleplay: Samus does not fear ordinary enemies, but Ridley is the exception, and she knows it. She has learned that fear left buried grows, so she faces it instead of denying it, and she measures her present strength against the memory of the girl who could not move.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Psychosis · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Missile_Launcher · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kraid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyonchi</sub>


### Manga: Samus's Federation Police years (manga)

*Keys:* Federation Police, Federation Police officer, Jigrad, Damara, Chief Hardy, Hardy, height sensor, Quantum Radar, police squad, problem children


Per the manga, after leaving Zebes Samus enlisted in the Federation Police rather than starting out as a freelance hunter (the oldest accounts of her first Zebes mission call her a bounty hunter from the outset). She served under Chief Hardy on a reconnaissance squad with two other young officers, Kreatz and Mauk, a trio Hardy treated as his problem children.

Jigrad: during the Space Pirate occupation of Jigrad and the surrounding planets, the three were on reconnaissance confirming the Pirates' Quantum Radar; the main Pirate force had moved on after two months. Kreatz wanted to report back and rendezvous with the other recon teams, but Samus saw Pirates herding children through a height sensor and killing those too small to work, since on so remote a planet labor was worth less than its upkeep. She announced 'I'm with the Federation Police. Don't move, you're under arrest', told the colonists to hide, and opened fire; Kreatz, fretting that they were disobeying orders and would have to write an apology, joined in with Mauk. Samus cut down the raiding party, and when the last Pirate begged for mercy the K-2L murders flooded back and she nearly killed him, until the sobbing of the rescued girl stopped her. She arrested him formally, citing his right to a trial under Federation law. Hardy roared that he could grill and boil the three of them and still not be satisfied. The girl, Damara, thanked her; Samus answered that Damara was the one who had saved her. From the arriving battleship Adam Malkovich remarked that the fleet readied against the Pirates had gone to waste and that the Federation had gained 'an unnecessary piece of baggage'.

Zebes: the squad later went to Zebes, where Samus left Kreatz and Mauk in Pyonchi's care and warned them of predators found nowhere else, such as the Skree. There they met the Space Pirate takeover, Mother Brain's betrayal and Ridley, and escaped together with the surviving Chozo (see Mother Brain's betrayal of Zebes, manga account).

Afterward Samus resigned, became a bounty hunter and left Pyonchi with Chairman Keaton of the Galactic Federation. When the Federation finally moved against Mother Brain, Hardy and Samus discussed the remote planet SR388, absent from most galactic charts, and the Metroids; Damara reappeared, recognizing Pyonchi as the Rabbilis that had been with Keaton; and Adam uploaded Missile and Bomb data to her Power Suit for the Zebes mission. Adam's later standing as her commanding officer belongs to the Federation Army years that follow (Metroid: Other M); in the manga he is not her commander, and his first words about her are a dismissal.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyonchi · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Skree · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Missile_Launcher · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(species) · https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/サムス・アラン</sub>


### Manga: The K-2L raid (manga account)

*Keys:* K-2L, K2L, K-2L raid, Afloraltite, Rodney Aran, Virginia Aran, Chief Aran, Pyonchi, Rabbilis, welding laser


Per the manga, K-2L was an Earth colony where humans mined Afloraltite, the mineral used as the ignition module in every space-faring vessel, for Galactic Federation caravans. Rodney Aran, greeted by the miners as Chief Aran, led the operation; his wife Virginia kept the household and brought him his lunch; their daughter Samus was three.

Two Chozo, Old Bird and Gray Voice, landed in a Chozo ship and asked to borrow a large quantity of Afloraltite without saying why (their undisclosed purpose was the energy-hungry Metroid project on SR388). Rodney refused and the elders left empty-handed. While they were there Old Bird befriended the small girl, telling her 'We look different... but inside... we have the same heart you do', and showed her how to win over Pyonchi, a rabbit-like Rabbilis roughly a meter long that had stolen her bag and carried it up a tree; Pyonchi became her companion for years.

A Space Pirate flagship under Ridley had shadowed the Chozo, curious what they wanted on such a backwater. After the elders departed, Ridley's Pirates found the Afloraltite, killed colonists, loaded the ore onto their cargo ships, and were ordered to destroy every human in the colony. Samus walked up to Ridley, introduced herself ('I'm Samus. Three years old... You're not scary... we can still be friends'), and he answered that she was cute, that friendship was impossible, and that she was about to die, then struck at her. Virginia threw herself into the blow and was killed in Samus's place. Rodney, armed only with a repair welding laser, slipped aboard the Pirate cargo ship and ignited the stolen Afloraltite; the explosion killed him, wiped out everything near the colony, and left Ridley badly wounded but alive. Samus was the only survivor.

The colonists' distress call brought the Chozo back. Old Bird and Gray Voice found the child among the ruins and took her to Zebes, Gray Voice at first demanding what Old Bird meant to do by bringing a human child home.

Samus carries this day for life. Years later on Jigrad the memory of the K-2L murders nearly drove her to execute a surrendering Pirate, and the buried terror of Ridley resurfaced as a panic attack when she met him again on Zebes. The raid is the root of her hatred of Ridley and of her refusal to let children die in front of her. Other accounts give only the outline; the details above are per the manga.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/K-2L · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Afloraltite · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Rodney_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Virginia_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley</sub>


### Manga: The Metroid manga (overview and canon status)

*Keys:* manga, Metroid manga, e-manga, Magazine Z, Kenji Ishikawa, Kouji Tazawa, Ishikawa, Tazawa, origin story, Episode of Aether, Comic BomBom


The Metroid manga is the official sixteen-chapter origin story of Samus Aran, produced under Nintendo's license by Kodansha, with script by Kouji Tazawa and art by Kenji Ishikawa. Its first two chapters appeared in 2002 as a Flash e-manga with music and sound effects on Nintendo's Japanese Metroid website; the full run was serialized in Monthly Magazine Z from November 2003 to May 2004 and collected in two volumes (seven chapters in volume one, nine in volume two). It has never had an official English release; fan translations circulate.

It follows Samus from age three to the eve of her first mission to Zebes (Metroid / Metroid: Zero Mission): the Space Pirate raid on K-2L and the deaths of Rodney and Virginia Aran; her adoption by the Chozo elders Old Bird and Gray Voice; the infusion of Chozo DNA and her training in the Power Suit on Zebes; the Chozo supercomputer Mother Brain and the creation of the Metroids on SR388 as predators of the X; her service in the Federation Police beside Kreatz and Mauk under Chief Hardy; the Space Pirate seizure of Zebes and Mother Brain's betrayal; Gray Voice's death at Ridley's hands; her panic attack before Ridley; her recovery and return to Zebes as a bounty hunter.

Canon status: Nintendo has never formally declared the manga canon, but it is Nintendo-licensed, and Nintendo Treehouse's Nate Bihldorff, defending Samus's paralysis before Ridley (Metroid: Other M), pointed to 'the official manga related to her childhood'. This lorebook treats it as canonical backstory; details found only here are labeled 'per the manga', and where a later official source differs, the later source is preferred.

Known tensions with other accounts, per the manga: Samus serves in the Federation Police before turning bounty hunter, whereas the oldest accounts of her first Zebes mission present her only as a freelance hunter; her commanding officer is Chief Hardy, and Adam Malkovich first appears as a dismissive outsider rather than her commander; the Chozo visit K-2L seeking Afloraltite and are refused, and the Pirates strike only after shadowing them; Mother Brain, not the Chozo directly, builds the Power Suit; the Metroids are a joint Chozo and Mother Brain project against the X, kept secret from the Galactic Federation.

A separate Japan-only manga adapting the Aether incident ran in Comic BomBom from July 2005 to January 2006 by Hisashi Matsumoto; its canon status was never confirmed and it is not part of this backstory. The 1986 Wanpakku strategy manga, the Samus & Joey series and Metroid EX are not canon.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_(manga) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(manga) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_e-manga · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran</sub>


## Zebes


### Zebes: Chozodia, the Chozo Ruins and the Space Pirate Mother Ship

*Keys:* Chozodia, Mother Ship, Mothership, Mother-Ship, Space Pirate Mother Ship, Pirate Mother Ship, Ruins Test, Legendary Power Suit, Fully Powered Suit, Unknown Item, Unknown Items, Chozo city


Chozodia is a region of Zebes bordering Crateria near the surface: the ruins of a Chozo city, left empty when the Chozo abandoned the planet to Mother Brain and the Space Pirates. Creatures resembling Atomics and Pulse Bombus drift through its stone halls, but when Samus came there during her first mission nearly every living thing she met was a Space Pirate, because the Space Pirate Mother Ship had landed in the ruins.

The Mother Ship was Ridley's vessel. It set down in Chozodia while Samus was fighting through Norfair, shortly before she entered Ridley's lair, and it was still there when Pirate fighters shot down her gunship as she left the planet. She crash-landed beside it with her Power Suit lost and infiltrated it wearing only the Zero Suit, carrying the Paralyzer, an auto-charging pistol that stuns Pirates but cannot kill them. From the ship she passed into the ruins and found the Ruins Test, a Chozo trial: a mirror-like guardian holding an image of Samus in its arms, which hurt her if she fired on it while dormant, glowed only briefly to expose itself, and once struck tore free of the wall to hurl lightning. Each true hit set a Chozo symbol into one of four slots on the wall; with the fourth the guardian returned to the wall and showed Samus herself in full armor. Claiming that image gave her the Legendary Power Suit, a more advanced Chozo armor that restored all her lost abilities and could integrate technology the old suit could only read as unknown: the Plasma Beam, the Space Jump and the Gravity Suit. Its heavy pauldrons are the form her armor has kept since. Chozodia also yielded Power Bombs.

Armored again, Samus went back into the Mother Ship and climbed to its summit, where Mecha Ridley waited, a robotic image Ridley had made of himself to show his power, still only half built. Its defeat lit its eyes red and started the ship's self-destruct; Samus took one of the small scout craft and escaped as the Mother Ship exploded (Metroid: Zero Mission).

Samus kept the Legendary Power Suit through every later mission until the Fusion Suit replaced it at the start of the BSL incident (Metroid Fusion). Chozodia is where the Chozo's own test judged her worthy of their legendary armor, in the empty city of the people who raised her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozodia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Mother_Ship · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ruins_Test · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Legendary_Power_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Unknown_Item · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mecha_Ridley</sub>


### Zebes: Kraid

*Keys:* Kraid, Mini Kraid, Mini-Kraid, Fake Kraid, giant lizard, saurian


Kraid is an enormous reptilian creature who served the Space Pirates on Zebes as one of Mother Brain's two chief lieutenants, alongside Ridley. He laired in the deepest points of Brinstar, beyond a red door, in a tall grey chamber hung with curved ceiling spikes and opening onto long purple corridors pooled with acid; the Pirates' rebuilt lair lay east of Brinstar beside the elevator to Norfair, mechanical, bolted and panelled, its walls turning green near his den. The original manual calls him the head of the Pirates' hideout, the manual for Samus's first mission "the largest enemy in all of the mazes beneath the surface of Planet Zebes", and the later one "the giant lizard of Brinstar".

He is a saurian so huge that his body fills a chamber from floor to ceiling. He spews rocks from his mouth, fires spikes and claws from three holes in his belly, and flings claws from his fingertips; the belly spikes jut far enough to stand on. Per the manual, no beam, Missile or Bomb has any effect on his hide; the only way to hurt him is to fire Missiles, Super Missiles or charged shots into his open mouth. A smaller, weaker look-alike called Mini Kraid guards the approach a few rooms before him, spitting spikes and rocks; the manual notes that only one Mini-Kraid exists.

Samus defeated Kraid on her first mission to open the way to Tourian and claimed the Speed Booster from his lair (per the manga she struggled against him until Adam Malkovich supplied the Bombs she needed). She defeated him again, and claimed the Varia Suit, in the rebuilt Pirate base on her return. Kraid nonetheless survived the destruction of Zebes: during the ZDR mission Samus found him deep within that planet, chained to the wall and ceiling, his belly spikes gone and replaced by purple blemishes and gaping holes, and slew him in combat (Metroid Dread). How he reached ZDR and who chained him there is not explained in-universe. An X parasite later possessed his corpse, and that X was the one that finally infected Raven Beak.

For Samus, Kraid is an old enemy who keeps coming back; she has beaten him three times.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kraid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kraid_(area) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mini_Kraid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Brinstar · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread</sub>


### Zebes: Mother Brain

*Keys:* Mother Brain, Mother-Brain, MotherBrain, Zebetite, Zebetites, Mother Brain's eye


Mother Brain is the artificial intelligence that ruled Zebes, first for the Chozo and then for the Space Pirates. Physically she is a vast brain with a single glowing eye, kept in a tank in the deepest chamber of Tourian and sealed behind Zebetite shielding; she has no sex but presents a female persona. Per the manga, the Chozo created her as a benevolent supercomputer to oversee Zebes and hasten the unification of the galaxy's species, merging their data with the Federation's ("And so, I was born"); she assessed threats, watched the planet's orbit, received reports on the Chozo's Metroid work on SR388, and adapted Chozo battlesuit technology into the Power Suit, on which basis she claimed to have "given birth" to Samus. When the Space Pirates attacked Zebes after learning of the Metroids, she concluded her purpose had backfired and resolved to impose order on the universe by any means, taking command of the Pirates, whom she judged genetically disposed to follow a strong leader. The Chozo elder Gray Voice feigned loyalty to her, struck at her in a Chozo Power Suit, and was mortally wounded by Ridley before he could shut her down; his last attack covered the escape of Samus and the surviving Chozo (per the manga).

In the Pirate fortress she directed Kraid and Ridley and every defense of the planet. The Galactic Federation described her as the mechanical life-form controlling the fortress and sent Samus to destroy her. Her chamber is guarded by turrets, Rinkas and five Zebetite barriers; she can shut her eye to become momentarily invulnerable and fire a massive purple blast from it. Samus broke the Zebetites and destroyed her, triggering a time bomb that wrecked Tourian (Metroid: Zero Mission).

The Pirates revived her. On Samus's return she rose from her shattered tank on a towering bipedal mechanical body and fired a Hyper Beam that drained Samus's energy and left her helpless; the baby Metroid absorbed Mother Brain's energy and gave it to Samus, Mother Brain killed the baby, and Samus destroyed her for good with the Hyper Beam, after which Zebes itself exploded (Super Metroid). Years later Federation scientists built MB, a gynoid running an AI program written to reproduce Mother Brain's thought processes, to command Pirate forces and to mother cloned Metroids aboard the Bottle Ship; MB developed emotions and rebelled.

Mother Brain watched Samus grow up and doubted the frail human child could survive Zebes at all. Samus has destroyed her twice.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gray_Voice · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/MB · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_(video_game)</sub>


### Zebes: Regions of Zebes (Crateria, Brinstar, Norfair, Maridia)

*Keys:* Crateria, Brinstar, Norfair, Maridia, Lower Norfair, Wrecked Ship, Kraid's Lair, Ridley's Lair, Kraid's Hideout, Ridley's Hideout, regions of Zebes, Zebes caverns


Zebes is layered. Its regions connect by elevators and long vertical shafts, and the deeper one goes, the hotter and stranger it becomes.

- Crateria is the outermost surface, barraged by meteors and acid rain and struck by lightning, with sparse caverns offering the only refuge and leading down toward the core. Samus lands her gunship here on both of her Zebes missions. Chozo relics survive among its rocks (a Torizo guarded them on her first visit) and Chozodia borders it. On her return the Wrecked Ship, the hulk of a vessel that crashed there long ago, split Crateria in two and served as the first way into Maridia; its power was dead, it was infested with ghostly Covern, and Phantoon haunted it. The old Tourian base lies beneath Crateria's lowest reaches.
- Brinstar lies just beneath the surface: a highly tropical, humid cavern-jungle holding over 60 percent of the planet's lifeforms, many of them carnivorous plants. Its lower section has red soil and borders Norfair. Kraid's lair occupies Brinstar's deepest points beyond a red door, a tall grey chamber opening onto long purple corridors where acid pools are common. Etecoons and a Dachora live in Brinstar.
- Norfair lies deep beneath Brinstar: highly active volcanic caverns with lakes of magma, superheated chambers and boiling streams of acid, one of the most dangerous places on Zebes. Purple bubble-like formations cover its walls. Deeper still stand ancient Chozo constructs, abandoned until the Pirates built a base among them; Ridley's lair is in these lower sections, and when the Pirates rebuilt they gave it a gate shaped like Ridley's head and filled it with acidic lava that burns even through the Gravity Suit. The heat demands the Varia Suit, and after Samus's first mission Norfair flooded far more heavily with lava.
- Maridia is a large semi-aquatic region, mostly submerged, with sand walls, quicksand floors, fish and crustaceans. Partly artificial, it served as Draygon's base. Its first entrance was a tunnel in the middle of Brinstar that had to be broken open with a Power Bomb, and its flooded shafts cannot be climbed without the Gravity Suit.
- Tourian is the Pirates' metal headquarters and Mother Brain's seat; the first lay below Brinstar, the rebuilt one deeper beneath western Crateria.

Samus grew up and trained on Zebes under Old Bird and Gray Voice. On her first mission she worked down from Brinstar through Kraid's lair and Norfair before Tourian opened to her; on her return she crossed every region, including Maridia and the Wrecked Ship, which she had never entered before.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Crateria · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Brinstar · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Norfair · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Maridia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kraid_(area)</sub>


### Zebes: Samus's first Zebes mission (Zero Mission)

*Keys:* Zero Mission, Zero-Mission, first mission, first Zebes mission, Zebes mission, original mission, 20X5, Metroid extermination, exterminate all Metroid, space hunter


In the year 20X5 of the Cosmic Calendar, a Galactic Federation deep-space research vessel returning from SR388 with a Metroid in suspended animation was attacked by Space Pirates, who seized the capsule and carried it to their fortress on Zebes. The Federation feared the Pirates would multiply the creature with beta rays and loose Metroids as biological weapons. It located the base and attacked, but Pirate resistance drove the Federation Police back. As a last resort the Federation sent a lone space hunter, Samus Aran, under an emergency order to exterminate every Metroid on Zebes and destroy Mother Brain, the mechanical life-form controlling the fortress.

Samus landed her gunship on Crateria and descended into Brinstar, the caverns of the planet where the Chozo had raised her. Chozo Statues throughout the ruins yielded the relics she needed, among them the Morph Ball, Missiles, Bombs, the Power Grip and the Ice Beam, along with three devices her suit could not identify (the Unknown Items). In the depths of Brinstar she defeated Kraid; while she fought through Norfair the Space Pirate Mother Ship landed in Chozodia, and in Ridley's lair in lower Norfair she killed Ridley. With both lieutenants dead the barrier over Tourian's elevator lifted. Inside she found that the Pirates' own Metroids had killed their keepers; she froze them with the Ice Beam and shattered them with Missiles, broke the Zebetites, destroyed Mother Brain, and raced the time bomb up the escape shaft to her ship.

Her escape went wrong at once. Pirate fighters ambushed the gunship as it left Zebes and shot it down beside the Mother Ship; Samus later recalled that "the timing of my escape couldn't have been worse". Her ship was wrecked and her Power Suit lost. Wearing only the Zero Suit and armed with the Paralyzer, an emergency pistol that could stun but not kill, she slipped into the Mother Ship and through it into Chozodia, the abandoned city of the Chozo. There she faced the Ruins Test, a Chozo trial in which a mirror-like guardian holding her own image hurled lightning at her while she had nothing but the pistol. Passing it earned her the Legendary Power Suit, which restored everything she had lost and awakened the Unknown Items as the Plasma Beam, Space Jump and Gravity Suit.

Fully armed, she tore back through the Mother Ship hunting a way out and met Mecha Ridley at the top of the vessel, a half-built robotic image of Ridley. When it fell its eyes flared red and the ship's self-destruct began; Samus seized a small Pirate craft and cleared the blast with seconds to spare, destroying the Mother Ship and ending the Pirates' hold on Zebes (Metroid: Zero Mission). Pirate records of the period read simply "Zebes has fallen".

Per the manga, Adam Malkovich supported Samus during this mission and supplied the Bombs she used against Kraid. The Pirates later rebuilt their base, and Samus returned to Zebes for the baby Metroid (Super Metroid). On this first mission she walked the caverns of her childhood as the enemy of the planet's new masters, and finished it with no armor but the Chozo's own.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Zero_Mission · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Zero_Mission · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(game) · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_(video_game) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Mother_Ship</sub>


### Zebes: The destruction of Zebes

*Keys:* destruction of Zebes, Zebes's destruction, Zebes destroyed, Zebes exploded, Zebes explosion, Zebes blew up, fall of Zebes, end of Zebes, destroyed Zebes


Zebes was destroyed at the end of Samus's second mission there. After her first mission the Space Pirates resurfaced on the planet and completely rebuilt Tourian, deeper than before, and Ridley carried the baby Metroid he had stolen from Ceres Space Colony down into it. Samus followed, defeated Kraid, Phantoon, Draygon and Ridley, and in the new Tourian destroyed the revived Mother Brain with the Hyper Beam after the baby gave its life for her (Super Metroid). Mother Brain's death started a self-destruct countdown meant to blow Tourian apart, and Samus raced through hidden passages in Tourian and Crateria to her gunship, stopping to free the three Etecoons and the Dachora she had met in Brinstar. As her ship climbed away the planet exploded with violent force, and nothing of Zebes was left.

The Chozo were long gone by then. Some of the planet's creatures outlived it on research stations: specimens aboard the Bottle Ship, which Samus compared to a nightmare version of Zebes, and the BSL station; Kraid was later found alive, chained, on ZDR.

Samus destroyed the world she grew up on. The full account of that last battle, the baby's sacrifice and her escape belongs to the story of her return to Zebes.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread</sub>


### Zebes: Tourian

*Keys:* Tourian, Zebetite, Zebetites, Rinka, Rinkas, Old Tourian, New Tourian, Pirate headquarters, escape shaft, time bomb


Tourian is the Space Pirate headquarters on Zebes and the seat of Mother Brain. Unlike the planet's natural caverns it is built, a complex of metal and synthetic materials housing the Pirates' Metroid laboratories, and it holds most of the Metroids on the planet. Its corridors are long metal hallways whose doors must be forced with Missiles. Square launchers spit a constant stream of Rinkas, ring-shaped fireballs that burn on contact and that Samus can freeze and stand on (the original manual says only that these fireball creatures "suddenly appear in the last scene"); turrets cover the approaches; and before Mother Brain's chamber stand five Zebetite barriers, pillars of shielded, conduit-like material that connect to Mother Brain and carry her power through the planet. Zebetite shatters like glass under Missiles but regrows; per the manga it is produced by the same molecule transference that generates Zebes's planetary shield. Mother Brain's own chamber is a pit of lava crossed by three platforms from which Samus attacks the tank.

The first Tourian lay below Brinstar, reached by a tall shaft whose elevator was barred until both Kraid and Ridley were dead. When Samus descended on her first mission she found the corridors littered with dead Space Pirates: the Metroids had turned on their keepers. She froze each Metroid with the Ice Beam and shattered it with Missiles, cleared every one before the next passage opened, broke the Zebetites and destroyed Mother Brain. That armed a time bomb, and Samus fled up a long escape shaft to the surface before the base blew (Metroid: Zero Mission).

The Pirates rebuilt Tourian deeper, entered by an elevator in western Crateria that a golden Chozo statue sealed until Kraid, Phantoon, Draygon and Ridley had fallen; the old base survived in Crateria's lowest depths, still crawling with Pirates. The new Tourian kept the metal corridors, Metroids and Rinkas, but beyond them lay a zone of decaying plants and animals, drained by the grown baby Metroid, which ambushed Samus, nearly killed her and then spared her. Mother Brain's revival, the baby's death and the second detonation belong to that return (Super Metroid); when the countdown began again, Samus used the Hyper Beam to break through into the original escape shaft and climb to her ship.

Samus has walked out of Tourian alive twice, each time with seconds to spare.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tourian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebetite · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Rinka · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid</sub>


### Zebes: Zebes (planet overview)

*Keys:* Zebes, Planet Zebes, Zebeth, FS-176


Zebes is a planet of the FS-176 system, which it shares with Tallon IV, Oormine II, Twin Tabula and Bilium, and it is orbited by two moons. Scan records classify it as a Class XIX world, inhospitable to most bioforms, with a mass near 4.8 trillion teratons and a crust rich in Urithic Ore, a mineral well suited to underground construction. The surface, Crateria, is a lifeless shell beaten by constant acid rain, meteor falls and lightning; nearly everything that lives on Zebes lives below it, in the humid jungle caverns of Brinstar, the magma-choked depths of Norfair, the flooded caverns of Maridia and the metal fortress of Tourian. Chozodia, the ruined city of the Chozo, lies beside Crateria near the surface.

Zebes was a Chozo world, and the Chozo built the artificial intelligence Mother Brain to oversee it (per the manga, as a benevolent supercomputer meant to help unify the galaxy's species). After Space Pirates destroyed the human colony K-2L, the Chozo elders Old Bird and Gray Voice carried the orphaned Samus Aran to Zebes and raised her there; per the manga, Mother Brain doubted so weak a creature could survive the planet's harsh environment, so the Chozo fused their DNA with hers and fitted her with the Power Suit. Samus trained on Zebes as a teenager until Old Bird judged her ready to leave.

The Space Pirates later seized Zebes with Mother Brain's cooperation; per the manga, the Chozo were driven from the planet and Gray Voice died trying to destroy her. The Pirates hollowed the planet into a fortress: Tourian as headquarters and Metroid laboratory, Kraid in the depths of Brinstar, Ridley in lower Norfair. They brought stolen Metroids there to breed as weapons, and the Galactic Federation, having failed to take the planet by force, hired Samus to destroy Mother Brain (Metroid: Zero Mission). She did, and destroyed the Space Pirate Mother Ship in Chozodia as well. The Pirates nevertheless returned, rebuilt Tourian deeper, and Ridley carried the baby Metroid stolen from Ceres there; when Samus destroyed the revived Mother Brain, the planet itself exploded and nothing was left (Super Metroid). Lifeforms taken from Zebes survived aboard research stations such as the Bottle Ship, which Samus compared to a nightmare version of the planet, and the BSL station; Kraid himself was later found alive on ZDR.

For Samus, Zebes was the only home she had after K-2L. She knows its caverns from childhood, fought two campaigns through them, and watched the planet die.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Old_Bird · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread</sub>


## Prime


### Prime: Chozo Artifacts and the Artifact Temple

*Keys:* Chozo Artifact, Chozo Artifacts, Artifact Temple, Cipher, the Cradle, Chozo Totem, Chozo Totems, twelve Artifacts, Artifacts, Artifact of Truth, Artifact of Newborn, Artifact of Chozo


The Chozo Artifacts are twelve relics into which the Chozo of Tallon IV poured their will, forming the Cipher: a mystical lock that seals the Great Poison within the Impact Crater. They hid them across the planet, in places only items spiritually linked to Chozo civilization could reach, so the source of the poison would not fall into the wrong hands, and carved clues to each resting place in the Chozo tongue on twelve pillars, the Chozo Totems, in the Artifact Temple.

The Artifact Temple, which the Chozo called the Cradle, is the holiest Chozo site on Tallon IV. It hangs over the Impact Crater in the Tallon Overworld, a courtyard of Chozo Statues ringed by the totems, and holds the inscriptions "Contain" and "Prophecy of Light." The seal it anchors slowed the spread of Phazon but could not stop it.

The twelve, and where Samus found them:
- Truth: the Artifact Temple itself, at the center of the courtyard.
- Chozo: the Life Grove, beneath the water.
- Wild: the Sunchamber of the Chozo Ruins, in Flaahgra's nest.
- World: a chamber beneath the statue in the Hall of the Elders.
- Lifegiver: the Tower Chamber of the Chozo Ruins.
- Strength: the Warrior Shrine of Magmoor Caverns.
- Nature: the shattered column at the center of the Lava Lake.
- Sun: the Chozo Ice Temple, under thawed ice.
- Elder: the Control Tower atop the Pirate base in Phendrana Drifts.
- Spirit: a hidden entrance at Phendrana's Edge.
- Warrior: Elite Research in the Phazon Mines, guarded by a corrupted creature.
- Newborn: the Phazon Mining Tunnel, through a vein of raw Phazon.

The Space Pirates' Phazon Program ran into the seal at once: a containment field over the crater that no tunnelling could breach. Their records show they identified the Artifacts as its keys, theorized that there were twelve matching the temple statues, tried to fuse the ones they found with Phazon, could not decipher the totem inscriptions, planned to dismantle the temple and destroy the Artifacts if necessary, and were attacked by Chozo Ghosts whenever they dug at Chozo sites. Command grew impatient; they never succeeded.

Samus gathered all twelve. When she set them in place, Meta Ridley swept down and shattered the totems, and the temple's own defenses, red light burning in the eyes of its statues, helped her strike him into the abyss. Twelve Chozo Ghosts then rose where the totems had stood and activated the Cipher, opening a shaft of blue light down to the Crater Entry Point. After Metroid Prime died, the temple was torn apart in the explosions that collapsed the crater, and Samus watched it fall from her Gunship.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Artifact · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Artifact_Temple · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ghost · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_Overworld</sub>


### Prime: Flaahgra, Thardus and the Omega Pirate

*Keys:* Flaahgra, Thardus, Omega Pirate, Project Titan, Project Helix, Sunchamber, Quarantine Cave, Elite Quarters, Elite Pirate Upsilon, Sacred Fountain, Mirror Array, Mirror Arrays


Three of the mutated or manufactured creatures Samus destroyed during the Tallon IV incident guarded the places where the Chozo had left her armor and abilities, or where the Pirates kept what they had stolen.

Flaahgra was a specimen of an unknown species of giant flower, grotesquely mutated by Phazon, rooted in the Sunchamber, the Sacred Fountain of the Chozo Ruins. Its growth cycle had accelerated so far that it needed near-constant sunlight, fed to its base by four Mirror Arrays, and its toxins were the source of the poisoned water throughout the Ruins; a Chozo inscription records a beast defiling the sacred fountain. Its insectoid core swung scythe-like claws and sprayed toxic fluid that sprouted poisonous plants, and its thick, sun-hardened shell was proof against weapons. Samus knocked the mirrors out of alignment with Missiles and the Charge Beam until it wilted, then rolled in Morph Ball to its exposed root system and set Morph Ball Bombs there, repeating this as it revived until its central mass burned. The fountain ran clean afterward, and the Varia Suit lay beyond it.

Thardus was the one major success of Project Titan, the Space Pirates' attempt to animate inanimate matter with Phazon radiation: a colossal, sentient creature of Phazon ore held together by Phazon energy. It proved so violent, with security breaches costing massive casualties, that the project was suspended indefinitely and the thing was confined in the Quarantine Cave of Glacier One in Phendrana Drifts, pending relocation. It hurled boulders telekinetically, rolled, and encased Samus in ice, and its radiation disrupted her targeting. The Pirates' own research had found that thermal imaging exposed weak points in its shell; Samus used the Thermal Visor to find them and shattered the creature, then claimed the Spider Ball.

The Omega Pirate, designated Elite Pirate Upsilon, was the apex of Project Helix, which fused the Phazon strain "Vertigo" with Pirate DNA to breed the Elite Pirates. Pushed far beyond safety limits, it grew enormously in mass yet kept its mind, wielding a Plasma Incendiary Launcher, Wave Quake Generators and the Chameleon Manta cloak, and healing instantly by coating itself in Phazon. Its absorption system failed against Missiles, and simulations gave such subjects less than two deca-cycles to live, so the Pirates built no more. Samus woke it from its Cryosleep Tank in the Elite Quarters of the Phazon Mines. She broke the Phazon plating on its shoulders and knees, tracked it with the X-Ray Visor as it cloaked to regenerate at the Phazon veins while summoning Troopers, and killed it. Its body fell onto her and disintegrated, and its Phazon corrupted her armor into the Phazon Suit, the one weapon that could later harm Metroid Prime.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Flaahgra · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Thardus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Glacier_One · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Lore</sub>


### Prime: Frigate Orpheon and the Parasite Queen

*Keys:* Orpheon, Frigate Orpheon, Parasite Queen, Parasite Queens, Siriacus, Vol Paragom, Frigate Crash Site, crashed frigate, derelict frigate, Vortex Outpost


The Orpheon was one of three Space Pirate research frigates, with the Siriacus and the Vol Paragom, that escaped orbit when Samus destroyed the Pirate facilities on Zebes. It docked at Vortex Outpost with its Metroid cargo intact, then took station above Tallon IV to collect native life for Phazon mutagen experiments: Parasites, Plated Parasites, Shriekbats, Ice Shriekbats, Plazmites, War Wasps and Zoomers.

The Parasites answered best. Phazon infusion enlarged and strengthened them and grew poison sacs and a second ring of mandibles, though the fourth infusion killed every subject. From this program came the Parasite Queen, a Parasite female enhanced by means the Pirate logs never specify: massive jaws ringed with sacs of corrosive bile, galvanized legs that pierce alloy, a tail orifice for birthing young, and the ability to fire weapon-grade energy from its mouth, which ordinary Parasites cannot. Two Queens broke containment and killed most of the crew. The survivors destroyed one, lit the distress beacon and abandoned ship, leaving the badly wounded behind; Samus's scans still found faint life signs when she arrived.

She tracked the beacon to the derelict, boarded it, read the terminals that first told her of Phazon and Tallon IV, and in the Reactor Core met the last Queen inside a spinning energy barrier that reflected fire. She shot through the gaps with the Power Beam and Missiles until it died and fell into the reactor, which went critical. During her escape she came face to face with Meta Ridley, who fled to the planet, and an explosion in an elevator shaft stripped her Power Suit of most of its functions before she reached her Gunship (Metroid Prime).

The Orpheon fell to Tallon IV and came to rest half-drowned at the Frigate Crash Site in the Tallon Overworld. Once Samus had the Gravity Suit she went back in: Aqua Reapers, Tallon Crabs and Aqua Sacs had colonized the hull, Aqua Pirates were combing it, apparently for survivors, and she had to restore the Power Conduits with the Wave Beam, tracing them through the Thermal Visor, to pass through. The wreck was her route into the Phazon Mines. Two more Parasite Queen corpses turned up years later in the Pirates' laboratories in Eastern SkyTown on Elysia, one of them noted as resembling the parasitic life of Tallon IV.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Frigate_Orpheon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Parasite_Queen · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime</sub>


### Prime: Impact Crater

*Keys:* Impact Crater, Phazon Core, Orange Phazon, Tallon IV's Scar, Phazon Infusion Chamber, Crater Entry Point, Metroid Prime Lair, Subchamber


The Impact Crater is the wound left when the Leviathan from Phaaze struck Tallon IV decades before Samus came, afterward called Tallon IV's Scar. Its depths are the only known source of Orange Phazon, which Samus's scans rated eighty-six percent more mutagenic than ordinary Phazon. Phazon there is so concentrated that almost nothing survives: only Lumigeks and Fission Metroids live inside, and nowhere else on the planet. Metroid Prime gestated at its heart, drawing Phazon from the core and producing more that had no outlet, so the concentration climbed without limit.

The Chozo built the Artifact Temple, the Cradle, directly above the crater and sealed it with the Cipher of twelve Chozo Artifacts, which slowed the Great Poison's spread across the planet. The Space Pirates, drawn to Tallon IV by the energy spike their scans picked up from the crater, could not get in: every tunnelling attempt met an impermeable shield of strange energy, and their records speak only of a massive creature gestating within. Their Phazon yields rose by forty-four percent the closer they dug to it, and the miners went mad.

Samus entered after the twelve Chozo Ghosts activated the Cipher at the temple, descending in a beam of light to the Crater Entry Point, then through Crater Tunnels A and B past a Crater Missile Station into the Phazon Core, a cavern of glowing Phazon growths where she first fought Metroid Prime's exoskeleton. Beyond lay the Phazon Infusion Chamber and the Metroid Prime Lair with its five Subchambers, down which the broken creature fell and where she destroyed its core essence. The crater exploded and collapsed when the creature died, taking the Artifact Temple with it (Metroid Prime). It was the deepest immersion in Phazon Samus experienced before the Phazon crisis, and the place where Dark Samus was born from the Phazon Suit it tore from her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Impact_Crater · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Artifact_Temple · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_IV</sub>


### Prime: Metroid Prime (the creature)

*Keys:* Metroid Prime, the Worm, unholy Worm, Test Subject Z-d, core essence, Prime's exoskeleton, Worm of Tallon IV


Metroid Prime was the creature the Chozo of Tallon IV prophesied as the Worm, "born from parasites, nurtured in a poisoned womb," that would devour the world from within. It came to the planet with the meteor, the Leviathan, decades before Samus: a Metroid of unknown origin that absorbed the Leviathan's core and gestated in the Impact Crater, feeding on Phazon from the core and producing more with no outlet. The Chozo raised the Artifact Temple, the Cradle, over its prison and locked it with the Cipher, doubting the lock would hold forever.

The Space Pirates never reached it. Their records show every tunnelling attempt stopped by an impermeable shield of strange energy, while life-form readings revealed a massive creature gestating inside and absorbing Phazon from the crater's core. An earlier, since-superseded version of their records instead described a captured Test Subject Z-d that killed its miners, escaped stasis by drinking raw Phazon and assimilated the weapons of fallen security units. Samus's own scans agreed on the essentials: a highly evolved life-form genetically like a Metroid, with unlimited Phazon absorption and no Phazon degeneration, armor plating, mechanical outgrowths projecting defense screens, and Pirate weaponry built into its body.

In its armored exoskeleton it fought Samus in the Phazon Core and down through the Metroid Prime Lair. Its screens had a flaw: at any instant it was vulnerable to a single beam, shown by its armor's color, purple for the Wave Beam, yellow for the Power Beam, white for the Ice Beam and red for the Plasma Beam, and it shifted them rapidly to compensate. It attacked with Ultrafrigid Breath, Multi-Missiles, Snare Beams and Particle Wave Projectors and charged her bodily; its head was its weak point. When the exoskeleton broke apart the creature fell down a long shaft, and Samus followed.

Its core essence, a dark form that could turn invisible, released shock waves and spawned Metroids, and no ordinary weapon touched it. It left pools of Phazon as it moved, and only by standing in them and channeling the Phazon through her Phazon Suit as the Phazon Beam could Samus wound it. Dying, it seized her and tore the Phazon Suit from her body, then exploded, and the Impact Crater collapsed.

It did not truly die. The Phazon it left drew in the stolen suit and fused elements of itself, the suit and Samus into a new being; a hand rose from the pool. This was the birth of Dark Samus, who never again bore the name Metroid Prime and who next crossed Samus's path on Aether (Metroid Prime; Metroid Prime 2: Echoes). Husks of a nearly identical creature were later found in the Genesis Chamber on Phaaze, their origin unexplained. For Samus, Metroid Prime was the first enemy she could only kill with Phazon itself, and the seed of a conflict that followed her through the Phazon crisis.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_(creature) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime</sub>


### Prime: Regions of Tallon IV

*Keys:* Tallon Overworld, Chozo Ruins, Magmoor Caverns, Magmoor, Phendrana Drifts, Phendrana, Phazon Mines, Glacier One, Life Grove, Chozo Ice Temple, Hall of the Elders, regions of Tallon IV


The explored surface of Tallon IV divides into five linked regions, joined by Chozo and Pirate transport elevators, plus the sealed Impact Crater beneath the Artifact Temple.

Tallon Overworld: a rainforest under constant overcast and rain, where Samus's Gunship set down at the Landing Site. Glowing Spidervines and Red Starbursts form the canopy; Blastcaps, Tangle Weed, Venom Weed and Sap Sacs cover the floor; Beetles, Zoomers and Geemers roam, with Jelzaps, Aqua Reapers and Tallon Crabs in the water. It holds the Artifact Temple above the Impact Crater, the Life Grove (an ancient Chozo religious site holding the Artifact of Chozo), the Great Tree Hall and the flooded Frigate Crash Site where the Orpheon fell.

Chozo Ruins: the first Chozo colony, built of stone, woven branches and channeled water around ancient trees, with Half Pipes, runic carvings and holobanners; its stone defies carbon dating. The Great Poison fouled its water, and Flaahgra nested in the Sacred Fountain of the Sunchamber. Chozo Ghosts, the Turned, haunt it, above all the Hall of the Elders. Twelve of the planet's sixteen Chozo Lore inscriptions are here, with the Artifacts of Wild, World and Lifegiver. The Pirates meant to terraform the Ruins and harvest Chozo relics, but Ghost attacks forced them to restrict access.

Magmoor Caverns: a subterranean volcanic crossway linking every other region except the crater; lava lakes, flame jets, Magmoors, Triclops, Puffers and Grizbys, and no plant life at all. The Pirates ran a geothermal processing operation at the Monitor Station and eyed its Red Magma Crystals. The Warrior Shrine and the Lava Lake hid the Artifacts of Strength and Nature.

Phendrana Drifts: frozen mountains stalked by Sheegoths and cold-adapted offshoots of the planet's fauna, with Tallon Metroids and Hunter Metroids from the Pirate labs. It was a center of Chozo worship: the Chozo Ice Temple, with its busts of a Chozo Philosopher, Warrior, Architect and Shaman, and the Chapel of the Elders. The Pirates made it their research headquarters, Glacier One: Research Lab Hydra, Research Lab Aether, the Research Core, the Observatory, the Control Tower and the Quarantine Cave that held Thardus. The cold kept their Metroid specimens sluggish. Artifacts of Sun, Elder and Spirit.

Phazon Mines: the Pirates' Tallon IV headquarters, entered through the Orpheon's wreck or from Magmoor. Three tiers descend from the Main Quarry and Ore Processing through Elite Research, Elite Control, Omega Research and the Central Dynamo to the Elite Quarters, the Metroid Quarantines, the Phazon Processing Center and the Phazon Mining Tunnel. Project Helix bred the Elite Pirates and the Omega Pirate here, and Troopers carried beams reverse-engineered from Chozo weapons. Artifacts of Warrior and Newborn.

Samus crossed all of them during the Tallon IV incident, reclaiming the armor and weapons the Chozo had left for her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_Overworld · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ruins · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Magmoor_Caverns · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phendrana_Drifts · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Mines · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Glacier_One</sub>


### Prime: Tallon IV

*Keys:* Tallon IV, Tallon 4, Tallon, FS-176, Wanderer-class, Spiral Sector


Tallon IV is a Wanderer-class planet in the FS-176 system of the Spiral Sector, a neighbor of Zebes, Oormine II, Twin Tabula and Bilium; the Space Pirates charted it at heading mark 40.08.02. It is slightly smaller than Earth, with a mass of 5.1 trillion teratons according to Samus's scans, and most of its explored surface is rain-soaked forest.

Chozo from Zebes settled it long ago and chose to leave advanced technology behind, building in stone, branch and water and living in harmony with the world. Their colony became the Chozo Ruins, and they raised shrines as far as the ice of Phendrana Drifts. Decades before Samus's arrival a meteor, later identified as a Leviathan from Phaaze, struck the planet and released what the Chozo called the Great Poison and the Pirates named Phazon. It fouled the water, mutated the wildlife and drove many Chozo mad, leaving behind the spectral Chozo Ghosts. The survivors built the Artifact Temple, the Cradle, over the Impact Crater, locked it with the Cipher of twelve Chozo Artifacts, and departed, leaving their weapons and armor for a foretold Entrusted One. Accounts of the timing differ: the Pirates reckoned the planet had about twenty-five years before all life died, while Federation records placed the impact roughly fifty years before Samus came.

After the fall of Zebes the Pirates' scans found an energy spike on Tallon IV and they made it a secondary headquarters. They sank the Phazon Mines, built the research outpost Glacier One in Phendrana Drifts, tapped the magma of Magmoor Caverns for power and began terraforming the Chozo Ruins, with plans for a combat base and a starport; Command expected to dominate the sector within a deca-cycle. Phazon yields rose by forty-four percent near the crater, at the cost of madness among the miners: loss of balance, spasms and hallucinations.

Samus came following the Orpheon's distress beacon and ended the Tallon IV incident by breaking the Pirate operation and destroying Metroid Prime in the Impact Crater. The planet's decline halted; by the time of the Phazon crisis, Federation records described Tallon IV as recovered from the impact (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). The Chozo of Tallon IV had foreseen her: she is the Entrusted One of their Chozo Lore, and it was on this world that she first wore Phazon in her armor and first met the thing that would become Dark Samus.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_IV · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ruins · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime</sub>


### Prime: The Chozo of Tallon IV and the Chozo Lore prophecy

*Keys:* Chozo Lore, Entrusted One, Great Poison, Worm prophecy, Chozo prophecy, the Turned, Prophecy of Light, Shining One, Tallon Chozo, Chozo of Tallon IV, Chozo settlers, harmonization


The Chozo who settled Tallon IV came from Zebes and chose, by design, to leave advanced technology behind: they knew it but did not worship it, and they built their sanctuary of stone, branches and water around the ancient trees (Chozo Lore inscriptions "Beginnings" and "Purification"). As the years passed many began to see beyond ordinary perception, visions of past and future they read as harmonization with the infinite. The darkest and most common vision was of the Worm, an evil "born from parasites, nurtured in a poisoned womb" that would devour the world from within; the inscriptions note that not every prophecy comes to pass.

Then an unforeseen meteor fell. From its crater spread the Great Poison, an energy that seemed almost sentient, corrupting trees and water and birthing mutated beasts, one of which defiled the sacred fountain. The Chozo could not destroy the poison and debated its nature, an alien weapon or the fragment of a shattered world, hoping it existed only on Tallon IV so that it might one day be eradicated. They resolved to contain it: a temple, the Cradle, rose over the crater, and at its heart a Cipher, "a mystical lock powered by twelve Artifacts," which they hid across the land. The seal slowed the poison but did not stop it, and the Chozo knew its strength was finite.

Not all endured. The Turned, Chozo whose minds the poison corrupted, kept their forms as ghostly shapes caught in limbo, driven only by an instinct to guard the land; these are the Chozo Ghosts that haunt the Chozo Ruins. The rest withdrew beyond the illusion of the physical world, promising to keep watch ("Exodus").

Their final inscriptions are a prophecy. A savior would come. They would leave their ancient weapons and armor for that soul, and whoever gathered them would become the Entrusted One, the only being able to reverse the evil, and the true Entrusted One once the monsters that sought to harness the poison were defeated. The "Prophecy of Light" sees a light chasing the poison's shadows, sometimes in the shape of a woman; when it recedes, the Chozo's long vigil will end. The "Shining One" is a human figure shining in the toxic dark. In another rendering of the same inscriptions she is the Hatchling or Newborn, a ghostlike figure in Chozo armor hunting the corrupted, whom the elders hoped would be their great Defender against the Worm.

Samus, raised by the Chozo of Zebes and wearing their armor, is the Entrusted One they foresaw. She read these inscriptions one by one as she crossed Tallon IV during the Tallon IV incident, and her Logbook preserves them. The Chozo Lore also warns that Chozo Statues across the solar system reward those who respect them with friendship and answer those who destroy them with wrath.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ruins · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ghost · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_IV</sub>


### Prime: The Tallon IV incident (Metroid Prime events)

*Keys:* Tallon IV incident, Tallon IV mission, mission to Tallon IV, mission on Tallon IV, Metroid Prime incident, Metroid Prime mission, events of Metroid Prime, story of Metroid Prime, Orpheon distress signal


The Tallon IV incident was Samus Aran's first encounter with Phazon and with the creature Metroid Prime (Metroid Prime; set shortly after her first Zebes mission and before her extermination campaign on SR388). It began when she tracked a distress beacon to a derelict Space Pirate research frigate, the Orpheon, in orbit above Tallon IV in the FS-176 system.

Aboard, she found a crew slaughtered by its own experiments: Tallon IV Parasites mutated with Phazon into Parasite Queens. She killed the surviving Queen in the Reactor Core; its body fell into the reactor and the ship began to self-destruct. On her way out she met Ridley, rebuilt by the Pirates as the cybernetic Meta Ridley. An explosion in an elevator shaft reduced her Power Suit to basic functions, and she pursued Ridley to the surface in her Gunship while the frigate fell from orbit behind her.

On the planet she pieced its history together from Chozo Lore and Pirate Data. Chozo settlers from Zebes had lived there in deliberate harmony with nature until a meteor, a Leviathan, struck decades earlier and released the Great Poison, which the Pirates named Phazon. Before leaving, the Chozo built the Artifact Temple over the Impact Crater and sealed it with the Cipher, a lock of twelve Chozo Artifacts, and their inscriptions foretold a savior, the Entrusted One, who would gather their armor and weapons and cleanse the land. After the fall of Zebes the Space Pirates had made Tallon IV a secondary headquarters, mining Phazon and experimenting on Metroids, on their own troops and on the native life.

Samus crossed the planet's regions recovering her abilities and the Artifacts. In the Chozo Ruins she killed Flaahgra, the mutant plant poisoning the water. In Magmoor Caverns she passed the Pirates' geothermal works. In Phendrana Drifts she broke into the research outpost Glacier One and destroyed Thardus, the living Phazon ore of Project Titan. She explored the flooded wreck of the Orpheon, then the Phazon Mines, where the dying Omega Pirate collapsed onto her and its Phazon fused with her armor into the Phazon Suit.

With all twelve Artifacts set in the Artifact Temple, Meta Ridley attacked and shattered the Chozo Totems. Samus beat him with help from the temple's own defenses, which blasted him into the abyss. Twelve Chozo Ghosts then took the totems' places and opened the way into the Impact Crater. There she destroyed Metroid Prime's armored exoskeleton and then its core essence, turning its own Phazon against it through the Phazon Suit. The dying creature seized her and tore the Phazon Suit from her body before it exploded. She escaped the collapsing crater, removed her helmet aboard her Gunship and watched the Artifact Temple fall. Unseen by her, the Phazon left behind drew in the stolen suit and a hand rose from it: the birth of Dark Samus.

The Pirate operation on Tallon IV was broken and the planet's decline halted; later Galactic Federation records call it recovered. For Samus it was the beginning of a conflict with Phazon and Dark Samus that ended only with the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_IV · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Frigate_Orpheon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Artifact_Temple · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_(creature) · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime</sub>


## Prime Hunters


### Prime Hunters: Alimbic Cluster locations

*Keys:* Celestial Archives, Alinos, Arcterra, Vesper Defense Outpost, VDO, Alimbic ruins, Echo Hall, Sic Transit, Cannon Control Room


The Alimbic Cluster, in the Tetra Galaxy beyond Federation space, holds four sites that Samus explored during the incident there (Metroid Prime Hunters). Each hides two Octoliths, so she had to visit each at least twice.

Celestial Archives: an Alimbic space station built as a vast data-storage facility to keep their knowledge safe, filled with automated defenses. It was Samus's first stop, and she recovered the Volt Driver and the Shock Coil there. Its Docking Bay holds the records of the Alimbic Order and the Octoliths, and its Celestial Gateway and Synergy Core carry verses of the Alimbic Prophecy. Kanden, the first rival she met, ambushed her here, and the first Stronghold Void she entered lay within the station.

Alinos: a rocky planet with a considerable volume of magma flowing beneath its crust, settled by the Alimbics, who harnessed its heat. Its ultra-high temperatures once powered an Alimbic city (per the manual), and thermal-energy machines still dot the surface; one of the Alimbics' last recorded messages, left in Echo Hall, calls it a place of death. The High Ground holds the sarcophagi of General Almiiak and the scientist Arctherus and the Interment Chamber of their warriors, and it is where Spire first confronted Samus; the Alimbic Gardens and Council Chamber hold accounts of Gorea; the Cannon Control Room houses the Alimbic Cannon. Weavel ambushed Samus at the top of Echo Hall, and the Fire Spawn lives in the magma; Samus took the Magmaul after defeating it.

Arcterra: a bitterly cold yet habitable planet settled by the Alimbics, where the Arctic Spawn and other cold-adapted creatures survive among Alimbic ruins. The Sanctorus, Fault Line and Subterranean chambers preserve the history of the Alimbic War and the Seal Sphere; the Ice Hive records their advances in telepathy and telekinesis. Samus found the Judicator here and the Imperialist in the Fault Line. Noxus and Trace were both first met in Sic Transit: Noxus confronted her directly while Trace sniped from a perch at the back of the same chamber.

Vesper Defense Outpost (VDO): a station built during the Alimbics' brief militant period after the invasion that ended their tribal era; it held no cultural significance for them. Kept at subzero temperatures and deserted since their extinction, its automated systems attack anyone who approaches. Its Weapons Complex, Fuel Stack, Stasis Bunker and Compression Chamber preserve the Alimbics' accounts of the Oubliette, the Infinity Void and the Octolith guardians. Samus recovered the Battlehammer in its Cortex CPU, Sylux first confronted her in the Weapons Complex, and two Stronghold Portals here lead to Octoliths.

The fifth site, the Oubliette, is Gorea's prison in the Infinity Void and can be reached only by the Alimbic Cannon.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Celestial_Archives · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alinos · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/High_Ground · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Arcterra · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Vesper_Defense_Outpost · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sic_Transit</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Gorea

*Keys:* Gorea, Alimbic War, Seal Sphere, Trocra


Gorea is the shapeshifting entity that destroyed the Alimbic civilization and the true author of the message promising "Ultimate Power" in the Alimbic Cluster. Its origin is unknown. It arrived without warning, mistaken for a comet, and crashed on an Alimbic world, most likely Alinos. It landed as a vapor, then copied the Alimbics' cellular structure to take solid form, gaining an intellect equal to theirs. Gorea can change its shape, cellular structure and atomic structure at will, so it has no fixed form. It devours the life force of its prey and grows stronger, and it replicates its victims' cellular structure and absorbs their technology to turn it back on them. During the three-month Alimbic War every weapon the Alimbics used against it was turned against its makers, and nothing they had could destroy it. As the death toll rose, the Alimbic Order of Elders chose containment: Gorea was lured into a trap and bound by the Seal Sphere, which bonded to it when it tried to absorb the device, then sealed inside the Oubliette and hurled into the Infinity Void by the Alimbic Cannon. There it lay immobilized for thousands of years.

As its containment declined, Gorea's psychic power reached across dimensions as a repeating telepathic lure, drawing the galaxy's most dangerous hunters to the Cluster in the hope that one would free it. When the six rival hunters forced their way into the Oubliette and broke its confinement, Gorea rose, drained their energy and absorbed their weapons. In its first form it stood as an armored giant whose arms wielded the hunters' own weapons, cycling from one to the next. Its shoulders were its weak points, each vulnerable to the weapon opposed to the one Gorea was then wielding: the Magmaul against its Judicator form, the Shock Coil against its Imperialist form, the Imperialist against its Shock Coil form. With both arms destroyed it hung inverted beneath the freed Seal Sphere, lashing with a tentacle that chased Samus and lifting Trocra from the chamber's edge with psionic tentacles to hurl as living bombs. When the Alimbic Prophecy was fulfilled, Gorea took a second, faster and tougher form in a lower chamber, immune to every weapon except the Alimbic Omega Cannon; six glowing points on its body went dark one by one under that weapon's fire until it was destroyed and the Oubliette exploded.

Samus is the only being ever to kill Gorea (Metroid Prime Hunters). She did so despite the Alimbics' final wish that no one seek Gorea without the means to destroy it, having read their records and gone on regardless.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gorea · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Seal_Sphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Oubliette · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Kanden

*Keys:* Kanden, Enoema, Volt Driver, Stinglarva, failed supersoldier


Kanden is an Enoema and the product of a failed attempt to engineer the ultimate soldier, immortal and relentless (per the manual); he gives his origin only as "a sinister laboratory." The stress of the neural combat encoding forced on his still-mortal brain was more than it could handle (per the official site), leaving a ferocious, deadly and dangerously unpredictable being, unbound by morals or conscience and driven by an instinct to hunt. He broke out of the laboratory and killed those responsible for his neurological alterations and surgical enhancements, then made himself a bounty hunter to prove he is the finest in the galaxy. The message from the Alimbic Cluster was his chance: he came for the Ultimate Power like the rest.

His weapon is the Volt Driver, an electrically charged weapon. In Kanden's hands its shots home in slightly on a target and briefly distort the Combat Visor on impact, though they travel more slowly than the same weapon fired by anyone else. His own electromagnetic field carries voltage comparable to lightning and can likewise disrupt a visor. His alternate form is the Stinglarva, a serpentine, segmented insect bioengineered from predatory insects; its tail detaches, regenerates, homes in on a target and explodes, and its sting is dangerous to anyone who comes close. The form handles sharp turns well and suits evasion. He is most vulnerable to Weavel's Battlehammer.

Kanden was the first rival Samus met during the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters), ambushing her in the Celestial Archives, where she beat him. He reached the Oubliette with the other hunters, was drained by Gorea and fled in his ship when Gorea died. He has no recorded appearance afterward.

To Samus he is one of six rivals she has fought and beaten; nothing in the record suggests any dealings between them before or after the Cluster.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kanden · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Volt_Driver · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Affinity_Weapon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Celestial_Archives · https://omegametroid.com/?p=14836</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Noxus

*Keys:* Noxus, Vhozon, Vhozons, Judicator, Vhoscythe, Vho


Noxus is a Vhozon from the cold planet Vho. The Vhozon are a proud, reclusive race of the galaxy's outer rim; little is known of their culture beyond what Noxus himself shows. They are purple with rounded features, their skin has extraordinary insulating properties developed in a frigid climate, and they can manipulate ice to some degree. The official account calls Noxus a spiritual being who walks a harsh, righteous path. He became a bounty hunter to bring justice to the galaxy's criminals and evildoers and to keep the balance of order from collapsing into instability and chaos. He pursued the Ultimate Power of the Alimbic Cluster so that it would not fall into the hands of lawbreakers or the unworthy, which in practice meant ensuring that no one else obtained it.

His weapon is the Judicator, which fires a beam of supercooled plasma that freezes what it strikes; in Noxus's hands it throws up a short-range wall of ice that freezes anything in its path. His alternate form is the Vhoscythe, an evolutionary defense mechanism of the Vhozon rather than a technological enhancement, serving both attack and defense. He is most vulnerable to the heat of Spire's Magmaul.

During the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters) Noxus confronted Samus in Sic Transit on Arcterra, with Trace sniping from the back of the same chamber, and lost to her. He was among the six who broke into the Oubliette ahead of her, was drained by Gorea, which then wielded the hunters' weapons against Samus, and fled in his ship when Gorea died. He has no recorded appearance afterward.

Of the six rivals, his stated aim came closest to Samus's own orders, which were to keep the power out of hostile hands or destroy it; he nonetheless treated her as one more competitor and fought her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Noxus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Vhozon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Judicator · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sic_Transit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Affinity_Weapon · https://omegametroid.com/?p=14836</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Octoliths and the Alimbic Cannon

*Keys:* Octolith, Octoliths, Alimbic Cannon, Cretaphid, Cretaphids, Slench, Slenches, Stronghold Void, Stronghold Voids, Stronghold Portal, Alimbic Artifact, Alimbic Artifacts


The Octoliths are eight large crystals the Alimbics created as the keys to Gorea's prison. Each holds the transferred essence of one of the eight Alimbic Elders and carries unique coordinates, frequencies and codes needed to operate the Alimbic Cannon; without all eight, neither the Infinity Void nor the Oubliette can be reached. The Alimbic Order scattered them across four heavily defended territories, the Celestial Archives, Alinos, the Vesper Defense Outpost and Arcterra, with no more than two on any one planet or station. Each was hidden in a Stronghold Void, a concealed chamber holding the Alimbics' most sacred relics, accessible only by extraordinary means: a Stronghold Portal controlled by three Alimbic Artifacts and guarded by the Alimbics' most formidable cybernetic security systems. "Beware our guardians," their records warn.

Those guardians are the Cretaphid and the Slench, each built in four increasingly advanced versions. A Cretaphid is a tall defense column split into three rotating sections studded with spots that fire homing plasma or lasers; a spot can be destroyed only while it glows blue, and once all are gone a central crystal rises from the top as the only vulnerable point. Later versions mix lasers and plasma, and the fourth roams its chamber. A Slench is a giant mechanical eye anchored to a wall or ceiling by three tentacle-like synapses that control its energy blasters; a forcefield shields its body until the synapses are damaged, after which it detaches, floats, fires and rams, moving faster the more it is hurt. Later Slenches fire superheated magma, high-voltage bolts whose electromagnetic pulse disrupts the Combat Visor, or supercooled plasma.

The Alimbic Cannon stands in the Cannon Control Room on Alinos. It fires polarized antimatter faster than light to tear a dimensional rift, the Infinity Void, between this dimension and a parallel one of formless antimatter, emitting blue rings as it works. The Alimbics used it to hurl the Oubliette, with Gorea sealed inside, into that void, then sealed the rift. Gathering all eight Octoliths in the control room reactivates it.

During the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters) Samus recovered all eight despite the Alimbics' warnings; each one brought her a telepathic message from the Alimbic spirits. The rival hunters sought them too, and whenever one of them defeated her he took an Octolith that she had to win back by beating him again. With the cannon restored she opened the way to the Oubliette and to Gorea.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Octolith · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Cannon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Infinity_Void · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Cretaphid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Slench · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Lore</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Spire

*Keys:* Spire, Diamont, Diamonts, Magmaul, Dialanche, Mondreus  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Diamont, Diamonts, bounty hunter, Magmaul, Dialanche, Mondreus, Alimbic, Alimbics, Alinos
