Spire is a Diamont, a member of an ancient silicon-based race from the planet Mondreus whose bodies are composed of organic rock containing molten ferrous compounds normally found only in a planet's core. He is believed to be the sole surviving Diamont, and he took up bounty hunting to solve the mystery of his people's disappearance, searching the galaxy for any clue to where they went or what became of them. The message from the Alimbic Cluster drew him because he hoped the Alimbics' source of ultimate power would also explain his own race's fate.

He withstands extreme heat and is the only one of the hunters who can walk in lava. His weapon is the Magmaul, made by the Diamonts and handed down through generations to him; it lobs cohesive bursts of superheated magma, and in Spire's hands its blasts are far more devastating, though the splash can burn him as well. He can transform at will, without any technology, into the Dialanche, a hypercondensed ferrous orb bristling with spikes that rolls at high speed over terrain and climbs flat surfaces, and which extends two retractable titanium limbs to spin and batter enemies; this appears to be a natural Diamont ability. He is most vulnerable to the cold of Noxus's Judicator.

Samus met and defeated Spire on the High Ground of Alinos, among the Alimbic sarcophagi, during the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters). He was among the six who broke into the Oubliette ahead of her, was drained by Gorea and escaped in his ship when Gorea died. His search for the Diamonts has no recorded resolution, and he has no recorded appearance after the incident.

Samus knows him as a rival she has beaten; his quest for his lost people echoes nothing she has said aloud, though she herself was raised by a race, the Chozo, that has all but vanished.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Spire · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Diamont · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Magmaul · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/High_Ground · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Affinity_Weapon</sub>


### Prime Hunters: The Alimbic Cluster incident

*Keys:* Alimbic Cluster, Alimbic Cluster incident, Tetra Galaxy, Prime Hunters, Metroid Prime Hunters, rival hunters, Alimbic System, Mission File 79109, Rogue Contract, Federation Empaths, Neural Thread


The Alimbic Cluster incident took place after the Tallon IV mission and before Samus's work on Aether (Metroid Prime Hunters). The Galactic Federation, through its Federation Empaths, intercepted and translated a repeating telepathic Neural Thread originating in the Alimbic Cluster, a largely uncharted region of the Tetra Galaxy outside Federation control. It read: "The secret to ULTIMATE POWER lies in the ALIMBIC CLUSTER." The Federation hired Samus Aran under a covert Rogue Contract (Mission File 79109): discover the meaning of the message, secure the power for Federation control, or failing that keep it secret or destroy it. The disposition of rival interests was presumed hostile.

Six other bounty hunters had intercepted the same message: Kanden, Weavel, Spire, Noxus, Trace and Sylux. Samus found only the ruins of the extinct Alimbics across four sites: the Celestial Archives, Alinos, the Vesper Defense Outpost and Arcterra. Scanning Alimbic Datashades, she learned that a shapeshifting entity, Gorea, had destroyed the Alimbic civilization and that the Alimbics had bound it in the Seal Sphere aboard the Oubliette, a prison hidden in the Infinity Void. The message had almost certainly come from Gorea itself. Samus nevertheless gathered the eight Octoliths, each guarded by a Cretaphid or a Slench and contested by the rival hunters, who took an Octolith from her whenever they bested her. In the same ruins she recovered the six rivals' own affinity weapons: the Volt Driver and Shock Coil in the Celestial Archives, the Magmaul on Alinos, the Battlehammer on the Vesper Defense Outpost, and the Judicator and Imperialist on Arcterra. With all eight Octoliths in the Cannon Control Room on Alinos she fired the Alimbic Cannon and opened the way to the Oubliette.

The rivals reached the Oubliette first, meaning to destroy the threat, but shattered Gorea's confinement. Gorea rose, drained their energy and turned their own weapons against Samus. She crippled it, used the six affinity weapons to fulfill the Alimbic Prophecy, and took up the Alimbic Omega Cannon, the only weapon able to destroy Gorea's final form. The Oubliette exploded as the hunters fled in their ships. In a vision, three Alimbic spirits appeared before Samus, unarmored, and expressed their gratitude; she then returned to her gunship and departed. The "Ultimate Power" was gone, so the Federation's fallback objective was met.

Samus defeated every one of the six rivals at least once during the incident and knows each by name, weapon and alternate form. Of the six, only Sylux is known to have kept pursuing her afterward.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Cluster · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tetra_Galaxy · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gorea · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Octolith</sub>


### Prime Hunters: The Alimbics

*Keys:* Alimbics, Alimbic race, Alimbic people, Alimbic civilization, Alimbic Order, Order of Elders, Alimbic Elders, Almiiak, Arctherus, Essence Transference, Datashade, Datashades


The Alimbics were a technologically advanced race of the Tetra Galaxy, beings of superior mental prowess, now extinct. Eons ago they colonized the region still called the Alimbic Cluster, settling the hot planet Alinos and the frigid Arcterra and building stations such as the Celestial Archives and the Vesper Defense Outpost. An Alimbic had three legs, two arms, a single eye and a floating head; a very durable exoskeleton let them endure both worlds' extremes. They could alter their form, and ultimately dissolve it, through a technique called Essence Transference.

Their records survive as Alimbic Datashades, blue packets of telepathic data scattered across the Cluster, invisible to the eye but readable with a Scan Visor and built to keep their integrity indefinitely. They tell the Alimbics' history. A peaceful tribal era ended when an alien invasion caught them unprepared; the survivors vowed never to be caught unprepared again and turned to military science, developing weapons, including bioweapons, that outpaced every enemy. General Almiiak led them to victory in the Tetra Border Wars, which his tomb inscription places two thousand years before it was written, long before the Alimbic War; he later formed the Alimbic Order of Elders, an eight-member council and the only government their records name, though another record says the eight Elders gathered to create the Order during the war against Gorea. The scientist Arctherus's discovery of Neurogenesis opened an age of peace, prosperity and advances in telepathy, telekinesis and extradimensional exploration. Their own scriptures admit that confidence became overconfidence: "Our pride was a veil over our eyes."

Then Gorea fell from the sky, first mistaken for a comet. The Alimbic War lasted three standard galactic months; death swept across their worlds, every weapon was turned back on its makers, and the Alimbics feared Gorea would consume the whole galaxy. Unable to destroy it, and with the death toll rising, the Order of Elders chose containment. They lured Gorea into a trap, bound it in the Seal Sphere, placed the sphere in the Oubliette and used the Alimbic Cannon to hurl that prison into the Infinity Void. To power the seal, the surviving citizens reduced themselves to pure telepathic energy through Essence Transference, and each of the eight Elders placed their essence in one of the eight Octoliths. The race vanished from the physical world, leaving its last recorded messages across the Cluster; the one in Echo Hall on Alinos reads, "We depart from this place of death." They asked that their disappearance not be investigated and warned any who came after not to seek Gorea unless they could destroy it.

On Alinos's High Ground stand the sarcophagi of Almiiak and Arctherus and the Interment Chamber of warriors buried so their spirits could return to the planet's core. Samus read these records during the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters), and when Gorea finally died, three Alimbic spirits appeared to her in a vision to express their gratitude.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Datashade · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gorea · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Seal_Sphere · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters</sub>


### Prime Hunters: The Oubliette, the Seal Sphere and the Alimbic Omega Cannon

*Keys:* Oubliette, Seal Sphere, Infinity Void, Alimbic Omega Cannon, Omega Cannon, Alimbic Prophecy, Gorea's prison  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Oubliette, Seal Sphere, Infinity Void, Alimbic Omega Cannon, Alimbic Prophecy, Gorea, Alimbic, Alimbics


The Oubliette is the Alimbic station built to hold Gorea forever. The Alimbics sent it into the Infinity Void, a parallel dimension of formless antimatter outside this galaxy that only the Alimbic Cannon can open, so that Gorea could neither escape nor be freed by accident. Its entrance halls hold Alimbic Datashades recording how Gorea was lured into a trap, sealed and sent away.

The Seal Sphere is the device at the heart of the prison. After every weapon failed, the Alimbic Order of Elders built it from the spirits of their surviving citizens, who reduced themselves to pure telepathic energy through Essence Transference. The sphere bonded to Gorea when the creature tried to absorb it, and it draws on both the Alimbics' combined psychic energy and Gorea's own captive psychic power, so that Gorea cannot escape while ensnared. It rested on the central island of the Oubliette's main chamber, ringed by a lethal pool. When the six rival hunters forced their way in ahead of Samus, meaning to destroy the threat, they broke Gorea's confinement instead and were drained. In the battle that followed Samus destroyed Gorea's arms, the sphere rose free, and she wounded it with whichever of the hunters' weapons Gorea was then vulnerable to, which weakened Gorea at the cost of the Alimbic presence within the sphere.

The Alimbic Prophecy, left in eight verses across the four sites' gateways and other chambers, foretold that the Alimbic power would materialize when six frequencies shimmered in a divine spectral order: Yellow Lightning (the Volt Driver), then the Greenwood (Battlehammer), an Orange Blaze (Magmaul), Blue Smoke (Shock Coil), Violet Crystal (Judicator) and a Red Stain (Imperialist). Striking six wall devices in the main chamber in that order with those weapons carried Samus and Gorea into a lower chamber where the Alimbic Omega Cannon waited at the bottom.

The Alimbic Omega Cannon was built by the Alimbics and left in the Oubliette, apparently as a last resort should the seal fail; their surviving records never mention it, saying only that no weapon of theirs could defeat Gorea. It has no ammunition limit, does not harm its wielder, and was the only thing that could hurt Gorea's final form; its shots blacked out the six glowing points on Gorea's body until the creature died and the Oubliette tore itself apart. Samus wielded it once, during the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters), and it presumably perished with the Oubliette. It is a different weapon from the Omega Cannon she later gained temporarily from E.M.M.I. Central Units on ZDR (Metroid Dread).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Oubliette · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Seal_Sphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Cannon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Infinity_Void · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gorea</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Trace

*Keys:* Trace, Kriken, Krikens, Kriken Empire, Imperialist, Triskelion  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Kriken, Krikens, Kriken Empire, bounty hunter, Imperialist, Triskelion, Alimbic, Alimbics, Arcterra


Trace is a Kriken, a vicious race that ranks among the most hated and feared in the galaxy for its ruthless imperialism; the Kriken Empire covers much of the galaxy. When a Kriken reaches a certain age its clan exiles it as a rite of passage, and the exile is not accepted back until it has expanded the Empire. Trace is such an exile, roaming the galaxy to find planets for the Kriken to conquer. The quest for ultimate power consumes him (per the manual): his every thought is bent toward bringing supremacy to the Krikens and honor to himself, and he saw the Alimbic Cluster's promise as the way to both.

His weapon is the Imperialist, a long-range quantum cascade laser with precision targeting that suits the sniper's role he prefers; while he holds still with it he can cloak himself and vanish from sight. His alternate form is the Triskelion, a three-legged war machine with a powerful lunge attack into which sophisticated Kriken military biotechnology lets him biomorph; it dashes at damaging speeds and can likewise cloak while motionless. His slender exoskeleton may not be as durable as it appears, which may be why he fights from a distance. He is most vulnerable to Sylux's Shock Coil.

During the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters) Trace perched at the back of Sic Transit on Arcterra, above the fight between Samus and Noxus, and sniped at her until she beat him. He was among the six who broke into the Oubliette ahead of her, was drained by Gorea and fled in his ship when Gorea died. He has no recorded appearance afterward.

To Samus he is a rival she has beaten and a scout for an empire that conquers worlds; nothing in the record shows any contact between them outside the Cluster.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Trace · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kriken · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Triskelion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Imperialist · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sic_Transit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Affinity_Weapon</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Weavel

*Keys:* Weavel, Battlehammer, Halfturret, Pirate cyborg, cyborg Pirate


Weavel is a Space Pirate turned cyborg and one of the six rival hunters drawn to the Alimbic Cluster (Metroid Prime Hunters). Before his transformation he served in a Space Pirate special unit, and the official site calls him a once-powerful Space Pirate General. He was critically wounded in a past clash between the Space Pirates and Samus Aran; the manual says he was badly injured in a past battle with her. Only his brain and spinal cord remained functional, and they were fused to a mobile life-support system (per the official site); the manual describes his body as reconstructed and fused with a Power Suit. The result is a formidable and ferocious warrior. Like the other hunters he came for the Ultimate Power; whether he would have delivered it to the Space Pirates or kept it for himself is unknown.

His weapon of choice is the Battlehammer, a heavy-duty repeating weapon powered by a miniature nuclear reactor whose rounds have a lethal blast radius; the records of the incident advise keeping one's distance from him. In Weavel's hands its rounds explode on impact for additional damage, with markedly larger blasts than when anyone else fires it. His cybernetic body can split into the Halfturret: the upper half walks on its arms while the detached legs remain as a turret carrying powerful projectile weapons, and both halves attack independently. He is most vulnerable to Kanden's Volt Driver.

In the Cluster, Weavel ambushed Samus at the top of Echo Hall on Alinos and lost to her. He was among the six who broke into the Oubliette ahead of her, was drained by Gorea, and fled in his ship when Gorea died. He has no recorded appearance after the incident.

Samus is the cause of his condition: she is the reason there is more machine than Pirate left of him, and the Alimbic Cluster was at least their second clash. He knows her as the hunter who nearly killed him; she knows him as a Pirate she once critically wounded.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Weavel · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Battlehammer · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Affinity_Weapon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters · https://omegametroid.com/?p=14836</sub>


## Prime 2


### Prime 2: Aether and Dark Aether

*Keys:* Aether, Dark Aether, Transdimensional Flux, Dark Vapor, Safe Zone, Safe Zones, Light Crystal, Light Crystals, Light Beacon, Light Beacons, Ingclaw, Ingclaws


Aether is a rogue planet without a sun, drifting in the Dasha Region on the edge of Galactic Federation space. The star-born Luminoth, led there by the scout X-Qar, settled it in an age their lore calls the Golden Age: a fertile, aged world of mountains, the Agon plains and the Torvus forest, with a Great Temple raised between the three domains. Because the planet's energy was failing, the Luminoth built Energy Controllers to preserve and regulate it; this stored energy, the Light of Aether, also powered a planet-wide weather control system and a light-based teleportation network.

About fifty years before Samus's arrival a Leviathan from Phaaze struck Aether. The Luminoth saw it coming and could not divert it. Agon was scorched into desert, Torvus was swallowed by the sea, and the planet entered Transdimensional Flux: half of its energy and part of the impacted matter were pulled into a newly born twin dimension occupying the same space, Dark Aether. The Ing were born of that dark world. Aether itself grew unstable; extreme climate shifts left most of the planet uninhabitable, and only the northern regions still support life.

Dark Aether is a twisted, poisonous mirror of Aether: dim, toxic, rich in Phazon, each region echoing a Luminoth domain (the Sky Temple Grounds, Dark Agon Wastes, Dark Torvus Bog, the Ing Hive and the floating Sky Temple). Its air kills any being not native to it, rooted Ingclaws thicken it further with Dark Vapor waste, and the surface waters of Dark Torvus are venomous. Luminoth scouts could not survive long there, so the Luminoth made Light Crystals, and when crystals ran short, cheaper and unstable Light Beacons, which pool Aether's light into spherical Safe Zones that cancel the atmosphere, heal those inside and burn the Ing. Dark energy can nullify a crystal for a time; the Light Beam or Annihilator Beam restores it, and a crystal charged by the Light Beam kills any Ing that steps into its glow. Travel between the worlds runs through portals, some natural and most opened by Luminoth machines; how the Ing open their own rifts is unknown. The Space Pirates forced temporary rifts open with a strange artifact found near the dimensional anomaly, which their science team dubbed the Dark Beam.

Samus's Varia Suit could not withstand Dark Aether's air; she lived from Safe Zone to Safe Zone until the Luminoth's Dark Suit, and later the Light Suit, shielded her. When she drew the last planetary energy from the Emperor Ing, Dark Aether collapsed and vanished with the entire Ing Horde. Aether is slowly recovering: its energy supply, once torn in two, is stable again, the Luminoth are mending their world under Federation supervision, and the G.F.S. Anhur later helped them dismantle the abandoned Pirate mining facility and carried off its Phazon ore.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aether · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Aether · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Light_Crystal · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Luminoth_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ing-Luminoth_War · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Energy_Controller</sub>


### Prime 2: Amorbis, Chykka and Quadraxis

*Keys:* Amorbis, Chykka, Quadraxis, Dark Agon Temple, Dark Torvus Temple, Hive Temple, Chykling, Chyklings, Dark Sphere, Head Module


Three guardians held the Energy Controllers of Dark Aether's temples, and Samus had to kill each before she could drain the stolen Light of Aether back to the Luminoth.

Amorbis (Dark Agon Temple, Dark Agon Wastes). Gigantic tunneling worms native to Dark Aether, immensely strong and nearly indifferent to pain, that sense prey on the surface from deep underground. In the temple three of them rose in turn from the sand, ramming Samus and fusing themselves to the Dark Sphere at the chamber's heart; joined to it, a Dark Amorbis grows armor, regenerates, sweeps the room with beams and fires dark energy that nullifies the Safe Zones, and a crystal it darkens cannot be recharged. Samus shattered each head's armor, let the dying worm inhale her in Morph Ball form and detonated bombs in the soft flesh of its mouth. When the last Amorbis died the Dark Sphere burst, and inside lay the Dark Suit.

Chykka (Dark Torvus Temple, Dark Torvus Bog). A single rapidly aging insect of both dark and light nature. Samus freed its larva from a cocoon above the temple's poisoned water; it circled her, spat acid and tried to drag her into its mouth with its tongue. Stunned, it slid under and surfaced moments later as a winged adult that rammed her and fired bursts of dark water, then, when its four wings were torn, fell back and rose as Dark Chykka, its body sheathed in dark energy with only its eggsac exposed, spawning Chykling swarms to defend it. Samus killed it; her scans found no trace of Ing possession in the corpse and no likelihood of resurrection. Afterward she claimed the Dark Visor.

Quadraxis (Hive Temple, Ing Hive). A four-legged Luminoth war machine of Sanctuary's making, several times the size of the Quads it resembles; an unfinished twin still lies in parts in the Sanctuary Temple. An Ing possessed it as Samus entered the Hive Temple and it went rogue in the Horde's service. The walker fired Annihilator-type blasts, homing missiles, turret fire and leg shock waves; Samus boosted into the orbs beneath its feet to cripple its knees until the body collapsed. Its Head Module then detached and fought on, commanded through the body's antenna. With the Echo Visor she targeted the antenna to drop the head's shield, destroyed its three sonic receivers (each loosing a Dark Quad), stunned the head and rode its hull by Spider Ball to bomb its weak points. The head crashed and exploded, and she took the Annihilator Beam from the wreck.

Six lesser Ing Guardians, the Warrior Ing and Darklings that held her stolen abilities, stood between Samus and these temples.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Amorbis · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chykka · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Quadraxis · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Light_Crystal · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Aether · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sanctuary_Fortress</sub>


### Prime 2: Energy Controllers and the Light of Aether

*Keys:* Energy Controller, Energy Controllers, Light of Aether, Transfer Module, planetary energy, Sky Temple Key, Sky Temple Keys, Dark Temple Key, Dark Agon Key, Dark Torvus Key, Ing Hive Key, Alpha Splinter


The Light of Aether is the planetary energy of Aether, the collected solar, bio and geothermal power that the Luminoth gathered when their sunless rogue world began to die. Their greatest minds built the Energy Controllers to preserve and regulate it: a Main Energy Controller in the Great Temple and three sub-controllers in the temples of Agon, Torvus and Sanctuary. Each region's share is bound to its own land. The Energy Controllers fed a planet-wide weather control system and a light-based teleportation network, and the Light brought peace and prosperity. The Luminoth venerate it; their farewells invoke it ("May the Light of Aether serve you well," "may the Light of Aether guide your aim").

The Leviathan impact split the energy in two. Dark Aether formed with Energy Controllers of its own, mirrors of the Luminoth temples, holding the stolen half: the Dark Agon, Dark Torvus and Hive Energy Controllers, and the Sky Temple Energy Controller beneath the Emperor Ing. As U-Mos put it, "Should one world gain control of this energy, the other will perish." To recover their half, the Luminoth built the Energy Transfer Module, a device whose core runs on light energy and can siphon power from one Controller and release it into another. They gave it to the Keybearers for an assault on the Sky Temple. None returned, and the Ing seized the Module and turned it on Aether, draining Agon, then Torvus, then Sanctuary until only the Great Temple's Controller still shone.

An Ing bearing the Module came for that last light inside the Alpha Splinter, the giant of a Splinter war pack hatched in the Temple Sanctuary. Samus killed the Dark Alpha Splinter and the Module bonded to her Varia Suit with no loss of performance; its light core also made her immune to possession. She drained each Dark Aether Controller in turn, after Amorbis, Chykka and Quadraxis, and returned the energy to the Agon, Torvus and Sanctuary Controllers, where the holograms of the fallen Sentinels I-Sha, A-Voq and O-Lir spoke to her and updated her Translator Module. Drawing the final energy from the Sky Temple destroyed Dark Aether. The Light Suit U-Mos gave her is itself energized by the Light of Aether.

Each Dark Temple was held fast by three locks; the Dark Agon Keys, Dark Torvus Keys and Ing Hive Keys were hidden across its region of Dark Aether. The Sky Temple required ten Sky Temple Keys, hidden by the Ing in Flying Ing Caches visible only to the Dark Visor; A-Kul set one in the Sky Temple Gateway before she died, and Samus recovered the other nine where the Keybearers fell: three in the Sky Temple Grounds, two each in the Dark Agon Wastes, Dark Torvus Bog and the Ing Hive.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Energy_Controller · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Light_of_Aether · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Energy_Transfer_Module · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sky_Temple_Key · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Temple_Key · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alpha_Splinter</sub>


### Prime 2: GFS Tyr and Bravo Squad

*Keys:* Tyr, G.F.S. Tyr, GFS Tyr, Bravo Squad, Squad Bravo, Exeter, Captain Exeter, Task Force Herakles, GFMC Compound, Dark Trooper, Dark Troopers, Angseth


The G.F.S. Tyr was an Anhur-class Galactic Federation warship, one of a line built to police and defend planetary systems and named, like other Federation vessels, for an Earth myth: Tyr, the Norse god of single combat. Its crew of Federation Marines was Squad Bravo of GFMC Task Force Herakles, a roster of twenty-two under Captain A.C. Exeter.

On patrol in the Dasha system the Tyr engaged an unknown Space Pirate frigate and crippled it; the frigate crashed on Aether and the Tyr followed, shooting down a Pirate transport full of reinforcements in orbit on the way. A storm on atmospheric entry damaged the ship and magnetic interference killed communications. Exeter made an emergency landing in the Temple Grounds and split his people into two forces: one to guard and repair the ship, the other to set up an operations base. They scavenged the Tyr for makeshift defenses, set a GM-42 "Growler" turret, a dated but effective point-defense design, at their security station, and raised the GFMC Compound beside a Splinter hive. Against science specialist B. Reevs's warning that the hive was a small part of a larger network, they attacked it; Exeter later wrote, "It's stupid to stir a hornet's nest, especially if you plan on sleeping under it." The Splinters, possessed by the Ing, came in waves. The engines could not be restored, the distress beacon was jammed by atmospheric interference, ammunition ran out. "Pretty soon we'll have to go to bayonets," gunner F. Triplette recorded. PFC L. Brouda: "If anyone reads this, know that we did our duty and fought well." PFC E. Denys, the last alive, found everyone at the ship dead and set out alone for the alien building nearby. Exeter's final report breaks off with "Each unit was doing fine, no problems... ...until they appeared."

Contact was lost eight days before Samus Aran accepted the contract to locate the troopers and render assistance. She found only bodies. In the hive's command chamber the Marines who had pushed into the Splinter nest rose before her as Dark Troopers, clumsy corpses driven by an unknown parasitic biomass, their armor and weapons still working; the rest, Exeter among them, lay dead in the compound near the terminal that held his final report, their logs intact. Comms specialist M. Angseth had wished aloud to be out taking care of business like Samus Aran instead of pushing buttons and sending reports, and PFC I. Crany had found it hard to believe that one person could take out an entire Space Pirate base. The Ing that killed them did not outlive her mission, and the Federation later sent another Anhur-class ship, the G.F.S. Anhur, back to Aether to dismantle the Pirates' mining facility with the Luminoth.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/GFS_Tyr · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/GFMC_Task_Force_Herakles · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/GFMC_Compound · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Command_Chamber · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Trooper_Logs · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Captain_A._Exeter</sub>


### Prime 2: Regions of Aether (Temple Grounds, Agon Wastes, Torvus Bog, Sanctuary Fortress)

*Keys:* Temple Grounds, Great Temple, Agon Wastes, Agon, Torvus Bog, Torvus, Sanctuary Fortress, Sky Temple, Ing Hive, Dark Agon, Dark Torvus, Sky Temple Grounds


Aether's habitable north holds four Luminoth domains, each with a dark reflection on Dark Aether.

Temple Grounds and the Great Temple. The crossroads of the Luminoth colonies lies beneath the Great Temple, built between the three domains as a place of peace and a monument to Luminoth achievement; it houses the Main Energy Controller, the Temple Sanctuary and the stasis chambers where the Luminoth slept. The Grounds hold the Hall of Honored Dead, a vast Splinter hive where the Marines of the G.F.S. Tyr died and rose as Dark Troopers, and the GFMC Compound they raised around their downed ship; Samus's gunship came down here too. Its reflection, the Sky Temple Grounds, is four broken, Phazon-soaked sections where Samus, having followed Dark Samus through a portal from the Marines' positions in the hive, lost her equipment; it holds the Sky Temple Gateway, whose shaft of light is the only road to the floating, upside-down Sky Temple where the Emperor Ing kept the last stolen energy.

Agon Wastes. Once green farmland, scorched to desert by the impact; Luminoth mining stations dot the sand among Sandiggers, Sandbats and Blueroot trees. The Agon Temple, Sentinel I-Sha's seat, was the first domain to fall, defended at the end by the child acolyte D-Chr. The Space Pirates built their Phazon mining facility here after their science teams detected Phazon in the Dasha system, holding Tallon Metroids and forcing rifts into Dark Aether; Samus fought them and Dark Samus in its Main Reactor. The Dark Agon Wastes beyond the portals are Phazon-laced Ing territory, and Amorbis guards the Dark Agon Temple.

Torvus Bog. A forest drowned by the sea, now warm marsh above and flooded Luminoth works below (the Hydrodynamo Station, the submerged Torvus Temple). Sentinel A-Voq died here resisting possession; B-Stz slew the Ing Horde's war chief in the Fourth Battle of Torvus before the domain fell. Grenchlers, Bloggs and the Alpha Blogg infest the waters. Dark Torvus Bog's surface lies under venomous dark water and is patrolled by Hunter Ing; Chykka guards the Dark Torvus Temple.

Sanctuary Fortress. Founded by E-Btr high in the cliffs, the first settlement and the summit of Luminoth engineering, defended by Quads, Rezbits, Ingsmashers and drones built under Sentinel O-Lir. The Ing corrupted the machines one by one; O-Lir's corpse bore the marks of Luminoth weapons, most likely his own rogue drones, and Sanctuary was the last domain to fall. Its hero C-Pul, the Ingslayer, felled over seven hundred Ing before dying in the Battle of the Hive. Pirate Commandos ambushed Samus on her arrival; later Dark Samus destroyed the entrance bridge to trap her and fought her in the Aerie. Its reflection is the Ing Hive, heart of the Horde, never taken by the Luminoth, where the rogue Quadraxis guards the Hive Temple.

Samus crossed all of them by Luminoth transports and portals, returning to U-Mos in the Great Temple between each domain.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Temple_Grounds · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Great_Temple · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Agon_Wastes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Torvus_Bog · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sanctuary_Fortress · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/O-Lir</sub>


### Prime 2: The Aether incident (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes events)

*Keys:* Aether incident, Metroid Prime 2, Prime 2, Metroid Prime 2: Echoes, Dark Echoes, Aether mission, Dasha, Dasha Region, Dasha system, Ing-Luminoth War, Ing Luminoth War


The Aether incident is Samus Aran's mission to the rogue planet Aether in the Dasha Region, where she ended the war between the Luminoth and the Ing (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes). It came after the Tallon IV incident and before the Phazon crisis.

The Galactic Federation contracted Samus to locate Squad Bravo of GFMC Task Force Herakles, Federation Marines aboard the G.F.S. Tyr who had chased a crippled Space Pirate frigate to Aether and gone silent eight days earlier. Lightning storms over the planet damaged her gunship and forced it down on the Temple Grounds. In the Splinter hive where the Marines had dug in she found them dead among the creatures; then the corpses rose as Dark Troopers, animated by an unknown biomass with parasitic tendencies. She glimpsed a figure in a black echo of her own armor, Dark Samus, and pursued her through a portal into Dark Aether. There Dark Samus shattered the Light Crystal shielding Samus, and a swarm of Warrior Ing stripped her of most of her equipment before she was flung back to Aether with only the basic functions of her Varia Suit. At the Marines' GFMC Compound she found the rest of the crew dead beside the wreck of the Tyr and read Captain A.C. Exeter's unfinished final report.

In the Great Temple she destroyed a Splinter war pack and the Ing-possessed Alpha Splinter; the Energy Transfer Module it carried bonded to her suit. U-Mos, last Sentinel of the Luminoth, told her how a stellar impact fifty years earlier had torn Aether into a light world and a dark one, how the Ing had stolen the Light of Aether, and that should one world gain control of that energy, the other would perish. He updated her Translator Module to read Violet holograms and marked the next temple on her map; the holograms of the fallen Sentinels I-Sha, A-Voq and O-Lir later taught her Amber, Emerald and Cobalt in turn.

In the Agon Wastes she fought the Space Pirates mining Phazon there and, in their Main Reactor, Dark Samus, who was raiding their Phazon stores; then she crossed to the Dark Agon Temple and killed Amorbis. In Torvus Bog she killed Chykka in the Dark Torvus Temple. In Sanctuary Fortress she battled the Luminoth's own corrupted mechanoids, fought Dark Samus again from the Aerie Access elevator up into the Aerie, and destroyed the rogue Quadraxis in the Ing Hive. Each victory let her drain a Dark Aether Energy Controller and restore its light to a Luminoth temple. Along the way she hunted down the Ing Guardians that held her stolen abilities and recovered the lost equipment of the Luminoth champion A-Kul.

With three temples restored, U-Mos gave her the Light Suit. She gathered the nine Sky Temple Keys still lying where the Keybearers had fallen across Dark Aether, rode the Sky Temple Gateway's beam into the floating Sky Temple and destroyed the Emperor Ing in its Sanctum. Taking the last of Dark Aether's energy began the dark world's collapse. Dark Samus, bloated with Phazon, barred her escape; Samus broke her apart and dove through a portal as Dark Aether and the whole Ing Horde ceased to exist.

Back in the Great Temple, Samus watched U-Mos wake the Luminoth from stasis. The survivors bowed to her as she left in her repaired gunship. Pirate logs of the period call her "the accursed Hunter"; the Luminoth remember her as the warrior who gave them back their world. The Federation later returned with the G.F.S. Anhur to help the Luminoth dismantle the Pirate mining facility.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ing-Luminoth_War · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aether · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Logs</sub>


### Prime 2: The Ing and the Emperor Ing

*Keys:* Ing, Ing Horde, Emperor Ing, Warrior Ing, Hunter Ing, Inglet, Inglets, Darkling, Darklings, Dark Pirate, Dark Pirates, Dark Splinter


The Ing (the Ing Horde, the Dark Tribe) are the shapeshifting natives of Dark Aether, born when the Leviathan impact split Aether in two. "Ing" is the Luminoth word for terror. In their own dimension they are amorphous dark metamorphs; they cross to Aether as gas but cannot survive long on its surface in their own bodies, so they possess creatures, corpses and even machines, which become Darklings: stronger, tougher, and in some cases gifted with new attacks. How the Ing open their rifts between the worlds is unknown. The Horde lives by fierce competition for rank, rites of passage in which the young fight to prove themselves, and cannibalism; the Keybearer G-Sch watched them feed upon their dead. Their one great weakness is bright, pure light, which they hate and fear: the Luminoth Light Beam burns them, and a Light Crystal energized by it kills any Ing that steps into its glow.

Ing Larva, the first and weakest form, swarm for protection, and Ingstorms gnaw through almost anything given time. Inglets are small laborers that cling to walls and spit dark matter. Warrior Ing are the Horde's soldiers: they melt into puddles, run up walls, slash with razor forelegs and hurl their bodies sheathed in dark energy; a swarm of them stripped Samus of her equipment, and those that absorbed her abilities became the Guardians she later hunted. Hunter Ing, the Horde's high-ranking warriors, phase out of local space to ambush with long tentacles; only they may possess Pirate Commandos, and they compete fiercely for the right. Darklings include Dark Splinters, the Dark Troopers raised from the dead Marines of the G.F.S. Tyr, Dark Pirate Troopers and Commandos, Dark Tallon Metroids, Dark Bloggs, Dark Grenchlers, and the corrupted Quads, Ingsmashers and drones of Sanctuary Fortress. The Luminoth could resist possession only briefly and chose death over serving as Ing weapons; the light energy in the Energy Transfer Module made Samus immune.

The Horde's seat on Dark Aether is the Ing Hive, which no Luminoth assault ever took, and its ruler holds the Sky Temple. The Emperor Ing is the eldest, first and strongest of its kind, swollen and mutated by the Phazon it absorbed. It molded its body around the Sky Temple's Energy Controller in the Sanctum, using itself as shield and weapon while siphoning the last stolen energy. Samus faced it in three shapes: a tentacled head over an armored eye that fires sustained beams; a regenerating chrysalis with eleven weak points that fills the chamber with poison; and a warrior-like final form guarded by Phazon-charged barriers that only beams of the opposite polarity or the Annihilator Beam could break. Its death and the loss of the last planetary energy unmade Dark Aether, and the entire Ing Horde vanished with it. The Ing are extinct.

The Space Pirates, who called them shadowlings, suffered raid after raid and steady attrition at their hands. To Samus they were the force that killed Squad Bravo and nearly killed her in her first minutes on Dark Aether; U-Mos warned her that had Aether fallen, the Ing would have turned to the stars for new worlds to ravage.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ing · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Emperor_Ing · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Warrior_Ing · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hunter_Ing · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Darkling · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Pirate_Commando</sub>


### Prime 2: The Ing Guardians and Samus's stolen equipment

*Keys:* Bomb Guardian, Jump Guardian, Boost Guardian, Grapple Guardian, Spider Guardian, Power Bomb Guardian, Ing Guardian, Ing Guardians, stolen equipment, Judgment Pit, Dark Torvus Arena, Alpha Sandigger


Minutes into her first crossing to Dark Aether, Samus Aran was ambushed in the Sky Temple Grounds. Dark Samus shattered the Light Crystal protecting her and a swarm of Warrior Ing tore away most of her equipment before she was thrown back through the portal to Aether. The Ing that carried off her abilities absorbed them, and several possessed Aether's creatures to wield the stolen powers. Samus hunted these Guardians one by one across both worlds.

The Bomb Guardian was the Ing that took her Morph Ball Bombs; it possessed the Alpha Sandigger, dominant male of its species, in the Agon Temple. Its mutated second head dropped and sprayed bombs, and head and tail were its only vulnerable spots: a stunning hit to the tail left the head open. The Jump Guardian, a Warrior Ing holding the Space Jump Boots, lay hidden in the Judgment Pit of the Dark Agon Wastes until she stepped into the Safe Zone, then leapt with shockwaves behind a barrier that fell only when it landed. The Boost Guardian, a Warrior Ing in the Dark Torvus Arena, a chamber with no Light Crystal or Beacon at all, hurled itself at her as a boosting ball and melted into a puddle that chased her across the floor. The Grapple Guardian, a Darkling Grenchler in the Sacrificial Chamber of Dark Torvus Bog, swung the Grapple Beam in place of its lightning; its eye and the flesh under its back shell were its weak spots, and a wound to the eye enraged it. The Spider Guardian, a Darkling Pillbug in the Dynamo Works of Sanctuary Fortress, raced along magnetic tracks; immune to her weapons, it died only when she drove it into the works' electrical nodes. The Power Bomb Guardian, a Darkling Sporb in the Undertemple of Dark Torvus Bog, fired Power Bombs instead of venomous needles and was all but invulnerable; Samus climbed the walls to four Bomb Slots and brought part of the ceiling down on it.

The Luminoth also kept the gear of their champion A-Kul for whoever came after her: her Seeker Missile launcher in a sacred vault, the device that let her move through liquid as if through air, which the Ing had taken, and the visor that pierced Dark Aether's eternal night. Samus retrieved them all. By the time she faced the Emperor Ing she carried every ability she had lost plus the Luminoth's Dark Beam, Light Beam, Annihilator Beam, Dark Suit, Light Suit, Dark Visor and Echo Visor, and a Screw Attack recovered from a Luminoth vault in Sanctuary Fortress. Samus's scans record the Guardians coldly as metamorphs that absorbed the power of each device; she took back what was hers and moved on.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Warrior_Ing · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bomb_Guardian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Jump_Guardian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Boost_Guardian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Grapple_Guardian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Spider_Guardian</sub>


### Prime 2: The Luminoth

*Keys:* Luminoth, Luminoths, Keybearer, Keybearers, A-Kul, Luminoth Lore, X-Qar, E-Btr, Sentinel of Aether, Sentinels of Aether, moth people, Hall of Honored Dead


The Luminoth are a tall, winged, moth-like people who settled the rogue planet Aether. Their lore says they were born not of Aether but of the stars: nomads who roamed for generations and, after meeting other enlightened races such as the N'kren, the Ylla and the Chozo, resolved to bind themselves to a homeworld as those peoples had. The scout X-Qar found Aether and became the first Sentinel of Aether. Q-Cis, the first Sentinel born on Aether, protected it for three centicycles; V-Mos followed, then J-Gnk, who died in battle defending the Great Temple, and then U-Mos.

An adult Luminoth stands over twice the height of a human, with slender limbs, three long digits and working wings. They fly unaided, have heightened reflexes and durability, and shape energy into shields and telekinetic barriers. A constant spiritual link joins them; A-Kul could feel where her dead comrades lay. They write in a three-dimensional holographic script keyed by color (violet, amber, emerald, cobalt) that Samus could read only after U-Mos and then each Temple Sentinel updated her Translator Module. Families are reckoned as houses (the mated Keybearers J-Stl and B-Stl share a house name), they honor their dead with statues in the Hall of Honored Dead, venerate the Light of Aether, and part with blessings such as "May the Light of Aether shine upon you." Samus's logbook rates their command of energy as the equal of the Chozo's, and their works bear it out: the Energy Controllers, Light Crystals and Light Beacons, the Dark, Light and Annihilator Beams, the Dark and Light Suits, and the mechanoids of Sanctuary Fortress such as the Quads, Ingsmashers and Quadraxis.

In the Golden Age they founded Sanctuary in the mountains under E-Btr, then Torvus and Agon, and raised the Great Temple between the three. The Leviathan impact began their Dark Age. Rifts from Dark Aether released swarms that possessed Aether's creatures, and the Luminoth named the enemy Ing, "terror". They learned that light burned the Ing and that the Ing could possess Luminoth too, so they built shielding and vowed to self-terminate rather than become Ing weapons. Ten Keybearers (A-Kul, J-Stl, B-Stl, S-Dly, G-Sch, S-Jrs, C-Rch, D-Isl, M-Dhe and J-Fme) carried the Energy Transfer Module against the Sky Temple; only A-Kul returned her key before dying. With the captured Module the Ing drained Agon, Torvus and Sanctuary, killing the Sentinels I-Sha, A-Voq and O-Lir and heroes such as the child acolyte D-Chr and C-Pul the Ingslayer, who felled over seven hundred Ing before dying in the Battle of the Hive. Other heroes include K-Tch, a farmer of Agon turned warrior, and B-Stz, who slew the Ing Horde's war chief in the Fourth Battle of Torvus and later died of old age in the temple he had defended. The elderly and the children took up arms; an aged Luminoth and an adolescent lie dead beneath the statues of X-Qar and V-Mos. At last the survivors entered life-preserving stasis in the Great Temple, to wake when the Ing were gone or to sleep forever, while U-Mos alone kept watch.

Samus Aran ended the war. The Luminoth woke, bowed to her as one, and she left them to rebuild in peace; they were last reported healing Aether under Federation supervision.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Luminoth · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Luminoth_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ing-Luminoth_War · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Keybearer · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hall_of_Honored_Dead · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Translator_Module</sub>


### Prime 2: U-Mos

*Keys:* U-Mos, U Mos, Umos, Sentinel of the Luminoth, V-Mos, J-Gnk, last Sentinel, Luminoth Sentinel


U-Mos is the fifth and last Sentinel of Aether, the one Luminoth who stayed awake through his people's long defeat. His mother V-Mos, the third Sentinel, died giving birth to him; he inherited the post when the fourth Sentinel, J-Gnk, died in battle defending the Great Temple. When the Ing had taken Agon, Torvus and Sanctuary and only the Great Temple's Energy Controller remained, the surviving Luminoth entered life-preserving stasis there; U-Mos remained on guard over the sleepers and the last of the Light of Aether, bracing for the Ing's last, terrible assault and praying for deliverance.

Deliverance came as Samus Aran. After she destroyed the Ing-possessed Alpha Splinter in the Temple Sanctuary and the Energy Transfer Module bonded to her suit, U-Mos appeared with the words "Do not be afraid, I am U-Mos, Sentinel of the Luminoth." He explained the stellar impact that created Dark Aether and the Ing's theft of the planetary energy: "Should one world gain control of this energy, the other will perish." He warned that "Should we fall, the Ing will look to the stars for new planets to ravage and conquer," asked her to recover the energy from Agon, then Torvus, then Sanctuary Fortress, and told her plainly, "You're our only hope, Samus." He called the Ing "creatures of shadow and darkness, knowing nothing of peace or mercy." He updated her Translator Module so that she could read Violet holograms and added the next temple's location to her map.

U-Mos guided Samus from the Great Temple throughout the mission, and she returned to him repeatedly for counsel. When the three temples were restored he gave her the Light Suit, armor blessed and energized by the Light of Aether itself, and sent her after the nine missing Sky Temple Keys. After the Emperor Ing died and Dark Aether collapsed, U-Mos woke his people; he and the other Luminoth bowed to Samus in farewell as she departed, with his blessing: "May the Light of Aether shine upon you!"

Like all Luminoth he is winged, psionic and able to shape energy on a par with the Chozo; Samus's logbook dates him at about 2.15 centicycles and records him as the only active member of his species she met on Aether. He is calm and formal in speech, addresses Samus by name, and treats her as an equal and a savior rather than a hired weapon.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/U-Mos · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ing-Luminoth_War · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Great_Temple · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Luminoth · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hall_of_Honored_Dead · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Translator_Module</sub>


## Prime 3


### Prime 3: Bryyo and the Reptilicus

*Keys:* Bryyo, Reptilicus, Bryyonian, Bryyonians, Lords of Science, Primals, Mogenar, Fuel Gel, Sfimas, Theseus, war golem, war golems


Bryyo is a planet of the Kalandor Region in the Federation Solar System, neighbor to Norion and Elysia. Its orbit leaves 48 percent of the surface in permanent sunlight and 48 percent in permanent night; only a 4 percent band of equatorial jungle is habitable. It is the largest known natural source of Fuel Gel, the fuel of Federation capital ships, and the proceeds of Bryyonian gel sales fund the military forces guarding the region; Federation scouts found the planet only ten years before the Phazon crisis. Its natives are now savage, but ruins across the planet testify to a fallen civilization. The scorched half, Bryyo Fire, holds the Temple of Bryyo above a lake of Fuel Gel; the frozen half, Bryyo Ice, is reached through a warp terminal; the Cliffside, the Thorn Jungle and the Hall of the Golems lie among the ruins.

The Bryyonians once lived in a golden age of peace. Fuel Gel carried them into space, where they met the Chozo, the Luminoth and the Ylla, and the old Primal Traditions faded. The Chozo warned them to balance old ways and new and not to adopt technology too fast; they were not heeded. When Sfimas, First Lord of Science, publicly denounced the Primals, the Age of Schism became the Age of War, "flame to a dry forest." The Primals turned to ancient dark powers; the land was poisoned and survivors huddled in a shrinking habitable zone. The Primals won, but Bryyo was dying. The exiled Science Lords, regretting their rejection of the Chozo, launched purification machines that saved the planet at the cost of splitting its climate forever into a scorched side and an icy side. Primal war bands hunted the surviving Lords; the last of them fused science with magic, raised stone war golems of the Mogenar class to guard himself, and worked with Fuel Gel to restore the land while the Primals sank into savagery. He sheltered a young Primal prophetess who foresaw a starborne plague, began preparing defenses, and when a Fuel Gel accident revealed his refuge he stayed behind so she could flee with their records: "Save our world from the starborne plague."

The Reptilicus are the Primals' descendants: tall six-limbed reptiles with four arms, armed with whips and chakrams, who tame Warp Hounds, build their own prisons and teleport short distances to corner prey. Higher thought has given way to savage bloodlust, and Phazon corruption hardened them against heat and Fuel Gel. Their strongest are the Reptilicus Hunters. All of them attacked Samus on sight.

Dark Samus's Leviathan struck Bryyo during the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption); Rundas was sent to halt the corruption and was consumed by it. Samus found the downed Griffin-class frigate G.F.S. Theseus at the Crash Site, restored its power and downloaded a map of the planet. At the Temple of Bryyo, where the Pirates were tapping the Fuel Gel lake, she killed Rundas. She destroyed the generators shielding the fallen Seed and in its core faced Mogenar, an ancient Reptilicus war golem reanimated with Phazon that drew power from the Seed through socketed orbs of pure Phazon; the other Mogenar-class golems on Bryyo remain inert or docile. Overloading the Seed's core ended the corruption but left Samus's own corruption deeper than before.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bryyo · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bryyo_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Reptilicus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mogenar · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Temple_of_Bryyo · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Theseus</sub>


### Prime 3: Elysia and SkyTown

*Keys:* Elysia, SkyTown, Sky Town, Aurora Unit 217, Helios, Theronian Bomb, Theronian, Spire Dock, Steamlord, Steamlords, Xenoresearch


Elysia is a cloud-shrouded planet of the Kalandor Region, neighbor to Norion and Bryyo, uninhabitable by most organisms; its atmosphere is too dangerous for human researchers. Its dominant inhabitants are the Elysians, mechanoids built by the Chozo. SkyTown is the research facility that floats above its clouds: a network of individual Research Pods, each held aloft by rocket-like engines beneath it and joined by skybridges and ziplines, which the Chozo began building about 1,500 years before the Phazon crisis to study the cosmos. Its sensors range from the local to the interstellar and supply the Galactic Federation with military intelligence. After the Treaty of Elysia the Federation withdrew its people and installed Aurora Unit 217, originally built for GF Naval Base Demeter, in a floating pod linked to the station's network, giving it control of the facility and of the Elysians, who interfaced well with it. 217 served as SkyTown's administrator, delivering tactical intelligence and stellar research; among its studies was the meteor that struck the Pirate Homeworld, a trail that led toward Phaaze.

During the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption) 217 was disabled by the Pirate-made Aurora virus, and about a week later Dark Samus's Leviathan tore through the clouds to Elysia's distant core. Most of SkyTown was destroyed; less than half remained when Samus arrived five months after the impact. Space Pirates seized the eastern district, raised a shield around the Seed, stationed Phazon Metroids in the Xenoresearch Laboratories, and watched as a Phazon virus corrupted the Elysians. Ghor, sent to cure 217 two weeks after the impact, was corrupted himself.

The Seed's shield could not be broken by any weapon, so Samus first carried the vaccine to 217 in the Aurora Chamber. Ghor cut 217's lines and wrecked her Gunship at the landing site; she killed him and repaired the connection. 217 then directed her to the three components of a Theronian Bomb scattered through SkyTown, including the Seeker Missile held in the Xenoresearch Laboratories. From the Spire Dock she dropped the assembled bomb with her ship's grapple, shattering the shield. In the Seed she destroyed Helios, a corrupted Elysian machine with four tentacle-like arms that led a swarm of Phazon-energized Swarmbots, and overloaded the core, ridding SkyTown of Phazon. Corrupted Steamlords, Chozo-built repairers of the Steambots and Tinbots that cloak themselves and emit visor-jamming pulses, still haunted the pods. 217 guided Samus for the rest of the crisis.

When the crisis ended, Samus returned to SkyTown to mourn Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda before flying on.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Elysia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_217 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Helios · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Steamlord</sub>


### Prime 3: Elysians

*Keys:* Elysian, Elysians, Elysian mechanoid, Elysian mechanoids, Treaty of Elysia, First Elysian, Chozo Searcher, SkyTown Data, Elysian lore, Data Pods


The Elysians are robotic sentients built by the Chozo on Elysia, custodians of SkyTown and keepers of their own history, which they recorded in Data Pods that Samus read. About 1,500 years before the Phazon crisis the Chozo began building SkyTown; some fifty years in, strained by storms and upkeep, they created the First Elysian, gave it self-awareness, and let it share that gift with the others. The Elysians count this as Year One of their race. Chozo Observation Satellites launched from the station studied distant worlds, and a Chozo Searcher found a far-off planet that was itself sentient, but the data was lost before it could be identified. The Elysians later understood it to be Phaaze.

In Year 400 the Chozo left for Tallon IV and entrusted SkyTown to the Elysians, asking them to keep searching for the sentient planet's secrets; the Elysians' own words are "We failed." By Year 600 supplies and fuel were critically low and they entered hibernation, sleeping 835 years while station data reached them only as dreams; the Leviathan's collision with Tallon IV registered on them as dream-like data. In Year 1435 armed aliens triggered SkyTown's defenses and the Elysians awoke. The newcomers claimed to come in peace, and negotiation produced the Treaty of Elysia with the Galactic Federation, which supplied fuel and parts in exchange for the station's scientific systems and databanks. Because Elysia's atmosphere was lethal to human researchers, the Federation proposed a single artificial intelligence in their place: Aurora Unit 217 was installed in a floating pod and given control of the facility and the Elysians, who describe the research that followed as a peaceful life.

Five months before Samus's arrival the Elysians detected a meteor-like object strike a distant world and traced it through a wormhole to the planet the Chozo Searcher had sought, now attacking other worlds. Their attempt to warn the Federation failed when 217 was crippled by an unknown virus. A week later a Leviathan struck Elysia; their sensors identified Phazon, the toxin that had already corrupted other worlds, a Chozo world among them. Space Pirates overran SkyTown and built a shield around the Seed, and within two weeks a Phazon virus consumed most Elysians and bound them to Pirate control. Hunter Ghor, sent to restore the Aurora, was corrupted and led the assault on the survivors. "There is no hope left for us." The last Elysians waited "before the brink of history's end" while one transferred their memories to a data pod.

SkyTown's mechanoids, including the Steamlords, Steambots, Tinbots and the Seed's guardian Helios, are Elysian in origin, and Samus fought their corrupted forms (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Raised by the Chozo herself, she read the records of this other Chozo creation, and after the crisis she chose SkyTown as the place to mourn her fallen comrades.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Elysian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Steamlord · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Helios · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Elysia</sub>


### Prime 3: Gandrayda

*Keys:* Gandrayda, Sammy, shapeshifter, shape-shifter, shapeshifting hunter, NZG41, Jovia XII, Proving Grounds


Gandrayda was a shape-shifting humanoid bounty hunter of unknown homeworld and species, hired by the Galactic Federation with Samus, Rundas and Ghor during the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Federation records likened her to the bioforms of Jovia XII: she could assume the form and abilities of most living things, including creatures far larger than herself, and each borrowed form carried that form's weaknesses. Her age could not be determined, though she seemed young. A mistress of stealth, she used a personal cloaking field to set up her attacks. She treated bounty hunting as a sport or hobby, and the Federation noted that she regarded Samus Aran as the chief rival she meant to surpass. She teased Samus with the nickname "Sammy."

On Norion she hid among the Space Pirates guarding one of the generators in the shape of a Pirate, cut them down from within and left the generator for Samus to restart. Dark Samus's Phazon burst at the Control Tower left her, like the others, generating her own Phazon; the Federation built her a PED that she wore around her neck and sent her to locate the Pirate stronghold. She found the Pirate Homeworld, was corrupted at its Research Facility and became a major Space Pirate officer under Dark Samus.

When Samus reached the Homeworld, Gandrayda contacted her disguised as a Federation Marine with the registration NZG41, claiming he had been captured in the Norion raid and offering a back way into the Craneyard. In the Proving Grounds of the Pirate Research sector she dropped the disguise: "You know, you should never trust strangers, Sammy. This is going to be fun." She tore open the roof to let in the acid rain, turned invisible, and cycled through Phazon-charged shapes of a Berserker Knight, an Aerotrooper, a Swarmbot, Rundas, Ghor and finally Samus herself in her Varia Suit, before entering Hypermode. Samus defeated her. Dying, Gandrayda shifted uncontrollably between the forms of a Pirate, Ghor, Rundas and Samus until Dark Samus assimilated her.

Gandrayda was the last of Samus's three comrades to die. Samus took her Grapple Voltage and later mourned her with the others at SkyTown.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gandrayda · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Homeworld · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption</sub>


### Prime 3: GFS Olympus and the Federation fleet at Norion

*Keys:* Olympus, GFS Olympus, G.F.S. Olympus, Olympus-class, Valhalla, GFS Valhalla, G.F.S. Valhalla, Aurora Unit 242, AU 242, Klar Nebula, Energy Cells, Energy Cell


The G.F.S. Olympus is the Olympus-class battleship that served as the Galactic Federation flagship during the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption), commanded by Fleet Admiral Castor Dane. Olympus-class ships were the first Federation capital ships designed around an onboard Aurora Unit, an organic supercomputer that lets them run with smaller crews and devote the saved space to battle systems; they are powered by scarce Fuel Gel, which makes each one of extreme value to the military, and they are usually led by an officer of flag rank. The Olympus carries Aurora Unit 242, the first Aurora Unit to receive the vaccine against the Pirate-made virus. Afterward 242 directed Samus throughout the crisis, appearing as a hologram aboard her Gunship with each new objective, and it was 242 that identified Phaaze as the source of all Phazon and Aurora Unit 313 as Dark Samus's instrument.

Samus was summoned to the Olympus in orbit of Norion, where Dane briefed her, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda on the virus crippling the Aurora network, then seven days old, and ordered them to deliver the vaccine. Space Pirate boarding pods and Troopers stormed the ship during the briefing, and a Berserker Lord delayed Samus's launch for Norion. After Dark Samus's attack she lay in a coma aboard the Olympus for a month and woke there fitted with a PED Suit. The ship later carried the fleet to the Pirate Homeworld, where its assault broke the Pirate forces once Samus had dropped the planetary shield, and it followed the captured Leviathan Battleship through the wormhole to Phaaze, where the fleet fought the Pirates in orbit and afterward celebrated the end of Phazon corruption.

Its sister ship, the G.F.S. Valhalla, had vanished four months before the briefing. After taking on new crew at Tivus, the Valhalla was on a training mission in the Klar Nebula when it was ambushed by an outnumbering Pirate fleet of Phazon-powered starships and boarded from a Space Pirate boarding pod; the Federation presumed it destroyed in action. The crew died and Aurora Unit 313 was removed, to be carried by Dark Samus to Phaaze. Samus found the drifting wreck in the Gaflar region: Marines impaled on Pirate blades, killed by grenades, crushed by elevators or half-eaten by Phazon bioforms, some crumbling to dust when she opened the doors. Its systems needed ten Energy Cells to restore fully and six to reach its data; in the Control Room she found 313's final warning about Phaaze that ended "...Darkness coming," and the codes the fleet needed to open the wormhole.

Other Federation vessels of the crisis include the Griffin-class frigate G.F.S. Theseus, downed on Bryyo, and the G.F.S. Anhur, whose crew recovered Phazon ore on Aether after the Aether incident, the ore that led to the Phazon Enhancement Device.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Olympus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_242 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Theseus</sub>


### Prime 3: Ghor

*Keys:* Ghor, Wotan VII, armorsuit, cyborg hunter, cybernetic hunter, Hunter Ghor


Ghor was a cybernetic bounty hunter hired by the Galactic Federation with Samus, Rundas and Gandrayda during the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). A veteran of the liberation war of Wotan VII, he lost about 94 percent of his original body in a battle to protect that world; only 6 percent of his birth-body remained, the rest cyberware. Federation records described him as gentle, logical and compassionate, not the most skilled of fighters, with a sense of humor. He championed the weak, the poor and the downtrodden, often working for free or giving his bounty money to his targets' victims. He had a high proficiency with mechanical systems and computer infiltration and an unusual mechanical empathy with artificial intelligences.

Ghor could merge his body into larger machines. He owned a giant robotic armorsuit built for his cyborg body that doubled as his gunship and carried plasma beams, attack claws and a multimissile system he could fire together as an alpha strike; an energy shield repelled fire from the front, leaving only his back-mounted generator exposed. Merged with it, his calm demeanor gave way to incredible aggression and violence.

On Norion he cleared the way to Generator C by destroying a Berserker Knight, and he was riding the elevator with the other hunters when Dark Samus struck them with Phazon. Fitted with a PED Suit, he was sent to Elysia to inject the vaccine into Aurora Unit 217 and destroy the Leviathan. He arrived two weeks after the impact, misused the device and was corrupted; the last Elysians recorded him leading the hunt for their survivors. He severed the lines linking 217 to the Federation network, then met Samus at SkyTown's landing site, seized her Gunship, damaged it and hurled it at her. "Don't you feel the power? Soon everything will be corrupted. Including you." Samus broke his generator, sent him skidding on frozen Fuel Gel, and beat him down even after he entered Hypermode. Coming to his senses, he tried to convert the ruined armorsuit into a gunship; it exploded, leaving him hanging helpless in the air, and Dark Samus drained the Phazon from him until his remains decayed and vanished. Samus took his Plasma Beam.

Ghor was the second of Samus's comrades to die. She grieved him with the others at SkyTown when the crisis ended.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ghor · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_217</sub>


### Prime 3: Norion

*Keys:* Norion, Planet Norion, Norion base, battle of Norion, Norion attack, Generator C, Aurora Unit 486, Kalandor Region, Kalandor


Norion is a planet on the fringe of Galactic Federation space in the Kalandor Region, neighbor to Bryyo and Elysia and dominated by humans. Once a barren world unable to support life, it was transformed into a hardy forest world by a terraforming project designed by Aurora Unit 486. The Federation regards it as strategically vital and keeps a heavy military presence there; its base is often the first line of defense against enemies operating outside Federation territory. The base is protected by a planetary defense cannon fed by three generators, A, B and C, and aimed from a Control Tower whose Jovian-steel door opens only when all three are powered. Federation Marines in the Norion system first tested the Phazon Enhancement Device, siphoning Phazon from backpack supplies to boost their armor.

During the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption) a Space Pirate force led by Meta Ridley struck Norion while the G.F.S. Olympus lay in orbit, shutting down the generators as Dark Samus's Leviathan bore down on the planet. Samus, delayed aboard the Olympus by a Berserker Lord, reached the surface after Rundas destroyed the Pirate ATC gunships hunting her. She restored Generator A alone; Gandrayda, disguised as a Pirate, cleared one generator from within; Ghor destroyed a Berserker Knight to open the way to Generator C, which Rundas then activated. Meta Ridley ambushed Samus there and the two plunged down the Generator C shaft fighting; she drove him off and Rundas caught her on a sheet of ice. At the Control Tower Dark Samus appeared and blasted the four hunters with Phazon. Samus alone stayed conscious long enough to fire the cannon, which destroyed the Leviathan moments before impact, and then she collapsed. She lay in a coma for a month aboard the Olympus.

Norion is the only recorded case of a Leviathan stopped before it reached a planet. The Phazon that infused the hunters there changed all four: Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda were corrupted and died, and Samus carried self-generating Phazon in her body until Phaaze was destroyed. Gandrayda later exploited the raid by posing as a Marine captured on Norion to lure Samus into an ambush on the Pirate Homeworld. For Samus, Norion is where she last fought beside the three hunters as allies.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Norion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Rundas · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ghor · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran</sub>


### Prime 3: Rundas

*Keys:* Rundas, Phrygisian, Phrygisians, Phrygis, Bes III, ice hunter


Rundas was a Phrygisian bounty hunter from Phrygis, a moon of Bes III known chiefly for ice mining. Like all Phrygisians he generated and shaped ice at will: he glided through the air on sheets of ice formed beneath his feet, hurled great chunks of ice as projectiles, froze objects and missiles in flight, and raised ice floes and stalagmites to trap enemies, making him deadly at any range. Galactic Federation records called him proud, cocky and arrogant, a hunter who considered himself without rivals in his field and kept a trophy from every target he captured or killed. His arrogance drove him to solo work and he rarely cooperated well, though he was quiet and dutiful when working alone and looked after his allies' safety.

Hired by the Federation alongside Samus, Ghor and Gandrayda to deliver the vaccine for the Aurora Unit virus (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption), Rundas was the hunter Samus owed most on Norion. He destroyed the Pirate ATC gunships closing on her, activated Generator C on his own initiative, and after she plunged down the Generator C shaft fighting Meta Ridley he caught her on a slide of ice. "Between the two of us, this should be a breeze." At the Control Tower, Dark Samus's Phazon burst felled him with the others.

Fitted with a PED Suit, Rundas was sent to Bryyo to investigate the Leviathan and halt its corruption of the planet. He overused the device and sickened; Samus tracked him across Bryyo by scanning his ice, which showed rising concentrations of Phazon. At the Temple of Bryyo in Bryyo Fire he froze a wave of Pirates that was overwhelming her, then sealed her exit with a ripple of ice and attacked. Phazon had thickened his armor, leaving only his neck exposed under heavy stress. Samus defeated him, and as he fell he was impaled on one of his own ice formations. A shadow of Dark Samus drew the remaining Phazon from his body, which crumbled to nothing, and the shattering ice left behind the Ice Missiles Samus carried afterward.

Rundas was the first of Samus's fellow hunters to die and the first she was forced to kill. He is one of the three comrades she mourned at SkyTown when the crisis ended.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Rundas · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phrygisian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Temple_of_Bryyo · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption</sub>


### Prime 3: The assault on Phaaze and the end of Phazon

*Keys:* assault on Phaaze, Aurora Unit 313, AU 313, end of Phazon, destruction of Phaaze, Phaaze's destruction, Leviathan Battleship, Phaaze wormhole, Sanctum of Phaaze, permanent Hypermode, Anti-Phazon


The Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption) ended with a Galactic Federation assault on Phaaze, the living source of all Phazon. After the Seeds on Bryyo, Elysia and the Pirate Homeworld were destroyed, Aurora Unit 242 revealed that Phaaze was the origin of the Leviathans and that Dark Samus was using the stolen Aurora Unit 313, implanted on Phaaze, to spread Phazon and Leviathans across the galaxy. Phaaze lay beyond the reach of conventional travel. The Space Pirates had armed a fifth Leviathan orbiting their Homeworld as a battleship; Samus disabled the planetary defense shield, the Federation fleet and its Demolition Troopers broke the Pirate forces, and she reached the transporter that put the Leviathan Battleship under Federation control. Fleet Admiral Castor Dane learned that the codes to open a wormhole were aboard the lost G.F.S. Valhalla; Samus powered the wreck with Energy Cells, heard Aurora Unit 313's last recording, which ended "...Darkness coming," and recovered them. The Battleship tore open a wormhole and the G.F.S. Olympus led the fleet through it to fight the Pirates above Phaaze.

The moment Samus landed, Phaaze's Phazon-saturated atmosphere overwhelmed her PED Suit. To survive she vented every trace of Phazon from her suit, which left her locked in permanent Hypermode; Phazon then seeped back into her energy reserves, and firing her weapons no longer bled it off. Her own Gunship ceased to recognize her. Had the Phazon filled her completely she would have died, and she held on only by purging it into Anti-Phazon units and corrupted creatures along the way. She passed an infant Leviathan and the discarded husks of Metroid Prime and descended to the Sanctum at the planet's core, where Phazon is at its purest.

There Dark Samus attacked her, generating echoes of herself in battle. Beaten, Dark Samus merged with Aurora Unit 313, which Phazon had mutated into a tentacled organism fused with her bioessence and symbiotically bound to the planet. Samus tore open its armored hatch and destroyed the core. Because unit and planet were one, its death set off a chain reaction: Dark Samus was annihilated with no sign of survival, and Phaaze's core detonated, destroying the planet as Samus fled. The fleet lost contact with her and feared the worst until her Gunship emerged and she reported the mission complete. With Phaaze gone the Phazon threat was ended; the Federation celebrated that Phazon corruption was truly gone, and the corruption that had nearly consumed Samus ended with its source.

The victory cost many Federation troopers and ships and the lives of Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda, and left Samus in evident distress. She returned to SkyTown on Elysia to mourn the three hunters, then departed alone. As she entered hyperspace an unidentified ship (Sylux's) followed her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Homeworld · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran</sub>


### Prime 3: The Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption events)

*Keys:* Phazon crisis, Metroid Prime 3, Prime 3, Metroid Prime 3: Corruption, Corruption incident, Aurora virus, Phazon virus, Aurora Unit virus, the four hunters, fellow hunters, Colossus


The Phazon crisis erupted about six months after the Aether incident (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes). The Space Pirate battleship Colossus had carried off the last Phazon from Aether, and inside its Phazon stores Dark Samus re-formed herself. She killed a third of the crew, enthralled the rest, flew alone to Phaaze, the living source of all Phazon, and returned riding a Leviathan; the Pirates followed her through a wormhole to Phaaze, and those who refused were shot. Her forces ambushed the G.F.S. Valhalla and stole its Aurora Unit 313, which she implanted on Phaaze to direct its Leviathan Seeds. Seeds were launched at Bryyo, Elysia, Norion and the Pirate Homeworld, and a Pirate-made Phazon virus crippled the Galactic Federation's Aurora Unit network.

The Federation hired four bounty hunters: Samus Aran, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda. Fleet Admiral Castor Dane briefed them aboard the G.F.S. Olympus, ordering them to carry a vaccine to the infected Aurora Units; Pirate boarding pods struck the ship mid-briefing. At Norion the hunters restored the base's three generators and Samus fired the defense cannon that shattered the incoming Leviathan, but Dark Samus blasted all four with Phazon at the Control Tower. Samus woke a month later aboard the Olympus: the blast had left the hunters' bodies generating their own Phazon, and the Federation had fitted each with a PED Suit to harness it. Rundas had been sent to Bryyo, Ghor to Elysia and Gandrayda to find the Pirate stronghold, and contact with all three had been lost.

Samus followed. On Bryyo she destroyed the Seed's shield, killed the corrupted Rundas at the Temple of Bryyo and the war golem Mogenar in the Seed's core. On Elysia she cured Aurora Unit 217 in SkyTown, killed the corrupted Ghor, built a Theronian Bomb to crack the Seed's shield and destroyed its guardian Helios.
