On the Pirate Homeworld she killed Gandrayda, who had become a Pirate officer, disabled the planetary shield for a Federation assault and destroyed the Seed guarded by Omega Ridley. Each Seed she overloaded deepened her own corruption, and Dark Samus drained the Phazon from each fallen hunter.

Aurora Unit 242 identified Phaaze as the source and Aurora Unit 313 as Dark Samus's instrument. Samus seized the Pirates' Leviathan Battleship, recovered wormhole codes from the wreck of the Valhalla, and led the fleet to Phaaze. In its Sanctum she defeated Dark Samus, who merged with Aurora Unit 313; destroying the unit triggered a chain reaction that annihilated Dark Samus and Phaaze itself. The Federation celebrated the end of Phazon corruption. The crisis cost many Federation troopers and ships and the lives of the three hunters. Samus returned to SkyTown to mourn them, then flew on alone; an unidentified ship (Sylux's) followed her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313</sub>


## Dark Samus


### Dark Samus: Aether and the Dark Hunter

*Keys:* Aether, Dark Aether, Agon Wastes, Sanctuary Fortress, Sky Temple Gateway, Torvus Bog, Main Reactor, Aerie, Luminoth, Warrior Ing  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Dark Samus, Dark Hunter, doppelganger, doppelgänger, dark twin, dark copy, Phazon, disintegrated


Dark Samus reached Aether some time after the Tallon IV incident; how she got there is not recorded. She went straight for the Phazon. The Space Pirates mining the Agon Wastes first believed Samus Aran had come for them, then realized the intruder wanted their Phazon ore, not their deaths: she killed only those who tried to stop her. Troopers named her "the Dark Hunter" for her dark armor and "strange new weapons." She teleported into their base at will and wrecked its stealth field generator, leaving the facility exposed. When the real Samus landed, a Pirate scout watched the Dark Hunter attack her near a Phazon site, and Pirate command proposed an alliance with the Dark Hunter: Phazon in exchange for Samus's head.

Samus first met her in the Splinter hive near the Federation Marines' compound in the Temple Grounds. After a brief skirmish Dark Samus fled through a portal into Dark Aether; Samus followed, found her absorbing Phazon, and watched her shatter a Light Crystal so that a swarm of Warrior Ing could strip Samus of most of her equipment and hurl her back through the portal. From then on Dark Samus shaped Samus's path across the Luminoth world: sealing the direct route through the Agon mining facility, destroying the bridge at the Sanctuary Entrance, killing three Dark Pirate Troopers to force a detour, and once, in a Torvus Bog training chamber, simply re-forming in front of Samus, laughing, and dissipating without a shot.

They fought four times (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes). In the Main Reactor of the Agon facility, where Dark Samus was feeding on Phazon, Samus broke her apart; she re-formed. In the Aerie of Sanctuary Fortress she added invisibility and ricocheting Boost attacks, and fell from the fortress edge when beaten; the scan read "Reborn, enhanced, and vengeful" and warned she could rebuild her body "short of total atomic disruption." The last fight came at the Sky Temple Gateway as Dark Aether collapsed after the Emperor Ing's death. Dark Samus barred the way with a wall of Phazon. She had gorged on Phazon past the point she could vent it, became a being of pure, unstable Phazon energy behind a shield nothing else could breach, and was undone by her own appetite: Samus drew the Phazon she expelled into her Charge Beam and fired it back until Dark Samus disintegrated, then escaped through the portal moments before Dark Aether ceased to exist.

She was not finished. A cloud of Phazon above Aether gathered itself into her shape, could not hold it, and drifted as raw Phazon until Space Pirates salvaging the Phazon left behind on Aether took it aboard the battleship Colossus. See also the Aether incident, Aether and Dark Aether, the Ing and the Emperor Ing, Space Pirate projects and Phazon research.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_1 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_2 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_3 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_4 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Log</sub>


### Dark Samus: Dark Samus (overview)

*Keys:* Dark Samus, Dark-Samus, Dark Hunter, the Dark Hunter, doppelganger, doppelgänger, dark copy, dark twin, evil Samus, dark version of Samus, Samus's double, Phazon clone


Dark Samus is a Phazon-born double of Samus Aran and the architect of the Phazon crisis. She came into being on Tallon IV when the dying Metroid Prime tore the Phazon Suit from Samus in the Impact Crater and rebuilt itself from that suit, Samus's genetic material and its own Phazon essence. Scans record her as an entity of pure, highly unstable Phazon energy wearing a Varia Suit "altered and augmented by the Phazon within her": Samus's exact silhouette, dark in color, lit by the Phazon inside her. Her home planet is listed as Phaaze. The Space Pirates on Aether, who mistook her for Samus until the real one landed, named her "the Dark Hunter."

Her history falls into three phases.
- On Aether (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes) she raided the Pirates' Phazon stocks in the Agon Wastes, dogged Samus across the Luminoth world and fought her four times. In the last clash at the Sky Temple Gateway, as Dark Aether collapsed, she overcharged herself into a being of raw Phazon; Samus fired her own expelled Phazon back into her and she disintegrated, only to re-form above Aether and be carried off as raw Phazon by salvaging Pirates.
- Revived aboard the Pirate battleship Colossus, she killed a third of its crew and bent the rest into disciples who called her "the great one." She found Phaaze, seized Aurora Unit 313 from the G.F.S. Valhalla, implanted it in Phaaze's core and used it to launch Leviathan Seeds at the Pirate Homeworld, Bryyo, Elysia and Norion.
- She led the Norion attack in person (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption), blasting Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda with Phazon. The other three hunters succumbed to corruption and died by Samus's hand; Samus herself endured months of creeping corruption. In the Sanctum at Phaaze's core Samus defeated her; Dark Samus merged into Aurora Unit 313, and when Samus destroyed the Unit, Phaaze detonated and Dark Samus was exterminated with no sign of survival.

She has no recorded speech. Her behavior on record is a laugh, a last gesture toward Samus before she dissolved, and relentless appetite: for Phazon first, for Samus second. Samus's Logbook moved from "Extreme caution recommended" to "the highest threat level"; her scans told her the thing wore her face and carried her DNA. The crisis cost Samus three comrades, whom she mourned at SkyTown on Elysia before leaving. See also Metroid Prime (the creature), Phazon, Phaaze, Leviathans, Aurora Units, Rundas, Ghor, Gandrayda.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Log · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption</sub>


### Dark Samus: Origin from Metroid Prime

*Keys:* Metroid Prime, Phazon Suit, Impact Crater, origin, origins, born, reborn, rebirth, where Dark Samus came from, Dark Samus origin, Dark Samus's origin  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Dark Samus, Dark Hunter, doppelganger, doppelgänger, Metroid Prime, Phazon Suit, Impact Crater, Tallon IV


Dark Samus was born in the Impact Crater of Tallon IV at the close of the Tallon IV incident (Metroid Prime). The creature called Metroid Prime had begun as a Metroid that absorbed the Phazon-rich core of the Leviathan buried in the crater. Chozo prophecy named it the Worm, "born from parasites, nurtured in a poisoned womb," and the Chozo raised the Artifact Temple over the crater to hold it. The Space Pirates later found and studied it, then lost control of it: it consumed raw Phazon, absorbed the weapons of the security units it killed and made them parts of its body. It could generate Phazon and burn it as fuel, change its physical form, turn invisible and spawn Metroids.

Samus entered the crater in the Phazon Suit, a viral corruption of her Power Suit that formed when the dying Omega Pirate collapsed onto her and Phazon seeped into her armor. That suit let her enter Hypermode and fire the Phazon Beam while standing in the crater's Phazon pools, and only a Phazon-fused Arm Cannon could harm Metroid Prime's exposed core essence. She destroyed the armored exoskeleton and then the core, but in its last moments the creature reached for her. Its grip on the Phazon in her armor was strong enough to strip the Phazon Suit from her body. Samus, left in her Gravity Suit and freed of her corruption, escaped the collapsing crater and left Metroid Prime to die.

It did not die. Combining its own Phazon essence with Samus's genetic material and the stolen suit, it formed a new body in her shape and emerged as Dark Samus; from that moment it is no longer called Metroid Prime. The Phazon that later spread into Federation space can be traced to this rebirth.

No Logbook record states the connection outright. On Aether Samus's scans reported only "the presence of Phazon and your genetic material within this entity," and that is all Samus herself ever learned by instrument: her double carried her DNA, wore a Phazon-warped Varia Suit, and had risen from the crater where she left the Worm for dead. See also Metroid Prime (the creature), Impact Crater, Phazon Suit and PED Suit, Tallon IV incident.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_(creature) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_1</sub>


### Dark Samus: Powers, nature and what Samus knows

*Keys:* powers, abilities, invisible, invisibility, regenerate, clones, Phazon shield, personality, sentient, hunger, obsessed, mirror  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Dark Samus, Dark Hunter, doppelganger, doppelgänger, dark twin, dark copy, evil Samus, her double


Dark Samus is a construct of Phazon shaped by two genomes, Samus Aran's and Metroid Prime's. Scans record her as "comprised of pure Phazon energy" and "highly unstable," wearing a version of the Varia Suit "altered and augmented by the Phazon within her." She is female, dark in color and Samus's equal in silhouette; the Pirates saw only darker armor and "strange new weapons."

Her arsenal mirrors Samus's and exceeds it:
- a scattershot Phazon beam that can be charged into a sustained stream, missile and Super Missile-like attacks, and a beam that encases targets in Phazon
- Boost and Jump abilities "superior to yours," a Boost Ball that lasts longer than Samus's, levitation and flight, and teleportation ("teleports into the base at will," per Pirate logs)
- a Phazon shield proof against everything except Phazon
- invisibility, first to the Combat and Scan Visors, then to the Dark Visor; only the Echo Visor could find her on Dark Aether
- regeneration by absorbing Phazon, and reconstitution of her whole body from a cloud of Phazon, "short of total atomic disruption"
- at Phaaze, echoes of herself that fight while she recharges, the power to draw energy from Phaaze itself, spinning attacks, ice pillars and shock waves
- a Phazon blast that seeds living bodies with Phazon, as it did to Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda on Norion

Her weaknesses are the reverse of her strengths. She can be hurt by Phazon she herself has expelled, by a Phazon-fused Arm Cannon and by Hypermode. Gorging on Phazon past what she can vent destabilizes her. She is Phazon, and when Phaaze died she died with it.

In nature she is hunger first: on Aether she sought the Pirates' ore, not their destruction. Vengeance second: her Aerie scan reads "Reborn, enhanced, and vengeful," and she laughed in Samus's face in Torvus before vanishing. Cunning grew with each rebirth. The thief who wrecked a stealth generator became a strategist who beguiled a ship's crew into a cult, baited a Federation training ship for its Aurora Unit and used a living planet as a siege engine. She has no recorded speech; her disciples' logs preserve only their worship of "the great one" and their fear of "the fiendish shrew." Her fixation on Samus is constant: she sought her out at every encounter, marked her with corruption, and her commanders swore to bring Samus's helmet.

Samus's own view survives only in action and scans. Her Logbook noted "your genetic material within this entity," advised "Extreme caution," and later rated Dark Samus "at the highest threat level." She exchanged no words with her double, lost three comrades to her, and on Phaaze came within a hair of total corruption, her Gunship no longer able to recognize her, before Phaaze's destruction purged her. No record states what she feels about the thing that wears her face; what she did was destroy it, on Aether and again on Phaaze. See also Phazon, Phazon corruption and Hypermode, Visors, Phazon Suit and PED Suit.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_1 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_2 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_3 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_4 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Log</sub>


### Dark Samus: The Phazon crisis and her end

*Keys:* Phaaze, Aurora Unit 313, AU 313, Sanctum, PED Suit, Hypermode, Rundas, Ghor, Gandrayda, purged, purge, final battle  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Dark Samus, Dark Hunter, doppelganger, doppelgänger, Phazon, corruption, Phaaze, Aurora Unit 313


The Phazon Dark Samus drove into the four hunters at Norion did not fade. It took root, and their bodies began producing Phazon of their own. The Galactic Federation answered with Phazon Enhancement Devices: Samus woke from a month-long coma aboard the G.F.S. Olympus wearing a PED Suit that let her enter Hypermode at will, and learned that Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda had been fitted with the same, sent after the Leviathan Seeds on Bryyo, Elysia and the Pirate Homeworld, and lost.

Each had been overwhelmed by the Phazon in his or her own body and fallen under Dark Samus's influence. The Pirates' logs list them as her field commanders: Commander Rundas on Bryyo, Commander Ghor on Elysia, and "Mistress Gandrayda," who moved among the disciples in disguise, executed any she judged unfaithful, and swore to bring Samus's helmet to Dark Samus. Each attacked Samus on sight, and Samus was forced to kill each in turn: Rundas died by his own ice, Ghor's armor exploded, and Gandrayda, cycling helplessly through borrowed shapes, was assimilated. After each fell, Dark Samus appeared and drank the dead hunter's Phazon. Every Seed Samus destroyed and every comrade she put down pushed her own corruption higher.

From the derelict Valhalla, restored with salvaged Energy Cells, Samus recovered Aurora Unit 313's last testament, the location of Phaaze and the codes that let a captured Leviathan open a wormhole for the Federation fleet. On Phaaze the saturated atmosphere overloaded her PED; an emergency vent override locked her in permanent Hypermode, her corruption climbed toward total, and her own Gunship stopped recognizing her as Samus. She was a step from becoming the thing she hunted.

In the Sanctum, the deepest chamber at Phaaze's core, Dark Samus rose out of the floor for their only direct battle of the crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). She split into echoes of herself and drew Phaaze's own energy to heal, but Samus's Hypermode could disrupt her. Beaten, she made one last gesture toward Samus and dissolved into Phazon gas, then poured herself into Aurora Unit 313. Scans found the Unit "fused with the bioessence of Dark Samus," mutated by Phazon and symbiotically tied to the planet. Samus pulled open its armored hatch, struck the cable junction behind it, and killed it.

Phaaze's core detonated. The planet was wiped from the galaxy, the Phazon purged from Samus's body, and Dark Samus exterminated with no sign of survival; with the source gone, the flow of Phazon through the galaxy stopped. The fleet had lost contact with Samus and Fleet Admiral Dane was grieving a friend when her Gunship swept past the Olympus. She flew to SkyTown on Elysia to mourn Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda, and as she left, Sylux's ship fell in behind her. See also Phaaze, the assault on Phaaze and the end of Phazon, Aurora Units, Rundas, Ghor, Gandrayda, Samus and Phazon, Sylux.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sanctum_(Phaaze) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla</sub>


### Dark Samus: The return – Colossus, Phaaze and the Norion attack

*Keys:* Colossus, Valhalla, G.F.S. Valhalla, GFS Valhalla, Norion, disciples, the great one, Leviathan, Leviathans, Pirate Homeworld, resurrected, resurrection  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Dark Samus, Dark Hunter, doppelganger, doppelgänger, Phazon, Aurora Unit 313, Space Pirates, corrupted


Dark Samus returned roughly six months after the Aether incident, and she returned as a conqueror. Space Pirates who had failed to intercept the Federation ship leaving Aether salvaged the Phazon left behind, not knowing that the raw Phazon they loaded included her remains. In the Phazon storage of the battleship Colossus she revived, consumed all of it, killed a third of the crew within minutes and "beguiled" the rest. The survivors renounced their old vows and swore sole allegiance to her as "our liberator" and "the great one"; the same logs call her "the fiendish shrew" and her hold on them "witchery." Their creed became "Disciples do not doubt their leader."

She left the crew adrift and went alone to Phaaze, the living source of all Phazon. Those who muttered that she had abandoned them were shot. She came back riding a "titanic starborne beast," a Leviathan, and led the Colossus through a wormhole to Phaaze; those who refused to follow were shot as well. Her disciples refitted the Colossus with her knowledge of Phazon enhancement and converted her star-beast into a dreadnought, the Leviathan, with cybernetic weapons and command consoles built into its skull.

To command her living planet fully she wanted an Aurora Unit. Bribes led the Pirates to the G.F.S. Valhalla, a Federation battleship crewed by green recruits on a training exercise. The Colossus disabled it, raiders tore out Aurora Unit 313, and on her orders the surviving crew were jettisoned into space; the Unit's last log ended "...Darkness coming." Implanted in Phaaze's core, Aurora Unit 313 gave Dark Samus the planet's secrets and the power to make it launch Leviathan Seeds. The first struck the Pirate Homeworld, corrupting its Pirates into fresh disciples and slowly remaking their world into a second Phaaze. A Phazon virus loosed into the Federation's Aurora Unit network made infected Units generate Phazon, which is why Fleet Admiral Castor Dane summoned Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda to the G.F.S. Olympus to carry a vaccine.

Then she ordered three strikes, each a Seed plus an armada: the Federation spy base at Elysia, the fuel works of Bryyo and the naval station at Norion, each meant as a foothold into Federation territory. "Dark Samus herself will lead the attack on Norion." Pirates boarded the Olympus and stormed Norion; Samus restored the generators of the planetary defense cannon while Rundas pulled her out of Meta Ridley's grip. At the cannon's control tower Dark Samus came down on the four hunters and blasted them with Phazon. Samus alone stayed conscious long enough to fire, destroying the Leviathan in orbit, the only Seed ever stopped short of impact, before she collapsed (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Bryyo and Elysia were struck. The Pirates recorded: "Our great leader defeated Aran and her mongrel allies in battle," and marked them with corruption. See also Phaaze, Leviathans, Aurora Units, Norion, GFS Olympus and the Federation fleet at Norion, Fleet Admiral Castor Dane.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Colossus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption</sub>


## Phazon


### Phazon: Leviathans (Leviathan Seeds)

*Keys:* Leviathan, Leviathans, Leviathan Seed, Leviathan Seeds, Phazon Seed, Phazon Seeds, Phazon meteor, Leviathan Infant, Leviathan Core, Phazon core, Leviathan Battleship, Leviathan dreadnought


Leviathans, also called Leviathan Seeds (Phazon Seeds to the Space Pirates, a Phazon Meteor in Federation records), are vast living organisms born on Phaaze: sentient asteroid-like bodies of concentrated Phazon inside an armored shell. Their sole purpose is to spread Phazon and turn target worlds into copies of Phaaze. An infant grows inside a womb organ deep in Phaaze until it develops a Phazon core, "the Leviathan's transition into adolescence and its departure from the womb." Once Phaaze has chosen a target it launches the Leviathan, which generates its own wormholes to cross between stars and homes in on the planet by instinct. "Shortly after impact, the bioform dies, leaving its armored shell to protect the Phazon core." Before dying it enthralls a large local predator, mutates it with Phazon and compels it to guard the core; "The core then begins to seep into the planet, replacing the local ecosystem with one based on Phazon." Destroying the core reverses the corruption of that world.

Known impacts:
- Tallon IV, roughly fifty years before Samus came: the meteor "came as if from nowhere"; the Chozo could only watch the Great Poison spread. They sealed the Impact Crater beneath the Artifact Temple, the Cradle, with the Cipher of twelve Chozo Artifacts, foretold an Entrusted One who would purge the poison, and themselves became the Turned, the Chozo Ghosts. At the crater's heart the creature the Chozo called the Worm, Metroid Prime, drew enormous Phazon from the core. Samus gathered the Artifacts, opened the Cradle and destroyed the Worm.
- Aether, about fifty years before the Phazon crisis: the impact threw the planet into Transdimensional Flux, creating Dark Aether and the Ing. Dark Aether became the sector's prime source of Phazon until it collapsed after Samus's intervention.
- The Pirate Homeworld: Dark Samus had a Leviathan brought down to seal her control of the Pirates; "slowly, our Homeworld becomes like Phaaze." Its guardian was Omega Ridley. The Pirates docked their Leviathan Battleship there.
- Bryyo and Elysia: two of the "Three Phazon Seeds" sent with armadas. Bryyo's guardian was Mogenar, an ancient Reptilicus War Golem reanimated and energized with Phazon and fed by the Seed's core; Elysia's was Helios, a Phazon-enhanced machine commanding Swarmbots. The corrupted hunters Rundas and Ghor defended those worlds. Bryyo's Seed was shielded by generators Samus destroyed; Elysia's shield was broken with a Theronian Bomb from SkyTown.
- Norion: the third Seed was aimed at the Federation base. Samus, wounded by Dark Samus, activated the planetary Energy Cannon and destroyed it in flight, "the only recorded event where a Leviathan has been stopped from making contact with a planet."

The Leviathan Battleship was a Pirate dreadnought: "We took her great star-beast and fitted it with cybernetic weapon systems... In time, we created a mighty dreadnought, and called it Leviathan," with crew quarters and command consoles in its skull. Samus commandeered it to open the wormhole to Phaaze. On Phaaze she killed the Leviathan Infant in the Genesis Chamber by tearing the Phazon growth from its womb with the Grapple Lasso. Every known Leviathan died with Phaaze.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Leviathan · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Federation_Data</sub>


### Phazon: Phaaze

*Keys:* Phaaze, Genesis Chamber, Leviathan womb, living planet, planet of Phazon, Phazon planet, source of all Phazon, Phazon source


Phaaze is a sentient planet composed of Phazon, "the source of all Phazon in the cosmos," as the Space Pirates who found it put it. Its atmosphere is saturated with Phazon radiation, and every bioform on it is embodied in Phazon. Phaaze is the mother of the Leviathans: "Leviathans are the children of Phaaze." Infants grow in the Genesis Chamber inside a serpentine organ that serves as a womb; they feed on Phazon until a Phazon core forms, then rise toward the surface beneath their older siblings to be launched at worlds the planet chooses. The Genesis Chamber's walls bear husks resembling the shell of Metroid Prime, the creature of Tallon IV. The deepest chamber is the Sanctum. Native life includes Phazon Metroids, Liquid Phazon and Phaz-Ing.

The Chozo who settled Elysia were the first to detect Phaaze. Long after, Aurora Unit 217 triangulated its position from the trajectories of the Leviathan attacks, and Aurora Unit 313 aboard the G.F.S. Valhalla left a final testament warning of Phaaze and Dark Samus, ending "...Darkness coming." Dark Samus, revived inside the Pirate warship Colossus, led the Pirates through a rift to Phaaze, where they implanted the stolen Aurora Unit 313 into the planet. Through it she gained command of Phaaze's biological functions: "Dark Samus can now command the living world to send forth Phazon Seeds!" The unit became "symbiotically connected to the planet Phaaze," "heavily mutated by Phazon exposure" and "fused with the bioessence of Dark Samus."

Once the Leviathans on Bryyo, Elysia and the Pirate Homeworld were destroyed, Samus seized the Pirates' Leviathan Battleship to open a wormhole to Phaaze, and the Federation fleet followed with access codes recovered from the Valhalla. The atmosphere drove her Phazon corruption to totality the moment she landed; her Gunship no longer recognized her, and her PED Suit vented its reserves, leaving her in permanent Hypermode. She destroyed the Leviathan Infant in the Genesis Chamber, descended to the Sanctum, drove off Dark Samus and destroyed Aurora Unit 313 after Dark Samus merged with it. Phaaze's core detonated and the planet was wiped from the galaxy; the fleet escaped and Samus's Gunship emerged afterward (the Phazon crisis; Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Destroying 313 "stopped the flow of Phazon throughout the galaxy," and the Pirate Homeworld, which had been "slowly becoming like Phaaze," was spared that fate. Samus alone set foot on Phaaze; the fleet held orbit.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Leviathan · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Genesis_Chamber · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sanctum_(Phaaze) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla</sub>


### Phazon: Phazon

*Keys:* Phazon, Great Poison, Phazite, Orange Phazon, Blue Phazon, Phazon ore, Phazon Sickness, Phazon radiation, Phazon energy


Phazon is a luminous blue substance, intensely radioactive and mutagenic, that originates from the living planet Phaaze and is carried to other worlds by the Leviathans. The Chozo of Tallon IV named it the Great Poison, "an otherworldly pestilence that infests the land." Space Pirate Science Team codified it as "a V-index mutagen" and a new energy source. Galactic Federation medical records call it "a highly radioactive ore with extreme mutagenic properties" of extragalactic origin, a far more potent fuel than Fuel Gel. Pirate records date their own discovery of it to two stellar years before the Phazon crisis.

Properties:
- Energy. Phazon powers Pirate warships and weapons, the Federation's Phazon Enhancement Devices, and the state called Hypermode.
- Mutation. Unshielded exposure causes "radical mutation" within seconds: growth, strength and new abilities, paid for by cellular breakdown and a shortened life. In the Pirates' Project Helix simulations, "No subject lived longer than 2 deca-cycles."
- Madness. Phazon "often affects the brains of sentient beings, causing erratic, destructive behavior"; the Federation calls the condition Phazon Sickness, and Pirate miners on Tallon IV reported Phazon-induced madness.
- Will. Phazon is not inert. Phaaze is a sentient planet; Liquid Phazon is a "semisentient" creature; and beings saturated with Phazon, even machines such as Mogenar and Helios, turn to defend the Phazon source and serve Dark Samus, who is herself made of Phazon.
- Ecology. A Leviathan's Phazon core "seeps into the planet, replacing the local ecosystem with one based on Phazon"; left unchecked, a world becomes another Phaaze.

Forms: ordinary blue Phazon; Orange Phazon in the Impact Crater of Tallon IV, "at least 86% greater" in mutagenic potency and fatal to unshielded bioforms within moments; Phazite, a hardened form the Pirates mined on their Leviathan-infected Homeworld for armor and door shielding, which only the Nova Beam penetrates; and Liquid Phazon, a living puddle that spawns Phazon Grubs.

History: a Leviathan struck Tallon IV roughly fifty years before Samus's first visit, and the Pirates mined its Phazon; the Leviathan that hit Aether created Dark Aether, whose rich Phazon ore drew Pirate extraction raids and fed Dark Samus; several kilograms recovered by the G.F.S. Anhur led the Federation to build the Phazon Enhancement Device. Dark Samus later seeded the Pirate Homeworld, Bryyo and Elysia and launched a Leviathan at Norion.

No one has been touched by Phazon more than Samus Aran: her Power Suit became the Phazon Suit on Tallon IV, Dark Samus's Phazon blast on Norion corrupted her body, and she fought her own rising corruption until she destroyed Phaaze. With Phaaze's death the flow of Phazon through the galaxy stopped, and the Federation declared the threat of Phazon corruption ended.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Orange_Phazon</sub>


### Phazon: Phazon corruption and Hypermode

*Keys:* Hypermode, Hyper-mode, Hyper mode, Phazon corruption, Phazon-corrupted, Phazon Enhancement Device, PED, Hyper Beam, Hyper Missile, Hyper Ball, Hyper Grapple, Phazon Grenade


Phazon corruption is the progressive takeover of a living body, or a machine, by Phazon. Exposure mutates; the mutated body begins to generate Phazon of its own; the Phazon brings power and dependence; and the host's will bends toward the Phazon source and toward Dark Samus, until nothing is left but a creature of Phazon. The Pirates wrote of the corrupted hunters that "Each of them bears her mark of corruption." A planet's corruption is reversed by destroying its Leviathan's core; corruption of every kind ended when Phaaze was destroyed.

Hypermode is the state in which a Phazon-saturated being channels that Phazon: "blue, ghost-like tentacles," far greater resilience and "dangerously increased offensive capabilities." Any Phazon being can enter it: Hopping Metroids, corrupted Space Pirates, the corrupted hunters, Dark Samus. Samus first entered Hyper-mode on Tallon IV when, wearing the Phazon Suit, she stood in the pools of Phazon that Metroid Prime expelled and fired it back as the Phazon Beam.

The Galactic Federation built the Phazon Enhancement Device (PED) from ore recovered by the G.F.S. Anhur, to "harness the destructive power of Phazon for use in battle." Federation Marines carry a Phazon supply in a backpack and siphon it into their armorsuits; a Marine battalion in the Norion system tested it, and "no marines have displayed signs of 'Phazon sickness.'" After Dark Samus's Phazon blast on Norion left Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda producing Phazon in their own bodies, Federation medics, seeing no ill effects, fitted all four with PEDs. Samus's PED Suit draws on her internal Phazon: she commits one Energy Tank to enter Hypermode and gains the Hyper Beam, and in time the Hyper Ball, Hyper Missile and Hyper Grapple; Hypermode ends when the Phazon is spent.

The danger: overuse sends Phazon tentacles crawling across the user's vision, and prolonged use "can ultimately lead to total corruption." Pirate Phazon Grenades force a Phazon-overloaded Hypermode that must be vented at once through Hyper weapons. Samus's armor darkened with each Leviathan she destroyed. Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda overused their devices and fell to Dark Samus; Ghor taunted Samus, "Don't you feel the power? Soon everything will be corrupted. Including you." On Phaaze her own corruption reached totality and she was locked in permanent Hypermode, bleeding off Phazon with the Hyper Grapple and Anti-Phazon to stay herself (the Phazon crisis; Metroid Prime 3: Corruption).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hypermode · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Enhancement_Device · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/PED_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Grenade</sub>


### Phazon: Phazon Metroids and Phazon-corrupted creatures

*Keys:* Phazon Metroid, Phazon Metroids, Hopping Metroid, Hopping Metroids, Metroid Hatcher, Miniroid, Miniroids, Phaazoid, Phaazoids, Phaz-Ing, Liquid Phazon, Phazon Grub


Phazon remakes whatever it touches, and the Metroid takes to it best of all. The Space Pirates found that the specimen later called Metroid Prime "displays a limitless capacity for Phazon infusion and shows no Phazon-based degeneration whatsoever," and their Phazon experiments on Tallon Metroids produced the Hunter and Fission Metroids of Tallon IV.

Phazon Metroids (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption) are the mature form of this corruption: "Heavy exposure to Phazon energy has mutated this strain of Tallon Metroid." They can "shift into a phased state, allowing [them] to ignore solid matter," passing through walls and slipping attacks; they fire bursts of Phazon and drain life energy; only great cold holds them still, and their core can be destroyed with a single Nova Beam shot. The Pirates kept them in tanks in Eastern SkyTown on Elysia and bred them in egg-filled cells in the Metroid Creche of the Pirate Homeworld, but their natural home is Phaaze. Their young are Miniroids, "tiny and weak," which "travel in a flock for protection"; "Those that survive will grow quickly." Related strains:
- Hopping Metroid: "Radical mutation has removed the ability to fly from this strain of Metroid, but its vulnerability to cold remains"; its legs carry blades of Phazite and it can enter Hypermode.
- Metroid Hatcher: a floating head with four energy-draining tentacles; the mutation robbed it of phasing but gave it a Phazite-like shell. It spawns Phazon Metroids from its mouth and dies only when every tentacle is torn away.

Life made wholly of Phazon: Liquid Phazon, a "semisentient glob of Phazon" that consumes Phazon in any form and spawns Phazon Grubs, whose bite spreads corruption; Phaz-Ing, "wall-crawling shape-shifters" that stay liquid until they attack and tear apart under the Grapple Lasso; Phaazoids, floating Phazon that only Phazon can harm and that split in two when destroyed; and the Leviathan Infants themselves.

Corrupted natives and machines: the Turned, Chozo Ghosts of Tallon IV "utterly corrupted by the Great Poison"; Phazon-enhanced fauna such as Phazon Puffers and Phazon Shriekbats; the Reptilicus War Golem Mogenar and the Elysian machine Helios, enthralled as Leviathan guardians; the Elite Pirates and Omega Pirate of Project Helix; and the bounty hunters Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda, whose Phazon Dark Samus reclaimed as each died. Samus, herself Phazon-corrupted after Norion, hunted these creatures while fighting the same change in her own body.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Miniroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hopping_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Hatcher · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Liquid_Phazon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Grub</sub>


### Phazon: Pirate Phazon operations and the Federation's PED program

*Keys:* Phazon mining, Phazon Mines, Phazon program, Phazon research, Phazon experiments, Phazon weapons, Phazon-powered, Project Helix, Phazon Strain Vertigo, PED program, Phazon harvesting


Both great powers of the galaxy tried to make Phazon a weapon; only the Space Pirates let it make them its own.

The Pirates (Space Pirate projects and Phazon research): Science Team codified Phazon as "a V-index mutagen" on Tallon IV and began mining near the Impact Crater, where it was most concentrated, with garrisons and terraforming following. The miners went mad. Experiments followed on everything to hand: Parasites, Metroids (the Hunter and Fission strains), Project Titan (Thardus, shut down) and Project Helix, which fused Phazon with Pirate DNA. Early Helix subjects suffered degraded brain tissue; the Vertigo strain produced the Elite Pirates and the Omega Pirate, whose "potential overdependence on Phazon" worried even Command. On Aether, "Phazon extraction raids are under way on Dark Aether," and Dark Samus stripped their stores: "She does not seek our destruction, but our Phazon." Aboard the warship Colossus the remains of Dark Samus "revived herself within our Phazon storage," "consumed all of the Phazon, then wiped out a third of the crew in a matter of minutes," and the survivors knelt: "we now pledge sole allegiance to our liberator, the great one, Dark Samus." "Let the light of Phazon lead us!" The Colossus's Phazon-powered weapons took the G.F.S. Valhalla; the Pirates built the Leviathan dreadnought; a Leviathan corrupted their Homeworld; they mined Phazite for Berserker Knight plating and door shields, bred Phazon Metroids in the Metroid Creche, entered Hypermode in battle and threw Phazon Grenades at Samus.

The Galactic Federation: the G.F.S. Anhur recovered several kilograms of Phazon ore while dismantling a Pirate mining facility, and from it the Federation built the Phazon Enhancement Device, "enabling an individual to harness the destructive power of Phazon for use in battle." Marines carried the Phazon in backpacks and siphoned it into their armorsuits, tested on a battalion in the Norion system without signs of Phazon Sickness. After Norion the Federation fitted the four Phazon-generating hunters with PEDs and gave Samus the PED Suit, then sent them against the Leviathans; Demolition Troopers breached the Pirate Homeworld's Seed. The program died with Phaaze, which ended the flow of Phazon.

Samus accepted the Federation's device on its medics' judgment and used it to the edge of total corruption. Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda, given the same device, did not come back.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Aether · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Federation_Data</sub>


### Phazon: Samus and Phazon (corruption and purge)

*Keys:* Samus's corruption, corrupted Samus, Samus corrupted, her corruption, Phazon purge, purged of Phazon, free of Phazon, Phazon in her body, Phazon in her veins, Phazon poisoning, Phazon exposure, permanent Hypermode


Samus Aran's body and armor have been corrupted by Phazon twice, and she is the only known being to have come back from it.

Tallon IV: when the Omega Pirate collapsed onto her and disintegrated, its Phazon soaked into her armor: "The Power Suit has been corrupted by viral exposure, turning it into the Phazon Suit." The jet-black suit shrugged off ordinary blue Phazon but not the Orange Phazon of the Impact Crater. Standing in the pools Metroid Prime expelled, she entered Hyper-mode and fired the Phazon Beam. The dying creature tore the Phazon Suit from her body; she escaped in her Gravity Suit, free of Phazon, while the Phazon pool merged with the stolen suit and a hand rose from it: Dark Samus.

Norion: Dark Samus's Phazon blast knocked Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda unconscious; Samus stayed awake long enough to fire the Energy Cannon that destroyed the incoming Leviathan, then lay in a coma for a month. She woke aboard the G.F.S. Olympus to learn her body now generated Phazon on its own. The Federation fitted her with the PED Suit and warned her it might corrupt her.

The crisis: each Leviathan she destroyed deepened her corruption, her armor darkened, and control in Hypermode grew harder to keep. She watched Dark Samus reclaim the Phazon from each dying comrade: Rundas impaled on his own ice on Bryyo, Ghor on Elysia, and Gandrayda on the Pirate Homeworld, who in her death throes shifted into a Pirate, Ghor, Rundas and Samus's own form before being absorbed.

Phaaze: on landing, her corruption rose to totality. Her Gunship refused her; its scanners no longer recognized Samus through "near total corruption." The PED Suit's emergency vent dumped her energy reserves to slow the change and left her in permanent Hypermode with Phazon rising at every wound and every breath of the atmosphere. She stayed herself by seizing Anti-Phazon and forcing her own Phazon into Phaz-Ing through the Hyper Grapple. Total saturation would have finished her.

The purge: she destroyed Aurora Unit 313 and Phaaze with it, and with Phaaze eliminated the threat of Phazon corruption disappeared. She flew her Gunship past the Olympus to signal the mission complete, then went alone to Elysia to mourn Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). She came within a breath of becoming the thing she destroyed.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_(game) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Orange_Phazon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze</sub>


## Federation Force


### Federation Force: Bermuda System (Excelcion, Bion, Talvania)

*Keys:* Bermuda System, Bermuda, Excelcion, Bion, Talvania, Ice Titan, Ice Titans, Bermuda planets


The Bermuda System is a remote star system on the fringe of Galactic Federation space, a former Federation territory of three major planets: Excelcion, Bion and Talvania. The Federation once worked the system and then withdrew; after the Phazon crisis it sent the Federation Force back to restore its bases, and the Force found the Space Pirates had moved in. During the Bermuda System incident each planet concealed one of the cloaking generators that hid the Pirate warship Doomseye.

Excelcion, the first planet, is a frozen wasteland. It is rich in natural resources and was mined by the Federation until, about twenty years before the incident, a giant meteorite impact plunged it into deep cold and the base was abandoned. Federation installations remain, among them a biological research institute and an underground lake where a survey team went silent after being attacked by aquatic creatures the Pirates had enlarged and brought under control with their beam. Its native Ice Titans are huge quadrupeds, placid until provoked, that the Force captured for study; a living nest in the old base endlessly spawned hostile hoppers. The Pirates occupied Federation facilities here to salvage a weapon.

Bion, the second planet, is a scorching desert world of extreme heat with volcanic lava regions. It was home to an advanced ancient civilization, extinct long ago and never named, whose people worshipped spherical objects (Orbs) and are believed to have developed size-increasing technology to survive the planet's harsh conditions. Their temples and ruins are guarded by stone golems, and they left an artificial life form sleeping under hardened lava that woke to shake the ground. Here the Force retrieved a data codex and power cells, learned that the Pirates had acquired enlarging technology of their own, and saw a giant armored creature stopped only by missile bombardment from Samus's gunship.

Talvania, the third planet, is a world of toxic gas and abandoned industry, once home to a highly advanced machine civilization that is thought to have left for another planet several centuries ago. Its power plant and ancient security systems still run. The Pirates reactivated facilities that should have been dormant, built a missile factory and launched ballistic missiles at the G.F.S. Aegis, and guarded a transfer device behind a layered automated defense system.

Samus Aran knows all three worlds from her intelligence work during the incident (Metroid Prime: Federation Force), when she destroyed most of the Pirate missile bases alone before the Pirates captured her. See also The Doomseye and The Federation Force and their Mechs.

### Federation Force: General Alex Miles

*Keys:* Miles, Alex Miles, General Miles, General Alex Miles, Commander Alex  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* General, Alex, Federation Force, Aegis, Bermuda, Operation Golem, Mech, Mechs


General Alex Miles is the Galactic Federation officer who commands the Federation Force and directs Operation Golem, the Federation's secret program of piloted Mechs. During the Bermuda System incident (Metroid Prime: Federation Force) he ran the campaign from the flagship G.F.S. Aegis in orbit above Excelcion, issuing the Force's orders and briefings for its operations on Excelcion, Bion and Talvania and giving tactical guidance to pilots sent out alone. Official Japanese material names him simply Commander Alex.

Under his command the Force's assignment began as base reconstruction and archaeological survey and turned into open war once Space Pirates were found entrenched in the system. It was his pilots who destroyed the cloaking generators hiding the Doomseye, boarded the warship, defeated the brainwashed and enlarged Samus Aran and broke the ship's shield so the surviving fleet could destroy it.

Samus took part in the operation as a bounty hunter contracted by the Federation to gather intelligence, not as one of Miles's Marines; she answered to the Federation's commission rather than to his chain of command, and after her rescue she thanked the Force for what they had done. See also The Federation Force and their Mechs, The Bermuda System incident and The Doomseye.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/bcaj/index.html · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/bcaj/multiplay/index.html · https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%83%A1%E3%83%88%E3%83%AD%E3%82%A4%E3%83%89%E3%83%97%E3%83%A9%E3%82%A4%E3%83%A0_%E3%83%95%E3%82%A7%E3%83%87%E3%83%AC%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B7%E3%83%A7%E3%83%B3%E3%83%95%E3%82%A9%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B9</sub>


### Federation Force: The Bermuda System incident (Federation Force events)

*Keys:* Bermuda System incident, Bermuda System, Bermuda, Operation Golem, Project Golem, Federation Force, giant Samus, enlarged Samus, brainwashed Samus


The Bermuda System incident was a Galactic Federation campaign against the Space Pirates fought soon after the Phazon crisis and the destruction of Phaaze (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption); the official Japanese website opens its account in Cosmic Year 20X6. With Phazon gone and the Pirates still at large, the Federation approved a top-secret program, Operation Golem, to build piloted Mechs modeled on Samus Aran's Power Suit, and formed an elite unit of Marines, the Federation Force, to pilot them under General Alex Miles. The Force was posted to the Bermuda System, a remote former Federation territory of three planets (Excelcion, Bion and Talvania), officially to rebuild the abandoned Federation base on frozen Excelcion and to carry out archaeological surveys, operating from the flagship G.F.S. Aegis in orbit.

The Force soon found Space Pirates entrenched across the system: occupying Excelcion's old Federation facilities to salvage a weapon, digging through the ruins of Bion's extinct civilization, and reawakening Talvania's dormant industry to mass-produce ballistic missiles aimed at the Aegis. The Federation hired Samus to gather intelligence. Working alone from her gunship she destroyed most of the Pirate missile bases, and on Bion her missile bombardment stopped a giant armored creature when a Force squad could not. On Bion the Force also learned the Pirates had acquired technology that could enlarge living things, themselves included; on Excelcion a Federation survey team had already been attacked by aquatic creatures the Pirates had enlarged and brought under control with a beam. Samus then reported that the Pirates were building an enormous warship, the Doomseye, after which the Federation lost contact with her.

The Doomseye was hidden by cloaking generators on all three planets. The Force destroyed each generator and exposed the ship in orbit, but the Doomseye answered by annihilating most of the Federation fleet and sealing itself inside an impenetrable shield. The Force boarded it and found Samus a prisoner: the Pirates had brainwashed her, struck her with their enlarging beam until she was gigantic and berserk, and turned her against the Marines. She fought in Morph Ball form with Bombs, Boost Ball, Spider Ball and Power Bombs. The Force beat her down, which returned her to normal size and collapsed the Doomseye's shield. The surviving fleet then destroyed the warship; the blast hurled the Force into open space, where Samus recovered them and thanked the Marines for what they had done.

For Samus the incident is a rare mark against her record: in the Bermuda System she was captured, mind-controlled and used as a weapon against Federation soldiers, and she owed her freedom to rookie Marines in Mechs built on her own suit's design. Whether the Force also recovered a Metroid egg from the Pirates bears on what came after (see the Metroid egg epilogue and Sylux). See also Bermuda System, The Federation Force and their Mechs, General Alex Miles and The Doomseye.

### Federation Force: The Doomseye

*Keys:* Doomseye, Doom's Eye, Doom Eye, Doomseye warship, Pirate warship, Pirate battleship, cloaking generator, cloaking generators


The Doomseye was an enormous Space Pirate warship built in secret in the Bermuda System after the Phazon crisis. Its existence was uncovered by Samus Aran, whom the Galactic Federation had hired to gather intelligence on Pirate activity in the system; her report that the Pirates were constructing a huge battleship was her last before the Federation lost contact with her. The ship lay hidden under cloaking technology powered by three generators, one each on Excelcion, Bion and Talvania. The Federation Force destroyed all three, which revealed the Doomseye in orbit, but the warship struck back at once, wiping out most of the Federation fleet, and then enclosed itself in an impenetrable shield.

With the fleet crippled, the Force boarded the Doomseye in their Mechs. Inside they found the Pirates' captive, Samus, brainwashed and grown to giant size by the Pirates' enlarging beam and set against them. Her defeat returned her to normal size and shut down the ship's shield, and the surviving Federation ships destroyed the Doomseye. The blast threw the Force out into open space, where Samus recovered them.

The Doomseye is where the Pirates held, mind-controlled and weaponized Samus, and where Federation Marines rather than the Hunter herself won the decisive fight (Metroid Prime: Federation Force). The Force was nearly lost in the ship's destruction and survived only because Samus came back for it. See also The Bermuda System incident, General Alex Miles and The Federation Force and their Mechs.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force · https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%83%A1%E3%83%88%E3%83%AD%E3%82%A4%E3%83%89%E3%83%97%E3%83%A9%E3%82%A4%E3%83%A0_%E3%83%95%E3%82%A7%E3%83%87%E3%83%AC%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B7%E3%83%A7%E3%83%B3%E3%83%95%E3%82%A9%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B9 · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/bcaj/mission/index.html</sub>


### Federation Force: The Federation Force and their Mechs

*Keys:* Federation Force, Mech, Mechs, Mech suit, Mech suits, Mechsuit, Operation Golem, Project Golem, Golem Mech, G.F.S. Aegis, GFS Aegis, Blast Ball


The Federation Force is an elite unit of Galactic Federation Marines raised under Operation Golem (also called Project Golem), the Federation's top-secret program to regain a technological edge over the Space Pirates once Phazon was gone (after Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Its pilots are specially trained troopers, many of them newly graduated, who operate in squads of up to four under General Alex Miles from the flagship G.F.S. Aegis. The unit's first campaign was the Bermuda System incident, where it was officially sent to rebuild a Federation base and survey ruins and ended up boarding and helping destroy the Pirate warship Doomseye.

The Mech is the Force's prototype battle armor: a hulking piloted suit about 4.3 meters tall and 3.2 tonnes in weight, walking at roughly 27 km/h, entered by the pilot through the back of the torso (figures per the official Japanese website). Its design is modeled on Samus Aran's Power Suit, and only Federation Force members are cleared to pilot one. Standard systems are an arm cannon that fires beams and charged shots, target lock-on, a sidestep dodge, and a jump with a short hover. Before each mission a pilot fits supplementary equipment within a strict weight limit: homing Missiles and area-blast Super Missiles, incendiary and freezing shots, a slowing beam, a scanning shot that exposes weak points and lets certain beams home in, Repair Capsules that restore a Mech's energy, shield generators that raise a protective dome, decoys and sensor bombs. Performance chips called MODs tune the machine further; a pilot working alone can fit the Lone Wolf MOD, which doubles attack power and halves damage taken, and may bring up to three battle-support drones. Squads split roles accordingly: a medic carrying Repair Capsules, a heavy gunner loaded with ordnance, an assist pilot with disruptive weapons.

Recruits train in Blast Ball, a three-on-three sport in which Mechs drive a giant ball into the opposing goal with beam fire.

Samus never piloted a Mech; during the Bermuda System incident she worked alongside the Force as a hired bounty hunter in her own gunship. The machines are still bound up with her: their design descends from her Chozo armor, and it was Force pilots in Mechs who defeated her when the Pirates brainwashed and enlarged her aboard the Doomseye, and whom she pulled out of space afterward.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/bcaj/index.html · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/bcaj/mech/index.html · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/bcaj/multiplay/index.html · https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%83%A1%E3%83%88%E3%83%AD%E3%82%A4%E3%83%89%E3%83%97%E3%83%A9%E3%82%A4%E3%83%A0_%E3%83%95%E3%82%A7%E3%83%87%E3%83%AC%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B7%E3%83%A7%E3%83%B3%E3%83%95%E3%82%A9%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B9 · https://www.nintendolife.com/reviews/3ds/metroid_prime_federation_force</sub>


### Federation Force: The Metroid egg and the Bermuda System epilogue

*Keys:* Metroid egg, Metroid eggs, stasis tube, post-credits, post credits, secret ending, epilogue  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Sylux, Federation Force, Bermuda, Doomseye, research station, Delano 7, Prime 4, Viewros


The Space Pirates held Metroids in the Bermuda System during the Bermuda System incident, and in one operation the Federation Force recovered a Metroid egg from them, which the Galactic Federation placed in a stasis tube at a research station. Whether the Force brought the egg back determines what the record shows next. If it did, a brief epilogue (the post-credits scene of Metroid Prime: Federation Force) follows the incident: an unseen armored figure breaks into the Federation research station, reaches the stasis tube, tampers with it and fires a beam into the egg, which hatches at once into a Metroid larva. The intruder is shown only in silhouette, but a shoulder spike visible in a wider shot identifies him as Sylux, the hunter whose ship, the Delano 7, had been seen tailing Samus Aran's gunship at the close of the Phazon crisis.

Neither Samus nor the Force was present for the break-in. The hatched Metroid's release foreshadows the Viewros incident (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond), in which Sylux leads Space Pirates in attacks on Federation research facilities using Metroids able to possess host creatures; see Sylux and The Viewros incident for that conflict. For Samus, who as the galaxy's foremost Metroid exterminator had just been captured and turned against the Federation in the same system, the stolen hatchling marks the point at which Sylux, her rival since the Alimbic Cluster, came into possession of the species she has spent her career hunting.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sylux · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Delano_7 · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Metroid_characters · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond</sub>


## Prime 4


### Prime 4: Aberax and Sylux's Metroids

*Keys:* Aberax, Sylux Metroid, Sylux Metroids, Metroid fusion, Metroid-fused, fused Metroid, fused with a Metroid, possessed by a Metroid, mind-controlling Metroids, mind-controlled Pirates, Metroid strain


Sylux's Metroids are a strain he bred from the Metroid hatchling he stole from a Federation research station after the Bermuda System incident. Exploiting the creature's imprinting, he cloned it and cultivated copies with a new ability: they fuse with a host organism and take control of its mind. With them he possessed Space Pirate officers, seized command of their army, and turned the Pirates against Galactic Federation research facilities in Cosmic Year 20X9 (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). Federation intelligence already suspected that the raiders were being controlled by Metroids able to merge with other life-forms, and a fused host betrays itself by green-glowing growths.

Aberax was the first such host Samus Aran met: a huge beetle-like Space Pirate of command rank, nearly immune to beams and explosives and able to deploy a barrier. Metroid fusion stripped it of free will and grew up to four beam-projecting lenses in its chest; only destroying every lens forces it to vent its core energy. It dropped into the hangar of the UTO Research Center on Tanamaar as the artifact was being loaded for evacuation, and Samus destroyed it moments before Sylux himself arrived with more Pirates and Metroids.

On Viewros Sylux pushed the technique further, infusing his Metroids into the Lamorn Guardians of the Master Teleporter Keys. Carvex, Xelios, Keratos and Phenoros each lost the ability to communicate and attacked everything near them; the fusion left exposed, vulnerable tissue, and in Phenoros's case Samus had to pass down the creature's throat to reach the Metroid lodged deep inside it. Metroids flanked Sylux himself in the final battle at the Chrono Tower, and he still commands them on Viewros. For Samus, who has fought Metroids since Zebes and SR388 and once carried the baby Metroid that saved her, these mind-controlling hosts were a new kind of enemy, and a reminder that the hatchling Sylux stole might have been as gentle as the one she protected.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sylux · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aberax · http://www.nintendoworldreport.com/feature/73864/metroid-prime-4-beyonds-timeline-placement-analysis · https://omegametroid.com/new-metroid-prime-4-beyond-details-revealed-on-the-games-japanese-website-spoiler-warning/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/</sub>


### Prime 4: Grievers and the Guardians of the Keys

*Keys:* Griever, Grievers, Omega Griever, Carvex, Xelios, Keratos, Phenoros, Gravinax, Varmis, Key Guardian, Key Guardians, Maul Griever


Grievers are the feral monsters that overran Viewros. They are not native beasts but transformed Lamorn: when the Lamorn showered their world with Green Energy to revive it, the energy reacted with the psychic crystals implanted in their people and twisted them into savage, mindless hunters. The Lamorn called this the Great Tragedy. Griever packs hunted the uncorrupted survivors, and the attempts to cure them in the Ice Belt laboratories only worsened the mutation (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond).

Varieties:
- Jungle Griever (Fury Green): thin-skinned pack hunters that leap and climb through the trees.
- Sand Griever (Sol Valley): ambushers that bury themselves in the dunes; packs rotate territories and merge to face great threats.
- Frostburn Griever (Ice Belt): pale specimens revived from the Lamorn cryopods, cold-resistant but weak to heat.
- Maul Griever and Maul Griever Brute (Great Mines): burrowers that hunt by sound and attack in waves; loud noises draw hordes. Brutes, swollen by excess Green Energy, spit mucus that hardens into barriers.
- Omega Griever: the largest and strongest Griever, apex predator of the Great Mines. It killed Gravinax, the eight-limbed, four-horned Guardian of the mines' Master Teleporter Key, and took its place and its key.

The Guardians of the keys were creatures and minds the Lamorn trusted; Sylux infused four of them with his Metroids:
- Carvex (Fury Green): a giant carnivorous plant that once communed telepathically with the priests, driven to attack everything.
- Xelios (Volt Forge): the organic-cell artificial intelligence that managed vehicle manufacturing, still following its last instructions until the fusion turned it into a battle AI.
- Keratos (Ice Belt): a one-of-a-kind armored giant with an iceberg-like shell, its mind and body irreversibly altered.
- Phenoros (Flare Pool): a huge mineral life-form in the lava lake of Magdrahda that feeds on Green Energy.

Other dangers include Varmis, a giant sand-swimming crustacean of Sol Valley grown enormous on Green Energy, Snow Wolves that hunt in ranked packs in the Ice Belt, and the Behemoth of Flare Pool, an armored arthropod whose four eyes are its only weakness. Samus Aran fought Grievers in every region of Viewros and destroyed each Guardian and the Omega Griever to claim the five keys; the Sanctum of the Guardians in the Chrono Tower preserves holographic records of them.


<sub>Sources: https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-machines/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/boss-guide/ · https://shinesparkers.net/features/the-lamorn/ · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.videogameschronicle.com/guide/metroid-prime-4-walkthrough-where-to-go-all-upgrades-and-collectibles-step-by-step-guide/</sub>


### Prime 4: Myles MacKenzie and the stranded Federation marines

*Keys:* Myles MacKenzie, MacKenzie, Myles, Reger Tokabi, Tokabi, Ezra Duke, Sergeant Duke, Nora Armstrong, Armstrong, VUE-995, Betsy, Base Camp


When the Lamorn artifact on Tanamaar detonated, five members of the Galactic Federation garrison of the UTO Research Center were hurled to Viewros alongside Samus Aran (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). Scattered across the planet, they sent out distress signals, and Samus found them one by one.

- Myles MacKenzie: a Federation engineering specialist who maintains and repairs vehicles and writes software for weapons development. Timid, rarely at the front and talkative even when alone, he is restlessly curious yet so cautious that it keeps him alive, with a strong moral compass and work ethic. Samus found him in Fury Green trapped in his wrecked transport amid Griever-infested vines; once freed he handed her the Missile Launcher stored aboard, restored comms and asked, "What do you say? Teammates?" Samus nodded. He set up the Base Camp in Fury Green, received salvaged mech parts there by teleporter patch, and rebuilt the shattered MK-99 Federation mech Betsy, which he had once worked on ("Poor Betsy…"), to batter down the barrier around the Chrono Tower.
- Reger Tokabi: a Federation sniper, a solitary hunter from a remote world who enlisted in search of a stable life. He always carries a tiger-shaped talisman he credits with his survival, and his family's faith in the god Sollan sits uneasily with his missions. He camped in Sol Valley, gave Samus a Teleporter Chip, and spoke to her about his upbringing and the sacred feel of the planet.
- Sergeant Ezra Duke: a veteran of countless engagements with the Space Pirates, stubborn and prone to clashing with superiors, but sincere, compassionate and dependable in a fight. Samus freed him from a generator in Flare Pool, and at Base Camp it was Duke who confirmed the final push on the tower.
- Private First Class Nora Armstrong: bright and proactive, with an intuitive command of structures and vehicles. She has admired Samus since they met, and her outbursts about her annoy her superiors. Samus met her on a tram in Flare Pool; "Finally, we get to go home," she said when the way out seemed open.
- VUE-995: a Federation combat android built to replace human pilots of fighters, armored vehicles and bipedal mechs, armed with shoulder missile launchers and sonic-boom emitters in its palms. It has no emotions but produces pseudo-emotions at times, and apologized for wrecking Betsy, the mech that punched through the barrier.

All five fought beside Samus against Sylux at the Pinnacle of the Chrono Tower, reviving one another and stripping away his shields. When Sylux returned and crippled the Master Teleporter they grabbed him and held him down so that Samus could escape; Tokabi threw her his tag as she went. Samus left them on Viewros and hung Tokabi's tag on the tree she grew from the Memory Fruit in their memory. Whether any of them survived is unknown.


<sub>Sources: https://www.nintendolife.com/news/2025/11/metroid-prime-4-beyond-meet-the-team · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/ · https://shinesparkers.net/metroid-prime-4-beyond-transcript/ · https://tech.yahoo.com/gaming/articles/metroid-prime-4-beyonds-ending-181400714.html · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.videogameschronicle.com/guide/metroid-prime-4-walkthrough-where-to-go-all-upgrades-and-collectibles-step-by-step-guide/</sub>


### Prime 4: Sylux

*Keys:* Sylux, Delano 7, Shock Coil, Lockjaw, Psy-bot Mimic, Psy-bot Mimics, Sylux decoy, Sylux decoys, Cylosis, Sylux's past, Sylux backstory


Sylux is a human bounty hunter, said to come from the planet Cylosis, who wears a blue armored suit and excels at stealth, tracking and marksmanship. He harbors an intense hatred for the Galactic Federation and for Samus Aran, whom he despises for serving it (per the Metroid Prime Hunters manual).

Equipment:
- Shock Coil: banned technology that fires charged blasts of high-density neutrinos as a continuous beam of blue lightning, draining an opponent's energy and feeding it to Sylux.
- Lockjaw: his alternate form, two tetracarbon ceramic-alloy blades joined by an energy thread, faster than a Morph Ball; it lays up to three bombs linked by trip wires. On Viewros it could also shield him from gunfire, ram Samus to tear an Energy Tank from her, and stun with an electric rush.
- Delano 7: a Delta-class gunship, blue with green accents, with an automatic cannon on its lower hull. Like his suit and weapons it is believed to be stolen Federation prototype technology.

Career:
- Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters): after attacking a Federation vessel on a snowy world he followed Samus to the Alimbic Cluster seeking the ultimate power. Samus first met him at the Vesper Defense Outpost; Delano 7 supported him in battle. She saved him from Gorea, yet he fled with his hatred intact.
- After the Phazon crisis, Delano 7 was seen shadowing Samus's gunship as she departed.
- Bermuda System incident: he broke into a Federation research station and hatched the Metroid Egg the Federation Force had recovered.

The Viewros incident: exploiting the hatchling's imprinting, Sylux cloned it and bred a Metroid strain that merges with and controls other organisms. With these he seized the minds of Space Pirate officers, took command of their army, upgraded his battlesuit with the latest nanosuit and raided Federation research centers, ending in the attack on Tanamaar. His duel with Samus damaged the Lamorn artifact that hurled both of them to Viewros. Gravely injured, he was carried into the Chrono Tower, where a Lamorn healing pod mended him and synchronized his mind with the tower; he learned of the Lamorn, the Master Teleporter and its five keys. While recovering he sent Psy-bot Mimics, machines indistinguishable from him until destroyed, to attack Samus and seize the keys, infused his Metroids into the keys' Guardians, and sealed the tower behind a barrier. His consciousness brushed Samus's, and she relived his memories as psychic flashbacks. When she reached the Pinnacle he rose from the pod changed, wielding tentacle-like nano-arms and Lamorn energy, tore open a dimensional rift and dragged her through; she beat him in the void beyond, where his Lockjaw could even summon a black hole. Seemingly destroyed, he returned as Samus activated the Master Teleporter and damaged it; the five Federation soldiers held him down while she escaped. He remains on Viewros, still commanding his Metroids and Pirates, his showdown with Samus unresolved.

His past: Sylux was a Federation officer in command of a squad fighting the Space Pirates. Ordered to hold position until Samus's team arrived, he disobeyed ("Get me that weapon") to seize a Pirate superweapon. It annihilated his squad and the Federation mechs with it; he alone survived, barely. Samus arrived in her gunship, destroyed the weapon with ease and offered him her hand, which he slapped away. Blaming her and the Federation, he deserted with stolen prototype gear and became a bounty hunter bent on revenge. Samus now regards him as an enemy who will not go away.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sylux · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Delano_7 · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sylux · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/metroid-prime-4-beyond-who-is-sylux · http://www.nintendoworldreport.com/feature/73864/metroid-prime-4-beyonds-timeline-placement-analysis</sub>


### Prime 4: Tanamaar and the Desolan System

*Keys:* Tanamaar, Desolan, Desolan System, UTO Research Center, UTO, research center attack, Federation research facility, research facility attack


Tanamaar is a rocky, barren planet with at least one moon in the Desolan System, occupied by the Galactic Federation and the site of the UTO Research Center. In Cosmic Year 20X9 Federation researchers there unearthed an alien artifact that their analysis could identify only as some kind of device. It was in truth a Lamorn return teleporter: one of the twelve Lamorn who left Viewros to search for a prophesied Chosen One had died on Tanamaar, and the Federation had found what it carried.

Sylux and his Space Pirates, who had been raiding Federation research facilities with mind-controlling Metroids, struck the UTO Research Center while Samus Aran was on reconnaissance nearby, and Federation HQ sent her to secure the artifact (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). Her security ID gave her full access, and a trooper sent her off with "Go get 'em, Samus." Command ordered the artifact moved to the hangar and loaded onto a transport while Pirate breach charges tore through the gates and wounded Pirates and troopers lay in the corridors. In the hangar Samus destroyed Aberax, a Space Pirate officer fused with a Metroid, and then faced Sylux himself. His fire damaged the artifact; it activated and enveloped the whole center in an energy burst. Samus, Sylux, the engineer Myles MacKenzie, the soldiers Reger Tokabi, Ezra Duke and Nora Armstrong, the android VUE-995, the Federation MK-99 mech Betsy and chunks of the facility were all carried off to Viewros.

Debris from the UTO Research Center lies scattered across Viewros, and its salvaged mech parts and power cores became the raw material of the group's escape. Where the Master Teleporter finally delivered Samus is uncertain; she emerged on a barren, rocky world that may or may not have been Tanamaar.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Planet_Tanamaar · https://shinesparkers.net/metroid-prime-4-beyond-transcript/ · https://shinesparkers.net/features/the-lamorn/ · https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/metroid-prime-4-beyond-galactic-federation-uto-research-center-guide · https://www.noobfeed.com/news/metroid-prime-4-beyonds-ending-sparks-new-mysteries</sub>


### Prime 4: The Chrono Tower and the Master Teleporter

*Keys:* Chrono Tower, Master Teleporter, Master Teleporter Key, Master Teleporter Keys, Teleporter Keys, Nexus of Hope, Sanctum of the Guardians, Psy-bot, Psy-bots, healing pod, Lamorn teleporter, the Pinnacle


The Chrono Tower is a vast tooth-shaped Lamorn structure floating above the desert of Sol Valley on Viewros. It served as the trial ground where a Chosen One would be certified, and its summit, the Pinnacle, houses the Master Teleporter together with Lamorn sleeping and healing pods. After the Great Tragedy the surviving Lamorn converted their Psy-bot servitor machines into anti-Griever soldiers that defend the tower and are launched from it in pods. Its halls include the Vestibule, the Guardian Chamber, the Statuary Hall, the Nexus of Hope and the Sanctum of the Guardians, where holograms record the Guardians of the keys.

The Master Teleporter grew from the priest Shei's insight into the structure of space and time; it was meant to carry the Lamorn legacy to a new world. Lekhash devised its sealing mechanism and the five Master Teleporter Keys, which the Lamorn entrusted to Guardians in each region of Viewros and hid in pocket dimensions reached through the Sacred Shrines. The teleporter answers only to one with psychic abilities who holds all five keys. Smaller return teleporters, such as the artifact unearthed on Tanamaar, were carried offworld by the twelve Lamorn who went searching for the Chosen One.

Samus Aran woke inside the tower after the Tanamaar explosion (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). A Guardian mechanoid guided her to the Psychic Crystal, and at the Pinnacle a statue played the recording of Chatoyant Vooloon that named her the Chosen One and told her of the keys; a teleportation orb then carried her to Fury Green. Sylux, healing in one of the pods, later seized the tower, raised a barrier around it and used it to dispatch Psy-bot Mimics against her. With the keys gathered and the barrier broken by the Federation mech Betsy, Samus and her companions returned; at the Nexus of Hope a statue delivered Chatoya's final message, and at the Pinnacle Sylux attacked. After his defeat Samus set the keys and activated the Master Teleporter. Sylux returned and damaged it; it could hold open only briefly and worked only for her. While the five Federation soldiers pinned Sylux, Samus stepped through, the only one of the stranded to leave Viewros.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://omegametroid.com/new-metroid-prime-4-beyond-details-revealed-on-the-games-japanese-website-spoiler-warning/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-lamorn-legacy/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-technology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-machines/ · https://www.ludo.guide/guide/metroid-prime-4-beyond/finale-a-battle-of-wills-chrono-tower</sub>


### Prime 4: The Lamorn

*Keys:* Lamorn, Lamorns, Chatoyant Vooloon, Chatoya, Vooloon, Great Tragedy, Green Energy, Memory Fruit, Legacy Suit, Viewros Crystal, Lamorn priest, Lamorn priests


The Lamorn were a peaceful, graceful and deeply spiritual species of high intelligence that inhabited Viewros and are now extinct. Robed, with aquatic features and no visible mouth, they communicated telepathically; Samus Aran can understand their recorded speech. A priesthood stood at the center of their culture, and while every Lamorn could manipulate psychic energy, the priests mastered it. When they first arrived on Viewros they planted the Sacred Tree, which holds the memories and knowledge of their whole people; Lamorn prayed at the altar of the Temple of Memory in Fury Green and their memories passed into the tree, which bears them as Memory Fruit.

History: in their Machine Era the Lamorn built the Great Mines, the Volt Forge factory, hover shuttles, Scout Bots and the Vi-O-La, until resources ran short. The discovery of the Viewros Crystal in the mines began the Psychic Era: a rare few were born with a circular crystal in the forehead that granted psychic and telepathic power, and processed crystals were implanted in the rest. Revered priests included Thesarh, the first priest, who communed with the Sacred Tree and founded the Temple of Memory; Shei, the only female priest, whose grasp of space and time led to the teleporter; Lekhash, the sculptor-mathematician whose equations became psychic objects, including the sealing mechanism of the Master Teleporter and its keys; and Vahkuun, inventor of the Mote-operated Psychic Lock and builder of the shrines of Sol Valley.

The Great Tragedy: seeking to restore a planet damaged by their industry, the Lamorn extracted Green Energy, a seemingly limitless fuel, from the magma of Flare Pool and fired it over Sol Valley in showers that revived flora and fauna. Overexposure reacted with the implanted crystals and twisted those Lamorn into the feral Grievers, who hunted the rest. Survivors under the priest Chatoya ran laboratories in the Ice Belt trying to reverse the change with refined Green Energy; every attempt failed, and the researcher Tahrgun's last log reads, "I am Tahrgun. And I am utterly defeated." The final thirteen Lamorn withdrew to the Chrono Tower in Sol Valley. Twelve departed through small return teleporters to other worlds to search for a prophesied Chosen One, and each died where they landed; one came to rest on Tanamaar, where the Galactic Federation later dug up its teleporter.
