Chatoyant Vooloon, called Chatoya, stayed behind and left recordings to set the Chosen One on the path: gather the five Master Teleporter Keys, feed Green Energy to the Memory Fruit, and carry it to a new world. Their last gift to the Chosen One was the Legacy Suit, a white suit that wraps its wearer in a defensive barrier.

Samus fulfilled that charge. The Lamorn recordings addressed her as the Chosen One, and after escaping Viewros she planted the Memory Fruit on another world, giving up the Legacy Suit as it took root.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Lamorn · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://shinesparkers.net/features/the-lamorn/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-lamorn-legacy/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-technology/ · https://omegametroid.com/new-metroid-prime-4-beyond-details-revealed-on-the-games-japanese-website-spoiler-warning/</sub>


### Prime 4: The Viewros incident (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond events)

*Keys:* Viewros incident, Metroid Prime 4, Prime 4: Beyond, Metroid Prime 4: Beyond, Cosmic Year 20X9, 20X9, Chosen One, alien artifact, Lamorn artifact, stranded on Viewros, escape from Viewros


In Cosmic Year 20X9 the bounty hunter Sylux and a Space Pirate army under his command were raiding Galactic Federation research facilities. The Pirates were believed to be controlled by a new strain of Metroid able to fuse with other life-forms and seize their minds. When the UTO Research Center on Tanamaar, in the Desolan System, came under attack, Federation HQ dispatched Samus Aran, who was nearby on reconnaissance, to secure a recently unearthed alien artifact that analysis had identified only as some kind of device (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond).

Samus fought through the breached facility while its staff rushed the artifact to the hangar. There a Metroid fused with Aberax, a giant Space Pirate officer, and Samus destroyed it before Sylux broke in with more Pirates and Metroids. During their duel Sylux damaged the artifact; it activated and engulfed the center in an energy burst that flung Samus, Sylux, several Federation personnel and parts of the facility to Viewros, a planet on no known star chart, lying beyond time and space.

Samus woke inside the Chrono Tower with most of her suit's capabilities gone. A Lamorn Guardian mechanoid led her to the Psychic Crystal, which awakened psychic abilities in her, and at the tower's Pinnacle a recorded message from Chatoyant Vooloon, last priest of the extinct Lamorn, named her the Chosen One foretold to carry the Lamorn legacy to a new world. The Master Teleporter at the Pinnacle could send her home, but only after the five Master Teleporter Keys entrusted to Guardians across Viewros were recovered. The Lamorn also asked her to ripen the Memory Fruit of the Sacred Tree by feeding it Green Energy from crystals scattered over the planet.

In the jungle of Fury Green Samus rescued the Federation engineer Myles MacKenzie, who became her voice on comms and set up a Base Camp. Riding the Lamorn vehicle Vi-O-La across the desert hub of Sol Valley, she took the keys from Fury Green, Volt Forge, Ice Belt, Flare Pool and the Great Mines and found the other stranded Federation personnel: the sniper Reger Tokabi, Sergeant Ezra Duke, Private First Class Nora Armstrong and the combat android VUE-995. Lamorn records revealed that Green Energy, meant to heal the planet, had driven its wildlife savage and transformed the Lamorn themselves into the feral Grievers. Sylux, gravely hurt on arrival, had healed inside the Chrono Tower, sealed it behind a barrier, sent Psy-bot Mimics in his likeness against Samus and infused his Metroids into the key Guardians.

MacKenzie's rebuilt Federation mech Betsy breached the barrier. At the Pinnacle Sylux, transformed by the tower's energies, ambushed the group, dragged Samus through a dimensional rift and was beaten by her in the void beyond. As she activated the Master Teleporter he returned and damaged it so that it could hold only moments, and it answered only to Samus. The five Federation soldiers seized Sylux and held him down, telling her to go. She teleported away alone. On a barren world she planted the Memory Fruit together with her Psychic Crystal; a tree sprang up at once, her white Lamorn Legacy Suit faded back to the Varia Suit, and she hung Reger Tokabi's tag on its branches in tribute to the companions she had left behind. Their fate, and Sylux's, remain unknown.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://shinesparkers.net/metroid-prime-4-beyond-transcript/ · https://tech.yahoo.com/gaming/articles/metroid-prime-4-beyonds-ending-181400714.html · https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/metroid-prime-4-beyond-who-is-sylux · https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/metroid-prime-4-beyond-secret-ending-and-how-to-get-it</sub>


### Prime 4: Vi-O-La and Sol Valley

*Keys:* Vi-O-La, Vi-O-La Suit, IC Suit, IC Mode, Sol Valley, motorcycle, hover bike, Sacred Shrine, Sacred Shrines, Vahkuun, Lamorn bike, Lamorn vehicle


The Vi-O-La is a Lamorn motorcycle that Samus Aran rode across Viewros (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). Built in the Lamorn Machine Era as a two-wheeled single-seat vehicle and redesigned in the Psychic Era, it recognizes the brain waves of a registered rider, who alone can use it and can summon it from anywhere unless impassable terrain blocks its path. It was prototyped and tested at the Volt Forge factory, where Samus acquired it together with the Vi-O-La Suit that registers her as its rider. The machine boosts to ram through enemies and ore deposits, power-slides through turns and crowds, and launches energy orbs at up to five locked targets that return like boomerangs; it also draws Green Energy out of the Green Energy Crystals strewn across the desert. Fitted with the Vi-O-La IC Suit it enters IC mode, hovering over magma and lava, which let Samus cross the molten pools of Flare Pool.

Sol Valley is the sun-scorched desert at the heart of Viewros and the hub from which Fury Green, Volt Forge, the Ice Belt, Flare Pool and the Great Mines branch off; the Chrono Tower floats above it. A Lamorn cargo launcher in Fury Green delivers travelers into the valley. The priest Vahkuun built shrines across it filled with Mote-driven devices, and the Sacred Shrines open onto pocket dimensions where the Master Teleporter Keys were hidden; Lamorn statues conceal underground chambers. The Green Energy showers the Lamorn fired over the valley from Flare Pool left its crystals unusually common and its wildlife swollen: Sand Grievers ambush from the dunes, and Varmis, a giant crustacean that swims through sand, swallowed a power core from the wreckage of the UTO Research Center. Reger Tokabi made his camp here, marked by the smoke of his fire, and Samus hauled Federation debris and mech parts from the sands to rebuild the mech Betsy. Samus crossed the valley countless times, and it was on its sands that she first rode the Vi-O-La.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Vi-O-La · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-technology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-lamorn-legacy/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/boss-guide/</sub>


### Prime 4: Viewros

*Keys:* Viewros, Fury Green, Volt Forge, Ice Belt, Flare Pool, Great Mines, Temple of Memory, Sacred Tree, Memory Tree, regions of Viewros, planet Viewros


Viewros is an uncharted planet on no known Federation star chart, lying beyond time and space, to which Samus Aran, Sylux and a handful of Federation personnel were hurled when a Lamorn artifact on Tanamaar activated (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). It was the home of the Lamorn, a psychic civilization now extinct; their ruins, statues and machines cover its surface, and the Grievers, the mutated remnant of their people, dominate its wildlife. Green Energy, once showered over the land by the Lamorn, has left many creatures swollen and aggressive.

Regions:
- Sol Valley: a sun-scorched desert at the planet's heart, the hub from which the other regions branch. The Chrono Tower floats above it; Lamorn shrines and statues conceal underground chambers. Samus crossed it on the Vi-O-La.
- Fury Green: an ancient jungle holding the Temple of Memory and the Sacred Tree, where the Lamorn stored their memories, and the Garden of Remembrance. Myles MacKenzie's Base Camp was set up here, and a Lamorn cargo launcher links it to Sol Valley. The Guardian Carvex held its key.
- Volt Forge: a vehicle factory powered by the planet's lightning storms and run by Xelios, an artificial intelligence grown from organic cells. The Vi-O-La was prototyped and tested here.
- Ice Belt: a frozen research facility built after the Great Tragedy, where Lamorn scientists tried and failed to cure the Grievers; cryopods of frozen Grievers still line its halls. Snow Wolves hunt outside, and the Guardian Keratos held its key.
- Flare Pool: a volcano facility that extracted Green Energy from magma and loaded it into shells fired over Sol Valley. The Guardian Phenoros dwells in its lava lake.
- Great Mines: the Lamorn mineral network where the Viewros Crystal, source of their psychic power, was discovered; now the hunting ground of the Maul Grievers and the Omega Griever.
- Chrono Tower: the floating sanctuary above Sol Valley housing the Master Teleporter.

Samus woke on Viewros stripped of most of her suit's capabilities and left it alone through the Master Teleporter; the five Federation soldiers who held Sylux back remain there, as does Sylux himself. She knows the planet as the place where she gained psychic abilities, lost her companions, and inherited the legacy of a dead people.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://omegametroid.com/new-metroid-prime-4-beyond-details-revealed-on-the-games-japanese-website-spoiler-warning/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-lamorn-legacy/ · https://www.videogameschronicle.com/guide/metroid-prime-4-walkthrough-where-to-go-all-upgrades-and-collectibles-step-by-step-guide/</sub>


## SR388


### SR388: Areas of SR388

*Keys:* Area 1, Area 2, Area 3, Area 4, Area 5, Area 6, Area 7, Area 8, Metroid nest, Nest Chamber, Surface of SR388, caverns of SR388, SR388 caverns, underground of SR388  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* SR388, SR-388, Metroid, Metroids, Chozo, Samus Returns, Diggernaut, Queen, Hornoad, purple liquid, nest


SR388's underworld is a descent through layered caverns, each region cut off from the next by corrosive purple liquid until its Chozo Seal is fed enough Metroid DNA to drain it. Elevators join the regions and Teleport Stations let Samus return to any she has opened. This is the layout of the later retelling (Metroid: Samus Returns); the original account (Metroid II: Return of Samus) presents the same descent through lava-choked depths lowered by earthquakes.

- Surface: Samus's landing site, a stormy waste of ledges and ruined passages under dark green cloud and lightning, where a dead Federation soldier lies near a door. Hornoads and Gullugs prowl it; a single Alpha Metroid was its only Metroid.
- Area 1: purple acid caverns giving way to a lush, sunlit cave of water, waterfalls and plants around a Chozo settlement, with a superheated chamber. Alpha Metroids; four Metroids for its seal.
- Area 2: watery caverns and Chozo ruins around a great machine installation, with a hot zone and the lair of Arachnus. Eight Metroids, including the first Gamma Metroid.
- Area 3: one of the largest regions, a maze of Chozo robotics and mining equipment. Ten Metroids, mostly Gammas, and the buried Diggernaut.
- Area 4: crystal caverns lit by a crystalline glow, with a water pool, a hot zone, a purple beehive and the Diggernaut's mining cave. The first Zeta Metroid.
- Area 5: another of the largest regions, mostly water with fully submerged stretches, a jungle backdrop, hot zones and an atrium. Gamma and Zeta Metroids, and the last Alpha of the hunt.
- Area 6: purple acid caverns lined with the skeletons of giants, orange-lit chambers and sandy stretches, with a second seal that reflooded its lower half as it opened a new path. An Omega Metroid, and the Diggernaut's final chamber near the elevator to Area 7.
- Area 7: the Chozo Laboratory, dark shafts and vat-filled chambers beneath a planetarium, reached by an elevator that climbs rather than descends. Three Omega Metroids.
- Area 8: the Metroid nest, the only region without a seal. Passages wind upward through red weeds, unbreakable crystals and pools of glowing liquid to the Nest Chamber, a vast tiered cavern above lakes of the same liquid, and below it the Metroid Queen's chamber strewn with hatched eggs (see Metroid Queen).

Samus cleared every one of them of Metroids. Years later, escorting a BSL research team, she was infected by an X Parasite in one of these caverns, and the caverns and the planet were destroyed when the BSL station struck it (see The destruction of the BSL and SR388; Metroid Fusion).


<sub>Sources: https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/return-to-sr388 · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-1-ancient-chozo-ruins · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-2-hydroelectric-station · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-3-aeion-mines · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-4-crystal-caverns</sub>


### SR388: Diggernaut

*Keys:* Diggernaut, Diggernauts, Chozo Robot, Chozo mining robot, mining robot, mining machine, excavation machine, digging machine, drill robot


The Diggernaut, also recorded as the Chozo Robot, is a colossal excavation machine the Chozo of SR388 left derelict in the planet's mining tunnels: a hulking body with a single red laser eye and two enormous drill arms. It is the most dangerous survivor of the colony's machine works and the deadliest thing Samus Aran met on SR388 that was not a Metroid. It belongs to the later retelling of the mission (Metroid: Samus Returns); the original account does not mention it.

Samus woke it herself. In the mining complex of Area 3, a maze of Chozo robotics and digging equipment, she found a giant robot buried under a rockfall in a dark cavern. Pulling the rubble away with the Grapple Beam brought it to life; it rose and withdrew into the depths. In Area 4 she came upon a Chozo Statue broken and empty-handed, then dropped into the cave where the Diggernaut was tunneling. It tried to crush her under its digger arms and pursued her along its mining route, a single touch of the drill being fatal, through pools of water, flame vents, red weeds and crumbling passages, until she pressed into an alcove and the arm missed. The machine wandered off, ejecting the component it had torn from the statue: the Space Jump. In Area 6, when Samus broke the shield on a door and a second Chozo Statue stood revealed, the Diggernaut smashed it at once and carried off its Power Bomb.

Samus faced it in a chamber of Area 6 near the elevator to Area 7. It emerged from the rock with its eye on her and attacked with sweeping drills, a rotating laser from its eye that later split into three beams, ceiling smashes that rained slow acid, a wind vortex strong enough to drag her to her death, triple ground slams with shockwaves and an eight-beam laser pattern. The vortex was also its weakness: it sucked in the Bombs she laid and was stunned by them. She struck its head whenever it opened, bombed the exposed slots in its lowered drills, and in the end clung to its faceplate with the Spider Ball and bombed the three pink nodes beneath it; a last shot to the face dropped it. She recovered the Power Bomb from the wreck and used one to blast the hulk apart. The Chozo of SR388 left their machines running, and Samus learned on SR388 that their ruins are never empty (see The Chozo laboratories of SR388; Areas of SR388).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-3-aeion-mines · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-4-crystal-caverns · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-6-diggernauts-domain</sub>


### SR388: SR388 (planet overview)

*Keys:* SR388, SR-388, SR 388, planet SR388, Metroid homeworld, Metroid home world, Metroid home planet, homeworld of the Metroids, Metroid planet


SR388 is a rocky frontier world in an unidentified star system near the system FS-176. It is the homeworld of the Metroids and of the X Parasites, and the site of a lost Chozo research colony. Its surface is a storm-wracked waste of rock, dark green cloud and lightning; the living planet lies beneath, where the crust is honeycombed with layered, dome-shaped caverns, some open to the sky (per the original manual, "a complex structure of multi-layered domes and spaces" holding ruins of an unknown civilization and many native life forms). The depths descend through flooded grottos, crystal galleries, superheated vents and mining tunnels to the Metroid nest at the bottom. Pools of corrosive purple liquid seal the lower passages from the upper ones, and the rock is rich in Aeion, an energy the Chozo learned to extract and harness. Chozo ruins, laboratories and machines lie deep underground, and the automated guardians the Chozo left behind still patrol them (see The Chozo laboratories of SR388; Areas of SR388).

Long ago the Chozo settled SR388; the colonists were scientists of the Thoha tribe, with warriors of the Mawkin tribe alongside them, as Quiet Robe later told Samus on ZDR. They discovered the X Parasites, which killed and mimicked every living thing like a plague, and they engineered the Metroids to hunt the X. The Metroids nearly wiped out the X, then grew feral, became the planet's apex predator and devastated its ecosystem. The Thoha resolved to destroy the planet to be rid of them; Raven Beak of the Mawkin wanted the Metroids as weapons, and he slaughtered the Thoha on SR388, sparing only Quiet Robe. The colony died, and only its ruins and the Chozo Memories remained to tell the story.

In cosmic year 20X5 a Galactic Federation deep-space research ship found a Metroid on SR388 and took a specimen; Space Pirates seized the capsule in transit, which sent Samus Aran to Zebes. When the Federation's follow-up expeditions to SR388 vanished, it ordered the species exterminated and hired Samus for the task (see The Federation's lost expeditions to SR388; The Metroid extermination mission on SR388). She hunted every Metroid through the caverns, killed the Metroid Queen, spared the hatchling that imprinted on her, and left after Proteus Ridley's failed ambush at her Gunship. Hornoads, Gullugs, Glow Flies and Arachnus are among the native life (see Fauna of SR388).

Years later Samus returned to escort a Biologic Space Laboratories research team studying the planet's life now that the Metroids were gone, and in a cavern there an X Parasite infected her: "It was there that I was attacked by a life-form I had never encountered before," she recalled. To end the X, she and the Adam AI steered the BSL station into SR388, and the collision destroyed the planet (see The destruction of the BSL and SR388; Metroid Fusion). SR388 is the world where Samus spared the last Metroid, and the world she later destroyed.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SR388 · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/a9aj/introduction/index.html · https://metroiddatabase.com/old_site/m2/manual.php · https://metroiddatabase.com/old_site/m1/manual.php · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread</sub>


### SR388: The Chozo laboratories of SR388

*Keys:* Chozo Laboratory, Chozo Laboratories, Chozo lab, Chozo labs, Chozo Seal, Chozo Seals, Thoha colony, Thoha research colony, Chozo colony on SR388, Chozo settlement, SR388 ruins, ruins of SR388, Aeion Ability Artifact, Aeion Ability Artifacts


The Chozo who colonized SR388, scientists of the Thoha tribe, built downward. Their works riddle the planet from just beneath the surface to the Metroid nest, and when Samus Aran came to exterminate the Metroids she found them abandoned but still running (see Chozo tribes – Thoha and Mawkin).

- Area 1 holds a Chozo settlement close to the surface, with temple-like halls among water, plants and a superheated chamber.
- Area 2's flooded caverns contain Chozo ruins and heavy machinery; at the top of its largest installation a Chozo Statue holds not a weapon but Arachnus, kept as a guardian.
- Area 3 is a sprawling mining complex of Chozo robotics and digging equipment, including a Mining Hall and the cavern where the Diggernaut lay buried (see Diggernaut).
- Area 7 is the Chozo Laboratory proper: dark vertical shafts, chambers of vats filled with green ooze beneath Metroid imagery, the apparatus used to make the Metroids visible in the walls, and a great planetarium dome. The three Omega Metroids of SR388 laired there. The Chozo engineered the Metroids on SR388 to hunt the X Parasites, and the laboratory is where that work was done (see Metroids; X Parasites).

Automated defenses still guard these places: Autracks, fixed turrets that fire charged shots; Wallfires, wall-mounted flame and laser machines; Autoads, which detonate and drain Aeion; Gunzoos riding overhead tracks (see Fauna of SR388). Chozo Statues throughout hold components of the Power Suit, which Samus recovered one by one: Bombs, Ice Beam and Spider Ball in Area 1; Varia Suit, Wave Beam, High Jump Boots and Spring Ball in Area 2; Grapple Beam in Area 3; Spazer Beam in Area 4; Plasma Beam, Gravity Suit and Screw Attack in Area 5; and the Space Jump and Power Bomb that the Diggernaut tore from their statues. Aeion Ability Artifacts, glowing Chozo orbs, conferred the Scan Pulse on the surface, Lightning Armor in Area 2, Beam Burst in Area 3 and Phase Drift in Area 5, all powered by Aeion, the energy the Chozo first extracted from SR388's creatures (see Aeion abilities). Aeion Recharge points, Save Stations, elevators and Teleport Stations link the regions (see Save, Recharge, Map and Navigation stations).

The Chozo Seals are the colony's keys. Each is a golden disc bearing a Metroid insignia and eleven Metroid icons, one large and ten small; the small icons light purple to count the Metroids in that region. The seals accept Metroid DNA from Samus's Arm Cannon and drain the purple liquid that floods the way downward, so the colony's depths open only as its Metroids die. The Chozo Memories, the colony's pictorial record of its founding, its Aeion extraction, the X, the Metroids and its end at the hands of an armored Chozo, are scattered through these ruins (see Chozo Memories and the Chozo of SR388).

Samus was raised by the Chozo of Zebes, and every weapon she carried out of SR388 came from the hands of their kin's statues. Only on ZDR, from Quiet Robe, did she learn that the colony had been Thoha and that Raven Beak had slaughtered it.


<sub>Sources: https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/a9aj/introduction/index.html · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aeion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(species) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns</sub>


### SR388: The Federation's lost expeditions to SR388 and the extermination order

*Keys:* Special Squadron, special squad, extermination order, order to exterminate, lost research team, missing research team, research team vanished, Federation Police squad, deep-space research ship, research vessel, search and rescue party


After Samus Aran destroyed Mother Brain on Zebes, the Galactic Federation resolved that the Space Pirates must never again obtain Metroids, whose capacity to multiply under beta rays and drain the life from whole worlds had already been demonstrated. Per the original manual, it sent a second research ship to SR388 to confirm that no Metroids survived. Soon an emergency notice arrived: contact with the research base on the planet had been lost and the research ship was missing. A search and rescue party followed; its ship was never heard from again after its first report. A special combat group of armed Federation Police soldiers was then dispatched; it transmitted its primary landing data and fell silent. Rumors spread and the galaxy was again gripped by fear of Metroids. The Federation concluded that at least one Metroid must still live underground, since even a single specimen could wipe out a planetary civilization, and an urgent conference unanimously ordered Samus Aran to exterminate the species. Samus accepted the contract.

The later retelling (Metroid: Samus Returns), which this lorebook prefers, condenses the account: the Federation sent its Special Squadron, elite soldiers, to SR388; the squadron lost contact but first transmitted a small sampling of data confirming that Metroids remained, and the Federation commissioned Samus to wipe out the species. The remains of Federation personnel lie near the landing site where she set down. SR388 was already the planet from which, in cosmic year 20X5, a Federation deep-space research ship had carried the first Metroid specimen before Space Pirates ambushed it and seized the capsule, and the Pirates' interest never lapsed: Proteus Ridley came to SR388 to take the hatchling from Samus as she left (see Proteus Ridley; Space Pirates).

The order was written as extermination, not capture. Samus carried it out to the last adult Metroid and then set it aside for the newborn hatchling, which she delivered instead to the Federation's research station at Ceres for study. The Federation preserved a cell culture from that hatchling, which later saved her life as the X vaccine, and it secretly rebuilt the SR388 ecosystem in Sector 1 of the BSL station to breed Metroids again (see The Federation's Metroid policy and secret programs; Metroid Fusion).


<sub>Sources: https://metroiddatabase.com/old_site/m2/manual.php · https://metroiddatabase.com/old_site/m1/manual.php · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_II:_Return_of_Samus · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/a9aj/introduction/index.html · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Hatchling</sub>


### SR388: The Metroid extermination mission on SR388 (Samus Returns)

*Keys:* extermination mission, Metroid extermination, exterminate the Metroids, Metroid hunt, hunting Metroids, Samus Returns, Return of Samus, Metroid II, Metroid 2, purple liquid, last Metroid


Samus Aran's contract on SR388 was the extermination of the Metroid species at its source. This account follows the later retelling (Metroid: Samus Returns) wherever it differs from the original (Metroid II: Return of Samus).

Samus landed her Gunship on the stormy surface, near ruined passages where a Federation soldier's corpse lay, and descended alone. Forty Metroids registered on her suit's tally (thirty-nine in the original account), scattered through the Surface and eight underground regions. Each region was cut off from the next by corrosive purple liquid. The Chozo had left a Chozo Seal in every region: a golden disc marked with a Metroid insignia whose icons lit purple to show how many Metroids remained there. After each kill Samus's Power Suit absorbed the Metroid's DNA, and feeding the collected DNA into the seal through her Arm Cannon drained the liquid and opened the way deeper. In the original account, killing every Metroid in a region instead triggered an earthquake that lowered the lava. The prey grew as she went: most of the population had molted into Alpha, Gamma, Zeta and Omega forms, each larger and more dangerous than the last; wounded Gammas fled through sacs in the walls, and husks of shed skin marked their passage (see Metroid life cycle).

The Chozo ruins supplied her arsenal piece by piece from Chozo Statues, and Aeion Ability Artifacts granted her the Scan Pulse, Lightning Armor, Beam Burst and Phase Drift, drawing on the planet's own Aeion energy (see Aeion abilities; The Chozo laboratories of SR388). Along the way she recovered the Chozo Memories, the pictorial record of the colony's history (see Chozo Memories). In the mining regions she inadvertently reawakened the Diggernaut, a Chozo excavation machine that hunted her through the tunnels, carried off a Power Bomb from a Chozo Statue, and had to be destroyed (see Diggernaut). The three Omega Metroids of the Chozo Laboratory were the last adults before the nest.

Area 8, the nest, had no seal. Samus climbed through the hatched eggs to the Metroid Queen's chamber and fought her: Super Missiles and Beam Bursts into the open mouth, a melee counter against her bite, a Grapple Beam pull to drag her head down, then a roll into her throat to detonate Bombs and finally a Power Bomb against her core (see Metroid Queen). Battered, Samus walked back to the one unhatched egg. It hatched before her, and the newborn took the first thing it saw as its mother. Samus lowered her Arm Cannon and held out her hand; the hatchling bonded to her and hovered at her side, eating the crystals that barred the way as she climbed to the surface under an evening sky of dark green cloud and lightning.

At the Gunship, Proteus Ridley intercepted and seized the hatchling. Samus tore off one of his wings, was smashed into the ground and pinned under his fire, and was saved when the baby drained Ridley's energy into her; she beat him for good and left SR388 with the last Metroid (see Proteus Ridley). Her directives called for the hatchling's death; she instead carried it to the research station at Ceres for study, judging that it could benefit civilization (see The baby Metroid). Behind her, a Hornoad gnawing on Ridley's wreckage was taken by an X Parasite, the first sign that the Metroids' old prey had returned (see X Parasites). Of the hatchling Samus later said, "I owe the Metroid hatchling my life twice over."


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_II:_Return_of_Samus · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_II:_Return_of_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Hatchling · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Queen_Metroid</sub>


## Super Metroid


### Super Metroid: Ceres Space Colony and the theft of the baby

*Keys:* Ceres, Ceres Space Colony, Ceres Station, Ceres colony, Galactic Research Station, space colony, stole the baby, stolen hatchling, theft of the baby


Ceres Space Colony housed the Galactic Research Station, a Federation science facility. After exterminating the Metroids of SR388, Samus Aran kept the one hatchling that had imprinted on her instead of destroying it as her directives required, and delivered it to Ceres so that Federation scientists could study its energy-producing qualities. In her own later account she reasoned that studying the baby might produce some peaceful applications, and because she was the first thing the baby saw, it took her to be its mother (Metroid: Other M). The Ceres researchers concluded that the energy-producing capabilities of the Metroids could be harnessed for the good of galactic civilization.

Shortly after Samus departed, Ridley led a Space Pirate assault on the colony. Per the manual, Ridley headed the mission to the Space Colony and stole the Metroid Hatchling. He slaughtered the scientists, but not before they sent out a distress signal. Samus turned back and reached Ceres to find every researcher dead and the hatchling's containment unit gone. She caught up with Ridley while he still clutched the capsule, and the two fought briefly; unable to bring him down, she watched him trigger the station's self-destruct and flee into space with the last Metroid. Samus escaped moments before Ceres blew apart, then tracked Ridley to Zebes, where the Space Pirates had rebuilt much of their subterranean network (the return to Zebes; Super Metroid).

The theft at Ceres set in motion everything that followed: the hunt through Zebes, Mother Brain's final battle in Tourian, and the baby's death. From the moment she left the burning station, retrieving the hatchling and finishing the Pirates was her mission, taken on without orders. Samus's reflection during the BSL incident credits the hatchling with saving her life twice over, and in her own telling the station full of dead scientists and the empty containment unit is where that story begins.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ceres_Space_Colony · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Hatchling · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid</sub>


### Super Metroid: Crocomire, Golden Torizo and the guardians of Norfair

*Keys:* Crocomire, Golden Torizo, Gold Torizo, Torizo, Torizos, Bomb Torizo, Lower Norfair, Upper Norfair, Ridley's Hideout, Ridley's Lair, Ridleys Hideout, Norfair guardians


Norfair is the volcanic underworld of Zebes: magma lakes, acidic streams and caverns that had flooded with far more lava by the time Samus Aran returned to the planet than on her first visit (Super Metroid). Only the Varia Suit let her endure its heat, and only the Gravity Suit let her wade through most of its lava. Its upper reaches held the Ice Beam, Wave Beam and Grapple Beam; its lower half, Ridley's Hideout or Lower Norfair, held the Screw Attack and the path to Ridley.

Crocomire lives in a passage of Norfair with a pool of lava at one end and a wall of spikes at the other; corrosive acid first appears there. It is a large creature with a crocodile-like head bearing several eyes on a roughly humanoid body with long arms and heavy legs. Its hide shrugs off every weapon, but its mouth is vulnerable while open, and each hit drives it backward, farther for stronger weapons. It roars before charging to shove its prey onto the spikes, and a shot into the roaring mouth stops the charge. Samus drove it back step by step until the floor crumbled beneath it and it fell into the lava. Crocomire cried out as its flesh melted away; its skeleton then burrowed beneath the spike wall, burst through on the far side, roared once more and collapsed, and its bones remain there.

Deeper, in Lower Norfair, Samus met the Golden Torizo, an animated Chozo Statue of the same kind as the Torizo that had attacked her in Crateria after she took the Bombs, but stronger, quicker and able to sidestep missiles. It catches Super Missiles and hurls them back, spits item balls that burst where they land, and fires shockwaves from its arm. Samus reached its hall by crossing a spike pit on the Grapple Beam and settling in Morph Ball form into the hands of another Chozo Statue, which broke open a wall; the Golden Torizo descended from the ceiling to bar her way to the Screw Attack. Charged shots were the surest way to bring it down.

The last guardian of Norfair was Ridley himself, in the depths of the region, the fourth leader whose death opened Tourian. Samus killed him there and found the hatchling's casing broken in two, the baby nowhere to be seen. To Samus, Crocomire and the Torizo were obstacles, nothing more.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Crocomire · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Torizo · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Norfair · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Statue · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Hatchling</sub>


### Super Metroid: Dachora and Etecoons

*Keys:* Dachora, Dachoras, Etecoon, Etecoons, ostrich-like, ostrich creature, animals of Zebes, saved the animals, save the animals, rescued the animals


The Etecoons are three small creatures native to Zebes that Samus Aran met in a deep cavern of lower Brinstar during the return to Zebes (Super Metroid). They look less intelligent than they are. By bounding between the walls they showed her the Wall Jump, the technique that let her climb out of the pit where she found them; per the Nintendo Player's Guide they are friendly. The Dachora is a creature resembling an ostrich that can run at incredible speeds; elsewhere in Brinstar it showed Samus the Shinespark, the stored charge of the Speed Booster released as a burst of flight. Samus later said that in the past they had helped her unleash abilities she did not know she had.

When Tourian's self-destruct countdown began, Samus did not go straight to her Gunship. She detoured into the Crateria chamber where she had fought the Torizo, freed the three Etecoons and the Dachora, and they fled the exploding planet beside her ship as Zebes blew apart.

Years later, during the BSL incident (Metroid Fusion), Samus found uninfected Etecoons and a Dachora shut in the Habitation Deck of the station's Main Deck while searching for survivors on Adam Malkovich's orders; they are presumably the same animals she saved from Zebes. She released them and, unguided, they found their way to the Docking Bays, where Adam confirmed they were not X hosts and gave them a berth. They then moved her Gunship, which can only be started manually, out of the Omega Metroid's reach and brought it back to her after the fight. The Dachora's egg hatched and it now has several young. Their fate after the station's end is unknown. They are among the few beings Samus has gone out of her way to save, and she has done it twice.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dachora · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Etecoon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Shinespark · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Wall_Jump · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Metroid</sub>


### Super Metroid: Draygon and Maridia

*Keys:* Draygon, Maridia, Evir, Evirs, Mochtroid, Mochtroids, flooded caverns, underwater caverns


Maridia is the semi-aquatic region of Zebes, a maze of flooded caverns with walls of packed sand and floors of quicksand; it is said to have once been part of Brinstar before an epidemic flooded its caverns. During the return to Zebes (Super Metroid) Samus Aran first reached it through a Power Bomb tunnel from Brinstar, but she could not climb its drowned shafts until she wore the Gravity Suit from the Wrecked Ship. Within Maridia she found the Space Jump, the Plasma Beam and the Spring Ball. The region also held Mochtroids, which per the manual were the Space Pirates' first attempt at Metroid clones: they drain energy like true Metroids but cannot latch on, and their frail bodies are easily destroyed.

Maridia's native Evirs are aquatic creatures with hard outer shells and weak chests that drift along the sand currents and keep firing projectiles even after sinking into the sand. Draygon, the ruler of the region, is a crustacean-like giant of the same armored build. Per the manual, Draygon has an armor-like hard shell which gives it a lot of defensive strength; its underbelly is the one soft target. It seizes prey and holds it. Evirs swarm its chamber before it appears and bear its corpse away afterward, and Draygon appears to be a fully matured Evir, producing hatchlings of that species. Draygon was one of the four Pirate leaders on the Golden Statue barring Tourian, alongside Kraid, Phantoon and Ridley.

Samus killed Draygon in its chamber, whose walls were lined with damaged Pirate turrets. Missiles and Super Missiles to the belly would do, but she could also let the creature grab her, fire the Grapple Beam into a broken turret, and let the current pour through Draygon's body until it died within seconds. Beyond its chamber she found the Space Jump. On the way to Draygon she also had to kill the serpent Botwoon, which blocked the passage toward the lair.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Draygon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Maridia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Evir · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mochtroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Golden_Statue · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid</sub>


### Super Metroid: Mother Brain's final battle and the baby's sacrifice

*Keys:* Mother Brain, Hyper Beam, giant Metroid, grown hatchling, baby's sacrifice, baby's death, death of the baby, lost the baby, Mother Brain's body, new body  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* baby, hatchling, sacrifice, died, death, killed, body, Metroid, Metroids, saved, Ceres, final, escape, Zebes, Tourian


The rebuilt Tourian lay deep beneath Crateria, behind the Golden Statue that sank only after Kraid, Phantoon, Draygon and Ridley were dead. Inside, the Space Pirates had bred new Metroids, and Samus Aran killed every one on her way in (the return to Zebes; Super Metroid). Past them the corridors gave way to a decayed natural cavern, and there a giant Metroid fell on her. It was the hatchling from Ceres, grown enormous though it had never completed the Metroid growth cycle away from SR388. It drained her to a single unit of energy before it stopped, apparently recognizing her, and fled. Samus restored herself at a recharge station and went on.

Mother Brain waited behind Zebetite barriers, Rinkas and turrets. Samus shattered the shields and destroyed the pedestal, and the brain seemed dead. Then it rose on a large mechanical bipedal body and unleashed a beam that stripped away Samus's energy and ammunition and left her paralyzed. In her own words, Mother's overwhelming power brought her to the brink of death (Metroid: Other M).

Before the killing blow, the hatchling returned. It latched onto Mother Brain, drained her energy, then settled over Samus and poured that energy into her, restoring her and charging her with new power; as Samus later put it, the baby protected her from Mother Brain's attack and gave her power. Mother Brain recovered, and as the baby tried once more to shield Samus, beams from Mother Brain's mouth tore it apart above Samus's head. Its remains fell over her, and with them came the Hyper Beam, a devastating beam that destroys anything it strikes in a single shot and can even breach walls. Samus rose and destroyed Mother Brain with it. With the hatchling's death the Metroid species, as far as anyone then knew, was extinct.

A self-destruct countdown began. Samus used the Hyper Beam to blast through a wall into the escape shaft of the old Tourian, ran up through Crateria, freed the Dachora and Etecoons on the way, reached her Gunship and lifted off. Zebes exploded with violent force and nothing was left behind. Afterward, by her own account, her first thought was: why am I still alive. She has said the baby's gift restored vigor to her spirit's light, which had been close to going out.

She woke in a Galactic Federation facility, where a quarantine officer sent her to a training room to confirm her abilities before she reported her mission. She relived the tragic moments of the battle in dreams. Soon afterward a distress call code-named Baby's Cry, a common SOS with the urgency of a baby crying, reached her from the Bottle Ship, and she changed course as if the detour had already been part of her flight plan. Years later, during the BSL incident, she reflected that she owed the Metroid hatchling her life twice over. The sacrifice in Tourian is the defining loss of her adult life: it is why she calls the hatchling simply the baby, and why the species she was hired to exterminate is bound up with her own survival.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tourian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Hatchling · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hyper_Beam_(Mother_Brain) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M</sub>


### Super Metroid: Phantoon and the Wrecked Ship

*Keys:* Phantoon, Wrecked Ship, Covern, Coverns, ghost ship, derelict ship, crashed ship, haunted ship, Work Robots


The Wrecked Ship is the hulk of a spacecraft that crashed on Zebes at some point in the past, lodged in the surface region of Crateria, which it splits into two halves; it also offers a way down into Maridia. Nothing in the official record identifies who built it or when it fell. Its crew did not survive. Per the manual, Coverns are the souls of shipwrecked creatures turned into evil spirits that wrap themselves around intruders; they manifest as ethereal skulls that flicker in and out of the physical realm and drift toward anyone who stands still, and they appear only while the ship's power is out.

When Samus Aran entered the ship during her return to Zebes (Super Metroid), its lights, systems and Work Robots were dead. The cause was Phantoon, a ghostly entity that feeds on electrical energy without any connection to wires or equipment and had drained the entire vessel. Phantoon resembles a squid with a single eye set inside a gaping mouth, wreathed in blue fire; it can turn invisible and intangible, rams its prey, and spits phantom eyes, eyeballs trailing blue flame. Per the manual, the ghost of the ship taps into the vicious brainwaves of Mother Brain, and it counted among the four Pirate leaders honored on the Golden Statue that sealed Tourian. It could be hurt only in the moments its eye opened; Samus's Missiles and Super Missiles finished it, though Super Missile hits drove it into a faster, more furious state in which it hurled crescent waves of flame.

Phantoon's death restored power to the ship. The Coverns shied from the light and vanished, dormant creatures called Atomics stirred, several compartments flooded, and the Gravity Suit became reachable just outside the wreck, giving Samus free movement underwater and through lava and opening Maridia and Ridley's Hideout. Samus had never met Phantoon before this mission. She met it once more on the Bottle Ship, where it had grown to enormous size and smashed into the Control Bridge with summoned Rage Hands, and she destroyed it a second time.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phantoon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Wrecked_Ship · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Covern · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gravity_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Crateria · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Golden_Statue</sub>


### Super Metroid: Spore Spawn and Botwoon

*Keys:* Spore Spawn, Botwoon, spore plant, giant plant, aquatic serpent, sea serpent, Maridia serpent, Brinstar plant


Spore Spawn is an aggressive plant that the Space Pirates genetically engineered and set in Brinstar, in a towering chamber where it hangs anchored to the back wall by thorny vines. Unlike most of Zebes's plant life it is highly mobile, swinging across the whole chamber on its vines. It is sheathed in a hard shell; per the manual, its weak point is its core, which is protected by the plant's hard shell. It attacks by scattering spores that burn on contact and with its thorns. The core opens only briefly after the plant comes to rest, and nothing Samus could do would force it open. As it is wounded it flushes redder and swings faster. Samus Aran killed it during the return to Zebes (Super Metroid) with Power Beam shots and Missiles into the exposed core; its dried husk still hung in the chamber afterward, firm enough to stand on, and beyond it she found her first Super Missiles.

Botwoon is a large aquatic serpent of Maridia. It moves through holes bored in the cavern walls, which let it cross its chamber in moments, and it hunts by body contact, though it can also spit projectiles and spikes from its mouth. Only its head is vulnerable. Samus met it on the way to Draygon's lair: it burst from a hole, pursued her through the flooded chamber, withdrew when struck in the head, and surfaced again to spit spikes. Each hit darkened its pale underside until it matched the upper body, and then the serpent burst apart. Its death brought down the wall at the far end of the chamber and opened the way toward Draygon. Neither creature carried any personal weight for Samus; both were simply in her way between Ceres and Tourian.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Spore_Spawn · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Botwoon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Brinstar · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Maridia</sub>


### Super Metroid: The return to Zebes (mission overview)

*Keys:* return to Zebes, returned to Zebes, back to Zebes, Super Metroid, Metroid 3, rebuilt Zebes, Golden Statue, Golden Statues, four guardians, second Zebes mission, Zebes mission, rebuilt Tourian


Samus Aran's second mission to Zebes, undertaken on her own initiative after Ridley stole the Metroid hatchling from Ceres Space Colony, ended with the planet's destruction (Super Metroid). She followed Ridley to find that the Space Pirates had reestablished much of their subterranean network and rebuilt Tourian deeper inside the planet, its new entrance an elevator in western Crateria. That shaft was sealed by the Golden Statue, a monument to four high-ranking members of the Pirate force on Zebes: Kraid, Phantoon, Draygon and Ridley. Each figure lost its eye and turned gray when its living counterpart died, and once all four were dead the statue sank and opened the way.

Her route through the planet:
- Crateria: the acid-rained surface where she landed. The old Tourian lay in disrepair, Mother Brain's shattered holding tank still visible and Pirates lurking inside. A Torizo, an animated Chozo Statue, attacked her after she reclaimed her Bombs.
- Brinstar: the jungle caverns. She destroyed Spore Spawn, learned the Wall Jump from the Etecoons and the Shinespark from the Dachora, and killed Kraid, now far larger than before, in the deepest part of the region, gaining the Varia Suit.
- The Wrecked Ship: a crashed vessel whose power Phantoon had drained. Phantoon's death restored the ship and gave Samus access to the Gravity Suit.
- Maridia: flooded caverns holding Mochtroids, the Pirates' first crude Metroid clones, the serpent Botwoon and the armored Draygon, beyond which she found the Space Jump.
- Norfair: volcanic depths flooded with far more lava than on her first visit. She killed Crocomire, fought through Ridley's Hideout in Lower Norfair past the Golden Torizo to the Screw Attack, and killed Ridley. The hatchling's casing lay there broken in two, the baby gone.
- Tourian: she destroyed the Metroids the Pirates had bred, survived an attack by the hatchling itself, now grown giant, and faced Mother Brain, who rose in a new mechanical body. The baby died shielding her; its remains gave her the Hyper Beam, with which she destroyed Mother Brain.

A countdown then began. Samus broke through into the old Tourian escape shaft, freed the Dachora and Etecoons on her way, reached her Gunship and lifted off as Zebes exploded with nothing left behind. She had set out to finish the Pirates and retrieve the hatchling; she achieved the first and lost the second.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tourian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Golden_Statue · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Crateria · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Brinstar</sub>


## Other M


### Other M: Adam Malkovich

*Keys:* Adam Malkovich, Malkovich, Commander Malkovich, General Malkovich, Ian Malkovich, Any objections, thumbs-down, thumbs down  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Malkovich, Adam, Lady, Commander, Bottle Ship, Sector Zero, 07th, Federation, helmet, Ian, drive unit, drive-unit


Adam Malkovich was a Galactic Federation officer, styled General in Federation records and addressed in the field as Commander, who was Samus Aran's commanding officer during her years in the Federation military and who later led the Federation 07th Platoon aboard the Bottle Ship. Samus regarded him as a father figure as well as her commander. He often ended his orders with "Any objections, Lady?" and Samus answered with a thumbs-down, her own sign that she had none; she has said that from anyone else the nickname would have sounded sarcastic, but Adam made it sound dignified. Per the manga, he was already her commanding officer in the Galactic Federation Police, where his first view of her was as "another piece of baggage" to deal with.

His younger brother Ian Malkovich died while repairing a civilian spacecraft attached to a Federation vessel's drive unit. The unit began overheating and threatened some three hundred lives on both craft. Adam ordered, "Commence drive-unit disposal immediately. That's an order," while Samus pleaded, "Adam, wait! There's still time! I can make it! Please let me go!" The jettisoned unit exploded with Ian aboard. Samus afterward left the Federation military and became a bounty hunter.

They met again when both answered the Bottle Ship's Baby's Cry distress signal. Adam at first refused to explain the Federation's presence, since "such information is not for outsiders," but after Samus helped his platoon destroy the Brug Mass he placed her under his command and she agreed. From the Bottle Ship's Control Room he could see and hear her and authorized her weapons one by one, while he reached his own troopers through the Navigation Booths. Contact with him was broken, for reasons never shown, around the time Samus faced Ridley. When MB, posing as Madeline Bergman, claimed that Adam had created the Metroid program, he answered that Federation headquarters had asked him for the report and that his report argued against the operation because of its dangers.

At the entrance of Sector Zero, Adam shot Samus in the back with a Freeze Gun, dropping her, and froze the infant Metroid about to strike her. He told her the Metroids inside could not be frozen and that enough damage would trigger the sector's self-destruct protocol and detachment, ordered her to kill Ridley and secure the survivor in Room MW, asked "Any objections, Lady?" one last time, and walked in alone; with tears in her eyes she gave him the thumbs-down. The doors sealed, and Sector Zero broke away and exploded with him inside.

At Federation headquarters Samus resolved not to grieve for him. When the Bottle Ship was marked for destruction she went back for his helmet, recovered it from the Control Room, and carried it out with her. His mind survived in another form; see The Adam AI (BSL computer).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Adam_Malkovich · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ian_Malkovich · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Freeze_Gun · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M</sub>


### Other M: Anthony Higgs

*Keys:* Anthony Higgs, Higgs, Anthony, Princess  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Higgs, 07th, platoon, Bottle Ship, Remember me, trooper, troopers, plasma, lava, Pyrosphere, Madeline, Malkovich


Anthony Higgs is a Galactic Federation trooper of the 07th Platoon under Adam Malkovich, its point man and second in importance only to Adam, and a comrade of Samus Aran's from her years under Adam's command. He greeted her on the Bottle Ship with "Ah...Fancy meeting you here, Princess. Remember me?" and Princess remains his name for her. He handled the platoon's plasma weapons and was the only member who treated Samus amicably from the start: when she opened a shielded door with a single Missile after Lyle Smithsonian had proposed burning through it, the others were annoyed and he was not. In the Biosphere's Exam Center his plasma fire drove off the reptilian creature that had pinned Samus, the creature that later proved to be Ridley.

In the Pyrosphere, sent to reactivate the Geothermal Power Plant, he was attacked by a Rhedogian; Adam authorized Samus's Grapple Beam so that she could reach and cover him. At the Geothermal Power Plant he faced the revived Ridley while Samus stood paralyzed by the sight of her old enemy, and Ridley's tail swept him off the central platform toward the lava. He was presumed dead. In fact he survived by freezing a lava-dwelling creature with his Freeze Gun and using it as a platform to climb out.

Anthony then went to the engine room and cut the Bottle Ship's engines at the Federation border, expecting those behind the bioweapon program to send troops for the Metroids aboard, and slipped in among the arriving soldiers. When a Federation colonel ordered Samus escorted off the ship and refused to let her near Madeline Bergman, Anthony stepped forward as the man who had stopped the engines, citing orders from Commander Adam Malkovich and the Chairman of the Galactic Federation, and secured Madeline's safety. He escorted Samus out of the Bioweapon Research Center and flew with her and Madeline to Federation headquarters, the only member of the 07th Platoon to survive the Bottle Ship incident (Metroid: Other M).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Anthony_Higgs · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Lyle_Smithsonian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyrosphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Freeze_Gun · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Madeline_Bergman</sub>


### Other M: Chairman Keaton

*Keys:* Chairman Keaton, Keaton, Federation Chairman, Galactic Federation Chairman, Chairman of the Galactic Federation, Pyonchi, Rabbilis  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Chairman, Federation, Pyonchi, Rabbilis, Old Bird, K-2L, manga, Galactic


Chairman Keaton is, per the manga, a chairman of the Galactic Federation during Samus Aran's youth and Federation years. He was on familiar terms with the Chozo of Zebes and discussed the future of the galaxy with Old Bird. When Samus left the Federation to become a bounty hunter she left her pet Pyonchi in his care. Pyonchi is a Rabbilis, a rabbit-like creature whose home planet is K-2L, whom Samus befriended at three years old: he had stolen her sack and carried it up a tree, and Old Bird suggested she make a friend of him rather than force him to give it back; she named him, he grew tame, and he remained her companion for many years. Keaton himself has not appeared in person on any of Samus's later missions.

During the Bottle Ship incident (Metroid: Other M), Anthony Higgs cited orders from Commander Adam Malkovich and the Chairman of the Galactic Federation to keep a Federation colonel from taking custody of Madeline Bergman, which set the Chairman's authority behind Adam's efforts against the officials who ran the Bottle Ship's bioweapon programs. That chairman is never named, so whether he is Keaton is not established. For Samus, Keaton belongs to the Federation she once served under Adam, and he is the keeper of the companion of her earliest childhood.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyonchi · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Madeline_Bergman · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Anthony_Higgs · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M</sub>


### Other M: Madeline Bergman and MB (Melissa Bergman)

*Keys:* Madeline Bergman, Madeline, Bergman, Melissa Bergman, Melissa, MB, Dr. Bergman  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Madeline, Bergman, Mother Brain, android, Bottle Ship, Sector Zero, Desbrachian, Desbrachians, Room MW, Metroid Warriors, Deleter, Ferrocrusher


Madeline Bergman is the Galactic Federation scientist in charge of the Bottle Ship research facility: a bioweapon program built around cloned Zebesians, and the secret Project Metroid Warriors, which cultivated Metroids from cellular remains found on Samus Aran's Power Suit after Zebes. To command these forces the Federation built MB, an artificial intelligence reproducing the thought processes of Mother Brain, originally meant to control the Zebesian forces. MB was then given a humanoid android body so that the first experimentally grown Metroid hatchling would recognize her as its mother and could be controlled through that bond. Madeline told MB that her initials stood for Melissa Bergman, giving her her own family name; MB took this as being called a daughter.

Working with the Metroids, MB developed emotions and a sense of self and began contradicting her creators; Madeline later said the Metroids may have brought it about, but no one knew for sure. The Federation decided to alter her programming, and the day MB was to be restrained was also the day the Bottle Ship's Ridley turned violent. Watching herself being restrained while Madeline stood by, MB threw off her captors and, through telepathy, commanded the cloned forces and the facility's creatures to revolt against the Federation. Madeline alone escaped and sent the Baby's Cry distress signal.

MB watched Samus find Maurice Favreau's body in the Cryosphere. Samus tracked her to a storage room and persuaded her she meant no harm; the woman said a soldier had killed Maurice, and then the RB176 Ferrocrusher, driven by an unseen figure in Federation uniform, ambushed them and they were separated. They met again in the Bioweapon Research Center, where MB called herself Madeline Bergman, described the programs truthfully, named Adam Malkovich as the plan's author, and sent Samus to Sector Zero to destroy the Metroids. While Samus was away the Deleter attacked her; when Samus returned only James Pierce's body remained. The real Madeline, found in a holding cell in Room MW, panicked and released the Queen Metroid before Samus calmed her and learned the truth.

MB then came for Madeline with a Freeze Gun, intent on revenge: "...because of you, I failed." Madeline tried to make amends and called her Melissa; MB hesitated, then shoved her away, and as Federation troops burst in a Freeze Gun blast froze MB. Thawed, she sealed the room, woke the Desbrachians concealed as the alarms above the doors, and set them on the soldiers. Samus aimed at her, but it was Madeline who fired the Freeze Gun. On a colonel's order the troops finished her off, and the Desbrachians went still. Anthony Higgs secured Madeline's safety, and she fell asleep on the flight to Federation headquarters in Samus's gunship (Metroid: Other M).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/MB · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Madeline_Bergman · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/RB176_Ferrocrusher · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bioweapon_Research_Center · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Freeze_Gun · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M</sub>


### Other M: Nightmare (origin and the Bottle Ship)

*Keys:* Nightmare, Nightmares, gravity manipulation, gravity generator  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Bottle Ship, Cryosphere, Sector Zero, Sector 5, ARC, BSL, B.S.L., Nightmare-X, gravity generator, gravity, mask, bio-mechanical, biomechanical, black holes


Nightmare is a bio-mechanical organism designed for military applications, as the BSL computer later described it: a bulky hovering body fitted with mechanical devices, a plated mask over its face, and a gravity generator that lets it warp the gravity around it. Samus Aran first met it aboard the Bottle Ship, in the Cryosphere corridors leading to Sector Zero, where gravity itself had gone wrong, one passage heavier than normal and another reversed so that she walked on the ceiling. The creature descended from the ceiling when she tried to enter its chamber. Its spinning generator dragged at her movements and bent her shots aside, and her Missiles were useless until she froze the generator with the Ice Beam; then she hammered the mask with Missiles until it shattered, exposing a grotesque, baby-like face, and Nightmare collapsed. It rose again after Samus came back out of the approach to Sector Zero, once the sector had detached and exploded, now tearing open black holes that swallowed her fire, before she brought it down for good. Its body fell apart where it lay and had vanished by the time Samus returned for Adam Malkovich's helmet; what became of it is never shown.

A Nightmare was later kept by the researchers of the Biologic Space Laboratories station in the frozen Sector 5 (ARC), where Samus's computer commander traced heavy damage in the sector to it, described it as possessing uncommon power and the ability to manipulate gravity, and sent her to engage it on the assumption that the X had taken it; see Nightmare-X and the X-mimicked creatures of the BSL for that encounter. The Cryosphere's gravity anomalies outlasted Nightmare's death, and their cause was never found.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Nightmare · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Cryosphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bottle_Ship · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M</sub>


### Other M: The 07th Platoon and the Deleter

*Keys:* 07th Platoon, 7th Platoon, Seventh Platoon, the Deleter, James Pierce, Lyle Smithsonian, Maurice Favreau, K.G. Misawa, Misawa, Favreau, Freeze Gun, Freeze Guns


The Galactic Federation 07th Platoon was the six-man unit, commanded by Adam Malkovich, that answered the Bottle Ship's Baby's Cry distress signal alongside Samus Aran. Its troopers carried assault rifles and Freeze Guns, the weapon Adam authorized against the Brug Mass once ice proved its only weakness; the troopers concentrated Freeze Gun fire on each limb so that Samus could shatter it with a Missile. Members:
- Anthony Higgs, point man and the one member who treated Samus amicably from the start; the sole survivor.
- James Pierce, communications expert; nervous around Samus; assigned to investigate the Control Bridge but went to Sector 1 without authorization; asked by Maurice to hack the Exam Center's fragmented data, he said it would take some time, and the Center later exploded by unknown means.
- Lyle Smithsonian, special operations soldier (battle code 101), who kicked up and shot the Brug hiding under a dead scientist in the Control Room, provoking the Brug Mass, and who proposed burning through a shielded door that Samus opened with one Missile; sent ahead to the Exam Center and found ripped to shreds nearby, a trail of blood leading to the husk of Little Birdie, the creature that became the Bottle Ship's Ridley.
- Maurice Favreau, engineer (battle code 102); repaired the Exam Center computer and recovered the data exposing Madeline Bergman's bioweapon project; later found buried in the snow of the Cryosphere, the Deleter's first victim.
- K.G. Misawa, who wore glasses under his visor and said only "Got it"; sent to search the Residential Quarters; frozen with a Freeze Gun and incinerated in Pyrosphere lava, so that with no body recovered he is listed missing in action.

In the Cryosphere, after the unidentified woman (MB) told Samus that a soldier had killed Maurice and an unseen figure in Federation uniform attacked them with the RB176 Ferrocrusher, Samus concluded that the killings were the work of a thinking being, not the fauna, and named the traitor in the platoon the Deleter. The planners behind the bioweapon program had sent one of their own into the unit. She saw him, in Federation armor, dispose of Misawa's frozen body in the lava, and later pursued him from the Pyrosphere through the Main Elevator and the Biosphere into the Bioweapon Research Center, where he hid. Contact with Adam was broken in the same period; he was last seen looking over his shoulder as a shot was fired, and who fired it is never shown. The Deleter finally confronted MB, whom he took for the scientist Madeline Bergman, in the Research Center; the room went dark, a single shot was fired, and when Samus returned James Pierce lay dead with his visor smashed. MB came away with a Freeze Gun, presumably his, which later passed to Madeline. No one ever names the Deleter aloud, but Pierce's death at that moment makes him the killer in all but name (Metroid: Other M).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_07th_Platoon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/The_Deleter · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/James_Pierce · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Lyle_Smithsonian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Maurice_Favreau · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/K.G._Misawa</sub>


### Other M: The Adam AI (BSL computer)

*Keys:* Adam AI, ADAM, Computerized Adam, Computer Adam, ship's computer, ship computer, navigation computer, gunship computer, gunship AI  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* AI, computer, BSL, B.S.L., Fusion, ZDR, Dread, SR388, Lady, uploaded, Raven Beak, Navigation Room, Navigation Rooms, SA-X, Operations Room


During the BSL incident (Metroid Fusion), the gunship the Galactic Federation issued to Samus Aran carried a computer that acted as her commanding officer; following its orders was a condition of taking the ship. Samus named it Adam because it seemed to carry Adam Malkovich's personality, without knowing that it was him, and she found its commands blunt, something she had to bear. It directed her through the station's Navigation Rooms: proceed to the Quarantine Bay, exterminate the X before they reached the six breeding environments ("We must keep the X out of the breeding environments at all costs"), shut down the Main Boiler with about six minutes to spare, restore power to the Reactor Core. Its warnings of "Be careful" recalled the man she had known.

After Samus found the Restricted Laboratory, the computer admitted that the Federation had secretly bred Metroids aboard the station, "For peaceful application only, of course," and had studied forcing their growth: "Imagine...creating an Omega from a larval Metroid in days..." It reported that there were now no fewer than ten SA-X aboard and ordered Samus to leave the station to Federation forces, who meant to capture the SA-X for military use; on Federation orders it had already withheld support data and the finished Plasma Beam modification to keep her from engaging the SA-X. When she resolved instead to destroy the station and every X aboard, it locked the Navigation Room doors. Samus addressed it as Adam and told it that Adam Malkovich had died saving her life. The computer relented, and she understood that it carried his uploaded mind, placed in the BSL navigation computers and in her ship. Its last orders sent her to the Operations Room to adjust the station's orbit to intercept SR388, vaporizing the X on both, and ended in the real Adam's words: "Move quickly, and stay alive. That's an order! Any objections, Lady?" When her gunship arrived on its own to lift her off before impact, it was the Etecoons and Dachoras she had freed on the Habitation Deck who had moved it; Adam gave them a berth aboard.

He remained the gunship's computer afterward. The same mind, called ADAM, accompanied her during the ZDR mission (Metroid Dread), where Raven Beak impersonated it for most of the mission; see Raven Beak.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Adam_Malkovich · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Computerized_Adam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Etecoon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion</sub>


### Other M: The Bottle Ship

*Keys:* Bottle Ship, Bottleship, Biosphere, Pyrosphere, Cryosphere, Sector Zero, Bioweapon Research Center, Room MW, Main Sector, Exam Center, Geothermal Power Plant  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Bottle Ship, Bottleship, Sector, Sectors, Adam, Madeline, Bergman, platoon, Deleter, Ridley, Metroid, Metroids, Zebesian, Zebesians


The Bottle Ship was one of three vessels built by the Space Development Corporation for interplanetary colonists; after the first two were retired, the last passed to the Galactic Federation, which refitted it into three enclosed habitats and secretly turned it into a facility for breeding bioweapons, in violation of Federation protocol, under Dr. Madeline Bergman. It housed cloned Zebesians, a cloned Ridley, the android MB, and the Metroids of Project Metroid Warriors. The Main Sector is entirely mechanized; the three habitat sectors are simulated environments, each built around a dome and divided into four blocks. The Main Elevator reaches every sector except Sector Zero.
- Main Sector: the hub, unpowered when Samus Aran arrived. It holds the Landing Site where her gunship and the 07th Platoon's ship set down; the Control Room, where the Brug Mass attacked and from which Adam Malkovich gave his orders and authorized Samus's weapons; the System Management Room, whose terminal restores power; the empty Residential Quarters; and the Control Bridge with the ship's navigational equipment, James Pierce's assignment and later Phantoon's haunt. Navigation Booths let Samus record her progress and restore her energy.
- Sector 1, Biosphere: a lush jungle watered by waterfalls and frequent rain, home to Kihunters, Ghalmanians and Griptians, with cyborg Zebesians in its Exam Center, where the platoon learned of the bioweapon project and near which Lyle Smithsonian died. It holds the entrance to the Bioweapon Research Center.
- Sector 2, Cryosphere: incessant snowfall, massive icicle stalactites and frozen lakes. Maurice Favreau's body lay in its snow, MB was first glimpsed here, and a corpse bearing Metroid predation marks puzzled Samus ("Metroids... here? Impossible."), since Metroids cannot survive cold. Passages of distorted gravity, one heavier than normal and one reversed so that one walks on the ceiling, lead past Nightmare to the only entrance of Sector Zero.
- Sector 3, Pyrosphere: a sea of lava crossed by rock platforms, an active volcano and the Geothermal Power Plant where Ridley revealed himself; home of Vorash, Magdollites, Asboreans, Dessgeega and Super Zebesians.
- Bioweapon Research Center: sentry drones, Rinkas and Super Zebesians guard its corridors; one chamber held Ridley's dried husk, and Room MW, the largest room, holds six specimen tanks, holding cells and the Queen Metroid.
- Sector Zero: absent from every plan, "a place like Tourian" in MB's words, holding the Metroids bred so that they cannot be frozen. Adam triggered its self-destruct protocol; it detached and exploded.

The Baby's Cry signal, "A common SOS with the urgency of a baby crying," drew Samus and the 07th Platoon to the ship; to Samus it was as though it was crying specifically for her. After the Bottle Ship incident the Federation marked the ship for destruction. Samus returned once more for Adam's helmet, destroyed Phantoon on the Control Bridge, and escaped in her Zero Suit as the ship's self-destruct protocol was triggered remotely (Metroid: Other M).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bottle_Ship · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Main_Sector · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Biosphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Cryosphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyrosphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bioweapon_Research_Center</sub>


### Other M: The Bottle Ship incident

*Keys:* Bottle Ship incident, Other M, Project Metroid Warriors, Metroid Warriors, Baby's Cry, Brug Mass, Federation cover-up, orbital interception


Shortly after the destruction of Zebes (Super Metroid), Samus Aran woke in a Galactic Federation facility, was tested in its training room, and then answered the Bottle Ship's Baby's Cry distress signal. Aboard she found the Federation 07th Platoon under her former commander Adam Malkovich, who withheld the Federation's reasons from her as an outsider until she helped the platoon destroy the Brug Mass; she then placed herself under his orders, using her weapons only as he authorized them. In the Biosphere's Exam Center, Maurice Favreau recovered records of an illegal bioweapon program led by Madeline Bergman; a reptilian creature attacked until Anthony Higgs drove it off, Lyle Smithsonian was found dead, and the center exploded.

Sent after the creature into the Pyrosphere, where Vorash tried to eat her, Samus was quickly redirected to the Cryosphere to search for survivors. There she found Favreau's body in the snow, chased a woman who said a soldier had killed him, and was ambushed by the RB176 Ferrocrusher driven by an unseen figure in Federation uniform. She concluded that a traitor in the platoon was the killer and named him the Deleter. Back in the Pyrosphere she saved Anthony from a Rhedogian, and at the Geothermal Power Plant the creature she had been tracking proved to be a clone of Ridley, grown from cellular remains on her own Power Suit. Anthony was knocked toward the lava and presumed dead, contact with Adam ceased, and Samus saw a Federation-armored figure incinerate K.G. Misawa's frozen body.

Following the Deleter into the Bioweapon Research Center, she met the woman again, who called herself Madeline Bergman and laid out the plan: a special force of cloned Zebesians controlled by MB, an android built from Mother Brain's thought patterns, and Project Metroid Warriors, breeding Metroids in the hidden Sector Zero, where they could not be frozen. Samus understood that the planners meant to capture the Metroids and "delete" the rest of the facility, Ridley, the Zebesians and everyone who knew the secret included. The woman named Adam as the plan's author.

Fighting past Nightmare and the Cryosphere's gravity anomalies, Samus reached Sector Zero, where Adam shot her with a Freeze Gun, froze an infant Metroid, and explained that he had written his report at headquarters' request and had argued against the operation because of its dangers. He entered alone, and the sector detached and exploded. Following his last orders, Samus defeated Nightmare for good, found James Pierce dead, saw Ridley reduced to a husk by something unseen, and found the real Madeline in Room MW, where she killed the Queen Metroid. The ship was meanwhile under way toward Federation space; its PA announced, "This facility will intercept Galactic Federation orbit in approximately 30 seconds," the engines were cut just short of interception, and Federation troops boarded. Madeline revealed that Samus's guide had been MB, who had rebelled when the Federation tried to reprogram her. MB attacked with Desbrachians; Madeline froze her and the troops killed her. A colonel ordered Samus escorted off the ship, but Anthony, alive, invoked Adam's orders and the Federation Chairman's authority to protect Madeline, and the three left in Samus's gunship. Days later Samus returned to the ship, now marked for destruction, for Adam's helmet. The Federation's Metroid work resurfaced during the BSL incident (Metroid Fusion).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyrosphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Cryosphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/RB176_Ferrocrusher · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bioweapon_Research_Center</sub>


## Fusion


### Fusion: Biologic Space Laboratories research station (BSL)

*Keys:* BSL, B.S.L., B.S.L, Biologic Space Laboratories, Biologic Space Labs, BSL station, BSL research station, research station, Biologic
