<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes_Logbook · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Aether_Wildlife · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Dark_Aether_Wildlife · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Agon_Wastes_Wildlife · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Torvus_Bog_Wildlife · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Sanctuary_Fortress_Wildlife</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of Bryyo

*Keys:* Bryyo wildlife, Bryyo creatures, Bryyo fauna, Bryyonian wildlife, Reptilicus Hunter, Reptilicus Hunters, Hopper, Hoppers, Alpha Hopper, Gragnol, Gragnols, Gragnol Adult, Fargul Hatcher, Fargul Hatchers, Fargul Wasp, Fargul Wasps, Warp Hound, Warp Hounds, Korba, Korbas, Snatchers, Gel Puffer, Gel Puffers, Gel Ray, Gel Rays, Scorchbug, Scorchbugs, Shelbug, Gelbug, Bryyonian Shriekbat


Bryyo's surviving life is savage and Phazon-touched, catalogued by Samus during the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption) while she hunted the planet's Leviathan Seed.

- Reptilicus Hunters: Reptilicus chosen to become stronger, more resilient hunters. Phazon corruption gave them a cloak that only the X-Ray Visor pierces; fairly organized despite their primitive state, they wait in small groups for isolated prey in sealed courts, fighting with a chakram at range, a whip up close, and claws. They resist Fuel Gel and heat but lack the common Reptilicus's short-range teleport. Samus first fought them in Bryyo Cliffside's Grand Court. The Reptilicus themselves, six-limbed descendants of the Primals consumed by bloodlust, are recorded under Bryyo's history.
- Warp Hounds: teleporting hounds the Reptilicus tamed for war and hunting; they breathe thermal blasts and weaken in cold.
- Hoppers: fragile, venom-spitting pack hunters; the Alpha Hopper is tougher and territorial.
- Gragnols: weakly armored young that attack in groups; the Gragnol Adult carries an energy shield and a hard exoskeleton with a vulnerable tail.
- Fargul Hatchers: weapon-resistant brooders that birth swarms of Fargul Wasps, which defend their parent.
- Korba: vicious, resilient predators fixed for life to cavern ceilings above Bryyo's Morph Ball tunnels, where Samus met them most. Symbiotic Snatchers, worm-like swarmers, seize prey, inflate and float it up to the Korba, which expels its leftovers to feed them.
- Gel Puffers float on Fuel Gel vapor behind thin shells; Gel Rays leap from gel pools to feed; Gelbugs and Scorchbugs are gel-filled insects that explode when shot; Shelbugs of the Thorn Jungle bristle with spines.
- Bryyonian Shriekbats are bat-like dive-bombers. Nightbarbs here are aggressive chargers wrapped in an unstable energy field that overloads on contact and kills them. Geemers scavenge waste and spread disease behind shells only explosives crack.
- Atomics, creatures of pure energy, drift through the wreck of the G.F.S. Theseus and explode when destabilized.
- Phaazoids, floating Phazon life forms harmed only by Phazon-based attacks and splitting in two when struck down, appeared on Bryyo after Samus destroyed its Seed. Space Pirate Aeromines screen the Thorn Jungle.

The Korakk Beast, Mogenar, the Bryyonians and the corrupted hunter Rundas are recorded separately.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption_Logbook · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Bryyonian_Wildlife · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bryyo · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Reptilicus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Reptilicus_Hunter · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Korba</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of Crateria and Brinstar

*Keys:* Zoomer, Zoomers, Geemer, Geemers, Ripper, Rippers, Skree, Skrees, Metaree, Metarees, Waver, Wavers, Reo, Reos, Sidehopper, Sidehoppers, Side Hopper, Zeb, Zebs, Zebbo, Zebbos, Geega, Geegas, Beetom, Beetoms, Cacatac, Cacatacs, Zeela, Zeelas, Kago, Kagos, Fireflea, Firefleas, Samus Eater, Samus Eaters, Yapping Maw, Yapping Maws, Brinstar fauna, Crateria fauna, creatures of Brinstar, Zebes wildlife, Zebes creatures


Crateria is the storm-lashed surface of Zebes, pelted by meteors and acid rain; Brinstar is the humid cavern jungle directly beneath it, thick with carnivorous plants and home to more than sixty percent of the planet's life. Samus Aran grew up among these creatures during her Chozo upbringing on Zebes; per the manga, when a Skree dove at her companions she warned that the planet "has predators found nowhere else in the galaxy." She fought through both regions on her first Zebes mission (Metroid: Zero Mission) and again while retrieving the baby Metroid (Super Metroid).
- Zoomer and Geemer: small spiked crawlers that follow fixed paths along floors, walls and ceilings; per the manual they excrete a sticky fluid from their feet. Yellow Zoomers are the feeblest things on Zebes; the red Geemer is tougher and extends lethal spikes when threatened. Geemers are also scavengers that spread disease.
- Ripper: brainless, armor-shelled fliers that drift back and forth in straight lines. Beams and missiles bounce off them; only the Screw Attack or Speed Booster destroys one, though the Ice Beam freezes them into footholds. The jet-propelled Ripper II is faster and hits harder.
- Skree: bat-like ambushers whose wings double as drills. They hang from ceilings, drop on anything passing beneath, bore into the ground and explode in shrapnel; they attack Space Pirates as readily as Samus. The heavily armored Metaree of Brinstar is a tougher relative.
- Reo (also written Rio): clawed, fly-like divers that swoop from the upper walls and can withstand several hits; red ones are stronger than yellow, and groups are dangerous.
- Waver: fliers with two armored flaps that close into a shield; they flutter in wave patterns and ram.
- Sidehopper: thick-legged bipeds that pounce on intruders and can even hop along ceilings; among the strongest common creatures on Zebes.
- Zeb, Zebbo and Geega: insects nesting in Brinstar's ventilation pipes and air holes that pour out one after another to dive at intruders; individually feeble. Zebbos also haunt Crateria and Norfair's acid.
- Beetom: cricket-like leapers that latch on and cling like miniature Metroids until destroyed; beams cannot harm them, bombs and missiles can.
- Cacatac: walking cactus-plants that fire volleys of thorns; found from Brinstar to Norfair and Maridia.
- Zeela: eyestalked crawlers on fixed paths; a destroyed red Zeela leaves eyeballs that still sting.
- Kago: insect nests that erupt in swarms only if disturbed.
- Fireflea: bioluminescent drifters that are the only light in Brinstar's blackest rooms; they burst on contact.
- Samus Eater and Yapping Maw: a Rafflesia-like flower whose petals clamp shut on prey, and a buried pincer-worm that lunges from floors and ceilings to seize passers-by; only a Super Missile kills a Maw.
Skultera fish and tan Scisers swim Crateria's pools, green Alcoons rest beneath its ground, Boyon goo-balls bounce through Brinstar, and the Zero larvae of the Kihunters crawl its walls (see Zebesians and Kihunters). Kraid, Spore Spawn, the Torizo, the Etecoons and the Dachora are covered separately.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Brinstar · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Crateria · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zoomer · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Geemer · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ripper · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Skree</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of Elysia and the Pirate Homeworld

*Keys:* Elysia wildlife, Elysia creatures, SkyTown creatures, SkyTown drones, Pirate Homeworld creatures, Pirate Homeworld wildlife, Steambot, Steambots, Steamlord, Steamlords, Tinbot, Tinbots, Aeromine, Aeromines, Elysian Shriekbat, Elysian Shriekbats, Phaazoid, Phaazoids, Red Phaazoid, Puffer Mine, Puffer Mines, Phazon Harvester Drone, Harvester Drone


Elysia's wildlife is mostly machinery. The Chozo built SkyTown's drones and left the Elysians to keep them; after Dark Samus's Leviathan struck, Phazon corruption turned much of it hostile, as Samus found during the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption).

- Steamlords command SkyTown's worker drones. Chozo-built to repair fallen machines, they are lightly armored, cloak themselves and emit localized EMP fields that scramble visors. Samus met three: in the Steambot Barracks she could strike one only while it paused to repair a Steambot; on Skybridge Hera the bridge collapsed beneath the second; the X-Ray Visor exposed the third in Ballista Storage.
- Steambots are steam-powered, durably plated combat units that fight only under a Steamlord, armed with kinetic-beam pistols, missile racks and tool-arms. Destroyed, they collapse into heaps a Steamlord rebuilds; high heat renders them inert beyond repair.
- Tinbots, light-metal drones converted to security patrols, carry weak kinetic pistols; explosions or a Boost Ball send them sprawling and a charged Plasma Beam shot melts them, though they can enter Hypermode.
- Elysian Shriekbats are Chozo machines built to mimic real Shriekbats: they hang beneath the floating facilities in small flocks and dive-bomb intruders, detonating on contact. Their original purpose is unknown.
- Aeromines are Space Pirate drones deployed in hexagonal groups of eight (also on Norion, Bryyo and the G.F.S. Olympus) that attack one at a time and self-destruct at close range. Shield and beam share one power source, so a firing Aeromine drops its shield; the Pirates executed the first design team for the flaw.
- Phaazoids and the stronger Red Phaazoids, floating Phazon life forms immune to conventional weapons that split when destroyed, drifted through SkyTown after Samus destroyed Elysia's Seed. Phazon Metroids bred in the Pirate Xenoresearch laboratories are recorded separately.

The Pirate Homeworld, lashed by acid rain that eats through armor, is native ground for Puffers. The Space Pirates cybergenetically altered them into Puffer Mines, Phazon-gas-filled patrol animals that rupture on contact in a toxic cloud few attackers survive. The Phazon Harvester Drone, a Goliath-class heavy assault drone refitted with a Harvesting Beam Cannon and anti-aircraft missiles, has a hull no man-portable weapon penetrates; Samus stunned its sensor array, overloaded its anti-air system and called in her gunship's missiles to destroy it. Phaazoids appeared here too once the final Seed fell. Omega Ridley, Gandrayda and the Pirate Commander are recorded separately.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Steambot · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Steamlord · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tinbot · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aeromine · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Elysian_Shriekbat · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaazoid</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of Maridia and the Wrecked Ship

*Keys:* Mochtroid, Mochtroids, Evir, Evirs, Owtch, Owtches, Puyo, Puyos, Powamp, Powamps, Zoa, Zoas, Tatori, Tatoris, Oum, Oums, Choot, Choots, Sciser, Scisers, Skultera, Skulteras, Covern, Coverns, Koben, Atomics, Work Robot, Work Robots, Maridia fauna, creatures of Maridia, Wrecked Ship creatures


Maridia is the flooded quarter of Zebes: underwater tunnels, sandy caverns and quicksand floors beneath Brinstar, submerged by an ancient flood, where the Space Pirates built a sunken research facility and where Draygon and Botwoon lurked. The Wrecked Ship is a crashed vessel on Crateria's surface, powerless and haunted by Phantoon when Samus Aran explored it (Super Metroid). Samus crossed both while hunting the stolen baby Metroid; she could not fight Maridia's water until she took the Gravity Suit from the Wrecked Ship.
Maridia:
- Mochtroid: the Space Pirates' first, failed Metroid clones, frail things that can drain energy but cannot latch on; per the manual, "easy to defeat." Meeting them was Samus's first evidence that the Pirates were breeding Metroids on Zebes.
- Evir: hard-shelled drifters that sink down sand currents, firing projectiles even while buried; a swarm attended Draygon and carried its corpse away.
- Owtch: hard-shelled spike-crawlers that burrow into sand when they sense danger.
- Puyo: small, smooth green blobs that hop in short arcs at intruders.
- Powamp: placid green gas-sacs that inflate when threatened and serve as anchors for the Grapple Beam.
- Zoa: translucent blue shrimp that emerge from the sand one at a time and dart in threes.
- Yard: clawed snails that climb any surface and withdraw into armored shells; only explosives harm them.
- Tatori: a turtle-like giant easily mistaken for a mossy rock, violently protective of its young; standing on it makes it rise and spin in its shell.
- Oum: nautilus-like rollers with indestructible shells and lashing tentacles; only three were ever seen.
- Bull: hollow, gas-filled spore sacs that vent gas to ram intruders; they also drift through the Wrecked Ship.
- Choot: flat amphibians that leap straight up and glide down onto passers-by, kin to SR388's Chute Leech.
- Sciser and Skultera: red armored crustaceans climbing the underwater walls (tan ones live in Crateria, green on land), and bony armored fish on fixed patrol routes; Sciser remains litter Draygon's lair.
Wrecked Ship:
- Covern (Koben): skull-clusters described by the manual as "souls of shipwrecked creatures" that "wrap themselves around intruders." They materialize on top of intruders while the power is out and vanish for good once it returns.
- Atomic: beings of pure energy sealed in glass chambers until the power returns, then drifting free to shock anything they touch.
- Work Robot: slow, cylindrical bipedal machines, inert until the ship wakes, then firing ring-beams at anything that moves; indestructible.
Kago nests, Cacatacs, Yapping Maws, Boyons and a lone orange Geemer round out the two regions. Phantoon, Draygon and Botwoon are covered separately.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Maridia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Wrecked_Ship · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mochtroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Evir · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Owtch · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Puyo</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of Norfair

*Keys:* Novas, Sova, Sovas, Gamet, Gamets, Geruta, Gerutas, Multiviola, Multiviolas, Holtz, Squeept, Squeepts, Alcoon, Alcoons, Magdollite, Magdollites, Fune, Funes, Namihe, Namihes, Mella, Mellas, Memu, Memus, Puromi, Dessgeega, Dessgeegas, Desgeega, Norfair fauna, creatures of Norfair, Norfair wildlife


Norfair is the volcanic underworld beneath Brinstar: lakes of magma, superheated caverns and streams of boiling acid, with Ridley's lair in its depths and, in later years, a sprawling Space Pirate base. Its fauna are heat-adapted and far more dangerous than Brinstar's. Samus Aran crossed Norfair on both of her Zebes missions, the second time past Crocomire and the Golden Torizo on the way to Ridley (Super Metroid); the Varia Suit's heat shielding is what makes the region survivable at all.
- Nova (also recorded as Sova): fire-backed crawlers covered, per the manual, in "spiky, fireproof wool." They creep along floors and ceilings dripping flaming fluid on whatever passes beneath; blue ones are weaker than yellow. Some were later taken to the BSL station and the Bottle Ship for study.
- Gamet: fliers with a super-hard shell that wait inside air holes or magma, then burst out in vertical stacks to dive at a target; red Gamets have twice the strength.
- Geruta: armored, hot-blooded fliers with huge forearms that swoop in wave-like glides and rake prey with their claws; purple is standard, red more resilient.
- Multiviola and Viola: Multiviolas are fiery orange orbs that careen through caverns bouncing off walls; the blue Viola is their larval form, bound to patches of rock. On Samus's first mission some carried smaller fireballs orbiting them.
- Holtz: armored hoverers of lower Norfair and Ridley's lair that hang still until something approaches, then swoop on jets of ignited bio-propellant, even through lava; missiles barely mark them.
- Squeept: crustacean-like leapers living in magma pools, jumping out to grab prey; hard to destroy but freezable into stepping stones.
- Dragon and Alcoon: seahorse-shaped fire-spitters that rise from lava, spit a substance that ignites in air and sink back. The Alcoon is a walking, bipedal form that rests beneath the ground, leaps out and spits three fireballs; red Alcoons live in Norfair, green ones in Crateria.
- Magdollite: hulking humanoids coated in lava that lurk with only their faces above the surface, then fling globs of lava from an outstretched arm; only Super Missiles or Power Bombs kill them.
- Fune and Namihe: eyeless serpents living inside the rock walls with only their heads showing, spitting globules that burst into blue flame. A Fune can be destroyed or frozen into a foothold; a Namihe can only be frozen briefly.
- Mella and Memu: tiny swarming fliers. Mellas haunt Norfair proper; the fiercer Memu infest Ridley's hideout and pass through walls.
- Puromi: fiery serpents living in an acid pool, indestructible.
- Dessgeega: larger, purple, spine-studded relatives of the Sidehopper, aggressive on sight, native to Kraid's and Ridley's hideouts.
Zebbos rise from Norfair's acid, Cacatacs, Kagos and Yapping Maws persist here, and Trippers (flat-backed Rippers) ferry whatever climbs aboard. Ridley, Crocomire and the Golden Torizo are covered separately.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Norfair · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Nova · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sova · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gamet · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Squeept · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dessgeega</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of Phaaze and the G.F.S. Valhalla

*Keys:* Phaaze wildlife, Phaaze creatures, Phaaze fauna, Phaz-Ing, Phaz-Ings, Phazon Grub, Phazon Grubs, Liquid Phazon, Phazon Shriekbat, Phazon Shriekbats, Phazon Nightbarb, Phazon Nightbarbs, G.F.S. Valhalla, GFS Valhalla


Phaaze is a sentient planet of living Phazon, source of every Leviathan, and everything that lives on it is built from or saturated with Phazon. Samus entered it fully corrupted at the end of the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption).

- Phaz-Ings are wall-crawling shape-shifters that rest as puddles of Phazon and rise into solid form only to hurl Phazon bursts. They cross any terrain and keep their distance. When solid they shimmer yellow and a single Grapple Lasso pull tears them apart; Samus also vented her own excess Phazon into them with the Hyper Grapple, destroying them. They move in groups on Phaaze and infest the derelict G.F.S. Valhalla, where they arrived with the Space Pirate boarders and survive on the ship's Phazon.
- Phazon Grubs are immune to Phazon's mutagenic effects and carry it in their bodies; their bite infects victims with corruption, making them plague bearers. Drawn to bioenergy, they lunge, and their young spawn in numbers from Liquid Phazon.
- Liquid Phazon is a semisentient mobile puddle, the adult form of the Grub, which spawns more Grubs, consumes Phazon in any form, releases toxins when threatened and reshapes itself to dodge attacks. Contact can be deadly. It was also found on Norion and aboard the Valhalla.
- Tangle Weed, the Tallon IV plant, grows Phazon-enhanced in Phaaze's Drop Shaft, where it damages as well as snares and evades; Samus cleared the shaft by shattering its red Phazon crystals with the Hyper Ball.
- Phazon Shriekbats are Phazon-dependent, more aggressive than their kin, and die in a Phazon explosion when they dive. Phazon Nightbarbs are a separate Nightbarb species with Phazon-based attacks.

The G.F.S. Valhalla, the Federation ship the Pirates gutted for Aurora Unit 313, also carries Atomics, Hopping Metroids and Miniroids. The Leviathan Infant in Phaaze's caves, the husks of the Metroid Prime creature found beside it, Phazon Metroids, Dark Samus and Aurora Unit 313 are recorded separately.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaz-Ing · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Grub · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Liquid_Phazon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tangle_Weed · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:G.F.S._Valhalla_Life_Forms</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of SR388

*Keys:* Hornoad, Hornoads, Tsumuri, Tsumuris, Needler, Needlers, Moheek, Moheeks, Moto, Motos, Gravitt, Gravitts, Ramulken, Ramulkens, Yumbo, Yumbos, Mumbo, Mumbos, Septogg, Septoggs, Senjoo, Senjoos, Gullugg, Gulluggs, Yumee, Yumees, Gawron, Gawrons, Halzyn, Halzyns, Chute Leech, Chute Leeches, Octroll, Octrolls, Blob Thrower, Blob Throwers, Glow Fly, Glow Flies, Skorp, Skorps, Skreek, Skreeks, Wallfire, Wallfires, Autoad, Autoads, Autrack, Autracks, Gunzoo, Gunzoos, SR388 fauna, SR388 creatures, SR388 wildlife


SR388, the Metroid homeworld, is a maze of caverns, acid pools and abandoned Chozo ruins whose machines still patrol. Samus Aran crossed all of it during her Metroid extermination mission (Metroid II / Samus Returns), with the native fauna harrying her between Metroid lairs. Years later, escorting Biologic Space Laboratories researchers on the surface, she shot a Hornoad and was infected by the X parasite that emerged from it; the planet itself was destroyed at the end of the BSL incident (Metroid Fusion).
Crawlers and hoppers:
- Hornoad: frog-like hoppers with clawed feet that lunge once they notice an intruder and lose interest if it moves away; the manual says only "be wary." Per Chozo observation, the Hornoad was the first creature ever seen taken over by an X.
- Tsumuri, Needler and Moheek: snail-, urchin- and anemone-like crawlers creeping along walls and ceilings; harmless unless touched, though a Moheek's tendrils "hurt badly."
- Moto: armored, fiercely territorial beasts that charge anything entering their ground; soft only at the rear.
- Gravitt and Ramulken: a hermit-crab that hides in sand disguised as spikes and stabs with clawed feet, and a heavily armored one-eyed crustacean whose only weak point is its underbelly and which rolls into a ball to charge.
Fliers:
- Yumbo, Mumbo, Septogg and Senjoo: weak fliers patrolling back and forth; the Yumbo's spinning spiked face wounds on contact, the docile Septogg can briefly bear Samus's weight, the Senjoo rams with rotating spiked rings.
- Gullugg: big-eyed, bat-winged circlers that strike hard; on the later mission they hurled themselves like spiked bullets.
- Yumee and Gawron: nest insects that erupt one after another, the Gawron with a ring of spikes around its mouth.
- Drivel and Skreek: bat-like hunters of the deepest caverns that drop corrosive, explosive saliva, and lava-lurking ambushers that leap out screeching and spit paralytic blobs.
- Halzyn: helium-shelled drifters, mostly harmless until X infection taught them to snap shut and drop.
Ambushers:
- Chute Leech: flat, camouflaged leapers that lie dormant until prey passes, then spring up and glide down onto it; on the later mission they boiled out of ground fissures. The Octroll, a cephalopod relative, floats in acid pools and flutters up when startled.
- Blob Thrower: a huge rooted flower that stretches upward and spews spores (Blob Swarms, insect-like on the later mission).
- Glow Fly and Skorp: a wall-clinging glower that is the only natural light underground and leaps when threatened, and a wall-burrowing arachnid with sawblade claws.
Chozo machines: Wallfire (bird-shaped wall sentries spitting fireballs), Autoad (hopping robots "out of control"), Autrack (laser guardians with extending necks), Autom (flamethrower maintenance units), Gunzoo (armored security drones, later riding laser rails) and Shirk (spiked ramming drones). Arachnus, the Diggernaut and every Metroid form are covered separately.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hornoad · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tsumuri · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Needler · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Moheek</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of Tallon IV

*Keys:* Tallon IV wildlife, Tallon IV fauna, Tallon IV creatures, Sheegoth, Sheegoths, Baby Sheegoth, Magmoor, Magmoors, Blastcap, Blastcaps, Tangle Weed, Jelzap, Jelzaps, Triclops, Flickerbat, Flickerbats, Glider, Gliders, Bloodflower, Bloodflowers, Puddle Spore, Stone Toad, Scatter Bombu, Pulse Bombu, Plated Beetle, Lumigek, Lumigeks, Puffer, Puffers, War Wasp, War Wasps, Shriekbat, Shriekbats, Zoomer, Zoomers, Geemer, Geemers


Tallon IV's wildlife is known chiefly through the Logbook scans Samus made during the Tallon IV incident (Metroid Prime), when the Leviathan's Phazon was already warping the planet's ecosystem.

Tallon Overworld and Chozo Ruins:
- Beetles burrow through rock and erupt underfoot; the armored Plated Beetle can be hurt only from behind.
- Zoomers and Geemers are small spined wall-crawlers; a threatened Geemer pulls its head into its carapace.
- Blastcaps are toxic mushrooms that rattle a warning, then detonate at the slightest touch in a cloud of venomous fumes. Their spores ride solar winds and have reached Alinos in the Alimbic Cluster.
- Tangle Weed and Venom Weed are barbed, dimly sentient plants: Tangle Weed can only slow larger creatures, while Venom Weed lures prey and digests what it kills.
- Bloodflowers spew toxic spores from mouth-nodules; Seedlings fire dorsal spines; Sap Sacs and Aqua Sacs burst when struck.
- Jelzaps top the aquatic food chain: two armored skeletal halves bound only by electrical impulses, the upper carrying the nervous system, the lower the heart. One lures prey with its exposed core and snaps shut. Tallon Crabs swarm; Aqua Reapers are blind underwater tentacles.
- Reaper Vines are scythe-tipped tentacles rooted in rock. Stone Toads sit motionless in stone armor and swallow prey whole.
- War Wasps dive-bomb with venomous stingers and rarely stray from their hives; Ram War Wasps share a hive mind and Barbed War Wasps launch their stingers.
- Eyons are immobile eyes that fire energy beams; Plazmites hoard heat; Shriekbats roost on ceilings at an internal temperature near 121 degrees Celsius and explode when they dive. Pulse Bombus and Scatter Bombus are energy beings harmed only by electrical attacks. Scarabs, Plated Parasites, the Oculus and Plated Puffers complete the ruins.

Magmoor Caverns: the Magmoor is a fire-breathing lava serpent that tracks prey by smell and suffers in cold. Puffers drift gas-filled and rupture into lethal meta-viprium gas. Triclops are hard-shelled hunter-gatherers whose three-part mandibles mistake Samus's Morph Ball for rock and haul it to a storage pit; only a Morph Ball Bomb fed to one kills it. Puddle Spores are lava mollusks that fire energy globules; Grizbies carry superheated carapaces; Burrowers surface at tremors.

Phendrana Drifts: the Sheegoth is the supreme predator of the Drifts. Crystals on its back absorb energy and fire it back, its frigid breath freezes prey, and only its mouth and soft underbelly are open to harm; it hyperventilates after breathing. Baby Sheegoths turn to shield their weak backs. Samus fought an adult and its young guarding the Wave Beam in the Chapel of the Elders. Flickerbats are optically camouflaged scavengers visible only to the X-Ray Visor. Gliders are docile fliers with magnetic undersides; Samus latched onto them with the Grapple Beam to cross chasms, the basis of the sport called Glider Riding. Ice Shriekbats, Ice Beetles, Ice Burrowers, Crystallites and Ice Parasites fill the cold niches.

Impact Crater: Lumigeks, Phazon-charged reptiles that move in swarms.

Chozo Ghosts, Tallon Metroids, Flaahgra, Thardus and Meta Ridley are recorded separately.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Logbook · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Tallon_IV_Wildlife · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Impact_Crater_Wildlife · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_IV · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sheegoth · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Magmoor</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of the Alimbic Cluster

*Keys:* Alimbic Cluster creatures, Alimbic Cluster wildlife, Alimbic wildlife, Psycho Bit, Psycho Bits, Greater Ithrak, Lesser Ithrak, Ithrak, Ithraks, Petrasyl, Petrasyls, Quadtroid, Quadtroids, Voldrum, Voldrums, Arctic Spawn, Fire Spawn, Crash Pillar, Crash Pillars, Trocra, Barbed War Wasp, Barbed War Wasps, Alimbic Turret, Alimbic Turrets


The Alimbic Cluster's life forms were logged by Samus during the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters), on worlds the vanished Alimbics had seeded with sentry machines as well as animals.

Celestial Archives:
- Lesser Ithraks are nocturnal ceiling-clingers, fierce when disturbed; the rare Greater Ithrak looks identical, but its impenetrable frontal hide makes head-on attack useless, leaving only its rear open.
- Petrasyls are green, tentacled drifters that follow simple paths; harmless at a distance, they secrete a corrosive toxin that burns through almost any material on contact.
- Quadtroids, engineered from leech and reptile DNA, crawl on almost any surface, pounce and latch on to drain biological energy; only a Morph Ball Bomb breaks their grip. They also haunt Arcterra and the Vesper Defense Outpost.
- Psycho Bits are levitating spherical sentry droids with 360-degree sensors and short-range teleportation that emerge in swarms from blue replicator devices. The v1.0 fires energy blasts (its green model carries a heavy gun on a nanoscale nuclear reactor); v2.0 drains energy with an electromagnetic pulse; v3.0 on Alinos spits superheated magma; v4.0 at the Vesper Defense Outpost freezes prey with supercooled plasma.
- Voldrums are fast rolling robots with twin guns; Electro, Ice and Magma Voldrums each favor one element and fear its opposite. Guardians are androids that hunt in packs, and Crash Pillars are dormant cyborg sentries that levitate and smash intruders.

Alinos: Fire Spawn lurk in lava pits hurling fireballs; Red Barbed War Wasps fire igneous projectiles; War Wasps guard hives whose royal jelly is prized; Zoomers breed fast and carry flesh-eating bacteria; Geemers scavenge; Blastcaps and Shriekbats, both Tallon IV natives, have spread here.

Arcterra: Arctic Spawn are territorial cold-dwellers that hurl icy shards; Blue Barbed War Wasps, frozen-environment mutants, fire cold projectiles; Barbed War Wasps swarm in large numbers.

Oubliette: Trocra are unstable crystal colonies that detonate on contact.

Alimbic Turrets (v1.0, v1.4, v2.7), Cretaphids and Slenches are Alimbic defense machinery guarding the Octoliths; Gorea and the rival hunters Kanden, Spire, Noxus, Trace, Weavel and Sylux are recorded separately. Samus's Logbook from the cluster is the only record of several of these species.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters_Logbook · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Alimbic_Cluster_Life_Forms · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Alinos_Wildlife · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Arcterra_Wildlife · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Celestial_Archives_Life_Forms · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Vesper_Defense_Outpost_Life_Forms</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of the Bermuda System

*Keys:* Bermuda System, Excelcion, Bion, Talvania, Goliath Beetle, Goliath Beetles  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* creature, creatures, wildlife, fauna, beetle, beetles, animal, animals, monster, monsters, native, life forms


The Bermuda System, once Federation territory, holds three planets, Excelcion, Bion and Talvania, each of which housed a Space Pirate cloaking generator shielding the battleship Doomseye during the Bermuda System incident (Metroid Prime: Federation Force). Its wildlife is thinly recorded. The Federation Force's missions there ranged from an icy region to a desert, and the only native animals named in published accounts are the Goliath Beetles, giant insects the Force fought alongside the Pirates. Metroids were present as well: the Pirates kept the jellyfish-like creatures, and a Metroid egg carried off by the Force later hatched into a larva when a figure resembling Sylux reached it. On Bion the Force learned the Pirates had acquired technology that enlarged living things, and Samus herself, captured and brainwashed, was grown to giant size before the Force defeated her and she in turn rescued them from the dying Doomseye. Nothing more specific about Bermuda's native animals is recorded; the Doomseye, General Alex Miles and the Federation Force's Mechs are recorded separately.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of the Bottle Ship

*Keys:* Kyratian, Kyratians, Himella, Himellas, Ghalmanian, Ghalmanians, Griptian, Griptians, Mighty Griptian, Dragotix, Rhedogian, Rhedogians, Desbrachian, Desbrachians, Brug, Brugs, Brug Mass, Fumbleye, Fumbleyes, Joulion, Joulions, Volfon, Volfons, Gigafraug, Gigafraugs, Groganch, Cyclaw, Cyclaws, Bottle Ship creatures, Bottle Ship fauna, Biosphere creatures, Cryosphere creatures, Pyrosphere creatures


The Bottle Ship was a Galactic Federation vessel built to breed bioweapons in sealed habitats: the tropical Sector 1 (Biosphere), the arctic Sector 2 (Cryosphere), the volcanic Sector 3 (Pyrosphere), the Main Sector and the Bioweapon Research Center. Much of its stock was cloned from Zebes, so when Samus Aran investigated the ship alongside Adam Malkovich's 07th Platoon (Metroid: Other M) she met species she had thought lost with the planet, bred tougher and smarter than she remembered.
Zebes clones:
- Reo, Geemer and Skree: Reos burst from nests of biomass and grip Samus until shaken off; Geemers follow their target instead of pacing; cloned Skrees do not self-destruct and dive again and again, and the Cyclaw is a mechanically enhanced Skree variant found only here.
- Zero: Kihunter larvae with hardened shells that leap at intruders and, if left alone, pupate into cocoons; Morph Ball Bombs flip them.
- Waver, Sidehopper and Dessgeega: Wavers charge with their shields raised; the hoppers pounce.
- Nova, Magdollite, Skultera and Rinka: fire-crawlers that cause small explosions (Pyrosphere), lava-coated hulks that gape impossibly wide, armored fish that latch on in the Biosphere's water, and ring-shaped Rinkas launched from emitters.
- Mella and Himella: flame-spitting swarmers and their ice-adapted kin, blue-bodied with snowflake wings and red eyes, firing icicles and latching on before exploding (Cryosphere).
Bottle Ship natives and designs:
- Ghalmanian: chameleon-like creatures that turn invisible, then headbutt, spit acid or grab with the tail (Biosphere).
- Griptian and Mighty Griptian: armadillo-like beasts that roll into a ball to ram, claw, pounce and burrow; the Mighty Griptian's armor withstands even the Screw Attack.
- Dragotix: large creatures, docile unless something enters their zone of perception (Biosphere and Pyrosphere); a Little Birdie's shriek once roused one.
- Groganch: tree-like giants whose bark hides them in the forest; they fire gas-filled globes, and a yellow orb is their weak point.
- Kyratian: large amphibian-like humanoids with electrical discharge coils on their backs that hurl electric orbs, slam the ground and leap on foes (Cryosphere).
- Fumbleye, Joulion, Volfon and Gigafraug, all of the Cryosphere: a one-eyed ceiling polyp with a hooked tail that grabs victims and flings them into water; small hovering water-flea creatures that shock and explode in chains; horned arctic beasts hunting in packs of three; and a huge charging biped.
- Rhedogian: horned, one-eyed flying behemoths that ram, swipe, launch homing projectiles and fire an eye laser that can kill outright. One stalked and ambushed Samus repeatedly; she saved Anthony Higgs from it in the Pyrosphere and took her Seeker Missiles from the one she finally killed.
- Desbrachian: dormant ovoid creatures that only a Power Bomb wakes, then teleport, grab and slam; MB loosed them on Federation soldiers.
- Brug: tiny purple swarmers that gather around an Emperor Brug into a single Brug Mass (Main Sector control room).
Fune and Namihe worms haunt the Biosphere's subterranean control room. Vorash, Goyagma, King Kihunter, Nightmare, the Ridley clone, the Queen Metroid, Phantoon and MB are covered separately.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Bottle_Ship_Life_Forms · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Sector_1_(Biosphere)_Life_Forms · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Reo · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Geemer · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Skree</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of the BSL station

*Keys:* Zoro, Zoros, Zoro-X, Zombie Researcher, Zombie Researchers, Yameba, Yamebas, Genesis-X, Geruboss, Geruboss-X, Geruda, Geruda-X, Gelon, Gelons, Geron, Memu-X, Zozoro, Zozoro-X, Sciser-X, Halzyn-X, Hornoad-X, Moto-X, Gold Sciser, BSL creatures, BSL fauna, BSL specimens, X-mimicked creatures, mimicked creatures


The Biologic Space Laboratories research station (BSL) orbited SR388 to study specimens from that planet, from Zebes and from farther afield in sealed habitats: Sector 1 (SRX, an SR388 analogue), Sector 2 (TRO, tropical), Sector 3 (PYR, volcanic), Sector 4 (AQA, aquatic), Sector 5 (ARC, arctic), Sector 6 (NOC, nocturnal) and the Main Deck. During the BSL incident (Metroid Fusion) the X parasites killed and mimicked nearly all of them, so the creatures Samus Aran met were X copies: identical in form but often larger, more aggressive or newly armed, each releasing an X she could absorb through her Fusion Suit. Samus knew many of the originals from Zebes and SR388.
Main Deck and SRX:
- Hornoad: the SR388 hopper; mimicked ones spit a damaging fluid, while the few uninfected ones never attack.
- Zombie Researcher: station crew infested by X (per the manual, probably X that absorbed a slime creature's DNA), shambling and weak, reanimated whenever an X re-enters the body. Adam Malkovich judged them useful to the X only as food and knowledge.
- Moto: territorial armored chargers, soft only at the rear.
- Halzyn: helium-shelled drifters that snap shut and drop to crush whatever is beneath.
- Yameba: floating amoeba-like X masses that settle on prey and drain it painlessly, merging into ever larger blobs.
- Genesis: large crawlers in the dark ceiling spaces, drooling caustic saliva on whatever passes below.
- Geruboss and Geruda: X-enhanced Gerutas. The Geruboss (SRX, ARC) has metallic claws and shrugs off beams; the Geruda (PYR) glides upright.
TRO: Zoro, the Kihunter larvae, as red Zoro-X and far tougher blue ones; Reo, Ripper, Dessgeega, Puyo (red and spiked here), Skultera, and Kago nests that an X swells into damaging columns.
PYR: Sova leaving trails of flaming droppings, Sidehopper, Squeept, and Fune and Namihe striking from holes in the walls.
AQA: Skultera in large and golden varieties, Evir with orbiting projectiles, Owtch, Powamp (nearly indestructible, blocking tunnels until frozen), Yard, and Sciser with thickened spiked shells and claws that fire plasma, clustering into Gold Scisers.
ARC: Ripper, Waver, Kago and the Chute, adapted by the X to the cold and armed with acidic vomit.
NOC: Memu, one-eyed swarmers that pass through walls, and Zozoro, large glowing cave worms clinging to ceilings.
Gelon (also rendered Geron): inert X masses blocking passages, cleared with missiles or Power Bombs. Zebesian and Kihunter copies, the Blue X of ARC and NOC, and the named hosts Arachnus, Zazabi, Serris, Yakuza, Nettori, Nightmare, Neo-Ridley and the SA-X are covered separately.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:B.S.L_Research_Station_Life_Forms · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Sector_1_(SRX)_Life_Forms · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Sector_2_(TRO)_Life_Forms · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Sector_3_(PYR)_Life_Forms · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Sector_4_(AQA)_Life_Forms</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of Viewros

*Keys:* Viewros wildlife, Viewros creatures, Viewros fauna, Phenoros, Varmis, Swim Snatcher, Swim Snatchers, Pop Crawler, Pop Crawlers, Pyremite, Pyremites, Snow Wolf, Snow Wolves, Keratos, Denzard, Denzards, Glottagropper, Glottagroppers, Shren, Shrens, Aurengh Crane, Aurengh Cranes, Polaleisoarala, Winged Lizard, Winged Lizards, Dartwing, Dartwings, Blue Krauk, Crystal Crawler, Crystal Crawlers, Snare Weed, Ghaspore, Ghaspores, Sicklefly, Sickleflies, Vaernan, Vaernans, Blow Hopper, Blow Hoppers


Viewros, the Lamorn world Samus was stranded on during the Viewros incident (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond), has wildlife that turned aggressive when the Lamorn tried to nurture the planet with psychic energy. Samus's Logbook sorts it by region; Grievers, the feral former Lamorn, are recorded separately.

Fury Green (jungle): Aurengh Cranes are fruit-eating birds flying vast distances in flocks; Sickleflies are scissor-jawed dragonflies that swarm over decay; Shimmerstraps are bark parasites drinking sap; Glister Beetles signal with light, which betrays them to predators; the Empress Aleu is a vivid blue butterfly; Red Relic Ants farm fermented leaves underground. Homing Spores drift after anything nearby and burst; Mireworms lure insects with a plant scent and swallow them whole; Ghaspores are hard-capped mushrooms vulnerable only while ejecting spores; Glottagroppers hide underground, shoot needles and leap on strong legs and stubby wings; Rootspurs lash with a vine; Bristleworts are slow six-legged carnivorous plants that fire thorns; Shrens build hives and spray acidic venom at intruders without pursuing. Carvex, the self-aware carnivorous plant, is recorded separately.

Volt Forge: Denzards, one-eyed arthropods that run on electrical charge drawn from the sunless factory.

Sol Valley (desert hub): Polaleisoarala are featherless birds powered by sunlight through a red membrane; Gullbats store water from succulents; Sand Crawlers cling to cliffs conserving moisture; Web Crawlers spin strong but flammable nests; Sand Shrens charge with needle jaws; the Varmis is a huge sand-swimming crustacean, nearly immune to firearms except at its unarmored tail tip, that surfaced in eastern Sol Valley late in Samus's search for the Master Teleporter Keys.

Ice Belt: Snow Wolves hunt in packs, surround prey and feed in rank order, but overheat easily; Snow Roaches draw kin with pheromones and glowing shells and burst when struck; Ice Crawlers never stop moving; Blue Krauks fly in groups behind an antenna-signalling leader; Dartwings glide blind toward sounds; the Keratos is a unique giant shelled mutant that spits icy boulders.

Flare Pool (volcanic): Vaernans melt rock with digestive fluid and consume the gas; Heatstraps feed on seeping Green Energy; Blow Hoppers float on gas, swallowing and expelling lava; Leaping Spitters flip from the lava scattering lava bombs; Swim Snatchers gather energy-rich material with their feelers, and one made off with an Energy Tank of Samus's; Pyremites launch themselves and self-destruct; Swarm Flyers rise from the lava and return to it to spawn; the Behemoth is an armored fire-wielding arthropod vulnerable only through its eyes; the Phenoros is a massive mineral lava-dweller that eats Green Energy.

Great Mines: Crystal Crawlers eat Deborah crystals and climb with their fangs; Samus anchored Psychic Energy swings on them. Metareettas melt minerals with acid; Winged Lizards glide harmlessly in the shade; Cave Shrens spit poison and burst when dying; Goura Ghaspores shed corrosive spores; Spin Dartwings and Cave Roaches swarm the dark; Snare Weeds paralyze with electricity and burn readily; Pop Crawlers hurl glowing thorns.

The Lamorn's Psy-bots, Flight Drones and Mecha Rats are machines, not fauna.


<sub>Sources: https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/missable-scans/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-machines/ · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Carvex</sub>


### Creatures: Fauna of ZDR

*Keys:* Klaida, Klaidas, Muzby, Muzbys, Vulkran, Vulkrans, Autclast, Autclasts, Obsydomithon, Obsydomithons, Autsharp, Autsharps, Autsniper, Autsnipers, Armadigger, Armadiggers, Batalloon, Batalloons, Dizzean, Dizzeans, Slaaga, Slaagas, Hecathon, Hecathons, Yampa, Yampas, Quetzoa, Quetzoas, Dropter, Dropters, Caterzilla, Caterzillas, Daivo, Daivos, Sabotoru, Sabotorus, Blindfly, Blindflyer, Slidle, Slidles, ZDR fauna, ZDR creatures, creatures of ZDR, ZDR wildlife


ZDR is the Chozo-held world where Samus Aran was stranded after Raven Beak stripped her abilities and the rogue E.M.M.I. hunted her (Metroid Dread). Its wildlife is native, ancient and, in the old Chozo laboratories, partly mechanical; Samus met most of it with her Arm Cannon, the Melee Counter and a well-timed slide. After the X were released from quarantine in Elun, creatures across the planet became X hosts, behaving as before but releasing an X when killed.
Artaria (the cold, cavernous depths where Samus awoke):
- Klaida: four-legged, unarmored grazers that never attack but hurt and shove anything that touches them.
- Ply: small fliers with an eyeball-like head that track a target, then charge with a spiked tail; they come in flocks. The Blindfly of Ferenia is a hardier variant seen after the X outbreak.
- Muzby: large, faceless two-legged brutes that drag heavy arms along the ground; their tough hide shrugs off weak shots, and Samus can slide between their legs when they raise their arms.
- Dropter: small winged lungers. Caterzilla: burrowing worms that ignore Samus but choke narrow tunnels (also in Cataris).
Cataris (where magma is circulated to power the planet):
- Vulkran: small surface-clingers that store magma and spit several shots when threatened.
- Autclast: Chozo robots whose side cylinders slam down, streaking fire along the ground.
- Obsydomithon: rock creatures dormant in ore-like form near magma, transforming to fire a high-heat burst that passes through terrain.
Dairon (a biological research site with failing lights):
- Autsharp and Autsniper: robots that launch from docks with a spinning energy blade, and small treaded robots that climb walls and ceilings to fire a precise, punishing laser.
- Armadigger: violent four-legged natives that charge, wheel and charge again.
- Slidle: wall-crawlers found here, in Burenia and in Ghavoran.
Burenia (a marine research site, lower half underwater):
- Batalloon: floating aquatic creatures, deceptively durable, that drift toward whoever hits them.
- Dizzean: small jellyfish, weak alone but swarming.
- Slaaga: creatures with a yellow acid-storing hump that fire acid with precision. Daivo (also in Ferenia): hard-shelled creatures that spew a Daivo Swarm.
Ghavoran (a sunlit underground forest):
- Hecathon: huge ancient fliers drifting between trees that rarely attack first but, when struck, emit a light that decomposes other creatures.
- Yampa: spider-like stalkers that keep their jaws shut until lunging for a bite; they cannot reach a ledge above or below their own.
- Quetzoa: mid-sized insect fliers that home in and charge at high speed.
Ferenia and Hanubia: Sabotoru, slug-like crawlers that rush to block the nearest door by spreading their bodies and extending spikes. Chozo Soldiers, Robot Chozo Soldiers, the E.M.M.I. and the great beasts Corpius, Drogyga, Escue, Golzuna and Experiment Z-57 are covered separately.
